# To my disciples

## To my disciples

**Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami** Collected letters of His Holiness Srila Niranjana Swami

## Introduction

First of all we would like to express our respect to His Holiness Srila Niranjana Swami, who mercifully leads us along the path of devotional service, inspiring by his example and instructions.

This book contains excerpts from letters by Srila Niranjana Swami, which he wrote to devotees in 1991-1998. It is well-known that our spiritual master gets so many letters. Very often, the questions that the disciples ask have been already answered by Guru Maharaj in his letters to other devotees. Therefore, Guru Maharaj wants to publish such books of letters regularly, so that each disciple can find the answer to the question he is interested in.

Our spiritual master hopes that books like this can partly satisfy the disciple's need for personal association.

We hope that reading this book will inspire you in devotional service and strengthen your faith in guru and Krsna.

This book was published due to the combined efforts of many devotees. We thank Radha Kunda devi dasi who gave us the materials and helped with advice; Gita Priya devi dasi and Varuni devi dasi for editing; Sri Radha devi dasi and Nanda Kumara dasa for final proofreading; Bhakta Vitaly Nezvinsky for the design; Srisha dasa for the printing and everybody else who took part in the publication of this book.

## Devotional Service

You ask why people from so many different cultures and religious backgrounds are renouncing their former commitments, and becoming attracted to Krsna consciousness. This is a very important point to understand, because the answer is the essence of our Krsna consciousness philosophy. Krsna consciousness transcends all cultural, religious, and social designations which pertain to the body. It is not a sectarian philosophy or lifestyle, because it teaches us to see all living entities, in all forms of life, as parts and parcels of the Supreme Lord Sri Krsna. By delivering Lord Krsna's message purely, His "all-attractive" features, which can attract all living entities, are pulling so many conditioned souls beyond their social and cultural barriers, to the realm of spiritual harmony in love and devotion to God. Love of God is the essence of all religious teachings, but, unfortunately, by the influence of the present age of Kali, we now find that this essence is being neglected and even lost in most religious traditions. Service to God has become tantamount to service to man's physical needs. However, Krsna consciousness is offering a "spiritual revival" to all cultural and religious forms of expression throughout the world, by directly engaging everyone in the Lord's loving service through the process of sankirtan yajna. In this age, this method of worship is considered to be the most direct method for satisfying the Lord.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Thank you for your two letters dated 04.09.91 and 12.09.91 respectively. I was very happy to hear your realizations about purity in devotional service. Srila Prabhupada has instructed us that "Purity is the force", and that simply means that if we are motivated purely then Krsna can do anything He likes through us. He likes nothing better than to fulfill the desires of His pure devotees, so we must simply desire to voluntarily preach Krsna consciousness to enlighten others and make them very, very fortunate.

We just had a class on this point here this morning. In the purport Srila Prabhupada said: "...The first-class devotee of the Lord attains this stage by devotional service, but despite his loftiness, for the Lord's satisfaction he may play the voluntary part of a preacher of the Lord's glory and dovetail all into devotional service, even mundane interest, just to give the neophytes a chance to transform mundane interest into transcendental bliss..." This is one of Srila Prabhupada's glories. He taught us that even higher than relishing our personal relationship with Lord Krsna in spontaneous devotional service is to voluntarily preach to push other living entities forward in their relationship with Lord Krsna. That voluntary spirit to please Lord Krsna, in this most confidential way, is that actual "force" which compels the conditioned souls to take up the natural process of devotional service. When we make that our genuine desire, Krsna will want nothing more than to fulfill it. So I pray to Krsna that your purity will increase more and more so that you can become His fully surrendered instrument to spread His glories.

Essentially this is one answer to your question about my relation with Srila Prabhupada. I always feel connected to Srila Prabhupada by always trying to remember that it is only by his causeless mercy that I can do anything in devotional service. By his completely pure desire to see me advance in Krsna consciousness, I have been given the opportunity to become an object of his unlimited compassion. Even though I have no qualification to engage in devotional service, his prayers to Lord Krsna can make me qualified. Without them, I have no hope. It is this causeless mercy upon this wretched soul which "forces" me into an eternal debt to do as he would want me to do. I have become so fortunate to be considered his servant, and therefore I must please him by making others fortunate, too.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

In the meantime, you still must render service, and you ask if you can dovetail your inclinations for the service of the Lord. Yes, it is possible, but it requires guidance. It is not that someone can do anything that he likes to do, and say that it's for Krsna. This is a great misconception. If service is meant to be rendered for Krsna's pleasure, then we must first learn what is pleasing to Him. Srila Prabhupada would often use the example that if he asks his disciple to bring him a glass of water, and instead the disciple goes off to get him a glass of milk, thinking that milk is nicer than water. That is not service. He would say, "I've asked for water and you're bringing me milk! If you're my servant then you'll do as I ask!" So, in the same way, it's not that someone can paint a picture any way that he likes, play music any way that he likes, or dance any way that he likes, and say that it's for Krsna. Of course, on the other hand, someone may paint pictures, play music, or dance for a livelihood, and from the money he makes, he can offer it to Krsna's devotees for them to use it to expand Lord Krsna's glories. That is called karma yoga, and is also service for Krsna.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

You ask about fanaticism in devotional service. The point is that one should be careful to not become so absorbed in following the rules and regulations that one forgets the purpose behind them. The rules and regulations are meant to help one become conscious of Krsna. Krsna consciousness is the ultimate goal- not perfection in following rules and regulations. To the degree that one is always remembering Krsna then he transcends all rules and regulations. However, that is a very advanced stage of devotional service and cannot be imitated. Therefore, one should also be very careful to not think that he can act always spontaneously in the name of service to Krsna, disregarding the rules and regulations, without actually being on that advanced stage of devotional service. That is also fanaticism and can lead to fall down.

In summary, it is not a fact that adhering to rules and regulations is unfavorable for devotional service. Ultimately, it is abandoned, but only after we rise to the platform of raganuga bhakti, or spontaneous devotional service. In the meantime, we must follow the rules and regulations, but we must also be flexible enough to not forget what is most pleasing to Krsna.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

I have before me two letters from you- one given to me in India (undated) and the other dated 24.02.91.

The feelings described in both are similar. You are expressing anxiety to become a surrendered servant of Krsna, while simultaneously realizing your own inadequacies. There is nothing wrong in thinking this way. In one letter Srila Prabhupada writes to a disciple who was feeling in a similar way:

... "But, for you this feeling of insufficiency is good for progress. No one should think that now I am complete. It is good to think that I am incomplete and useless. Actually Krsna is unlimited and our energy is limited. Actually we cannot serve the unlimited, but Krsna's unlimited mercy induces us to serve, and He accepts. Actually we are unworthy. He is so kind that He accepts our little service as if it were very big and great."...

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

The important thing is that you are serving. You can't serve Krsna on your own. Everyone needs help to approach Krsna. So now, Krsna is teaching you how to feel helpless so that you will depend upon what He offers in the way of assistance. That assistance comes comes from the both the Supersoul within, and through the instructions of the Vaisnavas. Just like Krsnadasa Kaviraja Goswami writes in the Caitanya Caritamrta, Antya [1.2](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-antya/1.2): "My path is very difficult. I am blind, and my feet are slipping again and again. Therefore, may the saints help me by granting me the stick of their mercy as my support."

Krsnadasa Kaviraja Goswami is praying for the stick of mercy from the Vaisnavas, so that he can be supported throughout the difficult path. That stick comes in the form of instructions. Take shelter of those instructions, by acting upon them. There will always be resistance from the mind, because we have been conditioned to identify with it for so many millions of births. But gradually your intelligence will become strengthened by following the rules and regulations, and it will become easier. So go on serving Krsna with everything that you have, and He will help you.

In regards to your service, you have asked if it is possible to heal the physical bodies of others and consider it as devotional service. Yes, it is possible, but first you must become strong in devotional service. Otherwise, it is very easy to forget who you are doing it for. There are always so many bodies that need to be healed, but no one can be healed without the sanction of Krsna. We must always be conscious of that. So first, become strong in Krsna consciousness, and then see what Krsna reveals for your future service to Him.

The best way to become strong is through preaching and distributing Srila Prabhupada's books. This activity helps you realize that everyone is in a diseased condition of life because they identify with their physical bodies. And the devotees are meant to become expert physicians to cure them from the worst type of disease, called "avidya" or ignorance of the soul. This cure is the permanent solution to their problems whereas the physical cure is only temporary. They will still have to accept one body after another, only to become repeatedly diseased, old, and finally dead. Unless you come to this realization, you will think that healing bodies is an end in itself. These bodies are meant to be used for Krsna, otherwise they are simply being wasted for sense gratification.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Regarding surrender to Krsna, this simply means that we give up our own ideas of how we want to serve Krsna, and accept the ways that Krsna wants us to serve Him. You will find as you mature in devotional service that there will be a tendency in you to resist doing what you are supposed to do according to Krsna's instructions. But, by the mercy of Krsna or His devotee, you can actually hear that it is superior to follow Krsna's directions, and you voluntarily accept that direction— that is surrender. Surrender must be voluntary; it cannot be forced. If we are willing to hear from superior authorities, this voluntary spirit can be brought out by their association.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

What you are experiencing is the realization that there are so many dirty things in the heart. This realization comes naturally through purification in devotional service. When we come into contact with devotional service, by the mercy of devotees, the cleansing process begins, and the first thing we should come to realize is just how fallen we are. If we think that we are pure in desire, then how will we actually become purified? There will be no endeavor to cleanse the heart. We won't feel the urgency to follow the regulative practices of Krsna consciousness, and then we'll gradually fall away due to inattentiveness. Even the most advanced devotees genuinely think that they have no love for Krsna. They genuinely feel themselves fallen and filled with material desires. But, for advanced devotees, these thoughts always keep them in full dependence upon Krsna and in full Krsna consciousness.

So don't be discouraged, that these subtle motivations are there. Maya's trick is to make you think that you can't become Krsna conscious because you have too many material desires. But you should know that there's no choice but to become Krsna conscious. And you will, if you just continue with this process and don't ever become discouraged. In the Srimad Bhagavatam [2.3.10](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-2/3.10) it is stated:

"A person who has broader intelligence, whether he be full of all material desire, without any material desire, or desiring liberation, must by all means worship the supreme whole, the Personality of Godhead."

Srila Prabhupada would quote this verse so many times to prove that even if we have so many motives, if we just continue to worship and serve Krsna, we'll become free from those motives.

In closing, I will also include this nice quote that Srila Prabhupada once wrote in a letter to one of his disciples:

... "But, for you this feeling of insufficiency is good for progress. No one should think that now I am complete. It is good to think that I am incomplete and useless. Actually Krsna is unlimited and our energy is limited. Actually we cannot serve the unlimited, but Krsna's unlimited mercy induces us to serve, and He accepts. Actually we are unworthy. He is so kind that He accepts our little service as if it were very big and great."...

"According to Srila Rupa Goswami, "When one is actually liberated from material contamination, he can always remember an eternal devotee in Vrndavana in order to love Krsna in the same capacity."

The point is that, first and foremost, one must have at least come to the stage of anartha-nivrtti. This means there is no material contamination whatsoever in his heart, and he is therefore on the liberated platform. Then, he may choose to follow in the footsteps of a particular associate of the Lord, so that he may develop his love for Krsna in the same way as the associate. It is not that a devotee or a group of devotees can put forward the idea that someone is qualified to begin emulating an intimate associate of the Lord, as a means for further development into the spontaneous levels of devotional service. One must already be on that spontaneous level, by having strictly followed the rules and regulations given by the spiritual master and sastra. Moreover, that spontaneous attraction can only be achieved by the "extraordinary" mercy of the Lord. It is not something that is so easily accessible. Spontaneous devotional service is transcendental to all rules and regulations, and therefore should never be imitated.

However, even if by some extraordinary mercy of the Lord, one achieves the spontaneous platform, it is also stated in the Nectar of Devotion, p.126:

"A devotee who is actually advanced in Krsna consciousness, who is constantly engaged in devotional service, should not manifest himself, even though he has attained perfection. The idea is that he should always continue to act as a neophyte devotee as long as his material body is there. (my emphasis) Activities in devotional service under regulative principles must be followed even by the pure devotee. But when he realizes his actual position in relationship with the Lord, he can, along with the discharging of regulative service, think within himself of the Lord, under the guidance of a particular associate of the Lord, and develop his transcendental sentiments in following that associate."

And on page 125 of the Nectar of Devotion it is also stated: "Spontaneous love of Krsna as exhibited by the Vrsnis and the denizens of Vrndavana is eternally existing in them. In the stage of devotional service where regulative principles are followed, there is no necessity of discussing this love, for it must develop of itself at a more advanced stage."

Since you asked for details on this question, I will finish this point with the following two quotes from Srila Prabhupada's letters to two of his disciples:

... "So this is a teaching to the common man that we should not take Krsna immediately as intimately as the gopis, cowherds boys, and other advanced devotees treated Him. In the beginning we must treat the Lord with awe and veneration. Later on when we are perfectly on the spiritual platform, we treat Krsna more intimately, and therefore we forget the exalted position of Krsna. But, we never imitate the Brijbasis from the beginning as do the sahaji- yas. In the beginning a devotee must follow strictly viddhi-marga or regulated principles.

When one is on the Svarupa siddha stage, the devotee can treat Krsna on an equal level or sometimes he becomes even higher than Krsna.

Just like Mother Yasoda treated Krsna as being completely dependent upon her. So these stages are appreciated in many advanced stages of devotional life."...

..."So far your question about the gopis, in the beginning there is no such question. In the beginning we have to follow the principles of devotional service rigidly, like chanting 16 rounds, regularly following the instructions of the spiritual master, which includes study, temple worship, sankirtana, like that.

And the more one becomes perfect at following these principles, the more his relationship with Krsna becomes manifest. Following in the footsteps of gopis comes in the perfectional stage.

First you come to the perfectional stage, then you talk of gopis..."

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

To answer your question... one must have enough appreciation that one has "actually" been saved in order to feel appreciation for the person who was instrumental for the saving. What does it mean to be saved? It means to have the opportunity to engage in devotional service. Devotional service is beyond being saved, just as we so oftentimes hear that it is beyond liberation. You may have so many duties to perform, but who is it that has brought you to the point where you have been able to realize (or at least catch a glimpse) that the highest duties in life are those aimed for the satisfaction of Krsna? Even though you may oftentimes forget about this, still, having once contacted Krsna through devotional service, ultimately you can never forget. Devotional service is so powerful that even one moment's engagement can open the door for eternal engagement. That person who has inspired us for devotional service is our savior. That person who is meditating on how to bring us to the lotus feet of Krsna is our savior. That person who is giving us the opportunity for devotional service is our savior. Without his blessings we have no hope for devotional service. Therefore, when we actually catch a glimpse of the glories of devotional service, we can begin to appreciate how wonderful is that person who is giving us the opportunity to engage in devotional service. Without the mercy of a "devotee" we can never understand devotional service.

Therefore, although I am emphasizing the need to accept your duties related to family, beyond that I am always impressing the need to maintain a consciousness of Krsna. This duty lies beyond all other duties— beyond your marriage, beyond your daughter, and beyond, as you say, maintaining your sanity. Krsna will maintain your sanity. Without Krsna consciousness, all other duties are simply a waste of time. You must become just like the woman who is always thinking of her paramour. She is attentive to her duties, but in her heart she is thinking of her lover. Similarly, you must develop the art of remembering Krsna. That is the best way to maintain your sanity. If you try to maintain your sanity without Krsna, then you will be destined to failure. It is simply not possible. When the pressure builds up, take shelter in any one of the nine processes of devotional service. Sanity will automatically come. If you take shelter in something artificial, then it actually won't lead to sanity. Things may seem to get better, but that is only an illusion.

Regarding your further progress in spiritual life, at this stage I urge you to become more thoughtful about the actual service itself, and be careful to not confuse "any" service rendered with "devotional" service. Just yesterday I was reading a conversation Srila Prabhupada had in Mayapura in February, 1977. He was explaining that to be Vaisnava is not such a cheap thing. We must go through progressive stages before we can actually manifest the qualities of Vaisnava. He said that below brahmana there is no question of Vaisnava. And to come to the brahmana stage there must be "hearing, hearing, hearing." He repeated it three times. Then he said, "Without becoming brahmana, nobody is interested to hear. By hearing, he becomes fixed up in devotional service." He went on to say that when one is fixed up as a devotee, the lower qualities of passion, ignorance, lusty desires and greediness all go away.

This is progress. It is not that just by performing "any" service you will automatically advance. Yes, I know I always preach that we should always see how Krsna is arranging everything in our lives, and that if we are asked to do a particular service, we should see it as Krsna's arrangement. But a brahmana, as Srila Prabhupada explains, is interested to hear. He never abandons hearing because there is too much other service. Hearing also means learning the philosophy and developing one's power to distinguish between what is devotional service and what is service mixed with karma or jnana. First one must become situated properly in one's prescribed duty, and then from that platform make progress. Not that we just do "any" service. We must perform our prescribed duty and never abandon this hearing, chanting, and remembering, which is our most important duty in all stages of life.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

I am relieved to hear that your workload has been reduced to moderate levels. If you can remain steady at that, then I am confident that nobody will insist that you increase your workload to the point where it becomes intolerable for you. Your steadiness in your service will depend largely upon your ability to take shelter in hearing. In this regard, I am happy to hear that you are regularly reading from the 7th Canto of Srimad Bhagavatam. I also greatly relish the 7th Canto, and in fact just last month I decided to make a change from my usual commitment to give Srimad Bhagavatam class on the verse that is being studied locally. I am now giving Srimad Bhagavatam class no matter what city I am in, by reading a verse each day in the 7th Canto, Chapter 5, entitled, " Prahlada, the saintly son of Hiranyakasipu". In this way, I am getting the opportunity to progressively read from SB, along with the books that I read in the course of my travels. There are so many wonderful verses and purports in these chapters of the 7th Canto. It is a wonderful meditation. I have been particularly inspired by the verses where Prahlada Maharaja speaks about the power of the illusory energy, and her ability to cause those who are under her spell to think in terms of "friends and enemies". I have been giving many classes on this topic (as I have similarly done in the past). I want my disciples to learn how to behave like Vaisnavas, which brings us to your other point of concern.

You are right when you remember how serious I am about criticizing Vaisnavas. In Caitanya Bhagavat, Srila Vrndavana das Thakur writes, "If taking the side of one Vaisnava, a person blasphemes another Vaisnava, the blasphemer will perish. With one hand he serves the Lord, and with the other hand he tortures the Lord. How will any good come to him? The devotees are Lord Krsna's own body. One who knows this becomes very peaceful and very serious."(CB, Antya [4.391-393](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cb-antya/4.391)). I also cannot understand what good can be accomplished by publicly criticizing Narayan Maharaja. I am sure you are aware that I never indulge in such activities. Not only is there no good, but as Srila Vrndavana das Thakur has indicated, there is harm either for those who criticize or those who take sides from hearing such criticism. I have great respect for Narayan Maharaja's level of knowledge of the sastra, and I have no doubt that he is an elevated Vaisnava, but you should know that I have always kept a respectful distance from him, primarily because I am convinced that he does not place the same emphasis on his preaching that Srila Prabhupada placed. I never fault him or criticize him for the differences he may openly speak about when referring to some things that Srila Prabhupada either said or did. That is his realization, and he is not bound to see everything in the same way that we, as Srila Prabhupada's followers, see. Therefore, when I see those differences, I see them in the mood that Srila Vrndavana dasa Thakur has instructed us to see them. He also writes in the verses just prior to those that I have quoted above: "If one sees one Vaisnava quarreling with another Vaisnava, one should know the quarrel is not real. It is only Lord Krsna's pastime. Satyabhama and Rukmini quarreled, but the truth is they were in harmony. It only looked like they quarreled. In the same way the Vaisnavas never quarrel. Lord Caitanya arranges their quarrels for His pastimes." Of course, none of us personally witnessed Srila Prabhupada and Narayan Maharaja quarreling during Srila Prabhupada's manifest presence. However, if Srila Prabhupada were with us today, I do believe that Srila Prabhupada would not agree on the emphasis that Narayan Maharaja makes to those not qualified for the subject matter he speaks to the general masses. To illustrate this point just a little bit further, I will quote one letter which I sent to a godbrother last year on this subject.

"At this point, I am inclined to think that Narayan Maharaja's followers should serve in their own institution, and that they should face their own problems that will inevitably have to be faced when expanding Caitanya Mahaprabhu's mission in institutional form. In that respect, I have no doubt that Srila Prabhupada's vision, expertise, and training of his followers, far surpasses anything else on this planet. I hope that doesn't sound like a vain statement. But it's based upon my uncompromising faith in Srila Prabhupada's preaching vision."

It seems obvious that there are devotees who have gone to him for shelter. For whatever reason, they felt that his shelter was necessary for their further advancement in Krsna consciousness, despite the fact that Srila Prabhupada assured us that he has given us all that we need. In principle, I do accept that one's spiritual master can instruct and enlighten his disciple after he has physically departed through the mouth of another enlightened Vaisnava. But in principle, I am also completely convinced that one gets help from the Lord within the heart, by strictly following the instructions of his departed, initiating spiritual master. If I were to make such a fundamental shift in preaching Krsna consciousness as that which is being encouraged by Narayan Maharaja, I see that it would deviate me from the faithful execution of Srila Prabhupada's instructions-one of which is to preach within the institution that he so vigorously worked to expand and maintain for generations to come.

Therefore, in response to your question, "How to deal with this problem," the same principle will always apply. I request you to avoid listening to criticisms of Vaisnavas. Srila Prabhupada told us that the best way to avoid this is to simply get up and leave. However, on the other hand, you should also remember that I've instructed you not to take sides on the issues as well.

This also means that if those who glorify Narayan Maharaja do so with the agenda (either gross or subtle, intended or unintended) of deviating you from serving Srila Prabhupada's mission, you should also not side with them. We should always be eager to hear the glories of Vaisnavas, but not at the expense of minimizing the service of other Vaisnavas. Srila Prabhupada so perfectly expanded this point in the purport to SB [7.5.12](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-7/5.12) (again, in this wonderful section of purports in SB):

"...Everyone should be friendly for the service of the Lord. Everyone should praise another's service to the Lord and not be proud of his own service. This is the way of Vaisnava thinking, Vaikuntha thinking. There may be rivalries and apparent competition between servants in performing service, but in the Vaikuntha planets the service of another servant is appreciated, not condemned. This is Vaikuntha competition. There is no question of enmity between servants. Everyone should be allowed to render service to the Lord to the best of his ability, and everyone should appreciate the service of others. Such are the activities of Vaikuntha. Since everyone is a servant, everyone is on the same platform and is allowed to serve the Lord according to his ability..."

Therefore what need is there to take sides? Simply go on faithfully hearing from your spiritual master, and from those who are glorifying Lord Krsna and Srila Prabhupada by their own example and words. As I have tried to show by example, a disciple must have firm faith in the mission of his spiritual master. I expect those who have become my disciples to imbibe that same faith in Srila Prabhupada's mission. Moreover, I would hope that my example can also serve to help convince you and if need be, others, that we are capable of glorifying Srila Prabhupada without criticizing others. Let this be your instruction to remember and follow.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Regarding your potentially contradictory desire to "stay in this temple at the lotus feet of Sri-Sri Radha-Gopivallabha till the very end", and your request for my blessings and instructions on how to be more concentrated on the service of Sri Sri Radha Gopivallabha, I would therefore have to completely agree with…Prabhu. You should appreciate your good fortune of being nicely engaged in devotional service at the lotus feet of Sri Sri Radha Gopivallabha. They can certainly fulfill all desires, even those which may appear contradictory. Your safest position would be to let Them decide for you. Having full faith in Their decision, you can then proceed unreservedly to serve Them, daily meditating upon Their personal form and Their personal needs which They so kindly manifest solely for the benefit of Their servants.

This year in Mayapura, during the Deity installation ceremony of the Panca Tattva Deities, after the abhiseka I was requested to come onto the altar along with a few of my godbrothers to do the installation mantras requesting the Lord to appear and accept service. I was thinking, "Who am I to request the Lord to appear?" But then during the function, it became obvious that it was not me, but Srila Prabhupada, who was requesting Them to appear. And the very function which we performed behind the curtain was done with Srila Prabhupada's picture, first placing certain articles in his hands and then placing them on the hearts of the Deities, while chanting mantras meant to petition the Lord to appear. It was a wonderful meditation, and it helped me to realize how my spiritual master was giving me the opportunity to assist him in rendering such personal service to the Lord.

Similarly, as you know, Radha Gopivallabha were installed personally by Srila Prabhupada in 1971, and I began serving Them in 1973. I began personally cooking for Them in 1974, and in January of 1975, I remember performing my first arotik to Them. Although my service to Them on the altar did not last for long, still, I can clearly remember intimate times when the curtains were closed, and I would stand on the altar gazing into Gopivallabha's eyes offering prayers to Them to keep me engaged in Their loving service. From this letter I can see that Radha Gopivallabha are still kindly allowing me to serve Them. Please, therefore, go on with full concentration.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

There is one concept, however, which has caught my attention, and that is the point about souls joined together with other souls who are in another world. This "joining together" as I understand from Vaisnava teachings, means serving together. And to be serving together, both living entities must share the same object in their service. If one's consciousness is fixed continuously upon the lotus feet of the Lord, then it can be said that such a person is not in this world, but is always with Krsna. And Krsna is always surrounded by loving devotees. In that sense when a living entity has achieved a very advanced stage of devotion to Krsna, one of Krsna's loving devotees, a resident of the spiritual world, may then "join" with that devotee to train him in a particular service to Krsna. But as I said, we are talking about a very rare and elevated soul. Not a sadhaka bhakta, or one who is practicing the regulative principles of devotional service.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Yes. I know what you mean. There is no superficial explanation. Devotional service is Lord Krsna's internal potency. There is no way to gain access to devotional service by external maneuvers. We can only gain entrance by our sincere desire to render service. When that desire is awakened within the heart, the Lord's internal energy acts to give the living entity protection. You have been blessed with devotional service. Please continue to pray sincerely for the opportunity to remain eternally engaged within the Lord's internal potency, Hare Krsna, and everything else will continue to come.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

During my last visit to…we talked about your service and we concluded that you should concentrate on your FFL service. I have since received encouraging information that you have steadily taken up this service and that you are encouraging others to assist as well. I am very pleased to receive such a report. As you know I am very inspired when a disciple can become steady in their service, but I am even more inspired when I see that by their steadiness, they are inspiring others to assist.

This type of example is what makes devotional service dynamic.

Please, therefore, continue rendering this valuable service and by your efforts, you can also assist our efforts to preach Krsna consciousness in a very important city of Central Asia.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Thank you very much for your letter. I am very happy to hear that you have such a nice, regulated service as cooking for Their Lordships. Now please make sure to prepare everything in the right consciousness. Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityananda are persons, and They like receiving Their meals on time, and prepared with Their desires in mind. You should also keep a high standard of cleanliness yourself. Make sure that you are always clean before entering Their kitchen, and that you are also clean when you cook. When the proper meditation is there, then the consciousness becomes purified, and through this process, and the process of hearing about the Lord's pastimes, you will gradually be raised to Krsna consciousness.

## About accepting a spiritual master

January 29, 1990

Dear devotees,

Hare Krsna. All glories to His Divine Grace Srila Prabhupada. During my last visit to the Soviet Union, I was approached, on several occasions, by devotees asking if they could become my disciple. Since I'm naturally reluctant to accept disciples, I wanted to first provide a detailed explanation why I feel this way, so that anyone who may share a similar aspiration could gain a better understanding about my position on this subject. I also wanted to describe what I expect from someone, who, after understanding my position, still wishes to take initiation from me. It is my hope that this letter will fulfill both of these purposes.

First, I must say that my original intention for visiting the Soviet Union was simply to go and help enthuse the devotees in their service to guru and Krsna. I also wanted to give my friend, Charles, an opportunity to visit the Soviet Union, so that he would feel encouraged to continue to support the preaching efforts there. The one thing that I didn't expect, was to find so many uninitiated devotees eagerly looking for a spiritual master. I had heard that the preaching was becoming successful in the USSR, but I never realized that so many new devotees were coming. I also never expected that devotees there would want to become my disciples.

As far as my second visit was concerned, I did have a better understanding of what to expect before I went, but I still hoped to maintain my original intention. But now it has become obvious that I can no longer just avoid the many requests I've been getting to become my disciple.

So, when I say that I'm naturally reluctant to accept disciples, I simply mean that accepting disciples is not something that I'm particularly desirous of doing. However, I know that I must accept this responsibility, having been ordered to do so by Lord Caitanya and Srila Prabhupada. It's a duty, and therefore I take the many responsibilities that come with that duty very seriously.

Consequently, considering that there are so many devotees in the Soviet Union eagerly looking for a spiritual master, I feel that, more important than immediately encouraging someone to think of me in that way, I should first place more emphasis on what I would expect from an aspiring disciple. I also feel that I should give these devotees a chance to learn a little bit more about me, than what they may have learned from hearing a few of my lectures. Once I'm confident that I've been better understood, then the cultivation of a guru/disciple relationship could begin; and even then, it would be with the understanding that this would be developed over time. The spiritual master should test the disciple and conversely, the disciple must also test the spiritual master, by placing relevant inquiries before him.

In the beginning, I feel that I must provide the opportunity to have some personal communication (either by correspondence, or within a group discussion) with each interested devotee. However, I don't, in any way, need, or expect to become involved in assigning him his respective duties. It is beyond my means to direct the day to day affairs of my disciples. I expect these details to be worked out with their GBC and local authority. I will elaborate on this point later in this paper.

By taking this position, I'm not implying that I intend to remain aloof from an aspiring disciple. My hope is that an aspiring disciple will subsequently develop the right understanding of what to expect from me. I can best fulfill my responsibility to him (or her) by not being burdened into directing him in his day to day duties. More important, is that I am available to help with his (or her) development of Krsna consciousness.

Among the many guidelines Srila Prabhupada has given for accepting the responsibility of spiritual master, one particular instruction stands out in my mind— "the spiritual master must see that his disciple becomes free from sinful activity." That means that the spiritual master must see that his disciple is becoming Krsna conscious, and he must see that his disciple is engaged in devotional service. Otherwise, how else can one give up sinful activity? I'm not so foolish to think that simply because I have given someone initiation they will automatically take their vows seriously, and will never again commit sinful activity. I also know that my responsibilities go far beyond simply giving someone initiation. I must also give personal instruction. Of course, on the other hand, I realize that even though I may take extreme measures to insure that a disciple will not fall from his vows, there's still every possibility that this could happen. Srila Prabhupada has given the example that there is always a chance of an accident— even on the royal roads.

I am being very careful to not cheapen the process of diksa, by indiscriminately giving initiation to someone that has not received sufficient training in devotional service. I believe that intention of mine is apparent by the fact that up till now, I have initiated only seven disciples; although there are many others aspiring to become my initiated disciple. Considering how we have seen some of my godbrothers become affected by accepting too many unqualified disciples, I have every intention of continuing to be cautious. There is a scriptural injunction that a spiritual master should not accept too many disciples; especially unqualified disciples. In Srila Prabhupada's case, his purity and impact on so many of his followers was so profound that his personal presence prevailed throughout our whole ISKCON society. As such, he did accept unqualified disciples, but later on, by his mercy, so many of them became elevated.

Srila Prabhupada did this for the sake of firmly establishing this movement before his departure. He was undoubtedly a direct representative of Lord Caitanya; a pure, mahabhagavat devotee of the Lord, who demonstrated to the world his capacity to accept so many disciples and not become the least materially affected by his large following. But it is also an injunction that one should follow in the footsteps of the acharya, and not imitate him. There is a gulf of difference between the two.

So when I say that I don't want to cheapen the process of diksa, I mean that I don't want to give initiation to untrained disciples, who just stay for a short while, and then when they're confronted with a big test, they fall away. I am not interested in attracting some followers for name and fame, and personally I greatly fear developing an attraction to it. As I said, it's a responsibility that I must accept on behalf of Lord Chaitanya and Srila Prabhupada, for the spiritual benefit of the disciples. That means that by giving them initiation, their lives should improve, and they should be brought closer in their relationship to Srila Prabhupada and Krsna through the disciplic succession.

So, I've emphasized that there must first be training in Krsna consciousness. By training I don't necessarily mean that one must first be trained in some practical skills (i.e. playing mrdan- ga or kartals, cooking, cleaning, book distribution, etc). Although all these types of training are helpful, and even, in one sense, essential, still it is not the type of training that I am referring to as a prerequisite for diksa.

But rather, I'm referring to the necessity of being trained to become dependent upon Krsna in all aspects of devotional service. In order to experience such a helpless dependence one must accept a higher authority. And one must work and perform duties defined by a higher authority for his own purification and development of Krsna consciousness.

Of course, first and foremost of all duties given by Srila Prabhupada, is to chant 16 rounds daily of the Hare Krsna mahamantra. Other essential spiritual duties are to attend mangal arati, practice the regulative principles, regularly attend classes in the sastras, and associate with other devotees. These are described in the Caitanya Caritamrta as purascarya. The word purah means "before" and carya means "activities". Prabhupada writes, "When one is actually advanced in the purascarya-vidhi, he is recommended by the local temple president for initiation." (CC Madhya 6, p.56)

I would also like to add a most essential duty to be practiced by every member of this Krsna consciousness movement, which is to regularly read from Srila Prabhupada's books. This could come under the heading of "regularly attending classes in the sastras", because all classes should be given from Srila Prabhupada's books. But if Krsna provides the time to read more, then we should definitely take it.

However, one must also accept duties to assist Srila Prabhupada's mission. These duties may greatly vary according to one's particular social and spiritual position, and to list all the variables is beyond the scope of this paper. However, one should determine his duty in consultation with one's local authority, and then one should try to stick to his duty to make progress in devotional service.

One should learn how to accept prescribed duties in a humble and submissive way, and thus cultivate the proper Vaisnava mentality of ***trnad api sunicena taror api sahisnuna/ amanina manadena kirtaniyah sada harih***

"One should chant the holy name of the Lord in a humble state of mind, thinking oneself lower than the straw in the street; one should be more tolerant than a tree, devoid of all sense of false prestige, and should be ready to offer all respect to others. In such a state of mind one can chant the holy name of the Lord constantly. " (Siksastaka 3)

In reference to this verse Srila Krsnadasa Kaviraja Goswami has stated the following in the Caitanya Caritamrta (Adi lila [17.27-30](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-adi/17.27))

"A devotee engaged in chanting the holy name of the Lord should practice forbearance like that of a tree. Even if rebuked or chastised, he should not say anything to others to retaliate.

For even if one cuts a tree, it never protests, nor even if it is drying up and dying does it ask anyone for water. Thus a Vaisnava should not ask anything from anyone else. If someone gives him something without being asked, he should accept it, but if nothing comes, a Vaisnava should be satisfied to eat whatever vegetables and fruits are easily available. One should strictly follow the principle of always chanting the holy name, and one should be satisfied with whatever he gets easily. Such devotional behavior solidly maintains one's devotional service."

This should be the mentality with which one should accept his prescribed duties. It's understood that there will be difficulties and problems in this endeavor, but the determination to overcome these obstacles will come to the degree that one learns how to depend upon Krsna while executing one's prescribed duties. And by working in such a way, one will also very naturally experience the desire to increase one's dependence upon regularly hearing and chanting about Krsna in the association of devotees. This activity is the real safeguard which protects one from the onslaught of material allurements, and will therefore also protect one from falling back into sinful activities. As it is stated in Srimad Bhagavatam [1.2.8](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/2.8):

***dharmah svanusthitah***  
***pumsam visvaksena-kathasu***  
***yah notpadayed yadi ratim***  
***srama eva hi kevalam***

**"The occupational activities a man performs according to his own position are only so much useless labor if they do not provoke attraction for the message of the Personality of Godhead."**

So there is already a standard in the Soviet Union that no one can be recommended for initiation unless he (or she) has been a devotee for at least two years. In one sense, this standard is imposed to fulfill much of the same points that I've already made in this letter. However, my point is that I'm not saying that just because one may have been a devotee for two years, one is automatically qualified for initiation. I must also feel confident that he (or she) must have been trained in the development of Krsna consciousness in the way that I previously described. This confidence comes both from my own communications with the aspiring disciple, as well as through my communications with the GBC-designated local authorities. Therefore I definitely expect an aspiring disciple to work cooperatively (in the way I previously described) with his (or her) local authorities and I definitely frown upon independent behavior. I don't want to be part of a separate channel of managerial authority to aspiring disciples. This is not how Srila Prabhupada expected the Krsna consciousness movement to be spread.

I also want it to be known that I'm not taking this position exclusively towards the devotees in the Soviet Union. In fact, in most cases, whenever I travel in areas where I do not ordinarily visit, I make it known to the local authorities that if any of the devotees should want to pursue a guru/disciple relationship with me, they should gently be discouraged, and they should be told that I prefer that they be encouraged to first try to establish a relationship with someone who visits more regularly. But since it appears that, Krsna willing, I may be able to visit the Soviet Union from time to time, I'm putting forward the same position that I've presented to other devotees who have similarly approached me, and who serve in areas outside my immediate GBC jurisdiction.

And last, but certainly not least important, I also want it to be understood that by taking this position, I am, in no way, making any allusions towards the policies of other initiating spiritual masters in our ISKCON society. This letter is simply an honest admission of my own limitations, and I know that I am in no position to judge the ability of my godbrothers to deliver their disciples from sinful activities. In fact I am very encouraged to see just how spiritually powerful some of my godbrothers are, and the proof can be seen in the behavior of their disciples. In this connection, Srila Prabhupada has cited the following point in the purport of Caitanya Caritamrta Antya-lila [3.143](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-antya/3.143):

"It is said, phalena pariciyate: one is recognized by the result of his actions. In Vaisnava society there are many types of Vaisnavas. Some of them are called gosvamis, some are called svamis, some are prabhus, and some are prabhupada. One is not recognized, however, simply by such a name.

A spiritual master is recognized as an actual guru when it is seen that he has changed the character of his disciples. Haridasa Thakur actually changed the character of the professional prostitute. People greatly appreciated this, and therefore they all offered obeisances to Haridasa Thakura and glorified him."

I also appreciate the changes I've seen in the lives of so many devotees as a result of the preaching of some of my godbrothers. I wish to be able to follow in their footsteps, and I emulate their ideal example.

So I hope that my position is now more clear and that all interested parties will seriously consider what I've said.

I look forward to my continued visits to the Soviet Union, and, as well, I look forward to associating with all of you in service to Srila Prabhupada.

Hoping this meets you well and enthused in devotional service.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Thank you for your letter, received during my last brief visit in…On one hand, I'm relieved to see the honesty expressed in your letter, but, on the other hand, I'm also concerned, because, as you say, it is a serious problem.

It's not a new problem, though. Take, for example, this discussion between Srila Prabhupada and a disciple in Mayapura, 1975: Devotee:...But sometimes, from our material viewpoint, we see some discrepancies. Just like we think that...

**Srila Prabhupada:** Because material viewpoint. The viewpoint is wrong, therefore you find discrepancies.

**Devotee:** So we should think that we have the defect.

**Srila Prabhupada:** Yes. Acarya is explained, bhakti-samsan- ah: 'One who's preaching the cult of devotional service, he's acarya.' Then why should you find any discrepancy?

**Devotee:** Because we see...For instance, sometimes the acarya may seem to forget something or not to know something, so from our point of view, if someone has forgotten, that is...

**Srila Prabhupada:** No, no, no. Then...

**Devotee:**...an imperfection.

**Srila Prabhupada:** That is not the...Then you do not understand. Acarya is not God, omniscient. He is servant of God. His business is to preach bhakti cult. That is acarya.

**Devotee:** And that is the perfection.

**Srila Prabhupada:** That is the perfection. Hare Krsna. Devotee: So we have a misunderstanding about what perfection is?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Yes. Perfection is here, how is he preaching bhakti cult. That's all.

This devotee was also thinking that he was seeing some discrepancies in Srila Prabhupada's behavior, but Prabhupada clearly indicated that the devotee was not following the proper criteria. I've used this example, specifically to show, that even with Srila Prabhupada, these doubts arose; so it's not difficult at all to accept that such doubts could arise about myself. When I study my own qualification, I know that I have none. I'm filled with disqualification. But, if there is any qualification in me, it's only that I'm trying to strictly follow Srila Prabhupada. Srila Prabhupada thoroughly explained this point on many different occasions. I will cite a few references as follows:

..."Our system, parampara system, is that, for example, I am just a disciple of Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati. I don't say that I am liberated, I am conditioned. But because I am following the instruction of Bhaktisiddhanta, I'm liberated. This is the distinction between conditioned and liberated. When one is under the direction of a liberated person...The same thing: Electricity. The copper is not electricity, but, when it is charged with electricity if it is touched, that is electricity. And similarly, this parampara system, the electricity is going. If you cut the parampara system, then there is no electricity. Therefore it is stressed so much. Sa kalena mahata yogo nastah parantapa

\[Bg [4.2](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/4.2): 'In course of time the succession was broken, and therefore the science as it is appears to be lost'\].

The electricity is lost." (Morning Walk, Bombay, 1/4/77)

"Yes, \[those who follow\] they're also pure devotees because they're following my instruction. Just like a technician, he is expert, but somebody is assisting him. So the assistants, because they are following the instruction of the expert, therefore their work is also complete. So it is not necessarily that one has to become pure devotee immediately. Just like we are also following the instruction of our spiritual master. I don't claim that I am pure devotee or perfect, but my only qualification is that I am trying to follow the instruction of the perfect. Similarly... This is called disciplic succession. Just like here it is stated that Krsna is the original spiritual master and Arjuna is the original student.

"...If we follow Arjuna and Krsna, then we get the perfect knowledge. We may not be cent percent perfect, but as far as possible, if we follow the instruction as it is, that much perfect. In this way one will get perfection. So one has to follow. The same example, try to understand, that a perfect, expert technologist or technician or mechanic is working, and somebody is working under his instruction. So this somebody, because he is strictly working under the instruction of the expert, he's also expert. He may not be cent percent expert, but his work is expert. Is that clear? Because he is working under the expert. Do you follow? So if you follow pure devotee, then you are also pure devotee. It may not be one is cent percent pure. Because we are trying to raise ourself from the conditional life. But if we strictly follow the pure devotee, then we are also pure devotee. So far we do, that is pure. So pure devotee does not mean one has to become immediately cent percent pure. But if he sticks to the principle that 'we'll follow a pure devotee,' then his actions are...he is as good as a pure devotee. It is not I am explaining in my own way. It is the explanation of the Bhagavat. Mahajano yena gatah sa pan- thah. We have to follow the footprints of pure devotees." (Lecture, Los Angeles, 11/25/68)

So it is the duty of the disciple to examine the spiritual master to see, "Is he strictly following his spiritual master?" Not by seeing, but most important- by hearing. This examination must be there, because one must surrender to a guru in order to advance in spiritual life.

"And the process is pranipata, pranipatena. Pranipata means full surrender. Pra means prakrsta-rupena, fully, and nipata means completely becoming a blank slate. Blank slate. Nobody should approach a bona fide spiritual master just to argue with him and just to, with a desire that 'I shall see what kind of spiritual master, no. This is useless. You have to select a spiritual master.. My guru maharaja, my spiritual master, used to say that you have to select a spiritual master not by seeing but by your ear, by hearing. And you don't select a spiritual master who has got a very good hair or beard or some very beautiful feature. 'Oh, he is a very good, nice looking.' No. You must hear. Tad viddhi pranipatena. Sruti. The whole process is sruti. The Vedas are called sruti. The ear has to give aural reception.

"So here also the same thing is recommended by Lord Krsna, that pranipata. First of all, you have to find out a bona fide spiritual master, and then you should surrender unto him. This is the first process. Pranipatena pariprasnena. Pariprasnena means by sincere inquiries. Not only surrender, you must be intelligent enough to inquire. Not that when something is heard, and there is no question. No. There must be some question.

Pariprasnena and sevaya. So surrender, inquiry, and seva, service" (Lecture BG 3.34 NY 8/12/66)

Therefore, a candidate should take his time in examining the spiritual master sufficiently before initiation, not after.

"Now here is another verse, Krsna says, tad viddhi pranipatena pariprasnena sevaya, in the Bhagavad-gita. Tad viddhi pranipatena pariprasnena sevaya upadeksyanti te jnanam jnaninas tattva-darsinah. So you have to learn from guru by three processes. What is that? First process is that you must surrender. Tasmad gurum prapadyeta, surrender. You have to find out such an exalted person where you can willingly surrender, "Yes." Therefore it is enjoined in the sastras, before making a guru, try to study him, whether you can surrender there. Don't accept any guru all of a sudden as fanatic. No, don't do that. That is the injunction. And guru also must study the disciple who wants to become a disciple, must study him, whether he's fit for becoming a disciple. This is the way of making relationship between guru and disciple. Everything is there provided we take them seriously. Then we can train up how to become bona fide disciple, how to find out bona fide guru, how to establish our relationship with guru and act accordingly and make our life successful." (Lecture, London 8/22/73)

But once our intelligence has been satisfied, and we see that this is the person to whom I must surrender, then the surrender must actually be there.

"The disciple accepts the spiritual master as the Supreme Personality of Godhead. As stated by Vishvanatha Cakravarti Thakura in Gurvastaka, sakshad-dharitvena: 'One directly accepts the guru, the spiritual master, as the Supreme Personality of Godhead'. 'One should accept the spiritual master not in the sense that the Mayavadi philosophers do, but in the way recommended here. Since the spiritual master is the most confidential servant of the Lord, he should be treated exactly like the Supreme Personality of Godhead. The spiritual master should never be neglected or disobeyed, like an ordinary person." (SB [4.28.43](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-4/28.43) purport)

So throughout this whole letter I've tried to give you the proper philosophical background to this problem according to parampara. But now, we must try to address your problem. Is it me? You? Both? Am I not strictly following? You must become convinced, one way or another. But you must also be careful to make sure that this critical tendency is not just a device that maya has offered you to avoid the surrender that's necessary to whomever you accept as your guru. Ultimately, guru is one, because he's giving Krsna, and service to Krsna. That is the spiritual master's duty. He must try to inspire this surrender to Krsna, by his words and by his example. And when the disciple actually hears the words of his spiritual master, and acts upon them with confidence and faith, then this inspiration to serve will increase. We will always be tested by maya, every step of the way. And she very expertly places doubts in our minds, so that we have all the excuse that we need to avoid this surrender. Therefore a very strong faith in the spiritual master is essential, so that we can get the extra strength and assistance that we need to not surrender to maya. If there's doubts, we should ask submissively, and try to get them cleared up, just as Srila Prabhupada encouraged his disciple to do in the following letter:

"Of course, weakness in Krishna Consciousness we should always feel. That is a good symptom. We should never think that we are strong enough. But the source of strength is Krishna and His manifested representative, the Spiritual Master. We get this instruction from Chaitanya Charitamrita that we receive the seed of Krishna Consciousness through the Spiritual Master and Krishna. Therefore, we should serve both simultaneously for continuous supply of spiritual vitality. So you are now serving Krishna under the instruction of your Spiritual Master, so there will be no scarcity of supply of strength unless there is some weakness on your part in the matter of absolute faith in these two shelters. So if you have still some doubts in putting your faith in the service of your Spiritual Master and Krishna, then you can clear it up. I am always prepared to assist you." (SPL to Jaya Govinda, Oct. 15th, 1969.)

We must always feel that, "My spiritual master is giving me so much!" He's giving me so much instruction and is always trying to dispel my doubts so that I can advance in devotional service." We must begin to develop this feeling of an eternal debt, which is our offering of gratitude for his having planted the seed of devotion within our hearts. This attitude creates a loving bond between the spiritual master and disciple, which helps us to remember him, more and more, as Srila Prabhupada so nicely describes in the following letter:

"I beg to thank you for your letter dated 16th January, 1976 along with the enclosed donation. Unless there is loving feeling, how is it possible for you to always make offerings to me? The spiritual master is always instructing his disciples and they in turn are always trying to serve their spiritual master. It is a reciprocal relationship of love." (SPL, Bhurijana, 31st January, 1976)

So, I can understand that you are a sincere girl, and that you want to overcome this obstacle that's choking your enthusiasm for devotional service. I beg that you please deliberate upon everything that I've given you, and try to go forward in devotional service with this desire to increase your surrender. I pray that Krsna will help you to overcome this test, and that you will advance nicely in Krsna consciousness.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

You ask what is my opinion about a free spiritual development of my disciples. You also ask if they are allowed to look for their own ways to find knowledge. And you have also revealed that the motivation behind these questions is based on your preference to "find your own path", supported by Lord Krsna's statement in Bhagavad-gita "It is better to engage in one's own occupation, even though one may perform it imperfectly, than to accept another's occupation and perform it perfectly. Duties prescribed according to one's nature are never affected by sinful reactions." (BG 18.47)

First, if we are to use Lord Krsna as our main reference for absolute knowledge, then we must accept everything He says in the Bhagavad-gita. Not that we can quote Him from one place to support our opinion, and then disregard what He says elsewhere, (especially in His same philosophical treatise), because it does not support our opinion. In BG [4.34](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/4.34), Lord Krsna also clearly states: "Just try to learn the truth by approaching a spiritual master. Inquire from him submissively and render service unto him. The self-realized souls can impart knowledge unto you because they have seen the truth." Srila Prabhupada often quoted this verse to emphasize that one must learn the truth about the Lord by both hearing from and serving an unalloyed devotee of the Lord. If we are to accept this verse, then we would have to conclude that it is just not possible to gain perfect knowledge of Krsna by adopting one's own method of research.

***athapi te deva padambuja-dvaya-prasada****-* ***les anugrhita eva hi***  
***janati tattvam bhagavan-mahimno na canya eko 'pi ciram vicinvan***

**"My Lord, if one is favored by even a slight trace of the mercy of Your lotus feet, he can understand the greatness of Your personality. But those who speculate to understand the Supreme Personality of Godhead are unable to know You, even though they continue to study the Vedas for many years." (SB** [**10.14.29**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/14.29)**)**

From this verse, we can understand that we just cannot gain access into the mysteries of the Lord, without receiving His causeless mercy. I could list dozens of verses from scripture to illustrate this point, but rather than placing the emphasis on scriptural reference alone, I have particularly quoted this one verse above to bring up the point that it's not through study of scripture alone that one receives the Lord's mercy. One must understand the scripture and its conclusions through the medium of a devotee of the Lord.

All knowledge culminates in knowing Krsna, and Krsna states in BG [18.55](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/18.55), "One can understand Me as I am, as the Supreme Personality of Godhead, only by devotional service. And when one is in full consciousness of Me by such devotion, he can enter into the kingdom of God." It is through devotional service that Krsna reveals Himself. But access to devotional service can only be achieved by the mercy of both the Lord and His devotee. It is the devotee who is the medium for gaining increased access to the Lord's mercy. However, it is also the Lord's mercy which brings us into the association of His devotee.

In this connection, you have asked, "What is the causeless mercy of the Lord?" And you used a statement from Srila Prabhupada that "if a person is trying to find the Lord, He will help him even if this person has no spiritual master and is dirty." I'm not aware of any such statement. However, even if he did make this statement, the important point to understand is that Krsna's help comes by bringing a person, who is sincere, to a bona fide spiritual master. I will cite one reference here from Srimad Bhagavatam [1.19.36](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/19.36):

. .One can achieve the nucleus of the devotional service of the Lord by the mercy of the spiritual master and the Personality of Godhead. The spiritual master is the manifested representative of the Lord to help one achieve ultimate success. One who is not authorized by the Lord cannot become a spiritual master. Srila Sukadeva Gosvami is an authorized spiritual master, and thus he was inspired by the Lord to appear before Maharaja Parik- shit and instruct him in the teachings of Srimad-Bhagavatam. One can achieve the ultimate success of going back to Godhead if he is favored by the Lord's sending His true representative. As soon as a true representative of the Lord is met by a devotee of the Lord, the devotee is assured a guarantee for going back to Godhead just after leaving the present body. This, however, depends on the sincerity of the devotee himself. The Lord is seated in the heart of all living beings, and thus he knows very well the movements of all individual persons. As soon as the Lord finds that a particular soul is very eager to go back to Godhead, the Lord at once sends His bona fide representative. The sincere devotee is thus assured by the Lord of going back to Godhead. The conclusion is that to get the assistance and help of a bona fide spiritual master means to receive the direct help of the Lord Himself."

So, in summary to the first part of this question of yours, I don't encourage my disciples to freely seek knowledge independent of the guru 'parampara. However, I do allow more freedom in the acceptance of "prescribed duties" which is what Krsna was referring to in the above-mentioned Bhagavad-gita quote ([18.47](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/18.47)) that you cited to support your idea for "finding your own path". But even though there can be more freedom, still the word "prescribed" implies that it still must come from some "higher authority". In this connection, I will quote from Srila Prabhupada's purport to BG [3.35](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/3.35):

"...One should therefore discharge his prescribed duties in full Krishna consciousness rather than those prescribed for others. Materially, prescribed duties are duties enjoined according to one's psychophysical condition, under the spell of the modes of material nature. Spiritual duties are as ordered by the spiritual master for the transcendental service of Krishna. But whether material or spiritual, one should stick to his prescribed duties even up to death, rather than imitate another's prescribed duties. Duties on the spiritual platform and duties on the material platform may be different, but the principle of following the authorized direction is always good for the performer, (my emphasis) When one is under the spell of the modes of material nature, one should follow the prescribed rules for his particular situation and should not imitate others."

Spiritual duties are those given by the spiritual master, but material duties are those defined in sastra according to one's psychophysical condition. These are social duties, (i.e. brahmana, ksatriya, Vaisya or sudra). The definition of such duties are provided in sastra and are implemented within any social context, governed by authorized spiritual leaders and administrators. Therefore the higher authority, in this regard, is sastra. Lord Krsna states in the 16th Chapter of Bhagavad-gita: "One should therefore understand what is duty and what is not duty by the regulations of the scriptures. Knowing such rules and regulations, one should act so that he may gradually be elevated."

From the tone of your letter, it seems as though you are doubting the character of the administrators there, because they are forcing you into submission rather providing the inspiration you need to voluntarily follow their instructions. Certainly our philosophy mandates that in order to be situated in devotional service, we must voluntarily render service to the Lord. We cannot be forced to serve Him. But in the first stages of devotion, or vaidhi-bhakti, as described by Srila Rupa Goswami, one is engaged in obligatory service to the Lord, simply out of obedience to the order of the spiritual master or in pursuance of the scriptures. Therefore even to come to the preliminary stages of devotional service, one should seek out a bona fide spiritual master, hear from him, and render service to him. It is very difficult to unconditionally submit to any authority without first establishing this principal relationship. So if one is genuinely sincere to advance in devotional service, gradually everything will become revealed to such a devotee, in the ways that we previously described.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Although it seems that the main intention of your letter is to elicit an answer from me as to who is your spiritual master, my intention is to give you some guidelines as to how you can choose- for it is you that must make that choice. I cannot say, "I am your guru, you must surrender to me", for a spiritual master must always teach that one must surrender to Krsna- not to himself. It would also not be proper for me to say that you must surrender to Harikesa Maharaja, and accept him as your guru. However, since, in one sense, you are surrendering to me for instruction, as you have indicated by quoting Arjuna, (sisyas te 'ham sadhi mam tvam prapannam), I must accept the role as your spiritual master and give you instruction. Then just as Lord Krsna told Arjuna, vimrsyaitad asesena yathecchasi tatha kuru, "Deliberate on this fully, and then do what you wish to do", I must also say the same.

When the aspiring disciple sees that Krsna is manifesting Himself through a devotee who consistently preaches that one must surrender to Lord Krsna, and when one develops the confidence that this devotee also consistently behaves as a surrendered servant of his guru and Krsna, then one voluntarily chooses to increase his faith and realization by surrendering to him as his guru. This initial surrender must be voluntary, and should not happen any other way. Nonetheless, one should come to this point of surrender, as quoted in the following letter from Srila Prabhupada.

"So the first step is that one should consider carefully whether or not here is a bona fide Spiritual Master to whom I can surrender myself without any hesitation." (SPL to Vrndavana, Nov 17th, 1970.)

Once the surrender is there, gradually everything becomes revealed.

"Satisfaction of the spiritual master is the secret of advancement in spiritual life. The Lord is the original spiritual master, and a person in the disciplic succession can convey the message of Lord as it is to his sincere disciple. We cannot manufacture our own process, therefore mental speculation does not at all help us in spiritual life. One simply has to surrender himself to his guru and everything will be revealed to him." (SPL, Sriji devi dasi, 7th November 1974)

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

You ask how to know who is your guru. Srila Prabhupada has stated in one letter "The eternal bond between disciple and spiritual master begins from the first day he hears. Just like my spiritual master, in 1922 he said in our first meeting, you are educated boys, why don't you preach this cult. That was the beginning, now it is coming to fact. Therefore the relationship began from that day."

This may not sound like a direct answer to your question, so I will try to elaborate. Srila Prabhupada indicated that in his own life, his eternal relationship with Srila Bhaktisiddhanta began from the moment he heard the transcendental order which was to become the primary object of his meditation in his devotional service. Srila Prabhupada molded his life around this order, and therefore his relationship was secure by this constant remembrance. Similarly, in accepting a spiritual master, one should try to distinguish just whose instructions have become the object of one's meditation. In other words, in a neophyte stage, when one is unsteady, and one's mind is flickering, whose instructions consistently help him become secure in devotional service? Whose orders have become the foundation of one's devotional life? Service is rendered to a person. Ultimately we must reestablish this relationship of service to Krsna, but it is service to our guru which makes this connection. The disciple must search within and with confidence determine from which medium is Krsna most consistently appearing in the form of His instructions, and with faith and confidence that this is the person who can take me back to Godhead, accept him as guru and serve his instructions. Conversely, the spiritual master recognizes his disciples by observing the above-mentioned behavior towards his instructions. If you can't decide, then that should be a good indication that you're not ready to decide. Be patient, and, in time, Krsna will reveal.

You ask what is most important in communications between the guru and disciple. On the part of the spiritual master, what is most important is that he delivers Krsna's message without adulteration. For the disciple, what is most important is that he regularly hears, and he must be prepared to put forward the most relevant inquiries for advancement in spiritual life. These points are discussed by Srila Rupa Goswami in the Nectar of Devotion.

"Srila Rupa Gosvami states that his elder brother (Sanatana Gosvami) has compiled Hari-bhakti-vilasa for the guidance of the Vaishnavas and therein has mentioned many rules and regulations to be followed by the Vaishnavas. Some of them are very important and prominent, and Srila Rupa Gosvami will now mention these very important items for our benefit He mentions the basic principles as follows: (1) accepting the shelter of the lotus feet of a bona fide spiritual master, (2) becoming initiated by the spiritual master and learning how to discharge devotional service from him, (3) obeying the orders of the spiritual master with faith and devotion, (4) following in the footsteps of great acaryas (teachers) under the direction of the spiritual master, (5) inquiring from the spiritual master how to advance in Krsna consciousness...."

You ask how a disciple should interact with another guru who is not his diksa guru, and the reason for such interaction. The main interaction is, once again, hearing, although one may not necessarily have the same faith in another Vaisnava, who is accepting disciples, as one does in one's own initiating spiritual master. Nonetheless, he should still be formally treated on par with one's own spiritual master. In the process of hearing from another elevated Vaisnava, one may find his instructions begin bearing a significant influence in his own spiritual advancement, and they gradually strengthen his relationship with guru and Krsna.
