Hence a natural relationship begins to manifest, by which a disciple may come to accept this elevated Vaisnava as a siksa guru. With the diksa guru's permission, he may inquire from his siksa guru in the same manner as he would with his diksa guru, but should never reject his initiating spiritual master, unless he's proven himself to be fallen. This is a grave offense, and one must avoid it at all costs.

I was recently asked what my standards are for accepting candidates for second initiation. The response following this message was my reply.

I would like this response to be disseminated to all of the leaders throughout the former Soviet Union, but I do not know how to do that most effectively. I was hoping that you could help me. There is no need to send it out immediately. It may only cause confusion being so close to the time when I will be giving initiations in Moscow. However, please send this out some time after the first week of January, so that my policies on second initiation will be clear for all future recommendations.

Thank you very much.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Regarding requirements for second initiation, the candidate:

1\) Must have been initiated for at least 3 (THREE) years.

2\) Must have a spotless record of morning program attendance. (Of course an occasional miss is acceptable, but it must be clearly a rare occurrence).

3\) Must have been steadily engaged with a submissive service attitude for the three-year period.

4\) If the candidate has not served in that temple for three years, then a recommendation from the candidate's previous authority is required as well. If the previous authority is unwilling to give a recommendation, then the candidate must wait until he has served three years at the present temple where he is serving.

5\) Must be engaged in direct preaching activity, regular Deity worship (or proposed Deity worship after second initiation), or must have such an exemplary Vaisnava behavior that it warrants a recommendation for second initiation so that the example can serve well for others to follow.

6\) Must pass an examination according to the local standards.

7\) They must write a brief letter to me, assuring me that they have been strictly following the principles and chanting all of their rounds since the time they were given first initiation (including the two years prior to first initiation).

And they must assure me that the above criteria will continue to be met by them after second initiation as well. They should also give a brief report of their service now and what their service will be in the immediate future. The Temple President must read that letter and must sign it as well.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

If your present condition is too limiting, then I would prefer that you just wait until some future date when you can attend a yajna, rather than sending your initiation in the mail. I have only given initiation like that in three cases, two of them were devotees with terminal cancer. One died two days later, the other, two months later. The third instance was with a devotee who is permanently too diseased to travel. Your case is not similar to any of them, and I do not want to start a new standard.

Meanwhile, I do hope that you are fully prepared for the responsibilities that lie ahead with your new child. According to Lord Rsabhadeva's instructions, one should not become a parent unless they can save their dependents from the repetition of repeated births and deaths. That is a heavy responsibility, and demands a very high standard of Krsna consciousness to fulfill it. Please therefore, continue to seriously keep a high standard of Krsna consciousness, by strictly following the regulative principles and by daily taking your doses of Krsna katha. This will benefit yourself, and just as importantly, your child.

Remember, Prahlada Maharaja heard Narada Muni's instructions while still within the womb of his mother.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

I received the message containing the carefully translated letter from Bhakta**...**and have noted the contents. However, with regards to his initiation, since I have not seen any indication anywhere in your letters that there is a recommendation for his initiation from**...**dasa, there is nothing for me to discuss with him concerning this matter. I have a strict policy that I will not discuss the particulars about any aspiring disciple's initiation, unless and until there is a formal recommendation from his Temple authorities. It is not something which the aspiring disciple should start dealing directly with me. THERE MUST BE A RECOMMENDATION.

If**...**dāsa is unwilling to give a recommendation because he does not know him well enough, and if you are not willing to give him a recommendation for the same reason, then the matter is closed until either you or**...**dasa are prepared to recommend him with confidence.

In this regard, I will include one letter which I wrote a couple of years ago, which i believe is in one of my booklets of letters. If Bhakta…has these booklets, then the letter should already be there somewhere within it already written in Russian. If not, then somebody will need to translate it.

I hope that my position is now clear in everyone's mind.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Just a few weeks ago, I received by E-mail translations of many letters that were handed to me in January of this year. Your letter was among them. I am sorry that it took that long, but I have also warned again and again, that disciples should not give me letters in Russian because it is not always so easy for me to find somebody to translate them for me. This case is a clear example in this regard.

I gave them to somebody to translate them for me in January, and it took almost six months before he sent them back to me.

You have written:

"How I can live with such desire to get initiation without any hope to get it?"

There is no need to feel hopeless in this case. In principle, I do not disqualify people from initiation if, by circumstances, it is too difficult for them to attend the temple programs regularly. However, I do expect such aspiring disciples to be chanting their rounds daily, to be following the regulative principles, to be having their own daily morning program, to be attending the temple weekly, to be giving some of fruits of their labor to their local temple (or, to be rendering a steady service for their local temple) and to have been an aspiring disciple for at least 5 years. The 5-year minimum requirement is applied for those who cannot attend temple programs. This requirement enables me to observe the candidates commitment to "Krsna consciousness," based on the fact that it is often too difficult for local temple authorities to make such observations when the candidates do not visit several times a week.

My point is that there is no need to feel hopeless. You should remember the instruction of Srila Bhaktivinode Thakur given in reference to patience. Srila Bhaktivinode Thakur states that a devotee must go on always expecting Krsna's mercy, whether it will be now, in 10 years, or in many lives to come. This is called "dhairya," patience. If you go on executing your prescribed duties faithfully, you should expect Krsna's mercy.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Regarding your dream, I will not say that I consciously appeared in your dream, because if I did, that would not be true. I, therefore, did not give you an instruction to go to Moscow. However, if by this dream, you have become inspired to cultivate detachment, then even though I did not personally appear in your dream, still, I can now personally confirm that whatever instills detachment from maya and attachment to Krsna's service is connected to Krsna. For whatever reason, Krsna appeared to awaken a deeper attachment to Him in the form of this dream. Therefore, there is no need to completely reject it.

Regarding detachment in householder life, you may want to acquire copies of a series of lectures that I recently gave on householder life. I believe that the contents of these lectures will be beneficial for you, and will help you to gain a better understanding of the instructions that will follow.

It is a householder's duty to earn an honest means of livelihood to maintain his "household" and family. He should learn to offer the fruits of his livelihood to the service of Krsna by regularly hearing about Krsna's pastimes in the association of saintly persons. In the execution of his household duties he should gradually awaken a spirit of detachment, being always careful to not implicate himself, and his family in too many worldly affairs. I therefore, recommend that in the spirit of these instructions, you find the means by which you can earn an honest means of livelihood to take care of your family. Establish yourself and your family within a community of like-minded devotees. Having a stable position in Krsna consciousness is essential for qualifying for initiation. I cannot accept as disciples someone who is always unstable, and who has no regular association of devotees more experienced than themselves. Therefore the above criteria must be met, along with the following criteria which was passed by the Russian National Council in their meeting of October 5-6 of this year:

First initiation

The person who recommends should make sure to the best of his capacity that the candidate:

1\. Has been chanting the Hare Krishna mantra every day and strictly following the 4 regulative principles without breaks for at least past 2 years

2\. Out of these 2 years, has been chanting the pranama mantra of the Spiritual Master of at least 1 year

3\. Has successfully passed the Bhakta-program exams

4\. Has been steady in his sadhana (get up early, morning program - at home or in the Temple, reading Srila Prabhupada's books for at least 1 hour a day)

5\. For the past 2 years has been regularly visiting the Temple programs (For the temple residents - every day)

6\. For the past 2 years has been engaged in regular practical service within the authority system of ISKCON. (His direct spiritual and managerial authority should confirm and describe this service)

7\. If the authority has some doubts even though the candidate meets the above terms, he can give a recommendation with reservation. In this case he should present his doubts to the respective spiritual master and decide about the recommendation together with him.

Regarding #6 above, if a householder cannot be engaged in a regular practical service within the authority system of ISKCON, although he CAN at least regularly attend a temple program or authorized Nama Hatta, I personally, at times, will make an exception and will accept the candidate. But only after having observed his/her activities for five years. With regards to yourself, and your wife, because I have known you for so many years, since you had also been inactive for some time, I would want to see you in a stable situation as described above for at least three years before I could consider any recommendation.

I hope that this is clear, and you now have the answers you were looking for.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

June 10, 1993 No. San Juan, CA

Dear Bhaktin,

Hare Krsna. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Yes, I did receive your letter dated March 21, 1993, a little over one month ago when I returned from the former Soviet Union. In that letter you referred to some devotees who spoke to you about some of my so-called qualifications, but it seems that those same devotees failed to mention that I tend to fall behind with my correspondence. I would put that into a list of my disqualifications. Presently, it has become too cumbersome for me to give separate attention to every person who approaches me for guidance in their spiritual life. In other words, I am becoming increasingly dependent upon the temple leaders to offer guidance to aspiring devotees such as yourself. I suggest that you approach Krsna consciousness from that level first, and beyond that you could take guidance from me by attending my lectures when I'm in Boston.

With this in mind, I would like to draw your attention to the following resolution that was passed by ISKCON's Governing Body Commission this year in Sri Dhama Mayapura:

8\. \[Correspondence Resolution Effective 31 July, 1992\]

WHEREAS, sometimes there are misunderstandings between temple authorities and spiritual masters on whether recommendations for initiation have actually been given, which would be solved if the recommendations were always given in writing;

WHEREAS, occasionally, temple authorities have not fully understood the gravity of their responsibility in recommending candidates for initiation, which would be solved by clarifying the extent of the Temple President's responsibility in this regard;

WHEREAS, occasionally, candidates for initiation request initiation without having received proper recommendation; and WHEREAS, lack of standards in these matters has reduced the stature of the initiation process;

THEREFORE, IT IS RESOLVED: that the following standard for initiation recommendations shall be adopted as ISKCON Law: That before a candidate can be (first) initiated, the guru must receive a written recommendation from the Temple President under whom the candidate is currently serving. In the case of candidates who are not full-time ISKCON members, the recommendation must be given by the Temple President of a temple near the candidate who has familiarity with the spiritual status of the candidate. If said Temple President is also the prospective initiating guru, then special procedures must be followed (see Note below.) Future legislation will deal with situations where there is no Temple President with sufficient familiarity to make or deny a recommendation, as may occur in the case of congregational members who do not live near an ISKCON temple.

The recommendation of the Temple President must certify that the candidate has fulfilled the qualifications for initiation to the best of his understanding, and, specifically, it must state that the candidate has been engaged in favorable devotional service, has chanted sixteen rounds of japa daily, and has adhered to the four regulative principles, all for the previous twelve months. It must also state that the candidate is sufficiently knowledgeable in the philosophy of Krishna Consciousness and the structure and purpose of ISKCON to have passed the prescribed examination, and has accepted shelter of the guru since more than six months. If the candidate has not been serving continuously under the recommending Temple President for the previous twelve months, then the Temple President must still satisfy himself with the activities of the candidate for the full twelve month period by contacting other ISKCON authorities with knowledge of the candidate's activities during the balance time. The Corresponding Secretary shall prepare and make available to Temple Presidents and Initiating Gurus a standard form which may be used for the recommendation letter.

This formal recommendation process is not intended to replace or overshadow the (more important) need for other regular contact between the ISKCON devotees who are guiding the candidate and the prospective spiritual master regarding the spiritual progress of the candidate. Also, the prospective spiritual master is under no obligation to initiate a devotee because a recommendation has been given or is available to be given."

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Although this resolution does not completely address the question posed in your letters, it does bring to light the need for someone, in a situation such as yourself, to execute her devotional service in conjunction with their local temple and/or temple president. In other words, as much as possible, you should have some regular association with devotees. For instance, I know one devotee who has many children (although she is not a single parent), but still finds the time to attend the program in the temple every Saturday morning. She is also at the temple every Sunday, and conducts the Sunday School for the children in the temple. Some regulated contact with the temple devotees is of the utmost importance.

I appreciate your concern that, as a single parent, your time is very limited. I would not hold that against you as a disqualification. You must fulfill your duties as a parent, and at the same time, cultivate a consciousness of Krsna in all aspects of your life— including your duties as a parent. But association, through hearing and chanting about Krsna with other devotees, is the foundation upon which one may build such a consciousness. If I were to give you some direction for the immediate future, I would suggest that you begin with this instruction in mind, and put your thoughts about initiation aside until you could become committed to this way of thinking. Initiation is a natural outgrowth that begins from regulated hearing about Krsna and service to Him through His representatives.

Moreover, the GBC body passed another relevant resolution three years ago, emphasizing that a devotee should first demonstrate a basic understanding of the Krsna consciousness philosophy "before" he/she even begins to approach an initiating guru as a spiritual master. The resolution is written as follows:

92\. That before a devotee can be recommended for initiation or be officially encouraged to take shelter of a particular ISKCON spiritual master he or she should demonstrate a basic understanding of Srila Prabhupada's teachings by answering an oral or written test administered by the Temple President. The questions to be answered are:

1\. What are the qualifications of a bonafide spiritual master?

2\. Why are you convinced to follow the orders of the spiritual master in this life, and even life after life?

3\. Why does one worship the spiritual master like God ?

4\. Do you believe the spiritual master speaks the absolute truth? If so, why?

5\. Under what circumstances should the spiritual master be rejected ?

6\. What are the qualifications and responsibilities of a disciple?

7\. What is the unique position of Srila Prabhupada in ISKCON?

8\. Why do you accept Lord Krishna as the Supreme Personality of Godhead?

9\. Why do we follow the four regulative principles ?

10\. Why do we chant Hare Krishna?

11\. What is the position of the GBC Body?

12\. Explain the difference between the body and the self?

13\. What is ISKCON, and why should one remain in ISKCON?

I suggest that you make sure that you know the answers to these questions as well. I hope that this letter finds you in good health.

Hare Krsna, Niranjana Swami

August 14, 1993

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear Bhaktin…,

Hare Krsna. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

There are many things that I could say in response to your letter, but to avoid confusing you any further than you already are, I will express only those points which are most relevant.

1\) Nobody has the right to tell you who you should worship as your spiritual master. It is a decision that you, and you alone must make, based upon your own understanding of how to recognize who is your spiritual master. It's obvious that you are lacking that understanding, so I will try to help you by providing a few guidelines in the context of this letter.

2\) You say that your chanting pranama mantra to…Swami, but you're thinking of Swami. Just "thinking" of…Swami is, by no means, a reason to accept him as your spiritual master. This an extremely shallow understanding of the guru/disciple relationship. The thinking must be focused upon the instructions and example of a particular devotee, and not just some sentimental "thoughts".

When you're experiencing difficulty and you're looking for the inspiration to act, whose instructions do you try to remember? Whose instructions consistently inspire you to serve and make personal sacrifices for the pleasure of Krsna? Now, it may even be possible that the answer to both of these questions is a devotee who is not even an initiating guru. Then just follow his/ her instructions, continue to serve, and pray to Krsna to please reveal to you who is your guru. The point is that you should recognize your spiritual master on the basis of what you have HEARD. Consider the following quote from a lecture that Srila Prabhupada gave in 1966:

"And the process is pranipata, pranipatena. Pranipata means full surrender. Pra means prakrsta-rupena, fully, and nipata means completely becoming a blank slate. Blank slate. Nobody should approach a bonafide spiritual master just to argue with him and just to, with a desire that 7 shall see what kind of spiritual master? No. This is useless. You have to select a spiritual master. My guru maharaja, my spiritual master, used to say that you have to select a spiritual master not by seeing but by your ear, but by hearing. And you don't select a spiritual master who has got a very good hair or beard or some very beautiful feature. 'Oh, he is a very good, nice looking.' No. You must hear. Tad viddhi pranipatena. Sruti. The whole process is sruti. The Vedas are called sruti. The ear has to give aural reception.

So here also the same thing is recommended by Lord Krsna, that pranipata. First of all, you have to find out a bonafide spiritual master, and then you should surrender unto him.

This is the first process. Pranipatena pariprasnena. Pariprasnena means by sincere inquiries.

Not only surrender, you must be intelligent enough to inquire. Not that when something is heard, and there is no question. No. There must be some question. Pariprasnena and sevaya. So surrender, inquiry, and seva, service."

(Lecture BG 3.34 NY 8/12/66)

So these are a few simple guidelines which you should try to follow. You should not feel pressured to accept a particular guru. And neither should you feel that because you have not been able to choose your guru that there's something wrong. Even if you have to wait many, many years, you should wait until you are confident that YOU know who is your guru.

Not that somebody else has told you who is your guru. Don't be foolish by allowing yourself to be pressured into accepting a particular guru.

I hope this helps— at least a little.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

April 6, 1994

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear Bhaktin,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have your letter dated 1.01.94, and have noted the contents. You should discuss the matter about initiation with the Temple President, dasa.

In general, I expect devotees who are living separately to have been chanting 16 rounds/day and following the regulative principles for no less than 2 years. Beyond that there must be some kind of steady service, according to your means, and it must be a service that's recognized by the local authorities. When I say, "according to your means", it takes into consideration that not all devotees can render a service for the local temple in a full-time capacity. It's understood that devotees not living in the asrama will also have other responsibilities, but there must be some recognized service for the yatra, performed steadily for a period of no less than one year, and it must be clear to both the temple authorities and to my aspiring disciple that there cannot be any decrease of such service at any point in the future. The only acceptable change is an "increase" of service. It is not sufficient if you are only chanting 16 rounds and following the four regulative principles. I expect more than that. You must also attend temple programs at least three times a week.

I'm also concerned that my disciples living at home, either alone or with family, live according to the proper devotional standards. The home must be just like a temple (i.e. rising early, all food must be offered before the altar and must be only foods which are acceptable for offering, (no foods cooked by non-devotees), a high standard of cleanliness, both within the home and in personal hygiene, etc.) This must be there. If one lives with family, then the family must fully support this way of life. Of course, situations like this are not always within the devotee's control. But my disciples must deal with these matters, and must regularize their devotional life at home "before" taking initiation. Sometimes this cannot be done quickly, but nonetheless if the responsibility is taken, it goes a long way towards insuring that one's path back to Godhead will remain clear.

When I last spoke with dasa during the middle of February, he mentioned that he expected that the temple was going to open a cafe in the very near future. It seems that this facility could provide you with some suitable service. Endeavors like this always require reliable assistance, and that's just the type of service that I depend from my disciples— to be very reliable. Maybe you could discuss the matter with dasa and see if such an opening is available for you.

As you can see from this letter, it is not possible for me to clearly define a duty for you there in . This must be done with the local authorities. The primary duties that I impose upon all of my disciples is to chant 16 rounds daily, attend temple worship, follow the four regulative principles, and to take part in Lord Caitanya's glorious mission to reclaim fallen souls all over the world.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

June 13, 1995

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear…devi dasi,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

You have asked about the required minimum level of bhakti for being having the chance to receive 2nd initiaton. I suppose that you are suggesting a gradation level according to the descriptions given in Madhurya-kadambini. However, in evaluating candidates for 2nd initiation, I do not employ this system of gradation. Srila Prabhupada did not, so why should I? Although Srila Prabhupada was somewhat liberal, wanting to get his movement established, at a later stage in the movement, he definitely became more conservative, demanding that much greater care should be taken before recommending disciples for second initiation. But his evaluation was based primarily upon the individual's ability to remain steady in his devotional practices, while maintaining a sustained level of commitment to the execution of one's prescribed duties. He also expected that these disciples should demonstrate certain brahminical qualities.

Take, for example, the following two quotes:

"You were present in Hawaii when I was there and was particularly stressing that the presidents must be very careful on recommend- ing gayatri initiation. After all, we are criticizing false cast brahmins, if we ourselves are bogus brahmins then our position is very bad. Now that we are more and more trying to implement the varnashram divisions of society, we should not think that everyone has to become a brahmin. For example you are developing a farm there; so those who work the farm do not necessarily have to be a brahmin if they are not inclined to the brahminical standards. In this way, be careful about awarding the second initiation." (letter to Sudama, 26 May, 1974)

"By your recommendation I have chanted upon the enclosed sacred thread and agree to accept the devotee Jambavan Das Brahmachary for second initiation becomes twice born, or Brahmin. The qualifications of a Brahmana are listed in the 18th Chapter verse 42 of the Bhagavad Gita. "Peacefulness, self control, austerity, purity, tolerance, honesty, wisdom, knowledge, and religiousness—these are the qualities by which the Brahmanas work". So you should be very much careful to see that our devotees are following the regulative principles and standard practices such as attending morning Arati, classes, Sankirtan, and reading our books. We want to create some men who can give guidance to the human society, because at the present moment the society is without any intelligent head, namely Brahmins, therefore the whole world is confused for want of real guidance in the matter of ultimate goal of life. They have taken sense gratification as the ultimate goal and therefore everyone is frustrated, because the living entity has nothing to do with the temporary material senses. The senses will never be satisfied, Krishna must be satisfied and then the living entity as eternal part and parcel of the Supreme Lord is automatically satisfied. So the Brahmins are supposed to serve the whole society by distributing this important information. Please help me in this way and Krishna will bless you." (Letter to Bhutatma, 11 August, 1973)

With these references in mind, it should be clearer that Srila Prabhupada did not intend for everyone to take brahminical initiation. Therefore, there obviously must be some distinction between those who should aspire for brahminical initiation and those who just shouldn't. The brahmanas should be steady and should become conversant enough with the philosophy so that they can teach it to others. They should also teach by their example and that highest example is to always offer the results of one's work for Krsna's satisfaction. It's not that a devotee who is engaged in brahminical work can never perform another type of work. For the service of the Lord, when there is a need, a devotee can accept any type of work. But a devotee acting in the role of a brahmana should have an inclination for the above-mentioned qualities of brahminical work, and should become free from all pride. If one is proud of possessing such qualities, thinking himself superior to those who may not, then it will not be possible to remain steady. "Penance performed out of pride and for the sake of gaining respect, honor and worship is said to be in the mode of passion. It is neither stable nor permanent." (BG [17.18](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/17.18)).

You asked for more of an explanation of what it means to respect one's position of authority. Beyond just doing what the authority asks you to do in terms of pushing forward Srila Prabhupada's mission, it means that one should respect the level of responsibility given to that authority. It is a very difficult task to assume authority over those who are unwilling to follow it. What is the meaning of "authority" if it is always challenged in the name of "equality"?

Therefore we should be willing to respect that, for whatever reason, a devotee has been placed in a position of authority and he must therefore exercise that authority, always trying to keep the primary objective in focus. If he is artificially forced to placate everyone's independent desires, then, to that degree, everyone will also suffer. He must therefore be able to exercise some authority, and that authority should be respected. Naturally, we can get into the whole topic of the potential abuse or misuse of authority, and certainly there are many valid claims for such an analysis. But there must be clear evidence of such excesses. Otherwise, if there is not, respect must be there.

Hoping this letter meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

I do not accept recommendations just on the basis of one's past history in devotional service. I must also consider one's future, and yours is very unclear. I have been told that since the time that this recommendation was written, you have taken it upon yourself to get married, with no clear definition of your responsibilities to family life, the temple and your spiritual master. This must become more clear to everyone concerned before I will consider your initiation. Therefore we will discuss this in Novorossiysk.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

May 17, 1994 Kiev

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

To my disciples and aspiring disciples:

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have recently heard complaints by some leaders in Ukraine that an increasing number of aspiring disciples of different spiritual masters are becoming overly aggressive to get recommendations for first and second initiation. This letter is intended to clarify my position on recommendations so that at least those who are approaching myself for initiation will know my position on this matter.

It is not a fact that if somebody fulfills the requirements of chanting 16 rounds, following the regulative principles, and performing some service for the periods of time specified in the GBC resolution, that a candidate for initiation is "automatically" recommended for initiation. Still the temple authority must have the authority to determine whether or not the candidate is ready for initiation. The specified period of time given in the resolution was intended to set guidelines for temple leaders to follow, which means that nobody should be recommended if they have not been fulfilling the necessary requirements for "at least" the specified period of time. In some cases, it may be in the best interest of the candidate to wait longer, and that must be determined by the temple authorities.

In fact, there have even been cases where recommendations were given to me, but I had some reservations about the proposed candidate. Therefore, even the recommendation is not the final determinant for initiation. I also reserve the right to question the qualification of the candidate, and generally in such cases I will discuss the matter with either the candidate's authority, the candidate himself, or both before making my final decision.

I want this to be clear in everyone's mind, that initiation is not something cheap, and is not something that one should try to obtain by heavy pressure or by political maneuvering. It's quite possible that many have succeeded in getting recommendations, and ultimately even initiation by this way, but it by no means guarantees success on the spiritual path. In this regard I will quote Srila Prabhupada himself, from a letter written in 1969:

"Out of the ten kinds of offenses, the number one offense is to disobey the orders of the Spiritual Master. The instructions given to the disciple by the Spiritual Master at the time of initiation should be strictly followed. That will make one advance to the spiritual path. But if one deliberately defies such instructions, then his advancement is hampered from the very beginning.

This defying means to disconnect the relationship with the Spiritual Master. And anyone who defies and therefore disconnects the relationship with the Spiritual Master can hardly expect the assistance of the Spiritual Master life after life."

And also in the Nectar of Devotion, Chapter 5, it is stated: "Srila Rupa Gosvami warns, however, that if a person is properly initiated by a bonafide spiritual master, he should not think that simply by the acceptance of such initiation his business is then finished. One still has to follow the rules and regulations very carefully. If after accepting the spiritual master and being initiated one does not follow the rules and regulations of devotional service, then he is again fallen. One must be very vigilant to remember that he is the part and parcel of the transcendental body of Krishna, and that it is his duty as part and parcel to give service to the whole, or Krishna. If we do not render service to Krishna then again we fall down. In other words, simply becoming initiated does not elevate one to the position of a high-class brahmana. One also has to discharge the duties and follow the regulative principles very rigidly."

I'm not saying that there's something wrong with having a strong desire to get initiation. The main point is, however, that if one's actions are consistent with such a desire, and if one approaches a bona fide spiritual master, then certainly Krsna will make it possible for one to not only receive initiation, but to receive all the transcendental necessities to clear one's path back home, back to Godhead.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear…dasa,

PAMHO. AGTSP.

Regarding this grhastha aspiring disciple, I pretty much have the same standard as... Maybe not everyday of the week, but a grhastha should be attending at least 5 days a week. As with everything, there are exceptions to this. But the exceptions I've made have been only a few rare cases which were in my zone, and where I was personally able to monitor the grhastha's conduct for at least 4 years before I agreed to initiate them. Outside my zone, I've made no exceptions.

My reason for making these exceptions is primarily because I have seen that, for the sake of getting initiation, some grhasthas artificially abandon responsibilities to their families by moving into a temple long enough to get a recommendation. It's very artificial. Then once they're initiated, they simply return to their former ways. As much as possible, I therefore try to give personal guidance to grhasthas, and then watch as closely as possible, how the grhastha accepts the responsibilities that were given to him. I must be confident that there is some kind of morning program with the family, that they are chanting their rounds, that they are contributing in some way to the local temple (prana, artha, dhiya, vaca) either by life, wealth, intelligence, or words, and that their contribution has been sustained for a significant period of time. In one case, one grhastha disciple also responsibly conducted Nama Hatta programs— NOT AS A MEANS OF SELF-SUPPORT, but for preaching, and was encouraging people, who lived so far from the temple that they could not regularly attend temple programs, by providing them with programs and prasadam in between their visits to the temple. They supported themselves by prasadam sales, and also funded their programs by selling prasadam. Thus they were not attached to building up their own following for the sake of supporting themselves, but instead encouraged those who visited their programs to connect themselves to the local temple. This is exemplary preaching for a grhastha, and therefore whenever I see such potential in a grhastha I encourage it. But as I said, I try to watch it as closely as possible myself.

Moreover, if a grhastha should happen to live too far away from the temple, it is generally very difficult for them to relocate their families closer to the temple . Here in the FSU, it is not like the West where such opportunities are far more available. It is for this reason also, that I try to make exceptions.

As I see it, one major problem, is that most Temple Presidents are too preoccupied with their temples to carefully monitor the activities of such grhasthas. And qualified, traveling Nama Hatta preachers are also not available, and even if they are available, most lack enough experience to properly guide the grhasthas who may fall into this category. If the time ever comes when there are responsible traveling Nama Hatta preachers, who can regularly visit the homes of such grhasthas, properly evaluate their lifestyle, and see that they are preaching and not just developing a following for their own economic support, then I may become a little more liberal in giving initiation to grhasthas who do not regularly attend temple programs.

But I would need to be convinced that such Nama Hatta preachers exist, and that they are capable of making a serious evaluation.

In some cases, therefore, the shortcoming is in the local leadership and not necessarily with the grhastha themselves. But what can be done? I'm not inclined to initiate independent grhasthas who show no potential for making a substantial contribution to Lord Caitanya's Sankirtan movement.

I hope that this satisfies your inquiry.

Your servant, Niranjana Swami

## The relationship with the spiritual master

Thank you for this report, both on the affairs concerning the invitation for the Western devotees, as well as on your own devotional service. I am happy to hear that you are inspired by the service of giving knowledge to others, and if, as you say, you worked hard to get rid of all selfish motivations ,then Lord Caitanya will make you more and more of an effective instrument to do this. The real effect of our knowledge is that it must bring us closer to Krsna. If any knowledge does not achieve this end, then it is mayayapahrta-jnana, or one whose knowledge has been stole by the illusory energy .

Regarding your question about friendly dealings with the spiritual master, just this morning I was listening to one lecture from Srila Prabhupada where he stated the following: "Just like Caitanya Mahaprabhu says: ramya kacid upasana vraja-vadhu- vargena ya kalpita. "Oh, there is no better mode of worship than that which was conceived by the vraja-vadhus, the damsels of Vrndavana." Caitanya Mahaprabhu was very strict about women. In His family life also, He never played any joke with women. He was very joking, but all, all with men. He never played any joke with women. No. Perhaps only once He played joke with His wife, Visnu-priya. When Sacimata was searching after something, He simply played a joke "Maybe your daughter-in- law has taken it." That is the only joking we find in His whole life. Otherwise, He was very strict. No woman could come, when He was sannyasi, could come near Him to offer obeisances. They would offer obeisances from a distant place."

Of course, there are so many references about dealings between a sannyasi and women, but since this reference was so fresh in my mind, I chose to use that. But as you say, this is the negative aspect of friendly association with a sannyasi. Moreover, as far as external dealings are concerned, there are fundamentally NO outlets for "friendly" exchanges between a sannyasi and a woman. Perhaps, this is, also fundamentally, why many women become frustrated when trying to associate with their sannyasi spiritual master. They expect that there should be friendly exchanges. But what is the basis of friendship? Srila Rupa Goswami has given the basis as exchange between one devotee and another-- just as in the exchange of gifts. This is the actual basis of friendship. Not that one side takes while the other side gives. No. When there is exchange, then that is the basis. Therefore the spiritual master must always provide a forum for this exchange, while always strictly keeping within the formalities required between a sannyasi and a woman. The spiritual master gives knowledge. That is his gift to his disciple. The disciple in exchange renders service to the spiritual master according to his wishes. This means that the service must be rendered in respect of the spiritual master's position as a sannyasi. A woman must respect the sannyasa order, and try not to transgress the formal exchange that must be maintained to protect the integrity of the sannyasa ashram. When such a healthy respect is maintained, "THAT" is reciprocal service which is pleasing to the spiritual master, and "THAT" is the positive basis of friendship. If more and more women could understand this principle, and act upon it, they would actually grow in their relationship with their spiritual master, having pleased their spiritual master by this service attitude. So you should teach this principle to others. Just see how Krsna is now putting you in a position of teaching. If you follow this example nicely, and teach it to others, then this will be very pleasing to me. Thank you very much for asking this question.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Thank you very much for your words of appreciation for my instructions. Letters like this make the spiritual master very happy. The spiritual master "lives" for his instructions to be heard. Together we must glorify Srila Prabhupada by becoming examples of those who have heard from him how to serve the mission of Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

It is naturally disturbing to hear that you are unable to follow the regulative principles and to chant your rounds. But it is not so easy for the spiritual master to reject his disciple. Your spiritual master also feels responsible for your present condition, and he suffers in more ways than you can know, including the anxiety he feels for the suffering his disciple will have to experience as a result of his committing sinful activities. There is no way that you can accept "all" the suffering in the way you have requested, as long as the spiritual master feels compassion for the sufferings of others.

On this basis, how can I therefore reject you? I must pray to Krsna for the spiritual strength to help you someday realize that you cannot be happy by sinful activities.

Meanwhile, that you are not blaming others for your present condition is good. Please keep the association with devotees as much as possible. They will protect you from bad association.

As much as possible you should also serve the devotees. That service will also help you from becoming too entangled in serving nondevotees.

I hope to visit…later this year. Please, when I visit, do not be embarrassed to come and see me. I will be happy to see you.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

If one wants to progress in spiritual life, there must be strong faith in Krsna's representative. It is the most important ingredient for advancing in Krsna consciousness. Ultimately, we must have firm faith in Krsna, but that is not possible unless there is firm faith in guru. Any instruction, guidance, or encouragement that one seeks in spiritual life, should be sought to increase one's faith in the instructions of one's spiritual master. Without firm faith in a spiritual master, one lives in a world of duality or confusion, and may see apparent conflicts where they may not even exist.

There is nothing wrong in taking guidance from…Prabhu from the principle of his being a grhastha. If the argument is used that he is a grhastha and you are bramacarini, then what to speak of the fact that I am a sannyasi and you are a bramacarini? From social point of view, a sannyasi is certainly not supposed to associate with a bramacarini. However, from the spiritual point of view, the sannyasi is supposed to give his association, and therefore give Krsna, to anyone, whether they be a bramacarini, bramachari, grhastha, or lower than sudra. That is the principle of spiritual life. In distributing Lord Caitanya's teachings, there is no distinction of who is qualified to distribute this knowledge, whether he be a grhastha, bramachari, sannyasi, or whatever, provided that he is following the same principle. He must give his association to those who approach him, and must not take association with an enjoying spirit (na dhanam na janam na sundarim), but rather to give Krsna. Therefore, based upon that understanding, your desire to take direction from…Prabhu is not wrong or offensive, provided that these principles are strictly followed.

Nonetheless, you must make the decision yourself to whom you want to approach to cultivate the above mentioned faith, and it is probably more for this reason than for the other reason that your authorities were cautioning you about writing letters to…Prabhu. It's possible that your authorities were wondering why you were presenting your questions to him and not to myself. If you want to cultivate faith in a spiritual master, then the way to do that is by inquiring submissively and by rendering service. You must make a firm decision from whom you want this relationship, and then cultivate it by this process. When that relationship is strong, then any instruction, guidance or encouragement will only serve to deepen the relationship with guru and ultimately Krsna.

In fact, when the spiritual master sees that the disciple has faith, he may even instruct his disciple to present certain questions to somebody else. When Raghunatha das Goswami surrendered himself to the lotus feet of Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu, he received only the following instructions from Lord Caitanya: "Do not talk like people in general or hear what they say. You should not eat very palatable food, nor should you dress very nicely. Do not expect honor, but offer all respect to others. Always chant the holy name of Lord Krsna, and within your mind render service to Radha and Krsna in Vrndavana. " Then Lord Caitanya told Raghunatha das Goswami to get all the details about His instructions from Svarupa Damodara.

I hope that this serves as an answer to your question. You must carefully read what I have said, and then draw the conclusion yourself.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

A diksa disciple can have many siksa relationships. In some cases, the siksa relationship may be far more prominent than the diksa relationship. I believe that is what you are experiencing now in your relationship with…Maharaja. You should not be distressed with thoughts of betrayal. You are simply serving in a way which is natural, and which, when blessed by your initiating spiritual master, can serve as your primary connection in your progress towards Krsna. My duty is to see you progress in Krsna consciousness. Having given initiation to you does not give me a possessive claim to your service. If I have confidence that the instructions and siksa that you are receiving will carry you forward in your pursuit of pure Krsna consciousness, then why should I not be happy to see you make progress under the guidance of my godbrother? Be assured that I am happy that you are protected in devotional service, and I give my full support in the siksa relationship which obviously exists between yourself and …Maharaja. Our relationship need not change.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

As you can see, I am writing this letter from Vrndavana. I've had the great fortune to stay here for three weeks beginning the third week of last month. I will leave the day after tomorrow, flying first to Central Asia for a few days and then on to the United States, where, unfortunately, I will have to become involved in a court case against the Boston temple that has been going on for just over 20 years.

You might be wondering what I have been doing here in Vrndavana since I arrived. I had big plans, to reread Caitanya Caritamrta and Caitanya Bhagavat, to chant extra rounds, and to engage in minimal amount of correspondence. For the most part, I failed. Last year I came to India the month before Kartika. This year, I decided to come during the month of Kartika. I have now found out that there is a big difference. During Kartika there are so many senior devotees here from all over the world. Many of them visited me at all times of the day. Moreover, some were looking to me to lend a comforting ear. I have a difficult time refusing devotees who want my time, particularly, when they are standing before me face to face. The result is that I did manage to go on Vrndavana parikrama every day for about two weeks, and I managed to chant at least 28-32 rounds a day for the same period. I also got more opportunities for study than I've ever had since last year when I was here during the month of September. But as far as doing what I really wanted to do, and answering all of my letters is concerned, well, for the most part, that didn't happen either. I did manage to keep up with the "quick" correspondence, but it seems that the accessible spiritual master for which you are so intensely hankering remains inaccessible. My solace is that disciples such as your good self are so intensely praying to Krsna for divine intervention, that He will help, whether that help will come from instructions given by me in the past, or from those given by others. On Srila Prabhupada's Disappearance Day, I, along with a few godbrothers and disciples were reading from Srila Prabhupada Lilamrta. There we read just how much Srila Prabhupada emphasized vani over vapuh, as he was preparing to enter into his aprakata lila. I've come to realize more and more that when Srila Prabhupada speaks about vani, there is a big difference in the vani he has given to us from that which I may be able to offer to my disciples. Although I may often repeat what Srila Prabhupada has said within the context of my instructions, when coming from Srila Prabhupada to his disciples, his instructions were far more charged with the potency to carry them out. So, although I know I cannot imitate Srila Prabhupada, still, I know I must carry out his instructions. I am, therefore, praying to Sri Sri Krsna Balarama here in Vrndavana, that you and others will someday find the strength that you need within the instructions I give under his order.

Regarding your letter which you sent to me back in July, and your accompanying question, But actually there is one request - what do you not agree with and what can you approve in my reasoning?

I would like to comment on the following from your letter:

I discovered one astonishing thing - that you acted as Krishna's perfect instrument, you never acted whimsically and, if Krishna in your heart didn't inspire you to engage in my problems, Krishna enthused you to solve other problems, this is also a symptom of your sincerity.

I agree that I am generally not a whimsical person. I tend to not be impulsive when dealing with issues which I believe might bring outcomes which could be detrimental to the spiritual wellbeing of others. Many others have perceived this quality within me as well. My reasoning, particularly in this case, and when dealing with my disciples, is that I try to not pretend that I can give a "quick fix" to their problems. I do not pretend to invoke divine intervention in every word that I speak. As I said in our last discussion, "I am who I am, and I should not pretend to be something other than that. " I always try to depend upon Krsna, but I cannot and will not say that Krsna always intervenes to carry my efforts to the desired fruit. That is His choice, and I cannot demand that He must do as I will. But I must depend on Him, and I also must pray for His will and protection.

I am generally disinterested in treating a symptom, knowing that it will not bring about a positive solution. But, on the other hand, I may not always be able to immediately identify the "root" of the problem. Philosophically I understand that the root of all problems can be traced to a lack of Krsna consciousness, and as you and others experience, I generally try to apply Krsna conscious solutions to problems. But, sometimes, I also may not see or be able to get to the root of the problem, despite my efforts to treat it with a Krsna conscious solution. For the most part, it takes longer for the root of the problem to be manifest before me, at least sometimes longer than a disciple (or, for that matter, any dependent) may want it to take. With yourself, you have consistently felt that I have not gotten to the root of your problem, and you therefore remained dissatisfied with my efforts to help you. I cannot disagree. Still, I tried to get to the root, and as always, I prayed to Krsna to help me. But it's not that "Krsna didn't inspire me to engage in your problems. " I engaged in your problems in the best way I knew how, leaving the rest in Krsna's hands. Therefore, although our meetings brought no immediate fruit, still in all honesty, I cannot say that I ignored you. I just did not get to the root. If or when you feel help from Krsna, it can only come from our combined efforts to invoke His help. You want Krsna to help you, and I want Krsna to help you as well.

I trust that the more you will begin to experience Krsna's help, the more your desire to help me to help you, and especially to help others advance in Krsna consciousness, will grow. That may take time. But I can be patient and will continue to pray.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

You have written: "I'm ready to accept everything I deserved but what can I do to get your forgiveness? " My answer to that is that my forgiveness is very easily obtained. In fact, you have already obtained it. Whenever a disciple sincerely asks forgiveness, no matter how great the offense, I am ready to forgive. But, naturally the disciple should not take advantage of my open willingness to forgive. If you are conscious of the offense, then you should make every effort to avoid letting it happen again.

With respect to this last point, your next question is appropriately connected: "I also ask for your permission to accept…Prabhu as my siksa guru? " The best way to avoid this offense again, is to strengthen your connection to guru and Krsna.

A diksa disciple can have many siksa relationships. In some cases, the siksa relationship may be more prominent than the diksa relationship although that is not always the case. A disciple can have only one diksa guru, but can have many siksa gurus. You are now inspired in a way which is natural, and which, when blessed by your initiating spiritual master, can serve to strengthen your connection to the Lord's internal potency. My duty is to see you progress in Krsna consciousness. Having given initiation to you does not give me an exclusive claim to your service and worship. If I have confidence that the instructions and siksa that you are receiving will carry you forward in your pursuit of pure Krsna consciousness, then why should I not be happy to see you make progress under the guidance of my godbrother? Be assured that I am happy that you are now feeling more protected in devotional service under…'s guidance, and I give my full support for you to pursue a siksa relationship with him. Our relationship need not change, except that by Krsna's grace, it should only become stronger.

I suggest that you express your desires for a formal siksa relationship to…, and accept…whatever direction he may give in this regard.

I had previously written that I was unable to visit…as I had done each year in the past. I am sorry for my inability to visit. But please assure my other disciples that I have every intention of visiting this year, and hope to come before the summer is over.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Your letter arrived during my visits to Moscow and St. Petersburg where over 1, 000 devotees had gathered for 10 days to hear lectures from Harikesa Swami in the evenings. I had to give morning lectures on many of those days, and during the day, there were many meetings I had to attend. I also visited our gurukulas in Moscow, and if I had more time, I would have visited our radio station as well. Radio Krsnaloka has a very large listening audience. Some time back the station spontaneously organized an evening event for their listeners, and over 1, 500 people gathered.

Anyway, due to the demands on my time, I was unable to answer your letter. I just left Moscow the night before last, and now have a little breathing time for three more days here in Almaty before I fly to India on the 18th. So I'm taking this opportunity to write a short message.

I am definitely sensitive to your desires for "siksa". I have similar experiences with other nice devotees there in Canada (i.e. …dāsa in…). I have found the reciprocation I've had with him in a siksa relationship most rewarding. Although it is not formal, it is real and that is what is most important. He confides in me, trusts me and seeks guidance from me, and I am bound by his trust. I see no need to formalize our relationship into anything more than that, and for now, I would recommend the same to you.

Such a relationship takes time to build, and is not something that can be quickly formalized for an immediate result. There must be association and hearing. A simple letter like this can help, but I cannot expect, and you should not expect, that it will immediately make your devotional service spontaneous and free from a mechanical attitude. It is a start, and if it can at least give you more hope, then I would consider the efforts I've made in this letter worthwhile.

In other words, I am firmly convinced that a siksa relationship is not simply a cosmetic adjustment. There must be some substance, and I know as well, that the onus also lies upon me to deliver the substance which can give you renewed faith. I suggest that you try to listen to some of my more recent lectures, especially those which I gave in Ukraine in the early part of January.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

I see no "use" in meditating upon one's guru's relationship with Krsna, if the relationship you're referring to is his Svarupa siddhi, or eternal rasa. Unless he reveals it to his disciple, (and according to the above quote from the Nectar of Devotion, why would he?) it would only be complete speculation.

Of course, the relationship between the guru and Krsna should always be seen as that of a servant. The following quotes from the purports of Caitanya Caritamrta Adi [1.44-45](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-adi/1.44), help us to see it that way.

"Every living entity is essentially a servant of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, and the spiritual master is also His servant. Still, the spiritual master is a direct manifestation of the Lord. With this conviction, a disciple can advance in Krishna consciousness. The spiritual master is nondifferent from Krishna because he is a manifestation of Krishna.

Lord Nityananda, who is Balarama Himself, the first direct manifestation or expansion of Krishna, is the original spiritual master. He helps Lord Krishna in His pastimes, and He is a servant of the Lord.

Every living entity is eternally a servant of Sri Krishna Caitanya; therefore the spiritual master cannot be other than a servant of Lord Caitanya. The spiritual master's eternal occupation is to expand the service of the Lord by training disciples in a service attitude. A spiritual master never poses as the Supreme Lord Himself; he is considered a representative of the Lord. The revealed scriptures prohibit one's pretending to be God, but a bonafide spiritual master is a most faithful and confidential servant of the Lord and therefore deserves as much respect as Krishna."

"The relationship of a disciple with his spiritual master is as good as his relationship with the Supreme Lord. A spiritual master always represents himself as the humblest servitor of the Personality of Godhead, but the disciple must look upon him as the manifested representation of Godhead."

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

The ability to discriminate what is the best action in every situation comes as one advances in devotional service. It is not something mechanical that can be applied in every circumstance. In the neophyte stage of devotional service, our guiding principle should always be the instructions of our spiritual master. However, when there is uncertainty, then the most important principle is that within your heart, you must always try to do what is best for your devotional service to Lord Krsna, and then you have to leave the results up to Him. There will always be opposition and conflict as long as we are in the material world. There will always be forces acting against us, which will try to distract us from our full surrender to Krsna. That is maya herself, whose service to Lord Krsna is to test His devotees so that they can advance in their convictions to surrender to Krsna. Maya will always be there to test us every step of the way.

The desire to associate with the spiritual master is not sense gratification, but it should always be for the purpose of hearing from him. This is the most important aspect of association. Otherwise, it can become sense gratification, if there are other motives involved. You ask how to associate personally so as not to cause me trouble. The best way is by hearing attentively at lectures, asking questions, and by doing as you have just done by writing letters. You may write to me as much as you like. It is not tiresome for me to read a lot of letters, especially if it helps you to write to me. However, it is sometimes very difficult for me to answer letters, because of all the responsibilities I have. To answer a lot of irrelevant questions takes away time that I could be reading Srila Prabhupada's books, which is very vital to me. So please be conscientious about this when your write your letters, and make sure that your questions are relevant. Otherwise, you can just write to let me know how you are doing, without soliciting a response.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Yes, your attitude towards properly qualifying yourself for initiation is right. The spiritual master must test the disciple, otherwise if there is no real faith, then the devotee will have a very difficult time making spiritual advancement. It's not that once initiation is there then the business is finished. Prabhupada gives the example that sometimes a patient may feel some improvement from his diseased condition by applying proper medical treatment under the direction of a physician. However, if he becomes too overconfident, thinking that he's cured, he then thinks that he no longer needs to take his medicine, and thus falls into a complete relapse. So we must always depend upon the instructions of guru and Krsna for success in spiritual life. But real dependence means that we act upon those instructions, and not that we keep on appealing for more and more instructions on the same point due to our own lack of surrender. So keep up the attitude you expressed in your letter and maintain faith that Krsna will personally help you, and Krsna will help you. This is faith, and it is strengthened by the process of surrender.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

In your most recent letter, you've asked, "Can a disciple love his guru with his imperfect mind and heart without doing his duties directly?" The answer is that a disciple develops his love by hearing and following instructions. Love is not something cheap, like a commodity that can be purchased in a store. Although love exists in our natural state, we must bring it back out by association. There's no question of real love without Krsna, and the spiritual master's duty is to deliver Him, by delivering His instructions and service. In this letter you will find certain duties which I've emphasized above others. Try to follow them, and the connection will be there. In one letter to a disciple Srila Prabhupada has written the following:

"The spiritual master is always instructing his disciples and they in turn are always trying to serve their spiritual master. It is a reciprocal relationship of love." (SPL, Bhurijana, 31st January, 1976)

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

If the disciple does not have the opportunity to personally associate, otherwise known as vapuh, then he can still advance in his relation with his spiritual master by following his vani (his instructions). Ultimately vani is more important that vapuh, because it is through vani, that the disciple continuously associates with his spiritual master, even when his personal association is no longer available, as in Srila Prabhupada's ideal example. This association is just as personal, if not even more personal.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

There are two ways to associate with one's spiritual master- through vapuh and vani. The first is in the personal association and the second is through instructions. When the spiritual master is present, one should seek out the necessary instructions from him in order to remain fixed and steady in service in his absence. These are called relevant inquiries. Prabhupada refers to his relationship with his own spiritual master, in that he had only one question to ask of him, "How can I serve you?". Srila Prabhupada was convinced that he wanted to serve Srila Bhaktisiddhanta because Prabhupada heard nicely from him.

So first you must hear nicely, and become convinced that this is the person I want to serve as my spiritual master. This is the very first stage of devotional service called Sraddha, which means to hear with some faith. Then strengthen that faith by continuing to hear while acting upon what you've heard. Gradually your convictions will become stronger, and remembrance of your spiritual master's instructions will become your constant association.

The following quotes from Srila Prabhupada's letters to his disciples should also help you:

... "Thank you very much for your sincere endeavor to please the Spiritual Master. The key to success in spiritual life is to always endeavor to please the Spiritual Master, and follow his instructions. One who is determined in this effort will advance very quickly in Krishna Consciousness. So far personal association with the Guru is concerned, I was only with my Guru Maharaj four or five times, but I have never left his association, not even for a moment. Because I am following his instructions, I have never felt any separation."...

"We are not separated actually. There are two - vani or vapu. So vapu is physical presence and vani is presence by the vibration, but they are all the same. So Krsna when He was physically present before Arjuna is the same when He is present before us by His vani of Bhagavad Gita. So far I am concerned, I do not factually feel any separation from my Spiritual Master because I am trying to serve Him according to His desire. That should be the motto. If you kindly try to fulfill my missionfor which you have been sent there, that will be our constant association."

... "When you are helping my missionary activities I am always thinking of you and you are always thinking of me. That is real association. Just like I always think of my Guru Maharaja every moment, although he is not physically present and because I am trying to serve him to my best capacity, I am sure he is helping me by his spiritual blessings. So there are 2 kinds of association: physical and preceptorial. Physical association is not so important as preceptorial association."...

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

You say that, "Perhaps the Lord put me into a woman's body for my past sinful actions and I have not any hope to give you my devotional service, or to be near you somewhere." But, being in a woman's body does not mean that you have no hope for devotional service. Lord Krsna says in the Bhagavad-gita, [9.32](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/9.32):

O son ofPritha, those who take shelter in Me, though they be of lower birth—women, vaishyas \[merchants\] and shudras \[workers\]— can attain the supreme destination.

There are so many exalted Vaisnavis, who set the highest standard of devotion to Krsna by their own example. The important ingredient is that one serves with an attitude of love for Krsna, then one can transcend all bodily designations. Service to the spiritual master does not mean that there must be service to the physical body. We depend upon Krsna for all of our bodily needs. Service means to follow instructions, and that is available to everyone. By always remembering instructions, one is always with the s ' itual master.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

You asked about love for the spiritual master. It is natural to love that person who teaches you how to love Krsna. Although pure love for Krsna is already there in everyone's heart, due to our material contamination, it must be brought out by association. Without Krsna, there is no possibility of experiencing genuine love. He is the original fountainhead of all love. If the connection to Krsna is not made, then the experience is mundane, and will not endure.

So you must see your love for guru always in relation to your love for Krsna. However, real love also means unconditional, and is always accompanied by service. In the beginning stages of devotional service, the best way to express that love is by following the instructions of the guru with firm faith and confidence. On the mundane platform, we think that love must be expressed by serving the body and senses of our beloved. But the spiritual master is much more pleased when the disciple serves his instructions. That is the best way to show your love for the spiritual master. And when Krsna sees the determination that the disciple has to follow the instructions of guru, He blesses that devotee with increased attachment to Himself. Then we know that we are acting on the right platform.

You ask if it is possible to reach the level of pure devotion in this life. It is possible to achieve pure devotion, but only by the mercy of Krsna's pure devotee. Of course one never thinks that he can become equal or surpass one's spiritual master. The disciple always thinks himself to be in a subordinate position. But, the disciple must have firm faith that perfection is attainable, and as he feels himself advancing in Krsna consciousness, this confidence also increases.

The realizations expressed in this letter are evidence that Krsna is arranging for your advancement in devotional service. Whatever compassion I have is there only because Srila Prabhupada is showing so much compassion to me by allowing me to assist him in his glorious mission. He is kindly sending sincere girls like you to show other young girls less fortunate than yourself that life in service to Krsna is the only way to real happiness. When we experience compassion upon ourselves, then we must then share that experience by thinking of others who have no experience of such compassion. I was just giving a class last night from the following quote from Srila Prabhupada's letter dated August 14, 1976:

"If you feel at all indebted to me, then you should preach vigorously like me. That is the proper way to repay me. Of course, no one can repay the debt to the spiritual master, but the spiritual master is very much pleased by such an attitude by the disciple."

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear, Hare Krsna. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I'm sorry that I have not responded to your card prior to this. I think that you understand, though, how it has been difficult for me to get the time. I've just been very busy with my responsibilities in other areas.

Thank you for the offer to use your newly acquired skills to assist me in my endeavors. I must say that the most attractive one was the prospect that you will develop skills with the Russian language. This is still a great problem for me, since I just don't have a spare moment to learn any more Russian then what I can learn from my exchanges with the Soviet devotees. If you can learn how to read or write in Russian, I would certainly have things for you to do.

As far as graphics and desktop publishing goes, at this point in time, I don't have anything that I need done, but the best person to ask is Caitanya Nitai. He's obliged to produce newsletters and many other reports, brochures, etc. for congregational development. Maybe there's something that you could do for the temple, that he could suggest for you. But, I'll definitely keep it in mind, in the event that some personal need comes up in the future.

In regards to your spiritual life, it seems that you are perceptive of the powerful influence of Krsna's illusory energy.

Maya, is indeed very strong and we will be trapped by her glitter, unless we take shelter in Krsna. You say that everyone around you is also caught in maya's web, but you must try to appreciate that there are some who are overcoming her influence through the mercy of the Lord's pure devotee. If we simply strictly follow someone who's liberated, we can have great hope of being liberated ourselves. That is our hope, and our only hope. If we want to get out of maya s clutches, we must take help from someone who's not under her influence, just as if we are bound by our hands and feet by some rope, we must be released by someone who is not bound.

Srila Prabhupada is our source of hope and encouragement, and if we look to those who are strictly following for guidance, then we can be pulled along, too. In one letter Srila Prabhupada writes about his own relationship with his spiritual master as follows:

"Actually we are all conditioned souls so our demand from Krishna to accept us is unreasonable. From my personal point of view, I think that I am so sinful that I cannot even approach Krishna to show me any favor. But I have only one hope—my Spiritual Master—He is very kind. So someway or other He is dragging me towards Krishna. That is the only hope. Sri Chaitanya Charitamrita says therefore: Guru Krishna. By the mercy of the Spiritual Master, and by the mercy of Krishna, one gets into Krishna Consciousness. Narada Muni is our original Spiritual Master and he has dragged so many fallen souls towards Krishna, and we are also hoping to be dragged by Him through the disciplic succession. Otherwise, if we study our own qualifications, there is none—rather I have got so many disqualifications."

This letter is the ideal example of Srila Prabhupada's profound humility. He is teaching us that we must take shelter of the Lord's devotees if we actually want to remain hopeful about the best future.

I hope that that this letter finds you healthy and eagerly pursuing Krsna consciousness.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Regarding your own lack of faith and respect it seems as though it may be coming from the very same thing that I referred to above familiarity. Prabhupada describes this mentality in the following quotation:

"It is better service to Krishna and Spiritual Master in a feeling of separation; sometimes there is risk in the matter of direct service. For example he was giving me direct service by massaging, cooking for me, and so many other things; but later on by dictation of Maya, he became puffed up, so much so that he thought his Spiritual Master a common man, and was existing only on account of his service. This mentality at once pushed him down.
