The women should also collectively learn how to solve the bulk of their problems among themselves, rather than continuously bringing their problems to the temple presidents. I've not spoken a word with…, so, as I mentioned, I can't say why he's keeping you at a distance. But I do know that this kind of behavior by the women is often a problem in our temples. Of course, I'm not saying that it's always the mataji's fault, either. Sometimes, the men are also too fanatic, and they end up just suppressing the women. This is also a problem. But your safest position, is to just take it as Krsna's mercy, and learn how to depend more upon Krsna and the instructions you're getting from your authorities on all levels. Don't always demand their attention, and you may find that they'll become a little bit more sympathetic.

Regarding your preaching on book distribution, it's all right to preach, but you should not forget that your primary objective is to distribute books. Sometimes devotees, in the name of preaching, just spend a lot of time speaking with different people, because they don't want to throw themselves into the fire of sankirtan yajna. It's a lot easier to find somebody who likes to talk rather than having to confront people who may resist the idea of purchasing a book. This should be avoided. However, sometimes Krsna sends somebody who is really sincere and who has a lot of good questions. By all means, you can take the time to preach to them.

As far as the number of books you distribute is concerned, if you make your desire stronger, than Krsna can help to rectify that. Practically everybody starts book distribution by distributing only a very few books. But, if one sticks to his (her) duty, Krsna gives him the intelligence how to improve. Just like the point I was making the other day just before Srimad Bhagavatam class, about the devotee who was cooking the feast for several thousand people. He also had a very difficult time cooking large feasts when he first started doing his service. But Krsna helped him because he remained steady, and now he does it so nicely. The same thing applies with book distribution. That is your service, and you should try to accept that, and go on endeavoring.

So you should understand that because I'm being strong in this letter that it's not intended as a sign of rejection. You've asked me to please not reject you. If I had rejected you, then why would I take the time to give all this instruction to you. But I'm not going to pamper you. Do something about your situation, and rid yourself of your mental obsessions.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health and with increasing enthusiasm for participating in Lord Caitanya's Sankirtan Mission.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear Bhaktin,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I've enclosed a copy of my letter which was the answer to your first letter, I believe. As I mentioned to you in before leaving, I did answer this letter and mailed it to the address on the envelope, but evidently you never received it. The contents of that letter are somewhat outdated, because you have given up your art and choreography for the direct sankirtan mission, (as indicated by you in your second letter written in the train on the way to Riga). However, you may still show me a portfolio of your work, (if you have one), whenever it is convenient. A special trip just to give this to me is not required.

The indications in your last letter, as well as your second one, left me with the impression that you want some clear direction from me in regards to your service. I'm sensitive to the fact that you must overcome this psychological barrier, that you mentioned, when approaching nondevotees, since this is a common concern for many devotees when they first take up Lord Caitanya's Sankirtan Mission. However, I also know how much transcendental benefit can be derived from this activity, and those who dedicate themselves to learning the art of distributing Srila Prabhupada's books nicely will undoubtedly become strong in their convictions, and will derive incalculable benefit from this activity. Therefore for both your own benefit, as well as for the benefit of the fallen souls that you meet, you should go forward with confidence that this book distribution will bring me the greatest pleasure.

Yes, you can definitely write to me whenever you like, and keep me informed of your progress in devotional service.

Don't be afraid to tell me what I need to hear, rather than what you would like me to hear. There's a difference.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health and happily engaged in devotional service.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear Bhakta,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

The impressions I got from your letter, along with the information I received from Prabhu leads me to conclude that, for now, it is not important for us to develop a full temple in... Yes, what said is true. Lord Caitanya's glories must be spread through every town and village. But that does not mean that we need a full temple to spread his glories. In fact, in some cases, preaching is far more effective without having to manage temples. There is already a temple that's developing in Dnepropetrovsk, and that is nearby enough. However, although we don't need a temple in for now, we do need preaching. That is Lord Caitanya's vision for every town and village.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear Bhaktin,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

As I mentioned earlier today, many of the letters that were sent to me still remained unanswered, and yours was included among them. However, I'm trying to take the opportunity of my presence here in to answer some of those unanswered letters. I don't like to have to leave you feeling neglected, but the circumstances I described earlier made it too difficult.

It seems that, to some degree, I've already addressed some of the points in your letter during our meeting today. I'm hoping that you can see that I'm trying to see that ultimately everyone is helped. We don't want to see anyone discouraged from taking part in this wonderful movement; neither sincere souls like yourself, nor those who are presently more actively participating.

However, I cannot understand your reasoning for abandoning the distribution of Srila Prabhupada's books. If you are the "warrior" that you say that you are, then what better battle could be found than to try to nicely present Srila Prabhupada's books for others to purchase and read? Your own life is a perfect example of someone whose life was completely changed by reading Srila Prabhupada's books. Why should you deprive others from that great opportunity by abandoning this activity? Moreover, you have my full assurance that if you take up this mission, your realization of this philosophy and your conviction of Krsna's existence will automatically increase. I humbly beg that you please do not see this activity of selling Srila Prabhupada's books as some kind of materialistic activity for making money. Selling these books has nothing whatsoever to do with this material world. However, your motives for selling them should be proper. It should be done as an offering to your guru and Krsna.

Therefore I would humbly like to suggest that even if you have difficulty listening to , you do not use this as an excuse to disassociate yourself from the devotees, and abandon Srila Prabhupada's mission. As I already mentioned, we are trying to encourage everyone, and I've also spoken to about his manner of dealings, and I intend to speak with him more about it in the future. No matter who is in charge, there will be some degree of fault, but that does not mean that we should just disregard whatever sincerity that they have. Be patient, and steady in your sadhana and book distribution service, and, in time, Krsna will reveal more and more how you can become a great instrument for pushing on this glorious mission here in this material world.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

July 21, 1993

Dear devi dasi,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you very much for your nice report. Hearing about your increased convictions gave me great satisfaction. Just this evening, I gave a lecture to well over 200 devotees about Sarana- gati, and the six principles of surrender. For some reason, I was very enlivened this evening while speaking on this topic, and Krsna was giving me so many wonderful realizations that I was able to share with all of the devotees. The main theme we talked about was how Krsna encourages us through the process of surrender. I was getting the most wonderful realization of Krsna's words to Arjuna "ma sucah" or, "Do not fear". If we surrender to Him, we really have no reason at all to fear, because He has given His assurance that He will protect us in all circumstances. Realization of Krsna's protection is the most wonderful realization we can have in this material world. We can act with firm faith and conviction in all of our endeavors, free from fear of all kinds of adverse reactions. Krsna will protect us, and He is protecting you, too.

Yes, it took surrender to take up the service I had given to you. But Krsna saw that surrender, and reciprocated accordingly. This is Saranagati.

I'm very happy to hear that…and…have been"sparked" to continue with book distribution. I'm sure that your enthusiastic association has helped. And, hearing that they've gotten this seed from Rohini Suta Prabhu, is all the more encouraging, because I know that he's quite capable of helping this seed to grow into a beautiful fruit-bearing tree. Please tell them that hearing this has brought me great satisfaction. Two more devotees are entering into Lord Caitanya's Sankirtan lila, and through Saranagati, and their determination to invoke Lord Caitanya's pleasure by their endeavors, they will gradually enter deeper and deeper into the meaning behind Lord Caitanya's appearance in this material world. All glories to Lord Caitanya's Sankirtan devotees!!

Please use your intelligence, and try to set into place the best possible arrangement so that their chances to maintain their enthusiasm for preaching and book distribution is at the maximum level. This, and a strong program of reading Srila Prabhupada's books should be their top priority.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear dasa,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Yes, you made the right decision to accept this responsibility. It is a very important service, and you should take it up very seriously. You have my full blessings.

You can become Krsna conscious in that service, by seeing that you have been engaged in this service ultimately by Krsna. When difficulties arise, you should think, "Krsna has given me this service! Despite the difficulties, I must perform this service for His pleasure, and always endeavor to please Him." That is Krsna consciousness. Also, you MUST not allow yourself to become unregulated by that service and neglect your attendance to the morning program. Please always remember this. No business during the early morning hours! Chant all of your rounds, attend mangal arotik, guru puja and class. Then you can begin your duties. I cannot emphasize this enough. Otherwise, it will not be possible for you to be Krsna conscious.

In the beginning, while you're learning your new duties, it may not be possible to go out on sankirtan. But, once you learn how to execute your duties efficiently, I see no reason why you could not go out on sankirtan also. At least, you could dedicate one day a week for going out on sankirtan, and anything more than that, you should work out with your authorities. But personally, I feel that it is very healthy, spiritually, for you to dedicate at least one day a week for sankirtan, especially since you have the desire.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

November 13, 1993

Dear Bhaktin,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada. I have your letter in hand, and have noted the contents.

I recall that I already answered your letter when we got the opportunity to meet during my last visit to… However, since your letter was among several unanswered letters that I am an swering today, I thought that I would take the opportunity to write a few words of encouragement about the upcoming Srila Prabhupada Marathon.

This is the time of year when many devotees come forward to either personally distribute or to assist in the effort to distribute an increased number of Srila Prabhupada's books for his pleasure. It would be nice if you could be thinking this way, too. If, for some reason, you can't go out yourself, then maybe there's something that you could do to serve the book distributors. If there are some devotees who will be distributing locally, then maybe you could help by seeing that they get nice, hot prasadam every day. Or, maybe you could help them to transport their books, or do their laundry. If you think hard, you will find many ways in which you could take part in this effort. Best if you just ask the Sankirtan leader who will be coordinating this effort, since he(she) should know best what you could do. If not, then I'm sure that you could find some way to take part. This would be very pleasing to me.

I'm sure that you can appreciate the importance of distributing Srila Prabhupada's books. As you have written— your life completely changed by reading Srila Prabhupada's books. Just think how pleased he would be if one more person's life could be changed in the way that your life has changed. That is our sole purpose for living— to bring some pleasure to Srila Prabhupada.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear devi dasi,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have your letter (undated) which was given to me during my brief visit to . It was too difficult for me to answer it before leaving, so I was hoping that Krsna would give me a little time to answer it for you this way. It seems that He has.

Since your leaders have given you the choice to distribute either books or prasadam, and since up until now you have been successfully distributing books, I see no reason, whatsoever, why you should "switch" to prasadam. It may have been suggested that for some devotees who had difficulty, for whatever reason, distributing books could possibly become more inspired to distribute prasadam. That is true. But I do not see that in your case. I'm always very pleased to hear the results of your book distribution efforts— especially when I see your name often on the weekly sankirtan results. Your steadiness on book distribution enlivens me, because although you are not a "leading" book distributor, still you are a steady book distributor. This steadiness in your service will help you to deepen your convictions, as well as help you to make rapid progress in Krsna Consciousness. And naturally the more that you have to preach to convince nondevotees to take "Krsna" in the form of transcendental literature, the more you will become convinced. So I definitely prefer you to distribute books.

I hope that this answers your question to your satisfaction, and that you are healthy and happy in Krsna consciousness.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

April 7, 1994

Dear Bhaktin,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have your letter, dated 01.03.94, that was hand-delivered to me in Mayapura, and I have noted the contents.

Thank you very much for your description of your experiences on book distribution. Hearing stories about how Srila Prabhupada's books are being purchased, appreciated, and how they are positively affecting people's lives brings me unbounded happiness. Srila Prabhupada's books are my worshipful Deity. They're everything, because they are non-different from Srila Prabhupada. Thank you very much for taking up this service, because I know that it makes Srila Prabhupada happy, too. I have no other reason for living than for the pleasure of Srila Prabhupada.

In answer to your questions:

1\) You say that it is difficult for you to remain fixed in book distribution when the other book distributors are away from the temple. This is natural, but not insurmountable. As much as possible, you should associate with the other matajis who are distributing books when they are there in . They are not always traveling. Especially now that I've requested more distribution to take place in Ukraine, there must be devotees distributing in , too. Kiev is the biggest and most important city in the Ukraine, and we're building a nice, big temple for the people of - not for ourselves. The more books we distribute in , and the more preaching we do in , will give all the more people the opportunity to come and take the darshan of Sri Sri Gour Nitai, and to hear about Their transcendental glories in the association of devotees. In fact, just three weeks ago in India, I personally spoke with the wood carver who is preparing the deities of Sri Sri Gour Nitai that will come to . By Lord Caitanya's grace, They will be ready to come by October. We should be very eager to preach and distribute books in Kiev for this will certainly attract Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityananda to come and give us Their darshan.

2\) You can inspire other devotees to engage in book distribution by being an ideal example yourself. Inspiration from preaching and from book distribution is infectious. If we're inspired and enthusiastic it naturally affects others. But if we're just always struggling ourselves, then no matter what we say to others, we will not be able to inspire them. So YOU become inspired and that will naturally inspire others.

3\) I'm not sure who's opinion you're talking about when you say that there is an opinion that reading Srila Prabhupada's books is not as important as practical service. Moreover, I'm not sure if this statement is being taken out of context, either by you or by the person who said it. But, in principle, we "must" be reading regularly from Srila Prabhupada's books. There is the famous quote that Srila Prabhupada once made in a letter to Sastvarupa Maharaja in 1968:

"...We want to distribute our literature and books within as well as our prasadam, and injecting our Hare Krishna medicine within the ear. So, reading of the literature and hearing of the chanting is the medicine, and prasadam is the diet. So, if diet and medicine are properly administered the disease of maya will be cured.

But the physician must be always healthy. People may not say, physician is ill thyself. That means the preachers must be of highly elevated character, following strictly the rules and regulations and chanting regularly in the temple..."

This quote is quite clear. Prabhupada explains that we must always remain healthy, and therefore to insure good health we must take the right medicine-- that is reading his books and chanting the holy names. Practical service must be there, but the whole foundation of such service is based upon our regular hearing about Krsna from Srila Prabhupada's books. Of course, Srila Prabhupada did make many statements on this matter. He also said that if we attended daily the classes of Srimad Bhagavatam and Bhagavad-gita taught from Srila Prabhupada's books, then that was sufficient. Beyond that whatever time Krsna provided for reading should be taken, but we should not demand it. But he never minimized the importance of regularly reading or hearing from his books.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

May 10, 1994

Dear devi dasi,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada. I have your letter, translated for me by Murari Krsna dasa, and have noted the contents.

From your description it seems that the new stage of book distribution that you are moving into refers to a new stage of "consciousness". Krsna is exposing your motivations, and now you are getting the opportunity to see just how this sankirtan process is actually working. This is not ordinary book-selling activity. It is devotional service in very high doses, and by continuing the treatment the heart gradually becomes clean.

However, do not become overly discouraged by your competitive spirit. Competition also exists in the spiritual world, but in the spiritual world such competition inspires one to serve Krsna better, while simultaneously desiring to see others serve Krsna better, too. It is free from ill-wishing thoughts. Here are a few quotes from some of Srila Prabhupada's letters in this regard:

"The difference between competition and Maya. Here, competition is for sense gratification. How much I can enjoy my senses, and the other person sees how much he can enjoy his senses. In the spiritual world the competition is how to satisfy Krishna. There the center is one. If you draw innumerable circles they will not overlap. But here the circles will overlap. Even a small circle can overlap a large circle. This is because in the material world there are many centers, whereas in the spiritual world there is only one center. In the spiritual world whether the circles are big or small they will never overlap. In the spiritual world similarly, whatever or whoever is rendering the service, there is never competition of maliciousness." (SPL to Kirtiraja dasa, Jan 2nd, 1972.)

"So far your question about jealousy, spiritual jealousy means to think always that the service rendered by such and such devotee is so nice and pleasing to Krishna, so let me try harder to please Krishna than him, and then Krishna will notice me also. " (SPL to Sankarsa- na, June 13th, 1972.)

"Our competition is how to please Krishna more than someone else. There must always be competition. That gives life. That cannot be separated from life. The perfect society does not eliminate competition, but it eliminates envy, because everyone is weak before Krishna! LIL Vol 6 P. 41 & 42

"In the Vaikuntha factually there is no fault in anyone, but there is another type of competition. This competition is that one devotee thinks of other devotees how nicely they are serving the Lord. In the material would the attitude is that everyone likes to think that I am doing better than others. This is material conception. In the Spiritual Sky it is just the opposite: Everyone thinks that my contemporary devotees are doing better than me. We are trained to address Godbrothers as Prabhu, which means Master. This means we shall try to find out always the serving side of our Godbrothers. Sometimes there are misgivings, but that we should try to overlook." (SPL to Brahmananda, Nov. 15th, 1969.)

So now Krsna is not giving you such big results. He's trying to keep you humble. Just like the point that I was explaining in class the other night. Krsna could give us big results very quickly. For instance, He could give us the whole world, but if He did, would we be pure enough to handle it? Similarly Krsna could also give big results on book distribution, but if you're getting big results will you then start thinking that all these results were gotten by you? Big results can also bring big pride. But Lord Caitanya doesn't like to see pride in His devotees. So sometimes He arranges things in such a way to keep us in the proper "dependent" mentality. Just go on performing your service, always depending upon Him for success in your constant endeavor. Lord Caitanya will take care of everything else, and He'll take you back home, back to Godhead, for engaging in this service which is so dear to His pure devotee, Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you very much for serving Srila Prabhupada in this way. I'm confident that he is seeing all of your endeavors, and that he will bless you unlimitedly. In fact, you are already blessed, otherwise how could you be so fully engaged in this glorious mission.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health and always enthusiastic in devotional service.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

June 16, 1994

Dear devi dasi,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Your letter dated March 24, 1994, was waiting for me upon my return to America last week. I have read it and noted the contents.

Not every devotee can get big results on book distribution. I also distributed books for many years but never really got "big" results in comparison to other godbrothers who always steadily distributed a large quantity of books. But "big" scores should not be the only result that we should look for from our efforts on book distribution. The devotee who learns how to fully depend upon Krsna for the results of his endeavors, develops all good qualities such as tolerance, humility, patience and determination. These results are essential for our progressive development in Krsna consciousness. Do not become discouraged by your small scores. More important than the scores is the effort, and your desire to please your spiritual master and Krsna by this effort. The real result is that our attachment to Krsna should increase more and more.

Moreover, Krsna will sometimes withhold big results to keep His devotees from becoming too proud. Or, He may also want to make His devotee more dependent upon Him, making him feel completely helpless. Whatever Krsna's reason, a sincere devotee will think only of his duty, and will not abandon his duty due to some attachment for a result. Success or failure is in Krsna's hands. We are simply meant to execute the order coming down through the spiritual master.

So I am very happy to hear that you were going out on book distribution for the Gaur Purnima marathon. Whether you go out all the time, or only some of the time, must be determined by your authorities, but it would be nice if you continue to go at least some of the time. This service will help you become strong, and will give you much realization and purification.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health and happily engaged in Lord Caitanya's Sankirtan mission.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

June 16, 1994

Dear d.d.,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have your letter (undated) which was handed to me in Moscow and have enthusiastically noted the contents. Thank you very much for this nice report.

Having read this letter, now I can understand why you asked me to please elaborate upon the importance of college preaching during one of my lectures in Kiev. It's clear that you are imbibing the sankirtan spirit, and that you are appreciating the importance of this wonderful movement.

I was also very happy to hear that you are cultivating a whole district by distributing Srila Prabhupada's books from door to door. The story about Father Nikolai was very nice to hear. This is a perfect example which shows that the Christians can appreciate our movement because we are doing the real work of Lord Jesus Christ. You should try to get Father Nikolai to write a letter of appreciation of our work, and he should write it on his own letterhead. This may be very valuable for us in the future. Unfortunately, many Christian leaders do not understand our efforts, and instead they feel threatened by your preaching.

Please continue your efforts to distribute Srila Prabhupada's mercy, and continue to work cooperatively with the local authorities. This is very pleasing to me.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

June 15, 1994

Dear d.d.,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada. I received your letter via COM and noted the contents.

I am always thinking of my disciples who are making so many sacrifices for serving Srila Prabhupada's mission, and therefore I'm always thinking of the responsibility that I have to see that they remain inspired to continue their sacrifices. Although I am thousands of miles away on the other side of this planet, I'm sitting here thinking of this duty. Therefore I am writing.

There's so much suffering on this planet. I'm seeing it wherever I go. There's only a false sense of happiness— but it doesn't last very long. Materialistic people go from one hope to another, looking for the something that will fill the emptiness that remains in their hearts. They don't know that only Krsna can satisfy them. They create their false gods, their false objects of worship— and thus they've forgotten completely about Krsna. Without Krsna there is nothing. We have a duty here in this world. Otherwise, we really have no other reason for being here. If we forget this duty, we'll also fall into this false sense of happiness— thinking that we can find some way to satisfy ourselves separate from Krsna. Our only hope is to satisfy the Lord. If He is pleased by our efforts, then, and only then, can we feel real happiness. Krsna is most pleased when we make the supreme sacrifice to tell others about Him. And Krsna's devotees are pleased when Krsna is pleased.

Please therefore, continue to help remove all of this suffering. You are the greatest welfare worker for human society, because you are giving people their only real hope in life. Don't ever forget this.

If you simply remember these instructions, Krsna will give you all facility to push on with this mission. He'll give you all good association. He'll never forget you.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health, and happily engaged in spreading the Lord's glories.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

June 15, 1994

Dear d.d.

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada. I have received your letter and have noted the contents.

In response to your questions:

1\) Materialists always look at devotional service according to their own materialistic views. Devotional service cannot be understood by anyone except a devotee. To a materialist, it may appear that devotees are mercenary, but a genuine devotee is always anxious to employ everything in the service of the Lord. He knows that Krsna is the only beneficiary of all sacrifices, and therefore he also knows that everything belongs to Krsna. This is real renunciation. As we develop this consciousness more and more, then others will appreciate these qualities. But there will always be demoniac people who don't want to surrender to Krsna. Krsna describes them in the Bhagavad gita as mudhas, the lowest of mankind, those whose knowledge has been stolen by illusion, and those who are outright atheistic. They see everything according to their own mentality, and therefore they think that devotees are just after money and wealth for their own enjoyment. But Vaisnavas are very merciful, and therefore they are always anxious to help those who are materialistic by preaching to them about Krsna, and inducing them to make sacrifices for the Lord's pleasure.

2\) The inspiration to remain steady in the execution of your duty will come by continuous hearing about Krsna's glories from Krsna's devotees.

Hoping this meets you in good health and blissfully engaged in Lord Caitanya's Sankirtan mission.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear dasa,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you very much for your letter which I received from Mahasakti a few days ago. I was very happy to hear about your realizations from your latest endeavors on book distribution.

Certainly it's much more difficult to distribute books under the present conditions than the way you were distributing before. Now you are being exposed to the austerity of constant rejection. So many people to approach, but also so many people who are saying, NO! It's a real test for the false ego. But as you are also experiencing, by remaining steady in the face of so many obstacles, the purification is more acute, too. Lord Krsna explains in the Bhagavad gita, ([5.11](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/5.11)) "The yogis, abandoning attachment, act with body, mind, intelligence and even with the senses, only for the purpose of purification." That should be your purpose when you go out on book distribution.

You have made the right adjustment for book distribution in the city. Keep a very tight program, leaving no time for idle engagement. Make sure you read at least 1-2 hours every day, make sure you get sufficient rest, make sure you chant attentive rounds, and make sure you keep very strict regulation. This will help to keep your inner strength strong, enabling you to withstand the intensity of the engagement.

Your letter has made me very happy, because the spiritual master is always happy to see his disciple advancing in devotional service. Please continue distributing Srila Prabhupada's books with ever-increasing enthusiasm and determination, and make your life completely successful in sankirtan yajna.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

October 27, 1994

Dear devi dasi,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have your letter dated July 15, 1994 and your letter which was hand-delivered by dasa as well. Thank you very much for these letters. I am always happy to hear from you. Please do not think that I am neglecting you by taking almost three months to reply. From time to time, I fall behind with my correspondence. It always seems to be increasing, and without a permanent secretary it's very hard to keep up with all my communications. I want to assure you, however, that whenever you write to me, I read your letters, usually more than once, as soon as I get them. However, my thoughts of you and your service in Poland, are not manifest only when I receive your letters. I often think of how nicely you are serving Sri Sri Panca Tattva, and how you are always striving for opportunities to preach and distribute Srila Prabhupada's books.

When we are not spontaneously attracted to preaching Krsna's glories 24 hours a day, we must be motivated to tell others about Krsna by the order to preach. Lord Caitanya has given all of this order— to tell everyone about Krsna. And when a devotee makes the effort, sacrificing his own conveniences to induce others to take to Krsna consciousness, Lord Caitanya becomes pleased by that sacrifice. Whether we feel pleased or not, is secondary to the Lord's pleasure. Only when we are prepared to abandon all personal pleasure, can we actually hope to give pleasure to the Lord. Therefore we must become free from all personal motivation and ambition when preaching. Lord Caitanya has taught us, na danam na janam na sundarim.... we don't want wealth, we don't want followers, and neither do we want to enjoy the opposite sex. We simply want be engaged in the service of the Lord birth after birth. We should not even aspire to become free from our sinful reactions. Therefore Lord Caitanya says, "birth after birth". Even Srila Bhaktivinode Thakur prays that, "I don't mind if I take birth as an insect, but just let me take birth in the house of a devotee." We must therefore abandon all personal motivations completely, and simply take up the order with full faith that all success will be there. This will enable a preacher to continue— even if the Lord wishes us to continue birth after birth after birth.

Yes, it is a fact. Krsna never forgets the service rendered by His devotee. That is His mercy. Another name for Krsna is Bhava grahi Janardana, which means that he accepts the essence of a devotee's attitude. Even if it's mixed, Krsna takes the essence, and therefore as soon as devotee begins devotional service, He immediately gets help from the Lord.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

You may recall the lecture which I gave at the Moscow Sankirtan festival. I particularly emphasized in that lecture my anxiety that our temple devotees serve the book distributors and, in general, the book distribution mission by encouraging those who read Srila Prabhupada's books to make progress in devotional service. I am very anxious to combine the efforts of the book distributors and the preachers to expand Lord Caitanya's mercy in a way that will bring more and more living entities under the protection of the Lord's internal potency. I so much want to see everyone who comes to us for shelter to remain under the protection of devotional service. Please keep this in mind when you are distributing Srila Prabhupada's books, that we not only want these people to purchase Srila Prabhupada's books, but we want them to read them, and then to find shelter in the association of devotees.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear Bhaktin,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have your letter dated 1/1/95 and have noted the contents.

It takes great determination to remain steady on Sankirtan.

Therefore you need to distribute books in the association of devotees who demonstrate such determination. Association is very important. The mind will always reject that which is difficult. The mind looks for the path of least resistance. You need to be constantly reminded that this material world is not a place for enjoyment. Then you will be less inclined to look for a comfortable situation. Although it would certainly be nice, you cannot expect to have such determination without strong association. Therefore seek it out. If none is available, then just continue cooking and distributing prasadam for Food for Life. But you should still go out on Sankirtan for at least one day a week, even if the results are very little. Do it for your own purification.

Regarding your parents, when you say that you are having problems with them, I am assuming that they do not look favorably upon your involvement in Krsna Consciousness. Therefore you have to be patient with them and don't try to force them to accept your way of thinking. Not everyone can immediately accept Krsna consciousness, although we sometimes think that everyone will because it makes so much sense to us. Every living entity has different degrees of material covering. Some will simply not be able to immediately accept. Maintain contact with your parents. Show them that being a devotee does not mean that one rejects his family. Naturally, they will see that, as a devotee, you have different goals in life. They will be fearful of this, because a devotee's goals are not based upon the bodily conception of life. But any sensitive person can appreciate that a devotee develops all good qualities. A devotee is kind, merciful, sinless, friendly towards everyone— even the animals. You should be developing these qualities, and hopefully your parents will gradually begin to appreciate the favorable changes that have taken place in your life.

Hoping this letter meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

February 14, 1995

Dear Bhaktin,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

If you recall, when we spoke in Vinnitza, I explained that your letter was sent to Moscow to be translated. I received the translation the day I left Moscow on January 5. During the past month, I have been catching up on my North American correspondence, and now I am trying to catch up on my correspondence from the CIS.

After reading through your letter again, the impression I'm left with is that the main point of your letter was already addressed when we spoke in . The primary purpose of this letter, therefore, is to assure you that I have your translated letter in my possession, and that I am now fully aware of all the details that have concerned you.

I was very encouraged when I asked you if your efforts on book distribution were prompted more out of frustration from all of your other services, rather than the actual desire to distribute Srila Prabhupada's books. Your answer was clear, that it was not. Now, having read your letter again, it only serves to reconfirm this point. You are actually catching up the mission that Srila Prabhupada has left for his followers for generations to come.

I am very grateful for your sincere efforts to distribute Srila Prabhupada's books whenever possible. This is real selfless service. Materialistic people will always try to use every available moment when not working to gratify their senses. A selfless devotee uses every available moment to spread the glories of the Supreme Lord, for no other purpose than to please guru and Krsna. This service will never go unnoticed by Lord Caitanya.

Regarding your other efforts to use your skills as a lawyer in Krsna's service, at this point I see no need to bring these memories back to the surface. I also do not know why it appeared that your efforts were fruitless, but I can say with completely confidence, that any effort aimed for the pleasure of guru and Krsna is never fruitless. If our consciousness is fixed on the right goal, there is never any loss or diminution.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health and happily engaged in Lord Caitanya's Sankirtan Movement.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear devi dasi,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have your letter (undated) which was handed to me in Mayapura a few weeks ago.

Thank you very much for your sincere prayers. These prayers gladden my heart. They purify your heart and the hearts of all those who hear them.

You are very kind to the conditioned souls. You are giving them the greatest opportunity available for breaking the bonds of material attachment— transcendental knowledge found in Srila Prabhupada's books. Yet, still we see again and again, that these material bonds are made of very strong cords. Sometimes materialists think that we are beggars trying to live off the hard labor of others, or that we are simply interested in making business by the sale of our books. As much as we try to reveal to them our motives, Srila Prabhupada's motives, and Lord Caitanya's motives, still we will see that most of them cannot see the Lord's plan for them in all of this. This is their great misfortune. Still we must try to make them fortunate, and our efforts will never go in vain. Please be assured of this.

Even if most do not take a book, if you are Krsna conscious and desiring for them the opportunity for devotional service, there is undoubtedly some effect in their lives. Even though the conditioned soul may not be consciously aware of what you are doing, still that exchange with a devotee can become an experience which will bear an effect upon the soul within. I often think of my godbrother Prabhupada dasa who became Srila Prabhupada's disciple ten years after his early meeting with Srila Prabhupada because he could not forget the way in which Srila Prabhupada had approached him to put on a dhoti. Prabhupada dasa turned away and rejected even Srila Prabhupada's offer, and externally he was behaving like a materialist. But ten years later, by reassociating himself with devotees, he surrendered himself to Srila Prabhupada. I even remember when a devotee offered me a book at a book table set up where I was registering for classes in college. I was totally uninterested. Little did I know that that same book when seen on a book shelf in a book store would be the book that would change the whole course of my life.

So please never become discouraged, and simply go on with this confidential service for Lord Caitanya. Your life will become completely successful, Srila Prabhupada has assured us of this.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

There is much to learn for developing a membership program— more than I can possibly put into a letter to you at this time. But the most important principle behind membership is that first and foremost, you must see all members and potential members as living entities with a desire to become Krsna conscious. If you preach to cultivate this desire within them and demonstrate that you actually care about them and not their money, they will naturally reciprocate by becoming members of ISKCON. I have personal experience with this. Oftentimes here in the West, I will visit people in their homes, will bring them prasadam, or maybe even a small gift from India, and will use this as an opportunity to visit them. I will not ask them for anything, but will instead inquire about their well-be- ing both material and spiritual. In time, and through regular visitations many of these people have come to spontaneously give their support without even asking for it from them. One man that I regularly visit gives no less than $5,000 every year to the Boston temple. Others have become Life Members of the Boston temple several times. They want to help. That is the expertise of a good membership director. They have to feel some connection to the temple, and it is the director's responsibility to help make that connection. Gradually as more and more people become members, the program begins to develop some form.

Of course, there are also fixed guidelines for membership, such as the use of membership forms, benefits for the members, payment schedules, membership events, membership lists and records, etc., etc. Gradually these things will need to be employed, too. For instance, I know that Gopal Krsna Maharaja has produced some Life Membership forms in Russian, and they can be acquired, I'm sure, from devotees in his zone. But do not become too worried about getting all these things together before you can start a membership program. As with anything else, it will take time and patience. Most important is the desire and the determination to please guru and Krsna. Everything else will come in due course of time.

So there is so much here in this letter for you to get started with. Please take this matter seriously, and I am looking forward to hear of your efforts in this regards.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

I have a great concern for the overall preaching in Ukraine. I have been preaching here now for six years, and my responsibility as the GBC for Ukraine always forces me to analyze the effectiveness of our overall preaching efforts. It is for this reason that I often spend long periods of time meeting with the leaders, hearing their analysis of the preaching that is being conducted, and clarifying different policies with them which must be put into place. It is sometimes necessary to adjust our preaching according to time, place and circumstance. This means that everyone must work cooperatively, and nobody should think that it is their preaching alone, which is spreading Krsna consciousness. We will be most effective in our preaching when we work in a united way.

Therefore, based upon experience elsewhere, it has been found that the methods the devotees use to distribute Srila Prabhupada's books has the greatest impact on how people perceive our movement. Elsewhere in the world, where the Krsna consciousness movement has been established since the early days of ISKCON, our movement has built its reputation on its methods of book distribution, more than anything else. Unfortunately, in some places, although many books were distributed, the overall impact in the minds of the people was not as favorable as we would have liked it to be. Many people were disillusioned with our methods of book distribution. Devotees were, at times, just too aggressive. Much work has been done to change that image, but nonetheless, it would have been better if we could have avoided that altogether.

Therefore, since I am very concerned about our methods of book distribution, I oftentimes remind the leaders that it is their duty to carefully observe how our book distributors are behaving, and if there is any discrepancy, they should correct it.

From your letter, and previous letters as well, it's dear that you are very enthusiastic to distribute Krsna consciousness. I do not want to dampen this enthusiasm of yours by restricting you from preaching, however, it seems that based upon my persistent cautioning to the leaders, they perceive that you are one of those devotees that has a tendency to be overly aggressive in your approach to distribute Srila Prabhupada's books. I must respect their judgment.

Naturally, it would be best if you could change your approach, and learn to present Krsna consciousness enthusiastically, but sensitively as well. However, to do that, you MUST be willing to change, and you must be willing to learn how to present Srila Prabhupada's books from those who know how to present them in an acceptable way. Sometimes, this is not always possible. Devotees tend to become conditioned by their old habits.

For now, it is my strong opinion, and the opinion of the leaders as well, that it would be better if you would distribute prasadam instead of books. To get people to take prasadam, one does not have to be so aggressive. And the overall impact of prasadam distribution is undoubtedly favorable for our preaching as well. In time, with this experience on prasadam distribution, it may become possible for you to enter back into the field for book distribution again. This will depend upon your own ability to sensitize yourself to the people you meet, and it will also depend upon the judgment of the local leaders. I am appealing to you to please take up this service enthusiastically (and sensitively), and help us spread Krsna consciousness in way that will attract thousands and thousands of conditioned souls to the lotus feet of Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

June 21, 1995

Dear Bhaktin,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you for your letter. I have noted the contents carefully.

I'm happy to hear your appreciation for the importance of book distribution. Yes, this is our most important activity. Anyone who goes out into the public, with Srila Prabhupada's books in his/her hands, is immediately seen as a representative of Srila Prabhupada, and Lord Caitanya. This is a grave responsibility, and one which must be taken very seriously. Lord Caitanya is entrusting this responsibility upon all of us to present the message of Krsna consciousness in a way that others will be able to hear. Just like Srila Prabhupada, when he was on board the ship the Jaladuta, he was praying to Lord Krsna to be able to present the Lord's message in such a way so that it can be heard. Therefore every preacher, book distributor, and representative of Srila Caitanya Mahaprabhu must always be very vigilant to keep their motivations for spreading Krsna consciousness very pure. This means chanting attentive rounds, regularly hearing the message of Srimad Bhagavatam, worshiping the Deity, rising early in the morning, and cleaning one's heart by always keeping oneself engaged in serving the Lord. We do not have any time to be idle. Our lives are too short, and Lord Caitanya's mission is too important.

Therefore, as much as possible, please continue to try to distribute Srila Prabhupada's books, but do so always under the guidance of senior devotees.

Thank you very much. Hoping this meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

For some reason, membership is always seen as a program for making Laxmi. I prefer to never look at it that way. I see membership as a program for making people Krsna conscious. When that becomes our primary point of concern, then there is a natural reciprocation that takes place. People will want to engage their energies in service to Krsna. Not everyone can live in the temple, and not everyone should live in the temple. For those who cannot, there must be a program designed for making them feel a part of the temple. Moreover, there are many people who do not know about Krsna consciousness, and they can be drawn closer to Krsna through a well-developed membership program as well. Therefore membership preaching is very broad-based, and it MUST NOT always be looked at as a program which MUST generate Laxmi in order for it to be considered successful. I am convinced that if the basic principles that Srila Prabhupada has given us for helping others to progress in their Krsna consciousness are followed, then membership preaching will be successful (both in helping others to progress in KC, and in generating Laxmi for preaching). But unless the preachers and the leaders have such a vision, then membership preaching will simply be seen as a front for getting people's money without caring for their future as devotees of Lord Krsna. If membership is approached in this way, then I would say that better we do not do any kind of membership program at all. But I sincerely hope we would never have to come to such a decision.

As a membership preacher, you should not be demanding facilities to do your preaching. Rather, you should have faith, that once you have embraced this instruction, and have allowed it to seriously enter into your heart, Krsna will help you with all the facility that you need, as long as you keep the spirit of selfless preaching as the foundation for all of your activities. Naturally, there must be at least some basic assistance from your authorities for starting a membership program, and that is what I spoke to Vasudama Prabhu about before I left St. Petersburg.

I hope that this answers your questions in a way that is clearly understandable for you.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

July 31, 1995

Dear devi dasi,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada. Thank you for your letter dated May 31, 1995.1 have carefully read it and noted the contents.

I was very happy to attend that festival with Indradyumna Maharaja and Bhakti Brnga Govinda Maharaja. It was a wonderful festival, one which I will not so easily forget either.

Although your letter did not have any particular questions, you did ask for my blessings and for some instructions for sankirtan. I always emphasize to my disciples that the most important principle to remember is that sankirtan must be performed with the right attitude. As I mentioned in my initiation lecture yesterday, a devotee's behavior should never be offensive to anyone. One becomes free from offensive behavior when his mind and senses are controlled in the higher engagement of service to Krsna. A devotee goes out on sankirtan to please guru and Krsna. A devotee wants to see others become Krsna conscious, and is not interested in personal glory or profit for himself. With this mentality a devotee can remain ever-fresh despite the apparent offensive behavior which can oftentimes be made towards him when he goes out to distribute Srila Prabhupada's books. We should always think of ourselves as an instrument for giving Krsna consciousness to others. The best instrument is one who has no personal motive or ambition for pleasing himself. With these thoughts in mind, you most certainly have my blessings to continue to go out on sankirtan, and to benedict those residents of who have forgotten their eternal relation with Lord Krsna, to once again seek His association from reading Srila Prabhupada's books. This will make your life very successful, and you will become and remain happy in service to guru and Krsna.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

I am, Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear Prabhu,

PAMHO. AGTSP.

As we spoke about in Sucharevo last week, I said that I would send you some basic principles behind the "Contact Sankirtan" concept.

Please bear in mind, that the concept behind contact Sankirtan involves the utilization of a strong support system for maintaining the program. When I say strong support system, I mean that it requires funds, manpower, cooperation, an office, vehicle and equipment (such as a computer). But you should not be discouraged if all these things are not immediately manifest. When I started, I also had nothing, but gradually these things became available as the program started to grow.

Most important is that you need to develop a database of names and addresses of people who are potential purchasers of sets of Srila Prabhupada's books. Or, in other words, you have to define your "universe" according to the way that Sridhar Maharaja described it in his presentation last week. How to define it? Well the following is some examples of ways that you can start.

1\) Get names of people who correspond with the BBT. I already spoke with Sannyas dasa about this and he agreed to give out names to anyone who would responsibly take care of them.

2\) Get names of people who visit the temple programs. In most temples there is a guest book which people are encouraged to sign. However, the best way to encourage people to sign the guestbook is by telling them that by signing they will be put on our mailing list to receive news of upcoming events. (More about that later.)

3\) The book distributors should be encouraged to get names and addresses of interested people that they meet when they go out on local distribution. (This is the cooperation part.) Usually the book distributors will claim that they are too busy to get the names, but generally it is not very difficult at all if they actually meet interested people. They should just have a simple form that they can fill out right on the spot. When they meet an interested person, they should just ask them if they would be interested to receive our newsletter which includes news of upcoming events. Most people will not mind at all.

4\) Put inserts into the books being distributed locally. Print up a card that can be mailed in requesting more information. There are many samples of cards that can be used. I'm sure that

Sannyas dasa could help you with this one.

5\) At all preaching programs devotees should be encouraged to collect names in the same way. They should have a guestbook, and any interested persons who come up for books should be encouraged to sign the book to be put on a mailing list.

6\) Anyone who phones the temple to make inquiries should be asked for their name and address for the same reason.

7\) Anyone who writes letters to the temple making inquiries should be added to the mailing list.

8\) I don't know about in Russia, but in the West there are other groups who have mailing lists and they sell their lists to other groups. For instance there are vegetarian groups, philosophical groups, book buyers groups, etc. These mailing lists would be considered "cold" contacts, whereas names which we receive ourselves are either "warm" or "hot", depending where they came from. With "cold" contacts, you don't want to waste time visiting people if they have shown no interest in Krsna consciousness at all. Therefore what we used to do when we got such lists is that we used to put together a mailout packet, with a newsletter and a response card in the newsletter. If the people sent in a response card showing some interest, we would move their name into the "warm" category, and then would discard all the names of people who did not reply. (I think by now you can begin to see how important it is to have a newsletter, or at least "something" which can mailed out to people who show interest.)

9\) As the contact sankirtan leader, you have to always remind devotees about the importance of getting names. Unfortunately, it is commonly forgotten. But when everyone is reminded, and then the devotees start hearing the scores from the Contact Sankirtan party, gradually devotees start becoming more inspired to get names.

As your database of names grows and grows, gradually you are beginning to define your universe. Then from that list you can put together names and addresses into different geographical areas. Then devotees can go out to visit these people. Again, I don't know what the best time of day is in Russia, but in the U.S., nobody is home during the day. Therefore the devotees would have to leave everyday between 5:00 to 6:00 PM, and they would spend about 3 to 4 hours visiting people in the evenings.

The distributors would always travel in pairs. It's better that way, because sometimes you are invited in, and there may be only one woman at home. Therefore it's not good to go alone. They should bring with them some small packet of prasadam. Prasadam is essential. In fact, if they visited people, and they were not home, they would leave the prasadam at the door with a note, explaining that they were in the area, they stopped by to see them, the purpose of their visit, (to show them some of our books, or whatever) but since they were not home, they decided to just leave them a little sample of some of our vegetarian food. Then just leave the prasadam, with a note, and a card (business card is good) with your name, phone number, and address. Then the next time you visit them, they will definitely remember the prasadam, and there's a much better chance that they will invite you in. In fact, sometimes, people even call after the prasadam was left and they ask the devotees to return at a time when they are home.

Besides prasadam, the distributors should bring a variety of books, tapes, incense, life membership forms, and even some devotional paraphernalia. Naturally, you'll want to concentrate on encouraging people to buy books, so you should always make that the first in the order of your presentation. But sometimes, people are just not interested in buying books right away. They may want to think about it. You can then possibly interest them in other things. Membership forms are always good if people are very familiar with our movement, and they can afford to become members. But, before that can work there must be a very developed membership program. Don't make them members if they are not going to be treated like members, and especially if they don't get the benefits of becoming members. The membership program is a completely different program which should be developed side by side with your contact sankirtan, and it should be organized by somebody else. But if it organized, contact sankirtan can be a very effective way of both making members and selling sets of Srila Prabhupada's books together. When they become a member, they automatically get a set of Srila Prabhupada's books.

Regarding the newsletter, as I mentioned, this part of the program is absolutely essential. If you have nothing to mail to people, then you have no reason for getting their names. It doesn't have to be something expensive or fancy. It can even be printed on one sheet of paper. But the point is that "something" has to be written, printed, and mailed regularly (at least once every two months) so that you can maintain regular contact with the people on the mailing list. The newsletter is also something which is intimately connected with the membership program. Every membership program should have a newsletter. There are many different examples of newsletters, but more or less, they are what you make it. Again, it costs money to print and mail a newsletter every two months, but the whole concept behind the newsletter is to expand our preaching to the congregation. And the concept behind congregation building was definitely emphasized a lot in the Moscow seminars last week. It MUST be done.

Anyway, there are more details, but if I give you too much, then you'll just get caught up in the details. This is definitely something to start with. As I said, don't become discouraged. This is not the type of program that can be developed overnight. It took me many years to develop it in the U.S. But if you have the vision for it, and the desire and determination, the program can gradually grow and become very dynamic.

There are many devotees who are far more expert in these matters than I am. As you start developing the program, you could probably start communicating with them, too. But many details may vary from place to place. Most important is to start with the basics, and then gradually you can start working out the details.

I hope that this helps you to get started.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

Your servant, Niranjana Swami

## About faith and surrender

Because you're thinking that you still have some obligations to take care of, it seems that, in essence, what you are asking me is, "Am I capable of giving up all other obligations and fully surrendering to Krsna?". Ultimately every living entity is capable of fully surrendering, however according to Lord Krsna, sa mahatma su-durlabhah, "such a great soul is very rare". However it is also explained by Narada Muni in Srimad Bhagavatam that one may give up his worldly duties to engage in the devotional service of the Lord, but because he has not fully surrendered, he may fall from that platform at an immature stage. However, there is no danger, whatsoever of his being unsuccessful.

You must decide whether or not you feel the conviction to take more fully to the process of devotional service. It will always seem as though there are some obligations, because that's the nature of the material world. Maya is always giving us some other obligation to attend to. For you it's your mother and your enrollment in the institute. If you try to satisfy these obligations, you won't feel satisfied unless it's for Krsna's service. Yes, your degree can be used in Krsna's service. And, yes, your mother can take to devotional service, but can you remain Krsna conscious in that situation without being influenced by other worldly obligations? That is my question to you. I can say that it's possible, but it is certainly more difficult.

It seems that regardless of what you decide, it will still be at least a couple of months before you could do anything anyway, because of your job. I suggest that you think about what I have said during that time, and remain in communication with …dasa. By the time that your obligations to work are finished, it may become more clear to you just what you should do after that.

…dasa has related me some of the details of what took place with your son. Just a few days ago in Minsk, I was giving a class about bewilderment, describing how bewilderment arises from attachment. We also spoke how we sometimes see great devotees of the Lord becoming bewildered by Krsna's activities, including Lord Brahma and Lord Siva. Of course, their bewilderment is not the same as the bewilderment arising from mundane attachment. Their bewilderment arises from the will of the Lord.

Nonetheless, Srila Prabhupada writes in the First Canto of Srimad Bhagavatam in purport to the verse spoken by Bhisma to Yudhisthira Maharaja

\[O KING, NO ONE CAN KNOW THE PLAN OF THE LORD (SRI KRSNA). EVEN THOUGH GREAT PHILOSOPHERS INQUIRE EXHAUSTIVELY, THEY ARE BEWILDERED.\[\[PURPORT\]

Bhisma wanted to impress upon Maharaja Yudhisthira that since time immemorial no one, including such demigods as Lord Siva and Lord Brahma, could ascertain the real plan of the Lord. So what can we understand about it? It is useless also to inquire about it. Even the exhaustive philosophical inquiries of sages cannot ascertain the plan of the Lord. The best policy is simply to abide by the orders of the Lord without argument "

The point is that whenever bewilderment arises, a devotee will seek shelter by accepting the circumstances as the Lord's will. We might not be able to understand exactly the reasons why Krsna chose to take your son just at the point when he was trying to give up his sinful habits. There is an obvious network of sinful actions behind a violent death— the reaction. Nonetheless, before his death he was exposed to Krsna consciousness, Krsna prasadam, and the good association of devotees. There is no doubt that Krsna will give him a better opportunity in the next life to continue with devotional service as a result of that exposure, WITHOUT having to suffer the reactions for past sins, which were already suffered by his previous death.

So you must remember what is the best policy to follow under all circumstances. Krsna is all-good. His will is perfect, and if we have firm faith in His will, and continue to abide by His orders, He will reveal everything to us, just as the rising of the sun reveals everything that was previously in darkness.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

I can understand why you are bewildered. On one hand you are being taught a philosophy that teaches that we should learn to depend upon Krsna for everything, including our maintenance. I preach these points all the time. You surrendered yourself to the temple in that spirit, became dependent upon the temple, by equating your dependence upon the temple with dependence upon Krsna. And the temple encouraged that dependence by engaging you in a service, and by assuring you that you would have all that would be necessary to maintain yourself. Then all of a sudden, "Krsna" says that you are costing too much to maintain. However, that was never previously explained before in the philosophy, and all of a sudden you seem to be back where you were before you knew anything about depending upon Krsna.

This is exactly one reason why I always try to emphasize the need to depend upon Krsna "beyond" the institutional dependency. Yes, it is true, that surrendering to the institution is an indication of our surrender to Krsna. Moreover, your spiritual master is also within that institution. Therefore your surrender to him, in one sense, also binds you to surrender within the institution. But if we actually want to cultivate pure Krsna consciousness we must associate with Krsna from those who are giving us Krsna. If we get Krsna's association, (Who is always beyond any institutional boundaries), then the institution doesn't become everything for us. The institution is supposed to provide the association that's required to support our Krsna consciousness. If that support is there, then we can function in any sphere of existence, whether we are being economically maintained by the institution or not. If the institution is not providing you the support that you need for your Krsna consciousness, then you must get that support from wherever you can find it. I have therefore always tried my best to give you Krsna consciousness, while I simultaneously take an active role within ISKCON. It is my duty to serve the institution of my spiritual master. I cannot whimsically abandon that duty. Therefore, I dutifully instruct my disciples to work within ISKCON, to cooperate with temple authorities, and to associate with ISKCON devotees. But beyond all that, I try to give Krsna to everyone, so that whether they are closely connected to ISKCON or not so closely connected, they will still keep a connection with their spiritual master, and will hopefully appreciate that I am helping them to become Krsna conscious.

I believe that, to some degree, devi dasi has this realization. For several years, she was not associating with ISKCON. Neither was she associating with her spiritual master. But now she has come back to realize that her spiritual master was always her well-wisher, and was always sincerely trying to help her in her Krsna consciousness, even though she also had some bad experiences both within ISKCON and without. In that sense, she is proof. When she left the association of ISKCON, she was definitely not back to where she was before she knew anything about Krsna. Now she is back, though she is still not financially dependent. In your case, you have not left ISKCON, but you are no longer maintained by ISKCON. So, in that sense, there are some similarities.

Anyone who, even for a moment, surrenders in Krsna consciousness never loses. They are always the gainer. Now, please go on with your Krsna consciousness, despite that it may now be more of a struggle for you to find a means of economic support. You still have Krsna, and that will never be lost. He will help you overcome this struggle at this stage in life as long as you do not forget Him. Moreover, your spiritual master is always there as your well-wisher. He may not always be able to be on "emergency call," whenever you need him. I'm sorry, but unfortunately, my duties within ISKCON keep me on a demand that does not enable me to be always immediately "online" whenever there is a problem. As much as possible, therefore, I always try to be available in the form of my instructions. I hope that you can appreciate that this letter is an attempt to firmly establish this fact. Moreover, I hope that you will keep association with …dd, as well.

I am recommending her association as a replacement, since she may be more available for online services than I. But, as you have indicated, you should not overwhelm her with too much. Try to stick to the most essential points in your correspondence with her.

Also, you should be aware that, 1) I am on a very heavy travel schedule right now. 2) I am traveling in remote areas where telephone lines are not always so reliable. And 3) my computer broke down, and I'm presently borrowing one to answer this message. In light of these three factors, please be patient should you need to communicate with me once again. If communication is not required, I would still be happy to receive a report as things progress.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Please faithfully serve whoever inspires you to surrender to Krsna the most. Try to understand that it is Krsna Who is the object of all of our service. Krsna is the one Who has instructed us that everything that we do, everything that we eat, everything that we offer and give away, as well as all austerities that we perform should be done for Him. The guru is one who always reminds us of this by showing his dedication to the instructions of his guru, and his own dependence upon Krsna. If that inspiration to surrender is coming from me, I will not say to you that you must get it from someplace else. Please continue to serve in this way, if the inspiration is coming from some other source, then please take it from there. I want you to serve Krsna, through whomever gives Him to you the most.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Hare Krsna. I am very sorry that I did not answer your letter. There are many reasons why I did not reply.

1\) I was in India until early April and was doing a low level of correspondence while I was there.

2\) My computer broke while I was in India, and most significantly.

3\) I am diminishing written correspondence with those who are not initiated disciples. I do not believe such statements that I am the "only" hope for help. If I am the only hope then what is the value of my preaching? That would seem to indicate that those to whom I am giving personal time and energy to teach Srila Prabhupada's instructions are not learning enough from me to answer your questions. Then what good am I, if I cannot produce even one person who can give you hopeful instructions by which you can overcome this test? I would like to believe that there are many devotees in Moscow capable of giving you "hope". I suggest that you approach one of them with your problem and seek guidance from them. If you have any faith in me, then you will accept this as Krsna's arrangement for your hope, and that you will take shelter of good Krsna conscious guidance from someone in Moscow. I see no reason why Krsna will not help.

Meanwhile, in principle, I have agreed to accept letters of introduction from newcomers to Krsna consciousness, who would like to introduce themselves to me. On that basis I acknowledge that I do have your letter, and I would be both willing and happy to receive any further reports of your progress in Krsna consciousness as it becomes revealed to you through your association with devotees.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Today, all in the same day, I received four very nice letters, all giving personal testimony of obstacles that were overcome by taking shelter in the process of devotional service. I am often emphasizing this point, that by glorifying pure devotional service we develop faith in the power of pure devotional service. These letters all nicely illustrate how the faith of three disciples increased by keeping faithful to the instructions of their spiritual master. From time to time I get letters similar to these, but it was especially pleasing that four letters (two were from one disciple) all appeared to me in the same day. Thank you very much for bringing me this great pleasure.

Just seeing my disciples advancing through heavy tests increases my faith as well. I have no doubt that Krsna is present in our lives, and He will help any sincere devotee who approaches His service with the proper attitude of faith and love. Srila Krsnadasa Kaviraja Goswami states in Sri Caitanya Caritamrta (Antya [10.1](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-antya/10.1)): "Let me offer my respectful obeisances unto Lord Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu, who is always pleased to accept anything given with faith and love by His devotees and is always ready to bestow mercy upon them." Lord Caitanya is "always ready to bestow mercy". If He accepts what we have offered, He is always inclined to give something in return. Only a fool would not worship such a merciful Lord.

Please simply remain His faithful servant, and be assured that your realizations of His mercy will only increase more and more.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*
