I can do nothing to speed up the process for getting a reply concerning India. You pursued this idea on your own initiative. I consented only because, in principle, your idea was acceptable. Whether or not Krsna has given His ultimate consent, remains to be seen. If you have faith that He will, and that He wants this arrangement for you, then you must also be patient. At this point in time, I know of no other arrangements for you, other than practicing Krsna consciousness in the way you have already learned.

You have written:

Please help me although I don't deserve it.

Therefore the best help I can offer is to remind you about the importance of remaining dependent upon Krsna's decisions. We may make so many plans, with so many good intentions, but unless Krsna gives His sanction, our plans will remain unfulfilled. Not a blade of grass moves without the good will of the Lord. To see the good will of the Lord in everything is of the greatest importance. Whatever the circumstance, whether in Delhi, New York, the heavenly planets or hell, Krsna wants you to see it, and He knows best how to show everything that you need to know about Him to you.

Please, therefore, be patient and give more attention to the worship of your Deities, to your chanting, and to reading Srila Prabhupada's books. In time, we will see what plans Krsna has for you next. How long should you wait? At least until my next visit.

## Relationships between devotees and Management

I have no quick solutions to the problems you've raised. I oftentimes hear similar complaints that devotees have a hard time relating to one another. The tendency is to always blame others, although intellectually we may be able to understand that it is also due to some lacking within ourselves. And neophyte devotees will also oftentimes tend to blame external circumstances for their own lack of Krsna consciousness. These are all neophyte tendencies that have to be overcome by practice of bhakti yoga.

There is also a tendency to become too familiar with the devotees surrounding us, and therefore we claim that we have no association. But what it really comes down to is that we are not associating properly. If you honestly examine the time you have available, I'm sure that you'll find that there is a substantial amount of time to associate. But the best way to associate is to discuss topics related to Krsna and Krsna's pure devotees, while together serving the mission of one's spiritual master. It simply requires self-discipline. If you become more practiced in this most essential discipline of Krsna consciousness, you'll find that your consciousness will, in fact, improve.

As far as your temper is concerned, this definitely seems to be a big problem of yours. Unfortunately, I've also overheard your explosions on a couple of occasions, and found them very disturbing. Anger is due to excessive attachment, and you should see it as something that you must overcome if you want to remain living in the association of devotees. Krsna does not tolerate it if one does not attempt to control his anger in his dealings with devotees. It's the same neophyte tendency to blame others for difficulties confronted, and it's vented through anger towards them. One who is Krsna conscious will never blame someone else, but will see whatever difficulty he faces as Krsna's mercy to help him take shelter at Krsna's lotus feet.

So, I beg that you please don't lose out on the great fortune that Krsna is making available to you. Don't waste your time dwelling upon petty issues that will always come and go as long as we're in this material world. Take up Lord Caitanya's mission and experience His full mercy. You should take part in more preaching activities and actually go out and contact conditioned souls. This is the best way to learn tolerance, and is also the very best way to learn how to appreciate the association of the devotees that Krsna has put you in.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Regarding your question about being automatically doomed to lose your taste as a result of offenses, the answer is no. But if you have been committing such offenses, then the sastras mandate that the only way we can become free from the reactions from such offenses is by begging forgiveness from the devotee we've offended. Devotees are generally very merciful and forgiving, and if they forgive you, then Krsna will forgive you. But your feelings should be genuine and not pretentious. Otherwise, you'll simply commit offenses again.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

To be honest, I'm not sure what kind of response you're looking for. There were no questions in your letter; just an honest account of your state of confusion. You say, "... a lot of us don't want to talk about Krsna...", which seems to indicate that you are included. I could suggest that as the root cause of your problem, but I suspect that you would see it as overly simplistic.

From your description, though, it seems as if the devotees in the temple are not helping you in this regard. They should be encouraging you to participate in more Krsna conscious discussion. This gossip-mongering will never help anybody become Krsna conscious. From the account in your letter, it also seems as though you have developed an unhealthy attachment to …dasa. This could have also been avoided if you were able to get the proper association from more senior women. It is not proper for such confidential discussions, as those described by you, to take place between unmarried men and women. There's no license for such association within the Vedic tradition, and we should try to understand and apply the principle behind such restrictions, rather than acting upon impulse.

By making these statements, I'm suggesting that, to some degree, there was some flaw in the behavior of the devotees within the temple. However, even though they may not always act on the ideal platform, a sincere aspirant of spiritual life should be careful to not develop a self-righteous and overly critical attitude towards them. Srila Prabhupada would give the example that there is no use criticizing someone for having dirty hands if they're standing in front of the sink washing them. I would therefore suggest that you should also accept some of the burden for finding yourself in this situation, and should do more to seek out Krsna conscious solutions. Of course, that could very well be your intention for writing to me, and if this is so, my suggestion is that you make a sincere effort, with all humility, to improve your relationship with other women devotees. It may be that you have become overly infected with criticism about them, and therefore can no longer relate to them properly. The fact is that, any devotee on any level of devotional service, who indulges in any unnecessary criticism of devotees, puts himself in a very precarious position. By such behavior, he will never be able to gain a taste for associating with devotees and chanting the holy names of the Lord. Devotees are simply not ordinary souls, and we should always be careful not to diminish this fact.

So, this is one Krsna conscious suggestion I can make. Another is that you should make every conscious effort to regularly participate in kirtan and class. And if your schedule does not allow you the full freedom to attend in the morning, then you should make every effort to at least read daily from Srila Prabhupada's books, along with your regular chanting.

Again, I must repeat that my recommendation may sound overly simplistic, but the realization behind it, is that it's the only real solution to your lamentation. Krsna consciousness means just that, and the way we actually develop the desire to become Krsna conscious is to follow these most basic and fundamental principles of devotional service.

"My dear Arjuna, O winner of wealth, if you cannot fix your mind upon Me without deviation, then follow the regulative principles of bhakti-yoga. In this way develop a desire to attain Me." (BG [12.9](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/12.9))

I hope that my response helps you to gain strength in devotional service.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

You ask about accepting authority. Generally, when one moves into the temple, there is a person designated as an authority for the new devotees, however, gradually one's authority may change as one learns to adjust to temple life. You should cooperate with whomever is your designated authority. If you have some difficulty with your immediate authority, then you should present your problem to a higher authority. But you should be sensitive and consider that this person is a higher authority for a reason, and therefore he (or she) probably has a lot of responsibilities. He may not be able to immediately respond to your problem.

Nobody likes to sit and hear everybody's problems all day long. Therefore in approaching a higher authority it is a good policy to very humbly and sensitively present the problem, (best if it's in writing), and request that authority to please take up your concern at his earliest convenience. If there's no response within several days, then personally approach him, ask if he had time to read your letter, and remind him that you feel that your problem is serious and requires attention as soon as it is possible for him. Patience is a virtue, and a sensitive temple authority will definitely appreciate that you are dealing in this way, and he will be much more inclined to respond.

So these people, whose duties are to take care of the general management of the temple, are one type of authority. But you may have another type of authority as well. There may be a senior and respected devotee with whom you have developed a confidential relationship with, who, although she may not have a position of managerial authority, she has authority based on her knowledge of scripture and years of experience in devotional service. She should not be approached as an authority to direct you in your practical devotional service. You should accept this direction from your designated authority. But she may be able to help you to understand some of the problems that you may be confronting. And because she's someone that you've learned to trust, it is natural to seek such guidance from her.

So I've tried to give you some guidelines to go by, so that you can feel some shelter from these different authorities within the temple. If you can't be satisfied with any of the above persons, then you can write to me, and I will try to assist. Ultimately, the highest authority is found in Krsna, and therefore those whom we see as most closely representing Krsna's authority are more readily accepted. But if those persons recommend working cooperatively with the temple authorities, then that is also accepting their authority. It's not that that every time something needs to be adjusted that we should approach the spiritual master. There must also be some system of authority within the temple structure.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

In principle, I can say that all the temple authorities throughout our whole ISKCON Society should be careful to not mislead people into thinking that they can be provided residence and shelter before the candidate has a more mature understanding just what lies ahead for him (or her). Although our Krsna consciousness philosophy naturally instills faith in anyone who takes to the process, faith in the devotees themselves must mature through ongoing association. Unfortunately, neophyte devotees, who may have prematurely accepted leadership roles, may not always reflect proper Vaisnava behavior, and thus aspiring practitioners to Krsna consciousness can become quickly disappointed in either the process, or the association of other devotees. The result is that they tend to attempt to make progress on their own. This also can lead to many problems; too numerous to list at this time.

Such a situation has undoubtedly existed in some places of the Soviet Union, and, as you can well understand, it was unavoidable. Leadership training was difficult, since it was restricted to phone conversations only. For the most part, devotees were left to deal with situations that would pose a great challenge to even advanced leaders of our society. We should not find fault, but should trust their sincerity. You may have been a victim of such a circumstance, but, on the other hand, you could also learn something important for your own Krsna consciousness.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Regarding the criticisms you hear, this tendency is part of our material conditioning and must be cleansed away by the process of devotional service. Unfortunately, in the meantime, we may find ourselves subjected to such situations. Sometimes senior devotees have to take note of the faults in younger devotees in order to properly instruct them. But such faultfinding should not become a subject for public discussion. Your business is to have nothing to do with this, and therefore you should avoid all such discussions. But do not think that by so doing, you have become better than everyone else, and start faultfinding other devotees who sometimes have this tendency to find faults. Otherwise you will become guilty of the exact same thing. Best is to just set a good example yourself, don't look for faults in others, and try to appreciate all the good qualities devotees have for serving guru and Krsna.

Regarding your own lack of humility, this must also be improved upon. Best that you take shelter of a senior, and more advanced devotee, and act under his guidance. Then when younger devotees assume an authoritative role with you, be humble, and suggest that they raise their complaints to your authority. He'll know best whether or not you should be told about this. In general, our tendency is to always defend ourselves, and this should also be gradually overcome.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

You ask how to study Srila Prabhupada's books independently, and you've connected that question with another question, asking if you can continue discuss the philosophy with a bramacari friend. First I will quote Srila Prabhupada himself on this matter, and then I will elaborate:

"Your question is how much or how little brahmacaris should associate with the unmarried brahmacarinis in the temple. As brahmacari you should not mix at all with brahmacarinis. Actually they should not at all see the face, but that is not possible in your country. But so far as possible, remain separate and talk almost nil. Brahmacari is advised not to go near a young woman anywhere. A brahmacari should always address every woman as mother. In your country it is a little difficult, but the principle is to avoid. To talk secretly or privately is strictly prohibited. If you want to discuss philosophy and other matters you can discuss publicly in a meeting, but not privately. A brahmacari and unmarried woman in the same room together alone is strictly forbidden. "(SPL, Sankarsana, 30th September, 1972)

"...In the Scriptures it is said that a woman is just like fire & a man is just like a butter pot. The butter melts in the pot while in contact with fire. In your country association of man and woman is very common without restriction; thus the result is known to you better than I am able to explain. In spiritual life attraction of man & woman in the understanding of material body hampers very much, therefore, some sort of restrictions are necessary to check this hampering problem. In spiritual life there is no allowance of association of man and woman without being married...."

"...Brahmacharis and Brahmacharinis can live in the temple provided there is separate arrangement. The restriction is there because if they live together there might be sex impulses agitated. The whole principle is especially for the Brahmacharis to avoid causes which may give impetus to the sex desire. But in your country it is very difficult to make aloof the boys and girls because they are accustomed to mix together. So it is not possiblefor me to introduce this new system very strictly, because they are trained differently. Someway or other, if you are intelligent boy, you should make arrangement for the boys and girls to live separately; Brahmacharis at one place all together, and the Brahmacharinis all together at another place. We should always remember that sex life in spiritual advancement is great impediment. So keeping this point of view in front, we should intelligently manage things with great care. So that our aim of life may not be missed...."

In these particular quotes, Srila Prabhupada has made it perfectly clear that there is no question of an unmarried man and unmarried women meeting privately. These kinds of meetings simply lead to degradation, even if he's only a "friend". But, if he's meeting with you privately, he's certainly not a "brahmacari". If this person is actually interested in spiritual life, and is actually interested in becoming a brahmacari, then he should immediately discontinue such meetings in the name of discussing Krsna consciousness philosophy, and you should do the same.

Of course, in the above quote, Srila Prabhupada also said, "In spiritual life there is no allowance of association of man and woman without being married". That is another point altogether. Marriage is allowed in Krsna consciousness, but more important is that both the man and woman are first properly trained up in Krsna consciousness. Otherwise, what is the value of just being married. That is the same thing that the materialists are doing, but we see where such grhamedhi life is leading our modern societies. They simply combine out of some sentimental physical attraction, then they either kill their children in the womb, or create varna-sankara, (unwanted progeny). Therefore the husband, especially, must first be trained up in austerities and penances, so that he can take responsibility for Krsna conscious family life and not drag his dependents to hell. If you are thinking to be married, you should want such a Krsna conscious husband.

Therefore I recommend that, for now, you continue to be trained up as a properly chaste bramacharini, live with other bra- macharinis, and do not unnecessarily intermingle with any men; either married or unmarried. Take guidance from Mother… , and continue to cultivate your "bhakti-lata" under my instructions. If Krsna decides, in time, He may send you a suitable husband, if you depend upon His decision. In this connection, I would like to bring your attention to another important quote from the Srimad Bhagavatam 3.21.28 purport:

"The Lord awards all benedictions according to the heart's desire of a devotee, so the Lord informed Kardama Muni, "The girl who is coming to be married with you is a princess, the daughter of Emperor Svayambhuva, and so just suitable for your purpose." Only by God's grace can one get a nice wife just as he desires. Similarly, it is only by God's grace that a girl gets a husband suitable to her heart. Thus it is said that if we pray to the Supreme Lord in every transaction of our material existence, everything will be done very nicely and just suitable to our heart's desire. In other words, in all circumstances we must take shelter of the Supreme Personality of Godhead and depend completely on His decision. Man proposes, God disposes. The fulfillment of desires, therefore, should be entrusted to the Supreme Personality of Godhead; that is the nicest solution. Kardama Muni desired only a wife, but because he was a devotee of the Lord, the Lord selected a wife for him who was the Emperor's daughter, a princess. Thus Kardama Muni got a wife beyond his expectation. If we depend on the choice of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, we will receive benedictions in greater opulence than we desire."

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Although you are, as you say, "acting in the capacity of a controller," the fact is that the service that you have been given is just the opposite. You now have a greater responsibility to serve others and "NOT TO CONTROL" them. You should not fall into the trap and now foolishly think that you are superior because you have "authority" and therefore your duty is to tell others what to do. Just because the authorization is there from higher authorities does not mean your authority will be recognized. Now Krsna is giving you the opportunity to qualify yourself as an authority. One has real authority only when one is an exemplary servant of the servant of the servant of Krsna. Otherwise, at some point, that authority will be rejected by those who are subjected to it. Then where is the authority if nobody follows?

Therefore in Krsna's system of management, real authorities are those who actually represent the highest authority. They do not become proud of the responsibility they have because they are always conscious of the fact that they are servants and not masters. Therefore I always quote from the following purport from SB [3.9.39](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/9.39):

"Any person authorized by either the Lord or by His bonafide representative is already blessed, as is the work entrusted to him. Of course, the person entrusted with such a responsibility should always be aware of his incapability and must always look for the mercy of the Lord for the successful execution of his duty. One should not be puffed up because he is entrusted with certain executive work. Fortunate is he who is so entrusted, and if he is always fixed in the sense of being subordinate to the will of the Supreme, he is sure to come out successful in the discharge of his work. Arjuna was entrusted with the work of fighting on the Battlefield of Kuruksetra, and before he was so entrusted, the Lord had already arranged for his victory. But Arjuna was always conscious of his position as subordinate to the Lord, and thus he accepted Him as the supreme guide in his responsibility. Anyone who takes pride in doing responsible work but does not give credit to the Supreme Lord is certainly falsely proud and cannot execute anything nicely...." (Emphasis is mine)

Actually everything you need to hear is already there in that purport. Anything I could possibly add in this letter is effective only if I act in accordance with the instructions there in that purport. This principle of authority acts on all levels, because Krsna is the supreme authority, and we are all His servants. Ekale isvara krsna ara saba bhrtya (Adi Lila [5.142](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-adi/5.142)) Only the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Krsna, is master, and all others are servants." Srila Prabhupada is the perfect servant of Krsna and therefore his purports and instructions are impregnated with the full potency of Krsna. We are simply accepting them as our life and soul, and we pray that others will do the same when repeated by us in an exemplary way.

So please do not see your new service as material management. The affairs of the temple are not ordinary material affairs. Everything is supposed to be centered around the service of Sri Sri Gaura Nitai. They are the worshipful Lords of all those who reside in that abode. Your duty is to help others remain conscious of that, and then you will not forget that yourself. Therefore first and foremost duty is to preach and everything else will follow. Srila Prabhupada writes in one letter dated November 21st, 1971: "Yes, preaching is more important than management. Just because you are preaching nicely and distributing so much prasadam, management will follow like a shadow and Krsna will send you unlimited help."

Preaching does not mean only to the nondevotees. Preaching must also be done to the devotees. And, of course, whenever Krsna provides you the opportunity you should always take advantage to go out and preach to the nondevotees as well. This will help to keep you always in the proper consciousness of the goal for which our temple is in ...

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Now, back to your anxiety about being unable to reveal your mind. I would be dissatisfied if you were to do something foolish due to not revealing your mind to anyone. Therefore every devotee must cultivate trust in someone to whom they can reveal their mind without the fear of being exploited for revealing such thoughts of weakness. However, you have written, "I can open my mind to very few people who will understand me." I am therefore assuming that there are devotees to whom you can reveal your mind. Then I would have to ask, "Are those devotees capable of dealing properly with what you've revealed to them?" If so, then I am neither so worried nor dissatisfied. If not, however, then I would be disturbed. You must have enough discrimination to determine the answer to this question yourself. There is a difference between revealing your mind to a friend, who is a friend on the basis of helping each other in Krsna consciousness, and to a "friend," whose friendship has been established on the basis of sharing a similar illusion. The latter may not only be powerless to stop you from doing something foolish, but also capable of encouraging you to do it. Of course, there is no assurance that even the most advanced devotee could help. At times, I have also felt powerless to help my own disciples who were determined to deviate in one way or another. But at least a more advanced devotee would certainly try to help, which means that they would be more capable of invoking Krsna's help as well. Therefore, please make sure that you have the confidence of a friend of the first category. If you don't have one now, then try to find someone. And if that's not possible, then you should not be fearful of revealing your thoughts to me. If you're afraid, then that means that you are afraid of the answer. Otherwise, why would you be afraid? You should not be afraid of such things. I've been signing my letters to you as your well-wisher for years. My intentions have never changed.

Regarding your question about association, it seems that you might not be listening to my lecture tapes on this subject. I have given numerous lectures about "how" to associate with devotees. I specifically preach often on this theme since association with devotees is the primary foundation for spiritual advancement. If someone is unable to associate with devotees, then his life can become too easily influenced by the attitudes of nondevotees. We are by nature social beings. In His instructions to Srila Sanatana Goswami, Lord Caitanya quotes a verse from Katyayana-samhita:

"It is better to accept the miseries of being encaged within bars and surrounded by burning flames than to associate with those bereft of Krsna consciousness. Such association is a very great hardship."

In the neophyte stage of devotional service, it requires discipline to overlook the faults and to see the good in others. The neophyte mentality is such that the mind looks for the faults in others to avoid looking at the same fault within himself. Most often the fault seen in others that bothers the neophyte devotee the most is the fault that he has. He likes seeing that fault in others because it enables him to avoid confronting it within himself. This is largely due to pride. We want to think ourselves better than others. It gives us a sense of dignity if we can see ourselves above others. But rather than excelling in Krsna consciousness, under the sway of illusion, we try to excel by material consciousness, by putting everyone else down. How to put them down? Find faults in their character.

A sincere devotee will become disgusted with such a mentality, as you have yourself admitted. If there is disgust within oneself for possessing such a mentality, then there is hope. That, in itself, is a sign of sincerity. But if you actually want to become free from such pride, you must make an effort to discipline yourself when the mind harbors such thoughts. You can't expect everything to happen automatically. You need to train the mind to see the good qualities. It actually takes a lot of work. Therefore you must practice. When speaking with other devotees, glorify the good qualities in others. Don't let yourself speak only of the bad. This glorification of devotees' good qualities will gradually neutralize the ill effects of your faultfinding thoughts. Don't be artificial. Sincerely look for good qualities. They must exist. If you can't see them, then you MUST know it is due to your own envious quality. There are good qualities in all living entities because Krsna is in everyone's heart.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Regarding your question about accepting service from junior devotees, you should always direct their consciousness towards serving a higher authority. If you carry the higher authorities' instructions to them, always carefully helping them to understand that both you and they are servants of the higher authority, then this will help you to become free from this enjoying spirit. You should not ask for personal service. You should be willing to do the needful yourself. When the younger devotees genuinely feel that you are not interested in the personal service for yourself, and that you are more concerned about them than yourself, then they will naturally reciprocate by offering to assist you. Then you can accept. This is the natural way to accept service, and not by instructing someone that they should help you or serve you.

The following are the instructions I gave to devi-dasi:

Although you are, as you say, "acting in the capacity of a controller, the fact is that the service that you have been given is just the opposite. You now have a greater responsibility to serve others and "NOT TO CONTROL” them. You should not fall into the trap and now foolishly think that you are superior because you have "authority" and therefore your duty is to tell others what to do. Just because the authorization is there from higher authorities does not mean your authority will be recognized. Now Krsna is giving you the opportunity to qualify yourself as an authority. One has real authority only when one is an exemplary servant of the servant of the servant of Krsna. Otherwise, at some point, that authority will be rejected by those who are subjected to it. Then where is the authority if nobody follows?

Therefore in Krsna's system of management, real authorities are those who actually represent the highest authority, and they do not become proud of the responsibility they have because they are always conscious of the fact that they are servants and not masters. Therefore I always quote from the following purport from SB [3.9.39](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/9.39):

"Any person authorized by either the Lord or by His bona fide representative is already blessed, as is the work entrusted to him. Of course, the person entrusted with such a responsibility should always be aware of his incapability and must always look for the mercy of the Lord for the successful execution of his duty. One should not be puffed up because he is entrusted with certain executive work. Fortunate is he who is so entrusted, and if he is always fixed in the sense of being subordinate to the will of the Supreme, he is sure to come out successful in the discharge of his work. Arjuna was entrusted with the work of fighting on the Battlefield of Kuruksetra, and before he was so entrusted, the Lord had already arranged for his victory. But Arjuna was always conscious of his position as subordinate to the Lord, and thus he accepted Him as the supreme guide in his responsibility. Anyone who takes pride in doing responsible work but does not give credit to the Supreme Lord is certainly falsely proud and cannot execute anything nicely...."

Actually everything you need to hear is already there in that purport. Anything I could possibly add in this letter is effective only if I act in accordance with the instructions there in that purport. This principle of authority acts on all levels, because Krsna is the supreme authority, and we are all His servants. Ekale isvara krsna ara saba bhrtya (Adi Lila 5.142) Only the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Krsna, is master, and all others are servants." Srila Prabhupada is the perfect servant of Krsna and therefore his purports and instructions are impregnated with the full potency of Krsna. We are simply accepting them as our life and soul, and we pray that others will do the same when repeated by us in an exemplary way.

So please do not see your new service as material management. The affairs of the temple are not ordinary material affairs. Everything is supposed to be centered around the service of Sri Sri Gaur Nitai. They are the worshipful Lords of all those who reside in that abode. Your duty is to help others remain conscious of that, and then you will not forget that yourself. Therefore first and foremost duty is to preach and everything else will follow. Srila Prabhupada writes in one letter dated November 21, 1971: "Yes, preaching is more important than management. Just because you are preaching nicely and distributing so much prasadam, management will follow like a shadow and Krsna will send you unlimited help."

Preaching does not mean only to the nondevotees. Preaching must also be done to the devotees. And, of course, whenever Krsna provides you the opportunity you should always take advantage to go out and preach to the nondevotees as well. This will help to keep you always in the proper consciousness of the goal for which our temple is here in Moscow. (End of quote).

So please give as much association as possible to these girls in …, and help them to become fixed up devotees in Lord Caitanya's Sankirtan Mission.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

In answering your questions, I will preface my answer by stating that there is no absolute law about how to conduct the bhakta program. As I already mentioned, inspiration based upon preaching is the most important. Moreover, just what you will do daily, and how you will deal with the individuals under your care, will vary according to the individuals themselves. There will be bhaktas and there will be guests. And their level of commitment will also be quite different from one another. You always have to be far enough "above" the situation so that you can see how to bring out the best in each individual. Don't be so attached to the structure that you miss out on the goal. On the other hand, there must be some structure, otherwise both you and those dependent upon your direction will flounder with no clear definition of what needs to be done. Therefore, as much as possible, your assistant should maintain the structure, which you should definitely take part in as well. But, at the same time, you should always be able to step out of it, so that if need be, it can go on without you while you either preach to a guest, counsel a bhakta, go out on a preaching engagement, meet with temple authorities, study, or whatever. Obviously, however, you must also make sure that your assistant gets his time away from thebhak- tas as well, and gets time for himself. Otherwise, he probably won't last very long. Your ability to inspire your assistant to be committed will depend upon your own example. You must show him that you are committed as well, and that your time spent on other affairs is always directly related to the well-being of the program.

I must tell you that my concept of what a bhakta program is today, and what it was almost 20 years ago when I first started it in Boston, is quite different. Although I appreciate the need for a well-structured bhakta program, as described in my previous message, I also expect that the bhakta leader should also develop the depth in his preaching to be able to preach to, inspire, and to be exceptionally careful to not demean independently-minded and intelligent people who are not necessarily bhakta-program material. Whether or not the preaching in is so departmentalized to the degree that it will be somebody else's responsibility to deal with such people, I cannot say. I suspect not. But, in my humble opinion, the bhakta leader should become well-rounded enough in his preaching that he is capable of inspiring and in some cases, "empowering" capable people to engage in devotional service even though they are not ready to conform to the rigidity of temple life. I suppose that to a large degree, I was not capable of doing that 20 years ago, and that's why I was not encouraged to do it. But I would like to see you come up to that standard.

Now, to your questions:

1\) Best if the bhakta leader has separate facilities, and the assistant stays with the bhaktas.

2\) Harinama as much as possible.

3\) Enough so that they had some clearly defined responsibility each day, but they were never "possessed" by any other temple authority. Again, it depends on the maturity of each bhakta.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Thank you for "bothering" me with inquiries like this. I relish being "bothered" by sincere inquiries from those who are anxious to serve. If more and more devotees would "bother" me with questions like yours, then I would feel no botheration at all.

I also appreciate the way you have objectively presented the matter to me. It's clear where everyone stands, without having to undergo investigations to answer your question (although since I did see this morning I did ask him his opinion about the matter.)

Being a former bhakta leader myself, I can definitely appreciate the service and it's value. No doubt, that if there is a well- disciplined program, with a local bhakta leader who is learned, sensitive, exemplary, tolerant, and peaceful (yes, that is what's needed), then we will attract intelligent people to take up Mahaprabhu's mission in a very committed way. I am pleased that your authorities are looking to you as a candidate for this service, and I will certainly give my full blessings. It's a great way to mature as a preacher.

However, do not be so rigid in the bhakta program conception. Undoubtedly, "training" is a keyword for the future. In practically all of my discussions with temple leaders in the FSU, the word "training" always comes up as our only hope for the future. New devotees definitely need to be trained, and thebhakta program is the best place for this. But for training, there must also be preaching. Your ability to train will be tested on the strength of your preaching. Therefore, just make sure that you always keep yourself preaching, and be careful to not get too involved in overly managing the new devotees lives, or being overly managed yourself so that your vision for their well-being gets too clouded by the pressing needs of day to day management. I'm sure that you are aware of this potential, and certainly is expert in understanding these matters and he willhelp you to remain fit for the job.

Regarding your other commitments, I'm glad that is supportive of the idea. Although sounds as though he is not very supportive, I suspect that if he visits the United States in another year and sees a few new bright-faced, well- trained, new bhaktas eagerly waiting to be infused with the book distribution mission, he will change his tune.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

I have your letter that was forwarded to me by Mahasakti dasa, and tried my best to understand the contents.

Usually Mahasakti dasa's translations are understandable to me. However, his translation of your letter left me feeling uncertain of its content. But I'll try my best to address the concerns you've placed before me.

If you want to act in an authoritative way, then you must determine for yourself from where you want to get that authority. The material world is full of so many authorities, and when one loses all sense of being a servant of Krsna, then one MUST become a servant to so many so-called authorities. Friends, family, society, our bodies, senses and mind, they all become authorities, but authorities that can never be satisfied. Satisfaction can come only when we become submissive to Krsna's authority. You need a good definition of the authority that you must follow.

You say that you want to be my servant. Then it seems that you are approaching me as an authority. It's a fact, the spiritual master must be our primary authority in life, otherwise, we have no other hope to establish our connection with Krsna's authority.

A grhastha should accept some honest and simple means of livelihood. He does not want to accumulate fortunes, but works as a matter of duty, accepting whatever Krsna provides. If Krsna sends fortunes, he accepts it and uses it for the service of Krsna. If Krsna sends practically nothing, a devotee continues to execute his duties; always faithful that Krsna knows what is needed. There is no need to worship the goddess of fortune independently from your worship of Krsna. Laxmidevi is always found with Narayana. You are , meaning a servant of Lord Narayana and Lord Narayana is worshiped by offering the fruits of one's labor for His satisfaction. Moreover, as stated in Srimad Bhagavatam 4.30.36, "The Supreme Lord, Narayana, is present among devotees who are engaged in hearing and chanting the holy name of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Lord Narayana is the ultimate goal of sannyasis, those in the renounced order of life, and Narayana is worshiped through this sankirtan movement by those who are liberated from material contamination. Indeed, they recite the holy name again and again." Therefore you must also satisfy Narayana by taking part in congregational chanting, as well as personally reciting His holy name again and again.

I had therefore previously encouraged your proposal to work in the bakery, an honest means of livelihood by which you could support yourself and family as a grhastha. Moreover, I had recommended that you maintain regular gatherings with other devotees who worked in the bakery by which you regularly hear and chant about Krsna with like-minded devotees. For whatever reason, these things did not manifest, and now you still have no means of livelihood, and no regular attendance to the established programs for increasing your Krsna consciousness.

However, because you have grhastha responsibility, I cannot give you exact detailed instructions about what to do to support a family. I can only give you guidelines, which is what I've tried to give in this letter. If you study Srila Prabhupada's life in the Prabhupada Lilamrta, you will see that he did not abandon his support to his family until he actually took sannyasa in 1958. He received the order from his spiritual master in 1922, during their first meeting. Srila Bhaktisiddhanta did not get involved with Srila Prabhupada's household affairs. He simply gave him inspiration and ordered him to preach the message of Caitanya Mahaprabhu.

You are spiritually very weak, and this is primarily due to lack of strong association. It is for this reason that you seem to tenaciously cling to the association of Victor. You had very little to say about your association with devotees, but you had so much more to say about Victor. I don't want to speak critically about him, especially since all I know of him is what you've told me in the letter. But the fact that you took so much effort to expand his glories, leads me to believe that you were trying to convince me that his association was good. But a serious devotee will establish the criteria of what's "good" and what's "bad" based upon what's good for his service to Krsna. Anakulyasya sankalpah, pra- tikulyasya varjanam. A devotee must accept everything favorable for the execution of devotional service and he must reject everything unfavorable. This should be the criteria for everything that we do. But despite all this association, you still have not been able to determine your duty and service to Krsna. I am therefore trying to give some definition to your duties, and based on this definition, you MUST then base your association on whether or not it directly supports you in these duties or not.

In your weakened state, you do not have the strength to vigorously preach Caitanya Mahaprabhu's message beyond the execution of your duties as a grhastha. Therefore seek strength in the association of those who are fixed in Caitanya Mahaprabhu's mission, not in all of the extrasensory nonsense. This will not bring you any closer in your service to Krsna, but will only distract you. Do not get so entangled in a means of livelihood that you become distracted from the primary goal of life and forget Krsna. Live simply, honestly, and use whatever available time you have for increasing your Krsna consciousness. This will make you happy, your spiritual master happy, and ultimately you will make progress in fulfilling the topmost perfection in the execution of all your duties- you will satisfy Krsna.

This letter addresses all three of your concerns in a clear and concise way. Please take this matter seriously.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

In principle, I am not very supportive about such arrangements made by persons who have no business and no authority to solicit devotees to leave the CIS for service in so-called "better" places. I cannot understand what business …dasa has approaching you with such a proposal. On that basis alone, this proposal does not have my support.

If the Temple Presidents of these two temples are looking for people to help, then the proper way to go about it is for them to contact other Temple Presidents and ask them if there are any devotees available under their jurisdiction who could travel to Germany and help, specifying the exact amount of time that they would stay, the commitments they are willing to make to bring the devotees and how to support them, and an exact description of what commitments would be expected from the devotees who would go to Germany. If Prabhu receives such a request, directly from the Temple Presidents, and if he considers you as a possible candidate, then, I would consider your proposal (although not necessarily approve). Until then, I will not even consider it.

In the meantime, you have a service in , very mercifully and undoubtedly arranged for you by Sri Sri Gaur Nitai. You should continue this service, arranging your schedule so that you may continue to keep your vows made to me in Prabhu's flat. If you want to learn English for studying Srila Prabhupada's books, then in time, Sri Sri Gaur Nitai will fulfill that need as well, just as They have fulfilled your need for devotional service all along.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

I am sending this letter to you, since it was your initiative which led to "Problems in " campaign that was conducted last month. I trust that you, in turn, will inform all the others who took part. It is important that everyone understands what transpired during my recent visit to ...

First of all, before I give you my impressions from these meetings, I would like to present a few relevant quotes from Srila Prabhupada's letters. I believe that these quotes accurately express the mood which I adopted when discussing the affairs of the yatra with dasa.

"One man is trained up with great difficulty especially in spiritual life. Everyone has got some weakness and deficiency. It is better to correct or mend it than to break it." (letter to Hansadutta 29/9/74)

"Each center remain independent, that's all right, but the president and other officers must themselves follow and see the others are following the regulative principles carefully, and giving them good instruction so they may understand nicely why this tapasya is necessary. And GBC and Sannyasis will travel and see the officers are doing this, and if they observe anything lowering of the standard, they must reform and advise...." (letter to Karandhara 22112172

"I do not want that S be removed from his position as I can see that he is sincerely following the principles at present. The GBC can not whimsically change the temple president, there is a resolution to this effect. Why have you threatened to remove him and unnecessarily created this situation? Please be very sober in your dealings with these temple presidents, they are undoubtedly rendering a valuable service and are worthy of respect and encouragement." (letter to Gurukripa 18/5/76)

These, and many more quotes can be found in Srila Prabhupada's letters expressing his concern that devotees be rectified and reformed, and not deprived of their service, with no serious consideration for the years of valuable, sincere service rendered by them. Of course, to the contrary, anyone who looks through Srila Prabhupada's letters will also find that Srila Prabhupada was also insistent upon a certain standard of behavior from his leaders, and he was not unwilling to remove somebody if that devotee had demonstrated that he simply could not reform.

In …dasa's case, in particular, I find that the former scenario applies far more than the latter. In fact, what strikes me the most in this case, is that prior to the "Problems in "campaign, there were never any complaints about dasa's behavior presented to me. I could therefore not understand why some of the letters I received were so definite in their conclusions that " dasa must give up his position," when nobody ever gave me the opportunity to address these weaknesses in character with him. It is a well-known principle in ISKCON, that when there are problems with a local authority, that everyone has the right to bring it to a higher authority. The purpose is obvious. It is understood that the higher authority should act in the way that Srila Prabhupada describes in the above three quotes from his letters. He should "correct and not break", he should "reform and advise," and he should not "whimsically change the president".

Taking Srila Prabhupada's mood to heart, therefore, I spent the major portion of my time speaking with dasa about all the major complaints which were raised about him in these letters. I found dasa quite willing to listen to his short comings in an undefensive manner, and I also found him very willing to listen to instructions how he could improve in his dealings both with the devotees within the asrama and the congregational devotees living outside the asrama. I am willing to stand behind my conclusion that dasa, despite his shortcomings, is an honest and sincere leader and he deserves the right to be allowed to continue with the service which has been bestowed upon him which, in itself, can become the means by which he can make further advancement in Krsna consciousness. I trust that you will share the same opinion, and that you and others who have joined in complaint with you, will patiently work with him to unite the yatra in devotional service, avoiding any tendency to divide, rather than unite. An important purport in Srimad Bhagavatam which might be worthy of quoting here at this time illustrates the principle of unity in Krsna consciousness.

"Since the sons of King Pracinibarhisat were all united in Krsna consciousness, the Lord was very pleased with them. Each and every one of the sons of King Pracinibarhisat was an individual soul, but they were united in offering transcendental service to the Lord. The unity of the individual souls attempting to satisfy the Supreme Lord or rendering service to the Lord is real unity. In the material world such unity is not possible. Even though people may officially unite, they all have different interests. In the United Nations, for instance, all the nations have their particular national ambitions, and consequently they cannot be united. Disunity between individual souls is so strong within this material world that even in a society of Krsna consciousness, members sometimes appear disunited due to their having different opinions and leaning toward material things. Actually, in Krsna consciousness there cannot be two opinions. There is only one goal: to serve Krsna to one's best ability. If there is some disagreement over service, such disagreement is to be taken as spiritual. Those who are actually engaged in the service of the Supreme Personality of Godhead cannot be disunited in any circumstance. This makes the Supreme Personality of Godhead very happy and willing to award all kinds of benediction to His devotees, as indicated in this verse." (purport to SB 4.30.8)

In conclusion, I do not expect that this matter is finished and all problems are solved. However, since I also wish to work in a united way with all of you to expand Krsna consciousness in… , I would therefore hope that in the future, if there are problems, that there should be more of an effort to work out these problems in the manner which I have described. It's always best that these matters are dealt with during my visit. "Problems in … " campaigns over E-mail, when I am thousands of kilometers away, are relatively much less effective.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

I am sorry that I could not write sooner. I first wanted to hear the opinions from the Vrndavana gurukula administrators about engaging devotees such as yourself in teaching here in Vrndavana. I learned that generally there are problems for those whose native tongue is not English, what to speak of in your case, in that you would have to learn English before you could teach here.

Nonetheless, the purpose behind my investigation was obvious. Your letter showed sincerity, and those who are sincere about wanting to be devotees need to be given encouragement and spiritual support in order to avoid falling too far away from Krsna consciousness.

The fact is that most of our strength (to progress in spiritual life) comes from the association we keep. We are by nature social beings, and when our lives are not somehow integrated with those whose primary aim in life is spiritual advancement, then, by association, we absorb the materialistic values of modern day civilization. Economic development and sense gratification replace our once cherished goal for going back to Godhead at the end of this life. Therefore, when answering Maharaja Yudhisthira's question how those who are "grha-mudha-dhi", or ignorant about the real goal in life can attain liberation according to the Vedas, Narada Muni emphasized that one must associate with saintly persons and must "always" hear from them about the pastimes of the Lord's different incarnations. The result of such regular association is that gradually detachment is awakened, in the same way as one who gradually awakens from a dream gradually realizes that his dream was not real. It is a gradual process, but if one does not attach himself to that kind of association, then he will attach himself to the association of those who support his worldly attachments. Mundane friendship, family and society are all based on this principle. We expand our association to find support for our attachments. Those whom we call friends essentially are not friends at all if they do not remind us about our only true friend (Krsna). We call them "friends" because they give us "something". But if they give support only to our false ego, then it's only because they want the same support for their false egos from us. Only saintly persons who either have a genuine attachment to Krsna, or who are at least cultivating an attachment to Krsna can actually be called friends in the true sense of the term. Those who have such attachment can gradually sever our knots of material attachment by reawakening the attachment within us to serve Krsna.

Just look at your own life and look who you have surrounded yourself with from one day to the next. I am not condemning your association. In fact, I am not even saying that you must give up your association. I am simply asking you to analyze the association which you are keeping right now in this point in your life, and tell me if there is any association from those who either have a genuine attachment to Krsna, or those who are at least cultivating an attachment to Krsna. My impression is that you are not, and that is why you are losing your taste for spiritual life. Your association at work is mundane. Your so-called friends are moving in the same direction. And although you may have accepted the association of your wife with a spiritual aim, she nonetheless also does not have the spiritual strength to keep both you and yourself moving in the right direction. There must be a major addition to the association that you're keeping right now.

Of course, despite what has been said, I realize that it is by the mercy of your spiritual master, and the association he has given to you, that you mustered up the determination to write a letter for help. Your letter revealed, at least to me, that you don't want to go any deeper than you are now, and that you are looking for somebody to pull you out before you go too deep. I am grateful to him for all the work he has done to bring you to where you are now. At least, deep down in your heart, you don't want to be absorbed in material life, although, as you say, you are already attached to that style of life. But now that you've turned to me, and not him, in your moment of desperation, I must take up the challenge and try to instill the required faith back in you to add this "additional" association that I've referred to above. Whether that association now is with him, with me, or with anyone else is not as important, as simply adding at least one more associate in your life who either has a genuine attachment to Krsna, or is at least cultivating (as the primary activity in their life) a genuine attachment to Krsna. That association must come in the form of hearing from them, just as you are hopefully, "hearing" from me now. Now please go on hearing.

For example, between September 21-26 in Minsk, I gave a series of Srimad Bhagavatam lectures on householder life in Minsk. I have been speaking a lot about householder life, because everywhere I go I see that there are many devotees becoming householders, their values in life are gradually changing, they are looking for a means of material support, and by so doing many are losing sight of the real goal in life. I have, therefore, given lectures of this nature in several places, hoping that they will inspire some to rekindle their attachment to Krsna, while at the same time, they work towards meeting the basic demands for maintaining family life. Copies of these lectures that were given in Minsk were given to Indriyesa dasa in Moscow when I passed through Moscow on September 27.1 believe that by now, Indriyesa dasa has made more copies available for my disciples to purchase. For now, I suggest that you purchase copies of these lectures from him, listen to them, and then after listening to all of them attentively, you should write to me again. This is all I am going to advise for you to do right now, so you need not fear as you have written "In my mind I am afraid, 'Will I be able to follow all that I will be advised?"' I believe that you should be able to do this.

Moreover, I am already thinking of ways that we could possibly increase our association with each other in the future. But a lot of that would depend upon how much you could be available to do that. If this letter, and the lectures that you will hopefully listen to can prompt you to make the change that you need, then I'm sure that you will figure out a way to do that. We could then discuss how to accomplish this in the future. For now, please simply increase your association by hearing as much as possible from those situated in one of the above two categories, and, if necessary, reduce your association with those who might prevent you from doing this.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

There is no need to unnecessarily look for suffering. Naturally, we should try to accept any suffering that comes our way as Krsna's mercy, and we should use that as an impetus to make further advancement. But it is not necessary to purposely make oneself miserable, thinking that is the way to spiritual advancement. Difficulties will automatically come. The service you render for Krsna's pleasure itself will purify, as well as your association with Krsna's holy name.

I am not sure that I quite understand what devi dasi is telling you. It is not clear from your letter, but what I have written above should help with your understanding of this.

Regarding your association with dasa, I believe that you already know how I would answer this. Obviously, it is not proper, and you should stop. If, for whatever reason, your association continues, then I consider this as grounds for not being allowed to remain living in a temple. The temple is supposed to be an "asrama", which means it should be a shelter for those seeking advancement in spiritual life. Unfortunately, too many times, we have seen that non-Krsna conscious activities filter into temple life, but we should not use that as an excuse to commit non- Krsna conscious activities ourselves. As much as possible, we should always try to maintain high spiritual standards in the temple, and this means that devotees living in the temple must be inspired to strive for such standards. Therefore, if you are actually concerned about dasa, then you will tell his authority dasa everything that dasa has revealed to you, and let dasa guide him. dasa must learn to develop trusting relationships with senior men. It is not good for him to take shelter from a bramacharini, especially considering that, by your own admission, you have motives for being with him, other than simply helping him. This will do no good for you or for him.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

When I was in the United States, I had inquired from… Maharaja how you were doing. I also was anxious to hear something from your immediate authorities, since I had mentioned in my last letter to you how I wanted to receive such reports. Now your report has come, too.

You have written:

I am doing my service like a machine. Usually, I come into contact with devotees only from necessity. What can I do? My heart is closed...

In the neophyte stages of devotional service, one tends to be mechanical in the execution of one's devotional duties. A neophyte devotee does not feel compassion, cannot see how Krsna is in other's hearts, and therefore tends to see how everything relates to his/her own needs rather than feeling the needs of others. One who still thinks mostly of himself/herself, will expect more from others, and consequently others who may also not be so spiritually advanced, will not be so inclined to give more of themselves. In such a situation, relationships become strained and mechanical, rather than being open and loving. However, when one has the good fortune of getting the association of advanced devotees, one begins to develop trust and thus can become more personal as a result of getting such association. It takes time to build trust. It seems experiences from your past have had an effect on your ability to trust, but still, you must try. Without trust, you will always remain mechanical, doing everything either out of fear, social obligation, and in general, lacking loving reciprocation.

As I mentioned to you before, you have every reason to trust your authorities there in . However, I cannot order you to trust. Trust cannot be legislated. It must come naturally. But, if you don't try to build trust, then it will not come. There must be an effort on your part. Therefore, please do your share for helping yourself. In order for the process to work, there must be submissive inquiries and service. You should inquire submissively from your authorities about how to advance in spiritual life, and you should faithfully serve their instructions to get the realizations you need to build trust. As your trust grows, your desire to reciprocate will grow, and you will feel yourself becoming more and more like a person instead of a machine. Please be patient, however. You cannot expect everything to happen overnight.

I look forward to hearing more reports from you in the future.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear Bhaktin,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have your letter dated October 7, 1993, which was hand- delivered to me in Moscow.

You ask if the desire to see your spiritual master is an impurity within your heart, and if it's important to have such a desire. It is not an impure desire to want to see one's spiritual master, pro

viding that one's attempt to see him is not motivated by the desire to get one's material desires sanctioned by him. Rather, one should want to "see" him to get the opportunity to "hear" him, ("My only wish is to have my consciousness purified by the words emanating from his lotus mouth." Sri Gura Vandana, verse 2) for hearing about Krsna is what cleanses the heart. Hearing is more important than seeing. This you have already noted in your letter.

Your question about relations between godbrothers (sisters) with relations to other devotees is not very clear to me. Are you asking how should disciples of different spiritual masters relate to one another? If that is your question, then the answer is that there is no need to make such distinctions.

Of course, there may be a natural bond of affection between disciples of the same spiritual master, by sharing one's realizations from the spiritual master's instructions. But one should be careful to not allow such relations to grow into some sectarian consciousness- thinking that these relations are superior to the relations of other devotees. There is no room for sectarianism in Krsna consciousness. We are all servants of Krsna, and we should relate to every devotee in that way.

Hoping this letter meets you in good health and absorbed in thoughts of how you will assist for the Prabhupada Marathon. I realize that your health will prevent you from distributing books yourself, but, nonetheless, it would be nice if you could do something to help.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

February 3, 1994

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear devi dasi,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have your letter dated December 8, 1993 (?). The question mark is there because in your letter you mentioned your experiences throughout the marathon, up until the end of the marathon. Is it possible you put the wrong date on your letter?

Yes, I did receive your Vyasa Puja offering. Thank you very much. The altar has been with me ever since I received it at the end of December. Regarding your responsibility towards thebhak- tins, yes sometimes it is necessary to be heavy, BUT, your effectiveness depends on your purity and example. Purity is the "force". We must earn the necessary respect required to be effectively heavy with new devotees "BEFORE" we take a strong authoritative position. To do that means that you must show that you are a good servant yourself. If they see that you're willing to make personal sacrifices, and that you're engaging yourself fully in Krsna's service, then they will more naturally respond to your authority and preaching.

Don't try to excessively mold and stifle new devotees. If they're sincere, they'll grow to appreciate you for being strong with them, as long as you're strong about the points that really count. Don't try to change all their bad habits overnight. In the beginning you may have to overlook some of their bad habits. But if you're strong and exemplary (being an ideal example is a must) about the basic devotional practices, then they'll gradually start developing good qualities and good habits. A Krsna conscious person naturally develops all good qualities - all with one stroke. First and foremost is to become Krsna conscious. That is your duty. You must be Krsna conscious and you must give them Krsna consciousness. This requires preaching. If you're just an authority to them, somebody who just always tells them what to do and what not to do, but if they don't hear any preaching of the philosophy, then they will not be inspired to fully embrace the devotional path.

You certainly have my blessings for successful book distribution in , but whether Krsna will sanction these blessings is completely up to Him. I'm simply a tiny jiva, trying to please my guru maharaja by preaching and inspiring my disciples to serve his glorious mission. I can say that I would be very happy to see you successful, distributing Srila Prabhupada's books without attachment to results. This would make me very happy.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health and happy in Krsna consciousness.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

December 1, 1994

Dear devi dasi,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Just before I left, I asked you if you still wanted me to answer the few questions at the end of your letter dated 20 September, 1994, and you asked if I would. So this letter is the answer to those questions.

1\) What is the way to develop in new devotees respect towards senior ones?

Especially when it's you who Krsna has placed in the position of "senior" devotee, or as an authority, the wrong thing to do is to use yourself as the example for the new devotees of someone who should be respected. Therefore the best way to teach new devotees how to respect senior devotees is to show them yourself. Invite senior devotees into the room when you are speaking to all the new devotees, and offer proper respect to that devotee. Give him/her a suitable cushion to sit on. Always refer to senior devotees as Prabhu, and speak words of glorification, explaining the nature of the devotee's responsibilities to the new devotees and his/her experience in preaching. It doesn't have to be flowery glorification, but enough to show that this devotee deserves respect. Or you could invite one of the older devotees who went through so many austerities in the early years, someone like Prabhu, and tell the devotees about some of the experiences that this devotee went through, and ask him to speak about these experiences to the newer devotees. There are many devotees in who could be called upon to speak to the newer devotees.

This all may sound like it's very external and artificial, but it's exactly the way I trained new devotees when I was the bhakta director in New York City in 1978. I always invited senior devotees into the room to come and speak to the new devotees, and I always dealt very respectfully, and spoke highly of that devotee in front of the new devotees. They naturally learned how to respect senior devotees by this training, and it was just like that— natural. I always strongly recommend this type of training for new devotees, and in my humble opinion, without it, I honestly feel that the new devotees are missing out on the most valuable foundation for their future in spiritual life. When I first heard that you were asked to take the service of taking care of the bhak- tins, I was thinking how wonderful it was that you were doing this program and is in effect in . So whether you are doing it now or not, is up to your authorities to decide, but at least somebody should be doing it.

2\) How to teach them to follow the instruction of authorities?

The answer to #1, takes care of this one, too.

3\) How to help them get rid of pride and false prestige?

You just have to help them follow the process of Krsna consciousness. Krsna will take care of the pride and false prestige. We can't take the pride out of their hearts. But Krsna can. Moreover, in your dealings, you must always show great tolerance of improper dealings towards yourself. Read in the 5th Canto and see how Jada Bharata curbed the pride of Maharaja Rahugana. Read how Lord Siva curbed the pride of Daksa in the 4th Canto. There are so many examples, and in each of these examples, the lesson is taught that a devotee helps those who are proud to become free from their pride by being completely humble and tolerant themselves.

4\) What is the way of making younger devotees work for Krsna in spite of their position in which they feel themselves very safe comparing with karmis, and don't have to work hard to provide their living?

The only way to motivate somebody to work for Krsna is by preaching. As I already said, the new devotees must have a very strong foundation for their growth in spiritual life. And the strong foundation is built by the preaching from the more realized devotees. They must hear about the glories of devotional service regularly. And it is the duty of the leaders to provide an inspirational challenge for them to take part in. If everybody is simply motivated by the need for personal maintenance, then what is the difference between our temples and an ordinary business? Of course, internally there can be some arrangements for maintenance, but still the preaching MUST be there. Otherwise, the preaching spirit is completely lost, and all enthusiasm for spiritual life gets pushed aside by material motivations. The ability to preach in the required way develops as a devotee matures, but the only way to gain experience is by actually preaching- even if we have to force ourselves to do it.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health, and fully absorbed in the Prabhupada Marathon.

I remain, Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

You've asked some questions related to your service, and I will give you answers, although there are many ways to answer these types of questions. There are no hard and fast rules for management, but there are guidelines that we can follow that are very helpful. You should also consult Harikesa Maharaja's book on management. There are some very nice points in this manual, that could help you in your service. It's not a manual for temple presidents only, (although it was certainly compiled with temple presidents in mind). However, other devotees who are in a position of managing devotees, can certainly benefit from reading certain parts of this manual. I believe that there are hundreds, if not even thousands, of this printed, so you should be able to acquire one without too much difficulty.

You ask how to divide up services among the matajis in the best way. The best way is to inspire others to take up the responsibilities, by preaching the philosophy. When I say preaching, I don't mean that we have to shake our fingers and tell devotees that if they don't execute their responsibilities they are in maya, and they'll just suffer and make everyone else suffer. You ask for the best way, and the best way is to inspire other devotees by being a good example oneself. And an expert manager will always try to motivate devotees by very carefully explaining how each devotee should execute his particular duty under the order of higher authorities. Don't say, you should do this, because "I" (meaning you) said so! No! You won't get respect from older devotees that way- and you'll lose respect from younger devotees, too. You should present in a detached way, how the instructions are coming down from the guru, GBC, temple president, like that! And your mentality should be that you are a servant of each devotee in the asrama by helping them in their service to guru and Krsna. When the devotees see that you're detached, but at the same time, you are not lazy yourself, they should become inspired to take up their responsibilities.

Yes, you should also be very tolerant! You must be tolerant! But your smiling shouldn't be pretentious. A devotee can smile because within his heart he knows that he's doing his best to serve his guru and Krsna, and he's simply anxious for the opportunity to serve. If others do not have such a desire to serve, or are very independent-minded, and don't like being told what to do, then they won't be happy. But when they see that you're happy within, and that your smile is genuine, they'll start thinking, "Maybe I should listen to what she has to say. She looks like she's happy, and I'm not happy." This is how you gradually get respect from devotees. You can't expect respect just because you've been given a position. This is very artificial. But Krsna has given you the position to give you the opportunity to become qualified. And the way you become qualified is by always thinking, "I have no qualification, so therefore I must always be dependent upon the instructions of my guru and Krsna, and by the grace of guru and Krsna, I can become qualified. Not by own ability."

So these are a few guidelines that may be able to help you a little bit in your service.

In regards to controlling your tongue, yes, that will come in time. When we come to Krsna consciousness we have to give up a lot of our own personal attachments, and therefore the tendency is to take shelter in prasadam, (or more specifically the tongue). As we advance, we gradually begin to experience a higher taste in the service itself, and control of the senses becomes more automatic. Of course, not that you should take this as a license to overindulge. There are consequences to that, too, and I'm sure that you are already experiencing them. Moderation is the best, but don't become too self-condemning if there's an occasional slip from time to time. At least, it's prasadam .You ask, "What is to be added about India?" If Krsna arranges for you to go, then you should welcome the opportunity to visit the holy dhama. But, it's not that you have to push for the opportunity. You shouldn't become envious thinking that, "They're going so why shouldn't I go, too!" I remember when I got my first opportunity to go to India. I had been living in the temple for over 2 1/2 years. I wasn't even thinking that I had any qualification to go, and I was just preparing to continue with my service of book distribution. But one other devotee who was able to get some money from his family to pay for his ticket (because the temple couldn't afford to buy anybody's ticket) said that his mother gave him enough money for two tickets. So he said to me, "Why don't we ask the temple president if you can go, too, and we can use this extra money for a ticket for you." I was his sankirtan leader, and he was thinking that I should even go before he did. But I told him that, if he wanted, he could just ask the temple president on my behalf, and I would just leave it up to Krsna.
