The next thing I knew, I was holding a ticket in my hand to go to India, and I had two days to get prepared. It was amazing to see how Krsna arranged the whole thing. It's always much better that way, and we're always more confident that we're doing the right thing when we don't try to "force" Krsna to do things the way we want them to happen.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

September 17, 1994

Dear Bhaktin,

Hare Krsna. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you for your letter dated 9/15/94. I have carefully noted the contents.

We do not sacrifice our opportunity for advancement by the association of demoniac persons if the basis of that association is built from the association with saintly persons. In other words, if, by the association of saintly devotees we become inspired to go out and give that association to others, then we will be protected from the association of nondevotees. Our protection is built on the strength of the right association, and on the strength of the order coming down in disciplic succession. Wherever there is Krsna, there is no room for maya. By meditating on the order, and by always thinking of Krsna, we will always be protected.

Moreover, a devotee should never really associate with demoniac persons. He simply tries to give association and not take. Of course, in the course of our preaching, we find that we certainly may become affected by having to deal with demoniac persons. But as we discussed in the lecture, those who are really demoniac and who are outright blasphemous to the process of devotional service, should really be avoided. We should simply pray for their deliverance.

In answer to your second question, one should "always" seek the association of more pure devotees. Without the association of pure devotees it is impossible to advance towards the ultimate goal of life. So it is not just by practicing tolerance that one will avoid falling into material association. In fact, even Kala Krsnadasa was in the direct association of Lord Caitanya, assisting Him in His travels, and he fell away from the Lord's association being allured by some gypsies. This pastime in Caitanya Caritamrta illustrates that even in the association of the Lord, Himself, one can become influenced. But later Kala Krsnadasa received the mercy of Lord Nityananda and other Vaisnavas, and was reinstated in devotional service.

The point is that there is no assurance that one will never fall down from the influence of the material nature. But the best protection against the influence of maya is to keep oneself always in the association of pure devotees by hearing from them and following their instructions.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health, and enthusiastic to take part in Lord Caitanya's Sankirtan Movement.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

May 10, 1994

Dear devi dasi,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you very much for your letter dated May 9, 1994. The way you expressed yourself in that letter was very pleasing to me, because it enabled me to see the depth of sincerity that is there in your heart.

Yes, the fact that you're distributing books and that you're taking up my instruction to write letters to people in that region is very pleasing to me. And now, Krsna has given you increased responsibilities, and I can see that you're feeling the weight of all these responsibilities. Responsibilities become even heavier, when Krsna gives us the responsibility for the spiritual lives of others. I see this responsibility as perhaps the most important aspect of your service right now. It cannot be taken lightly. Many of the points that you mentioned in your letter indicated that you're beginning to understand this.

For instance, you say that you can't see any reason for "increasing the family members" (or in other words preaching to bring new devotees into the asrama) because you're afraid that you will not be able to handle all the responsibility or, as you say, your mind will destroy you. This a very valid concern. However, unless you have a strong preaching spirit, how will the family that you're associating with now become inspired in their Krsna consciousness. You mustnow share your experiences and realizations by purely preaching them to the younger devotees to inspire them in Krsna consciousness. This is real leadership. Not just being an "authority". I recently instructed one of your godsisters, devi dasi about some of these points. She is in charge of the new bhaktins in . Because these instructions could also easily apply to you, I'm going to include them here in this letter. The following is what I wrote to d.d.:

"Regarding your responsibility towards the bhaktins, yes sometimes it is necessary to be heavy, BUT, your effectiveness depends on your purity and example. Purity is the force'. We must earn the necessary respect required to be effectively heavy with new devotees 'BEFORE' we take a strong authoritative position. To do that means that you must show that you are a good servant yourself. If they see that you're willing to make personal sacrifices, and that you're engaging yourself fully in Krsna's service, then they will more naturally respond to your authority and preaching.

Don't try to excessively mold and stifle new devotees. If they're sincere, they'll grow to appreciate you for being strong with them, as long as you 're strong about the points that really count. Don't try to change all their bad habits overnight. In the beginning you may have to overlook some of their bad habits. But if you're strong and exemplary (being an ideal example is a must) about the basic devotional practices, then they'll gradually start developing good qualities and good habits. A Krsna conscious person naturally develops all good qualities— all with one stroke. First and foremost is to become Krsna conscious. That is your duty. You must be Krsna conscious and you must give them Krsna consciousness. This requires preaching. If you 're just an authority to them, somebody who just always tells them what to do and what not to do, but if they don't hear any preaching of the philosophy, then they will not be inspired to fully embrace the devotional path."

And I also wrote the following to her just last week:

"As a leader of new devotees, you must learn to be more organized, and you must organize some time for yourself. This is required for anybody who is expected to give spiritual guidance to younger devotees. You should at least fulfill your reading requirements by preparing nice classes for the bhaktins. As I told you before, don't just be the devotee who tells them what to do. Establish your authority on the basis of preaching from Srila Prabhupada's books. Being an enthusiastic reader of Srila Prabhupada's books is also a good example to show for others."

I included the above point, because I wanted you to see that as a leader, it's also important that there be some internal development. However, in my opinion, that means taking time to carefully read and study Srila Prabhupada's books. At this stage of your Krsna consciousness, that is completely sufficient. The life of prayer is a valid approach in service to the Lord, and it is certainly one of the nine authorized aspects of devotional service. However, unless one is very advanced, it is very difficult to compose proper prayers. We therefore follow in the footsteps of the previous acaryas, and read their prayers in glorification of the Lord and His pure devotees. Srimad Bhagavatam is filled with the prayers of the Lord's devotees. Everywhere in Srila Prabhupada's books one can find prayers. Even in Bhagavad-gita Arjuna is praying to Krsna. However, we have a better approach to such prayers by having Srila Prabhupada's purports to guide us. Therefore, for now, I strongly recommend that you exclusively stick to reading Srila Prabhupada's books.

I realize that a crowded environment in the asrama makes it very difficult for you to find the time and the place to seriously read Srila Prabhupada's books. However, as the senior mataji, this is something which must be done. Even if you have to find a quiet place outside, where nobody can find you, or if you have to go to a library while you're out on sankirtan, these things can be done to get the necessary time to read from Srila Prabhupada's books and to prepare a nice class for the bhaktins. It may not be possible to give a class to them every day. But at least once a week, you should give some kind of organized class. If you can do it more than that, then certainly you should do so.

So please take all these responsibilities very seriously, and gradually pass on responsibilities to the younger devotees so that you do not become so overburdened that you cannot help anyone else. If you're genuinely helping them in their Krsna consciousness, then they will naturally want to reciprocate by helping you with the other responsibilities you have. This is personalism. When the atmosphere is surcharged with Krsna conscious exchanges, then very naturally, the "family" grows. It doesn't have to be something that's artificial. It will all come very naturally by Krsna consciousness.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health and happily engaged in Lord Caitanya's wonderful mission.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

## General instructions

November 17, 1995 Alachua, FL USA

Dear disciples of the …yatra,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

As all of you know, in early September I spent some time together with …dasa in South Russia. Despite the many obstacles, which took more time for us to deal with than we expected, fortunately we still had some time to speak about many different topics. dasa had questions about life in the temple, life as a devotee, and the problems temple leaders often confront with devotees in their temples. I was encouraged by the fact that dasa was not complaining, but rather, he was seriously concerned about finding ways to improve temple life. His questions to me reflected his sincerity of purpose, and I began to think of ways I could help him bring about the harmony amongst the devotees that he was eagerly looking for in ...

This letter is an attempt to help him in this effort. I'm writing to you, because you are my disciples. Although I have no authority over the affairs of the temple as a whole, (because I am not the GBC for that region) still I have a duty to educate all of you in spiritual life. Life in an ISKCON temple is the primary field of activities for applying all that you've learned from your spiritual master. The way that you deal with one another, in fact with everyone, reflects the quality of training that you are getting from your spiritual authorities in Krsna consciousness. As I was mentioning during the initiation lecture I gave in… , "A wrong act committed by a servant leads people in general to blame the master, just as a spot of white leprosy on any part of the body spoils all the skin." (SB, 3rd Canto) And conversely, if the servant behaves in a saintly way, that also reflects the training that he/she is getting in Krsna consciousness.

So although I am addressing several points in this letter which… Prabhu raised as points of concern, it is not that I am accusing you of being guilty for behaving in the ways that I will describe. This letter is not a chastisement, but rather it is an instruction to all of you, outlining a few of the many pitfalls one must avoid if one is to make progress in spiritual life. It is my hope that all of you, and others as well, can benefit from what I have to say, since, in spiritual life, there is always room for improvement.

1\) The Enjoying Spirit:

The spirit to enjoy separate from Krsna is the primary basis for our sufferings in this material world. In other words, we must ultimately pay the price - suffering - for any enjoyment for which we endeavor that has no connection with Krsna. In the Third Canto of the Srimad Bhagavatam, Srila Prabhupada uses the example that to have milk is a pleasure, but in order to get milk, one must also undergo the trouble of keeping a cow. In other words, material happiness is always contaminated with some distress. An intelligent person will avoid creating unnecessary distress, accepting whatever difficulties (or, in this example—distress) Krsna may place on his path, while endeavoring to please guru and Krsna. Material distress pushes a nondevotee to seek material pleasure as his relief. The devotee, however, sees whatever distress befalls him in the execution of devotional service as an impetus to take shelter of Krsna. There is no other way to get permanent relief from the sufferings of the material world.

***tasmad bharata sarvatma***  
***bhagavan isvaro harih***  
***srotavyah kirtitavyas ca***  
***smartavyah cecchatabhayam***

**"O descendant of King Bharata, one who desires to be free from all miseries MUST hear about, glorify and also remember the Personality of Godhead, who is the Supersoul, the controller and the savior from all miseries."**

Similarly when one lives in the temple with this same spirit to enjoy independently from Krsna's interest, he creates so many problems for himself. Moreover, because he is socially tied into a community, which is being systematically organized for the pleasure of guru and Krsna, he not only creates problems for himself but also becomes a source of disturbance for others. Temple life is organized to minimize this spirit of enjoyment by placing Krsna's desires as the object of our endeavors. That is the path to real enjoyment.

I have included, along with this letter, a transcript of a lecture I gave a few months ago in Lithuania. Whether or not the whole lecture can be translated for all of you to read, I cannot predict. But I hope that it can. One integral point of this lecture is the theme of working interdependently with other devotees within the temple (or any society), with the ultimate aim of pleasing Krsna. If it's possible for you to get this letter translated, all of you should please read it and try to understand the points that were made.

2\) Lack of Determination to confront obstacles

Determination is weakened by sense gratification. This topic is intimately linked with the topic mentioned above. As we mentioned above, there will always be so many obstacles. As soon as one takes to devotional service, there will be obstacles. But a devotee must be determined to pass over these obstacles by cultivating the mood of dependence on Krsna. No obstacle is insurmountable. It only seems insurmountable when we have forgotten Krsna.

"Things that are very difficult to do become easy to execute if one somehow or other simply remembers Lord Caitanya Mahaprabhu. But if one does not remember Him, even easy things become very difficult. To this Lord Caitanya Mahaprabhu, I offer my respectful obeisances." (Caitanya Caritamrta, Adi Lila, Chapter 14, [Text 1](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-adi/14.1))

3\) Discipline of spiritual lives

I have personally observed a tendency for the devotees living in our FSU temples to fall into a low standard of discipline. I am referring not only to discipline that comes down from higher authorities, but self-discipline as well. There is a tendency to be very spontaneous in action, but such spontaneity, which is not grounded in guru, sastra and sadhu, is simply a disturbance. It is the same sense gratifying spirit mentioned above, but only in a different package.

***sruti-smrti-puranadi-pacaratra-vidhim vina***  
***aikantiki harer bhaktir utpatayaiva kalpate***

**"Devotional service of the Lord that ignores the authorized Vedic literatures like the Upanisads, Puranas and Narada-pancaratra is simply an unnecessary disturbance in society." (Bhakti-rasamrta-sindu** [**1.2.101**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/rupa-brs1/1.2.101)**)**

Moreover, Krsna explains in the 16th Chapter of Bhagavad- gita, [Verse 23](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/16.23): "He who discards scriptural injunctions and acts according to his own whims attains neither perfection, nor happiness, nor the supreme destination."

The point is that there must be a standard of discipline that is accepted by everyone who lives in the temple. Therefore Srila Prabhupada always emphasized that all the devotees living in the temple must rise early for Mangala Arati, they must attend the classes and kirtans, and beyond that they must conform to some standard of discipline prescribed by the local temple authorities in compliance with the standards given by Srila Prabhupada for all the temples within the Krsna consciousness movement. We do not need to manufacture anything. Srila Prabhupada has given us everything.

We should not think that the temple is the place designed to facilitate "my" desires. We are supposed to be cultivating the consciousness that we are eternal servants of Krsna—not that Krsna is meant to serve me.

4\) Independent Movement from one temple to another

As we have already described, our activities are not supposed to be whimsical. This desire to move from one temple to another without consulting with authorities is one very practical example of whimsical behavior. I am very inclined to see my disciples remain and steadily serve in one temple, and I am very disappointed when I see my disciples whimsically move around from one temple to another. There will be so many shortcomings, no matter what temple one serves in. The best way to improve the situation is to become part of the solution and not part of the problem. When devotees stay, voluntarily accept inconveniences and mature in their Krsna consciousness, a temple will naturally improve. It becomes the kind of temple that new devotees will want to take shelter in and join. But if a temple is constantly drained by this whimsical moving around, the opposite effect will always take place.

Therefore please do not become a victim of this whimsical mentality. If there is some problem, then discuss the problem with your temple authorities. If it cannot be resolved on that level, then bring it to a higher authority. And, if all else fails, then you can bring the matter to me. But when presenting any particular problem to me, you must present a complete picture. Don't just write a letter of complaint, without including the version of your authorities as well. Otherwise, I must take so much time to investigate the situation myself, and this slows down the whole process of dealing with the matter.

There are other points, which were discussed between ̮ Prabhu and myself, but time does not permit me to address everything in this letter.

I hope that this letter meets all of you in good health, happily engaged in devotional service, and enthusiastically preparing to take part in the Srila Prabhupada Marathon. Certainly this year's Srila Prabhupada Marathon is the best example that could be used to support the points that I've raised in this letter. Every one of you should be planning to take part according to your capacity.

I am, Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear …,

Hare Krsna. All glories to His Divine Grace Srila Prabhupada.

I have read your letter (undated) and have carefully noted the contents.

In reply, I would like to quote from one letter written by Srila Prabhupada, which nicely summarizes the point that you've raised in your letter.

"I thank you very much for your nice sentiments so expressed in this letter; that I am not unreasonable - rather you have taken the responsibility on your part. Actually we are all conditioned souls so our demand from Krsna to accept us is unreasonable. From my personal point of view, I think that I am so sinful that I cannot even approach Krsna to show me any favor. But I have only one hope - my spiritual master - he is very kind. So some way or other he is dragging me towards Krsna. That is the only hope. Sri Caitanya-caritamrta says therefore: Guru Krsna. By the mercy of the spiritual master and by the mercy of Krsna one gets into Krsna Consciousness. Narada Muni is our original spiritual master and he has dragged so many fallen souls towards Krsna, and we are also hoping to be dragged by him through the disciplic succession. Otherwise, if we study our own qualification there is none- rather I have got so many disqualifications." (SPL to Brahmananda, 10thMarch 1969)

Of course, this quote is also a perfect display of Srila Prabhupada's humility, but as an acarya, he is also perfectly teaching us how we must be fully dependent upon the mercy of the Vais- navas. The fact is that without their mercy we cannot advance in devotional service.

"Unless they smear upon their bodies the dust of the lotus feet of a Vaishnava completely freed from material contamination, persons very much inclined toward materialistic life cannot be attached to the lotus feet of the Lord, who is glorified for His uncommon activities. Only by becoming Krishna conscious and taking shelter at the lotus feet of the Lord in this way can one be freed from material contamination." (SB [7.5.32](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-7/5.32))

So, the sentiments expressed in your letter are actually the right sentiments. However, we must also learn how to become better receptacles for that mercy. It is through the medium of service that enables us to experience that mercy.

So, at this point, you don't feel as though you have enough strength to distribute the mercy. But that strength to distribute mercy comes from having received it from Krsna's representative. Srila Prabhupada gives the analogy that the spiritual master does not say that he is distributing his own mercy. He is simply carrying the mercy that is coming from Krsna. Just as the rain cloud distributes the water everywhere without discrimination. But the rain cloud is not carrying its own water: it carries the water that has been taken from the ocean.

So to go out on book distribution, and to preach the glories of the holy name of the Lord means to distribute Lord Caitanya's mercy. So now, you must go for that mercy, both by hearing from Krsna's representative and by serving with faith and devotion. When you actually experience this mercy, you will develop the desire to distribute it.

In the meantime, you still must render service, and you ask if you can dovetail your inclinations for the service of the Lord. Yes, it is possible, but it requires guidance. It is not that someone can do anything that he likes to do, and say that it's for Krsna. This is a great misconception. If service is meant to be rendered for Krsna's pleasure, then we must first learn what is pleasing to Him. Srila Prabhupada would often use the example that if he asks his disciple to bring him a glass of water, and instead the disciple goes off to get him a glass of milk, thinking that milk is nicer than water. That is not service. He would say, "I've asked for water and you're bringing me milk! If you're my servant then you'll do as I ask!" So, in the same way, it's not that someone can paint a picture any way that he likes, play music any way that he likes, or dance any way that he likes, and say that it's for Krsna. Of course, on the other hand, someone may paint pictures, play music, or dance for a livelihood, and from the money he makes, he can offer it to Krsna's devotees for them to use it to expand Lord Krsna's glories. That is called karma yoga, and is also service for Krsna.

I suggest that you put together a portfolio of some of the art work that you've done, and the next time I'm in you can show it to me. But you should also continue to attend all the regular programs in , and associate with devotees as much as possible.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health.

Your 'well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear Bhakta,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I'm writing in response to the three questions you gave me during my last visit to Moscow.

1\) I'm really not sure that I fully grasp the meaning of this question. It seems as though you are asking if a devotee should take the initiative to instruct others, even though they have not asked to be instructed.

If this is your question, then the answer is yes, we should always endeavor to instruct others; primarily about Lord Krsna's teachings as given in the Bhagavad-gita and Srimad Bhagavatam. This is the order of Lord Caitanya, and it is coming down in disciplic succession. On His order, even though someone may not have the capacity to grasp the meaning of these teachings, he may still be able to overcome this inability to understand. This is the power of the disciplic succession. We must simply be connected. Just like electric current. It comes through a wire, if the wire is connected to the source of electricity. And if it is not connected, there is no current and therefore no effect.

2\) Our spiritual master wants to deliver the conditioned souls from material existence. That is his duty to his guru and Krsna. When we take up his mission, by strictly following his instructions, and becoming advanced in Krsna consciousness ourselves, then this helps to relieve his anxiety to see that everyone become Krsna conscious. He has voluntarily accepted this heavy burden to rescue conditioned souls. It is a very heavy burden, because the tendency for sense gratification is very strong, and most living entities resist his efforts to deliver them. But we should not see it as some type of material burden, in a negative sense. We often think that a burden is something that we always try to become relieved from. But this burden is a "burden of love". A devotee gratefully accepts such a burden because it increases his dependence upon guru and Krsna, and thus makes him dear to Krsna.

3\) Lord Krsna has given all living entities minute independence and therefore there will always be living entities who will fall from the spiritual world. Nonetheless the Lord is always desiring that the living entities remain with Him in the spiritual world. This is nicely described in the following purport from the 3rd Canto of Srimad Bhagavatam.

"...The cosmic manifestation gives the conditioned souls a chance to go back home, back to Godhead, and that is its main purpose. The Lord is so kind that in the absence of such a manifestation He feels something wanting, and thus the creation takes place. Although the creation of the internal potency was manifested, the other potency appeared to be sleeping, and the Lord wanted to awaken her to activity, just as a husband wants to awaken his wife from the sleeping state for enjoyment. It is the compassion of the Lord for the sleeping energy that He wants to see her awaken for enjoyment like the other wives who are awake. The whole process is to enliven the sleeping conditioned souls to the real life of spiritual consciousness so that they may thus become as perfect as the ever-liberated souls in the Vaikunthalo- kas. Since the Lord is sac-cid-ananda-vigraha, He likes every part and parcel of His different potencies to take part in the blissful rasa because participation with the Lord in His eternal rasa-lila is the highest living condition, perfect in spiritual bliss and eternal knowledge...."

I hope that these answers meet you in good health, and eagerly engaged in devotional service.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

The main theme of your letter centered around the development of desire for devotional service. You say that you feel so much like a hypocrite, because, on the one hand, you express one type of

desire, but on the other hand you see within yourself so many other contradictory desires. These material desires have been there within the heart since time immemorial, and they do not go away quickly. They must be uprooted by a process of purification.

Pure desire is something that we must cultivate. In the 12th Chapter of Bhagavad-Gita, Verses 8-9, Lord Krsna states: "Just fix your mind upon Me, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, and engage all your intelligence in Me. Thus you will live in Me always, without a doubt. My dear Arjuna, O winner of wealth, if you cannot fix your mind upon Me without deviation, then follow the regulative principles of bhakti-yoga. In this way develop a desire to attain Me." Krsna is herein giving us the means by which we can develop desire. Why? Because unless we are 100% Krsna conscious, we must undergo some process to bring out that original desire or pure love of God.

We will continue to be plagued by contradictory desires until we're completely pure. But as far as starting the process, which is the question you asked: "Where do I start"? You've already started. The process began by hearing about Krsna from the right source.

There are so many verses in the Bhagavatam and Caitanya Caritamrta which place emphasis upon the necessity to continually hear about Krsna from Krsna's devotees. Why? Because it cleanses the heart and purifies our desire. Therefore Prabhupada's quote "First deserve, then desire" becomes relevant. By taking shelter of the process given by Krsna, Himself, we can qualify ourselves to have our hearts cleansed so that we can ultimately express our pure love for Him. First we must hear, and then we must chant Hare Krsna, to continue the purification process. By accepting this means, ultimately that original love will shine through.

***nitya-siddha krsna-prema 'sadhya' kabu naya***  
***sravanadi-suddha-citte karaye udaya***

**"Pure love for Krsna is eternally established in the hearts of living entities. It is not something to be gained from another source. When the heart is purified by hearing and chanting, the living entity naturally awakens." (CC Madhya** [**22.107**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-madhya/22.107)**)"**

The fact that you've already heard about Krsna is quite relevant. That is the very first stage of devotional service. But, now having heard, you must continue the cleansing process. Krsna has made it so clear for you what you must do next, by the mere fact that you're feeling so guilty about your negligence of reading Prabhupada's books. Make a plan to read, and carry it out. Especially considering that you don't have the opportunity to regularly hear any classes, you must make a plan for continual hearing (or reading). In this connection Prabhupada gives a nice analogy.

"One should take shelter of the lotus feet of the Lord instead of trying unsuccessfully to stop desiresfor material enjoyment". As long as one is unable to stop the desire for material enjoyment, there is no possibility of becoming liberated from the entanglement of material existence. It may be argued that the waves of a river are incessantly flowing and that they cannot be stopped, but the waves of the river flow toward the sea. When the tide comes over the river it overwhelms the flowing of the river, and the river itself becomes overflooded, and the waves from the sea become more prominent than the waves of the river. Similarly, a devotee with intelligence plans so many things for the service of the Lord in Krsna consciousness that stagnant material desires become overflooded by the desire to serve the Lord.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Now regarding the contents of your letter of March 1,1 am encouraged by the degree of realization that has been so nicely expressed in the first part of your letter. Of course, in a few places, you have attributed your present state of well-being to my efforts to help you, but I must also say that I cannot forget that Lord Krsna is also in your heart as the all-pervading Supersoul, and He is also helping you, although maybe through me, because of your sincere desire to advance in loving devotional service. Of course, philosophically we know that this pure desire to engage in loving service to the Lord is innately existing in every living entity, but, in our conditioned state, it must be evinced through proper hearing from Vaisnavas. In that sense, you are receiving help from so many exalted devotees, what to speak of Srila Prabhupada himself. His books and instructions form the foundation for all of our devotional service, since it is he who has given us the teachings of Lord Krsna with absolute purity. He is the source of our inspiration. And he is pulling all of us out of the mire of material existence through his unlimited causeless mercy. We must simply hold on tightly by always remembering his instructions, and with confidence, proceed on our way back to the lotus feet of Lord Krsna.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

So, I remember you telling me that the time you were thinking to undergo the 3-months training at , was just for this purpose. But now, it seems as though your mind is suggesting otherwise, and, as you say, now so many anarthas are now coming to the surface. You ask how to deal with these anarthas? In Madhya Lila, Chapter 23, [Text 13](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-madhya/23.13), Srila Prabhupada writes the following:

..."Srila Bhaktivinode Thakura summarizes this growth of love of Godhead as a gradual process. A person becomes interested in devotional service by some good fortune. Eventually he becomes interested in pure devotional service without material contamination. At that point, a person wants to associate with devotees. As a result of this association, he becomes more and more interested in discharging devotional service and hearing and chanting. The more one is interested in hearing and chanting, the more he is purified of material contamination. Liberation from material contamination is called anartha-nivrit- ti, indicating a diminishing of all unwanted things. This is the test of development in devotional service. If one actually develops the devotional attitude, he must be freed from the material contamination of illicit sex, intoxication, gambling and meat-eating. These are the preliminary symptoms. When one is freed from all material contamination, his firm faith awakens in devotional service."...

When you see all the dirty things in your heart, it does not mean that you are becoming more contaminated. On the contrary, these desires arise as a result of purification. They were always there in the heart, but by the process of sankirtan-yajna, which cleanses the heart of all dust accumulated for years together, they are now coming to the surface. The best way to deal with them is to see them as impetuses to take shelter in the process of hearing and chanting about Krsna in the association of devotees. By such endeavors, the Lord in your heart will gradually take them away.

"Sri Krishna, the Personality of Godhead, who is the Paramatma \[Supersoul\] in everyone's heart and the benefactor of the truthful devotee, cleanses desire for material enjoyment from the heart of the devotee who has developed the urge to hear His messages, which are in themselves virtuous when properly heard and chanted. (SB [1.2.17](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/2.17))"

Of course, we'll find ourselves resisting, and we'll try to cling to these anarthas. But the real test is to maintain determination and desire to continue to hear about Krsna, under all circumstances, and to continue to discharge one's devotional service under the direction of the spiritual master. The main problem is if the contemplation continues unmitigatingly, it weakens this necessary determination. The result is that oftentimes devotees will find themselves having to adjust their lives in an attempt to satisfy these desires in a regulated way. However, the danger is always there, that even by fulfilling these desires according to both scriptural license and the guidance of the spiritual master, it still may lead one to go astray. (Please refer to Bhagavad-gita 3.34, verse and purport for a very important elaboration on this point.)

Therefore, the best protection is to have a steady program for hearing and chanting in the association of other devotees. The program that you outlined to me sounds very nice. I also suggest that you take some time to listen to tapes of my lectures for this will help you to maintain some connection. Although I don't regularly preach in Srimad Bhagavatam classes directly about book distribution because I strongly feel that one should preach about the philosophical points raised in the verse and the purport, you should understand that I am most pleased when a devotee takes up this highest welfare work. I think that you were there at the German Farm last year when I spoke about my convictions behind the distribution of Srila Prabhupada's books. Nothing has changed. I was just recently writing to a godsister of yours in the Soviet Union about book distribution. I'll share with you a portion of that letter:

... "In the second letter, there were no questions, except that you asked for my forgiveness for your lack of compassion towards the people to whom you distribute Srila Prabhupada's books. And you also asked that I give you shelter. You'll find that so much shelter is already there when you remember my instructions. I've asked you to please distribute Srila Prabhupada's books, and have given so many other supporting instructions in my lectures. You should understand that those instructions are just as much directed to you as those given directly to you. And by acting upon them, you will gain the strength that you need to continue with your service in book distribution. I am very pleased by the sacrifices made by those devotees who selflessly go out to the conditioned souls to induce them to take one of Srila Prabhupada's books. When I look and see how many thousands of people, whose lives have been changed from despair to hope by reading Srila Prabhupada's books, it makes me feel so proud of Srila Prabhupada, and so fortunate to have the opportunity to serve him. We must give as many people as possible this opportunity to have Srila Prabhupada's association."...

So I ask that you please try to continue to develop this resolute determination to perform this service, and that you keep me informed of your progress in these endeavors.

Regarding your chanting, there is no mechanical way to improve one's japa. It simply requires the same determination mentioned above, applied to the same process of hearing and chanting. The only difference is that the attentive hearing is reposed on the sound vibration of Lord Krsna's holy names, instead of his transcendental pastimes. All too often, we tend to ritualisti- cally chant our rounds as an attempt to fulfill our daily vows, neglecting the most primary aspect of self-purification based upon mental absorption, (i.e. Always think of Krsna) There are certainly external adjustments that we can make to help us avoid this, such as choosing the best atmosphere for chanting, and the best position, and the best time of day, etc. But, ultimately, we must try to submit our minds to the transcendental sound vibration, which is nondifferent from Krsna, and seek shelter in Him, rather than in our own mental concoctions. Simply endeavor, and Lord Krsna will help you.

Regarding your intake of prasadam, the best policy to maintain is to take in a regulated way, and eat until you are satisfied that you have taken enough prasadam to carry you over until the next time you take prasadam. In principle, because sankirtan requires thatyour body be strong and healthy, you should be more concerned about not taking enough prasadam, rather than taking too much. It's understandable that sometimes you may overindulge a little bit, and, as you say, Krsna prasadam becomes your shelter. Do not let this worry you too much, unless these times of overindulgence start becoming too frequent and it hampers your ability to perform your service. Then some adjustment should be made.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

So you should not become discouraged that your parents are asking you to finish your schooling, especially since they are favorable to your practices in Krsna consciousness. You are very fortunate that they are encouraging you in Krsna consciousness. Therefore, for the next couple of years, while you finish school, you should make sure that you make your practices at home as Krsna conscious as possible. Keep a nice altar, and prepare and offer all the food that you eat with love and devotion. Chant your rounds daily, continue to read Srila Prabhupada's books and try to associate with devotees as much as you possible can. Whenever you have a vacation, you should spend it with the devotees. I'm sure that Bhaktin Vera will provide you with a place to stay in Riga whenever you can come. And finally, you should also continue to distribute Srila Prabhupada's books with whatever available time that you have.

You should not feel that your time in school is simply being wasted by having to study a mundane education. You can take advantage of the situation to preach to your classmates and give them books also. Try to take shelter of the story of Prahlada Maharaja, and how he would take every opportunity to preach Krsna consciousness to his classmates- and they were all bom of very demoniac families! Your associates in school will naturally ask you questions about the beads around your neck, and why you don't eat meat. Take advantage of their questions to tell them about Krsna. Some students will probably be disinterested, and some will probably think that you're strange. But, this will help you to become even stronger in your convictions, because you will get strength from preaching to them. I'm confident that if you do this, the next two years will go by very quickly, and moreover, you'll probably find that other people will want to become more active in Krsna consciousness, as a result of all of your preaching.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Srila Prabhupada defines steadiness in the Bhagavad-gita purport to Chapter 13, Verse 12 as follows: "Steadiness means that one should be very determined to make progress in spiritual life." So that is the criteria we use in relation to understanding the meaning of steadiness. We must be steady in our determination to please guru and Krsna. That means that whatever duties we perform, we must see them in relation to that principle.

You used the term "permanent duties" as opposed to duties that are always changing. Although one may execute a particular occupational duty throughout his whole life, that is still not his permanent duty. That particular duty is prescribed according to his conditional nature, and is meant for his gradual purification from the bodily concept of life. So, in that sense, we are not even pujaris, cooks, book distributors or brahmanas. We are ultimately servants of Krsna, and we must come to that realization through the process of surrendering unto Him. That is the reason why Lord Krsna told Arjuna to ultimately abandon all of his duties and just surrender unto Him. Although Arjuna was acting as a ksatriya, which was his occupational duty, his perfection was that he surrendered to Krsna— not that he performed his duty as a ksatriya.

Of course, if we are given a fixed duty to perform for an indefinite period of time, we should accept it as if it is a permanent duty. But it is not necessarily a fact, that if our duties are changed a lot, that is an indication that we are not steady; although in some cases that may be true. If we are wanting to surrender to Krsna, that means that we should be prepared to do anything for Him, whenever He requires it. However, to attain that level of surrender, we should never change our most important duty, which is to always remember Krsna in all of our endeavors. That further requires that we never deviate from our duties of daily chanting Hare Krsna, worshipping the Deity, regularly attending the programs, and regularly hearing about Krsna in the association of Krsna's devotees. If you are steady in the execution of this devotional service, you will naturally be steady in whatever other duties are required for you to perform.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Sometimes these tests from Krsna come, and we are forced to make some decision for what is best for our service to Him. If we always try to do what's best for our service, then we should never fear that we have done something wrong. Krsna knows our intentions, even better than we do. It seems that your decision to take care of your body, so that you can continue to use it for Krsna's service was the right thing to do. So, please go on with your plans to distribute Srila Prabhupada's books, and I'm confident that he will shower his blessings upon you for the sacrifice that you are making.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

You say that you emulate the model of Janaka Maharaja, who demonstrates perfection in the execution of his prescribed duties. There is much to be said about this point. The understanding of one's duties, both social and spiritual, is the most fundamental aspect for spiritual growth. However, such an understanding is oftentimes bewildered; especially when pressing economic needs seem to take precedence to living within a spir- itually-guided social environment that enables one to maintain his spiritual values despite social demands. To work within a social environment which fosters such spiritual values, (i.e. daivi varnasrama) affords one a superior opportunity for achieving the perfection you describe. Otherwise one's occupational duties tend to both contradict and contaminate one's spiritual values. Granted, there are many morally acceptable occupations that one could engage in. However, spiritual perfection necessitates that we also transcend mundane morality.

I admit that, at present, ISKCON cannot present a "model" social system, or "varnashrama society" although it is essential, if we hope to encourage and include grihastas in their quest for fulfilling their economic necessities, while simultaneously pursuing their primary goals for their own Krsna conscious development. Nonetheless, in the absence of such a social context, all the more reason that one must hear from, and faithfully serve, advanced Vaisnavas for progress in spiritual life.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Sometimes it's hard to understand why Krsna deals with us in certain ways. But, one thing we should always be certain. However Krsna deals with us, it's always for our benefit. As devotees, we must always learn how to develop and maintain that consciousness. It's not something artificial, that we just convince ourselves that Krsna is personally involved. But, by developing that consciousness, Krsna actually feels more inclined to become personally involved. That is Krsna. If we're not Krsna conscious, then He entrusts the work of meting out both pious and sinful reactions through the agency of maya. But if we become conscious of Him, by the appropriate ways of hearing and chanting, then He personally deals with us in such a way to help us approach Him. In that sense, there are no reversals for a devotee. He sees every reversal, as an opportunity to increase his dependence on Krsna.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

It seems to be a common misconception that the holy name of the Lord has no effect if one is not initiated. It is true that it is stated in the Padma Purana, sampradaya-vihina ye mantras te nisphala matah, "If one is not actually connected with a bonafide disciplic succession whatever mantras he chants will not bring the desired result." In that sense one must receive the mahamantra from a bona fide disciplic succession in order to achieve pure love of Godhead. However, you have received the holy name from a bona fide disciplic succession and therefore when you are chanting the holy name there is certainly some benefit.

It is also stated in the Caitanya Caritamrta, Adi lila, Chapter 7:

"In other words, the chanting of the Hare Krishna maha-mantra is so powerful that it does not depend on official initiation, but if one is initiated and engages in pancaratra-vidhi (Deity worship), his Krishna consciousness will awaken very soon, and his identification with the material world will be vanquished.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

In regards to whether or not you should enter back into the institute, this decision you must make according to your own convictions. I'm sure that your family must be resisting the idea of your leaving the institute, since most people place more value upon mundane education than spiritual education, and that is probably more of the reason than any other for your hesitation. It is possible to maintain Krsna consciousness by doing both, but by living outside the association of devotees it becomes much more difficult and risky. So you must decide whether or not you will be able to maintain your Krsna consciousness in such a circumstance. In principle, it's always best to avoid it, but that may not be always possible.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

It's unfortunate to hear that you are still meeting so much opposition from your husband. As you say, it may even take 10 years before your situation is more settled. You ask what to do in the meantime, and how to gain deeper realizations.

I've been recently reading about the pastimes of Srila Hari- dasa Thakur. From his example we can learn how important it is to remain fixed in chanting the Holy Names of the Lord even in the midst of the most difficult circumstances.

Haridasa Thakur was always filled with love for the Lord, and thus he could not, for a moment, ever stop chanting the Lord's Holy Names. He was bom in a Moslem community, and therefore the envious Kazi, under the government of the Mohammedan Nawab, insisted that Haridasa either give up his chanting or risk severe punishment for adopting the behavior of a "Hindu". He was brought into prison and given a last chance to give up his behavior or face being beaten throughout all of the marketplaces, so that all the local Mohammedans could see what happens to one who takes up Hindu practices. They even told him that if he would simply preach from their scripture, they would excuse him, but Haridasa Thakur told them that even if they cut his body to pieces and killed him, he would never give up chanting the Holy Names of the Lord. Thus he was severely beaten, and if he were an ordinary mortal, he should have died after being beaten within the first two or three marketplaces. But he just kept on chanting, and felt no pain, while they continued to torture him throughout 22 marketplaces. He only felt pain in his compassion for the guards, who were beating him, because they feared that if they did not successfully kill him, they would themselves be beaten at the hands of the Kazi. Haridasa Thakur knew this and thus he willfully went into trance, while chanting within, and was accepted as being dead. The foolish Kazi thought that Haridasa Thakur's words were proven false, and that he was, in fact, dead, and therefore incapable of continuing his chanting of the Lord's Holy Names. They decided to throw his body into the Ganges to dispose of it. His body was swept downstream, but soon after, he awakened from trance, and came out from the Ganges and simply continued to chant the Lord's Holy Names. When the Nawab saw Haridasa Thakur, he could immediately accept him as a saintly person, and fell down at his lotus feet to beg forgiveness for the terrible atrocities that were encouraged by him. Thus Haridasa was free to continue to glorify the Lord, without restriction.

Srila Vrndavana das Thakur writes in this connection:

"This entire episode with Srila Haridasa Thakura is a replication of the heroic activities of Sri Hanuman in Sri Lanka. Indrajit, Ravana's son, had sent the Brahmastra, a weapon given to him by Lord Brahma, to capture Hanuman. Hanuman could easily have escaped it, but to show respect to the potency of Lord Brahma, Hanuman allowed himself to be bound by the Brahmastra.

Similarly, Srila Haridasa Thakura could have escaped the punishment of the Moslems, but by allowing them to torture him, he taught the world a lesson: Despite the extreme miseries of life, one must never stop chanting the name of Krsna."

Now, of course, your situation is not exactly the same as Haridasa Thakur's, but the principle to be learned is. "Despite the extreme miseries of life, one must never stop chanting the name of Krsna." That is my suggestion. If you want your realizations to increase, then simply continue to chant under all circumstances, and Krsna will reveal His plan for your future in devotional service. That freedom you do have, and you must properly use it.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Whenever I'm in Moscow it is nice that you are cooking for me, but you should understand that when I stay in a temple, where there are Deities being worshipped according to standard, I prefer to eat only prasadam that has been offered to the Deities. It is not that I don't appreciate all the hard work that you are doing to prepare nice prasadam, but, in principle, it's better for me to take Lord Caitanya's remnants rather than having a special cook for myself. So, you can continue to do this, only if it's cooked for Gaura Nitai's pleasure, and They eat it first. Otherwise, I won't eat it. This offering must also go on one of Their regular offerings. When in the temple it's not proper that we cook food intended for someone else's consumption besides the Deities, (unless, of course if someone is unhealthy and requires special prasadam) and then just offer it to Them at any whimsical time. It must always be offered at regulated times. The Deities are the central focus of worship, and all temple affairs (such as cooking) should be centered around Their pleasure.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

You ask how to develop spiritual qualities, because you're finding that it is insufficient to simply try to maintain control over your lower qualities. In the beginning there must be some restraint, because all the spiritual qualities are still covered. It is a fact that simply restraint from sense gratification will not last. That is the platform maintained by the impersonalist. They restrain from sensual pleasures, but because there is no higher taste, they fall down into sense gratification once again. The devotee, however, develops his original spiritual qualities by both restraint, and association with Krsna and Krsna's pure devotees. Through this association these spiritual qualities, that lie dormant within, are gradually rekindled. One must come to this platform to become free from all bad qualities, but, in the meantime, he must be patient to continue with the process. This process provides full opportunity for gaining a higher taste, which will ultimately protect us from maya's constant allurements.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

You say that you can't control your mean mind, and therefore you are creating so many problems for yourself and others. And you ask how to suppress your mind.

This is one of those questions that is perpetually asked because everyone faces this same problem. How to control the mind? It's not something that we can expect to immediately conquer over. We must learn patience and tolerance. These are two very important qualities for a devotee to develop.

Of course, there must also be faith in the holy name and in the order of the spiritual master. We must have faith that by both chanting the holy names and executing the instructions of the spiritual master, our lives will improve, and we will become more peaceful. That faith comes from taking the association of devotees who manifest these qualities, and by seeing how they characterize the qualities that you are wanting to develop. By serving such devotees, you will also develop their qualities.

All these things will manifest in time, if you just stick to the process. It is also very important that you read regularly from Srila Prabhupada's books. This will also help you to control your mind. Hearing from Srila Prabhupada is called "bhavausadhi", or the right medicine for our material disease. That disease is that we still identify with these bodies and minds. Just as a patient may not like the taste of the medicine, still he takes it because he wants to be cured. So just keep on taking the medicine.

*enechi ausadhi maya naibaro lagi*  
*hari-nama maha-mantra lao tumi magi'*

**"I have brought the medicine that will wipe out the disease of illusion from which you are suffering. Take this maha-mantra- Hare Krsna Hare Krsna Krsna Krsna Hare Hare Hare Rama Hare Rama Rama Rama Hare Hare. (Srila Bhaktivinode Thakur)**

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

You ask, "May we, being fallen and unqualified, offer our own prayers to the Supreme Personality of Godhead?" Before answering, I'll give you a few quotes from Srila Prabhupada's purports in Srimad Bhagavatam. (I'm sorry that I don't have the text references).

... "In other words, without the development of fine affection and love, one cannot offer prayers to the Lord very suitably."...

... "By glorifying the Lord with exalted verses, one becomes purified. Although we are unable to offer prayers to the Lord in an adequate fashion, our duty is to make the attempt in order to purify ourselves. It is not that we should stop our glorification because demigods like Lord Brahma and Lord Shiva cannot adequately glorify the Lord. Rather, as stated by Prahlada Maharaja, everyone should glorify the Lord according to his own ability. If we are serious and sincere devotees, the Lord will give us the intelligence to offer prayers properly."...

..."One can offer prayers to the Supreme Personality of Godhead directly, but if one repeats the prayers offered by great devotees like Lord Shiva and Lord Brahma, or if one follows in the footsteps of great personalities, one can please the Supreme Personality of Godhead very easily. For instance, we sometimes chant this mantra of Brahma-samhita (5.29):

"I worship Govinda, the primeval Lord, the first progenitor, who is tending the cows, yielding all desires, in abodes built with spiritual gems and surrounded by millions of purpose trees. He is always served with great reverence and affection by hundreds of thousands oflaksh- mis, or gopis." Because this prayer was offered by Lord Brahma, we follow him by reciting this prayer. That is the easiest way to satisfy the Supreme Personality of Godhead. The pure devotee never attempts to reach the Supreme Lord directly. The most important way to worship the Lord is to go through the disciplic succession of devotees."...

... "Nonetheless, everyone in devotional service can offer essential prayers to the Lord. Everyone is situated in a relative position, and no one is perfect in glorifying the Lord. Beginning with Lord Brahma and Lord Shiva down to ourselves, everyone is the servant of the Supreme Lord. We are all situated in relative positions according to our own karma. Yet every one of us can offer prayers with heart and soul as far as we can appreciate the Lord's glories. That is our perfection. Even when one is in the darkest region of existence, he is allowed to offer prayers to the Lord according to his own capacity. The Lord therefore says in Bhagavad-gita (9.32):

"O son ofPritha, those who take shelter in Me, though they be of lower birth—women, vaishyas \[merchants\], as well as shudras \[workers\]—can approach the supreme destination."...

... "One cannot offer prayers to the Lord with mundane words. One must become spiritually advanced by controlling the mind and senses. Then he can find suitable words to offer in prayers to the Lord. Quoting the following verse from the Padma Purana, Srila Sanatana Gos- vamiforbids us to sing any song not sung by authorized devotees."...

There are many more quotes, but I felt that these particular ones covered the spectrum required to shed light on your question. We should understand that our prayers cannot possibly approach the prayers already given by pure Vaisnavas, but nonetheless, Prabhupada says that we should attempt to offer prayers for our own purification. So we may certainly offer our own original prayers, but they should not be to the exclusion of those already given by the Vaisnava acaryas.

Sometimes, when I greet the Deities in the morning, especially Sri Sri Radha Gopivallabha, I compose simple prayers in my mind as an offering. But I find that by keeping them simple, it is easier to not lose track of the most essential ingredient, which is that the prayer is for Their pleasure- and not for mine. For example, I'm always trying to be conscious not to pray to Them to make life easier for me, by quickly giving me the results of my immediate endeavors. Certainly, we want results of our efforts for the Lord's pleasure and the pleasure of the spiritual master, but we must be prepared to accept whatever difficulties that befall us, as well. Just as the service itself is coming from Him, so also are the impediments, in order to increase our dependence upon Him. The more dependent we feel ourselves, the more we can become suitable instruments for His pleasure. When you think of it, there are unlimited statements we could make in our prayers, because prayer is meant for the glorification of Krsna. But Srila Prabhupada has repeatedly told us that the essence of all prayers is contained within the Hare Krsna mahamantra. Prabhupada also states in the Bhagavatam that everything required for praying to the Lord can be achieved simply by chanting Sri Krsna Caitanya Prabhu Nityananda...., and the Hare Krsna mahamantra.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Regarding some instruction, I can only encourage you to remain strict with yourself, and to not allow yourself to become overly dependent upon this temporary material nature. This material nature is very powerful, and she can make us forget that our life is meant to be always surrendered to Krsna's lotus feet. For instance, just right at this moment, while I am writing this letter, a very powerful rainstorm is passing through Sri May- apura Dham. The wind is very strong, the devotees are all being forced to go inside, the windows are shaking, and the rain is falling heavily upon the ground. However, just as the storm began increasing in its intensity, I could hear the kirtan downstairs in the temple room becoming louder and louder as the devotees present in Sundara arotik were taking shelter of the Lord's holy name. Maya is just like that for the devotee. Although she is very strong, when she seems to increase her intensity, the devotees always see that as an impetus to increase the intensity of their dependence upon the Lord. Always remember that the material world is not our real home, and that, as eternal spiritual beings, we are meant to be always at home taking shelter of the lotus feet of Krsna. There is no safer place in this material world.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

You should know me well enough to know that when I attempt to correct you on matters such as this, it's not that I am always asking you to pretend to be blind to what your intelligence tells you is something wrong. Your points have been heard. However, STILL, I believe that there is something that you can take heed of from situations like this as well. I have, therefore, delivered a message to you, one which you should also hear, and act upon, too.

I have firm faith in Krsna's ways, and I have firm faith that if we deal with such matters in a Vaisnava way, always trying to do what is best in Krsna's service, that Krsna will rectify matters in a way that is beneficial for all.

For instance, you may defend yourself for my cautioning to you about an imperialistic mentality. I simply took advantage of the situation to warn you about a particular trap which I had not warned you about before. The situation brought to light a lesson for you—something every devotee and managerial leader should always be cautious about. By your own choice, you could have maintained your focus on ' s shortcomings, and could have dismissed my words of caution about imperialism, by justifying that 's shortcomings were the issue and not an imperialistic tendency on your part. But you would have missed out on the opportunity to learn something about the dangers of an imperialistic mentality.

… also had a lesson to learn. However, his lesson was, I believe, something that he was not capable of hearing at the time when the subject was first brought up. Still, everything fell into place, in such a way that I believe he was far more capable of hearing it when it was presented to him in Kiev. My conviction is that Krsna was behind all of this. I also, am trying to think of what is best for preaching in , but that also includes trying to keep our senior devotees who are valuable assets for our preaching, and who have shown that they are sincere servants of their spiritual master.

If Prabhu can withstand this test that Krsna is giving to him, he will come out that much stronger in his own Krsna consciousness, and will be that much more of an effective assistant to Lord Caitanya's preaching mission. I prefer that kind of a result rather than pushing somebody out of the association of devotees, only to see him resentful towards ISKCON and ISKCON's authority.

With regards to your present complaint, the same point is there. Just what outcome Krsna will bring to all of this, is all up to Krsna. However, I prefer to deal with this matter in a similar Krsna conscious way, and I would hope that you would share such a vision as well. Meanwhile, my previous instruction to you still stands, and I trust that you will take heed!

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

There are many examples of devotees who found themselves in difficult situations due to obligations they were not able to immediately sever themselves from. It is not something new. Arjuna suffered due to affection to family, teachers and friends. Kuntidevi was dependent upon her Pandava sons, who were objects of Duryodhana's scorn. Bhisma fought on the side of Dury- odhana. The Lord sometimes puts His devotees into tribulation to increase His devotee's attachment to His lotus feet. Although you cannot immediately extricate yourself from this dependency, nonetheless you can still learn to see the situation Krsna has put you in as an opportunity to meditate upon Him within. Whatever external tribulations one may have to be put through, nobody can stop the internal meditation upon Krsna.

Queen Kunti prayed, "Let those calamities happen again and again, so that we can see you again and again, for seeing You means we will no longer have to see repeated births and deaths." Remember these examples, chant Krsna's names, and Krsna will help you.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

It seems that you have big plans for making money, but economic development is required only in grhasta life. My impression is that your householder life is now practically nonexistent. What are you thinking? What are all these big plans for big money? Big money has value only when used in Krsna's service. Otherwise, those who have big plans to make big money simply become degraded, they forget about the desires of their spiritual master, and think that they themselves are lords and controllers. The personality of Kali was given permission to reside wherever there was the hoarding of gold. From there, the pillars of sinful life flourished. Now, you are presenting yourself as a completely surrendered servant, but you cannot even maintain the responsibility you've been given as a dutiful Krsna conscious husband who gives proper protection to his wife. How can I expect that you will follow my instructions? What evidence is there that you are following my instructions now? If I sanction your desire to go to…, then what assurance can you give me that you will not simply go there and become so intoxicated with the desire for wealth that you will forget that you are a servant of your guru? What will you do with all of this wealth? Purchase some new sex life?

If you have any thread of intelligence left, you will see that I am making a concentrated effort to knock some sense into your head and to save you from becoming more and more degraded. Unless you can actually see what I am trying to do, then I cannot take part in offering sanctions for you to go to . Waiting for a reply.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

2\) I'm not quite sure to what extent you are inquiring about this "jumping up and down" in kirtan. I personally stood right in front of Srila Prabhupada in Mayapura, 1976 when Srila Prabhupada was pulling the rope of the big bell in the temple room. Prabhupada would start slowly and then he would start pulling the bell faster and faster. With his other hand he would motion for all of us dancing in front of him to jump up and down higher and higher and faster and faster to the speed of the bell. I could understand that Prabhupada was deriving great pleasure seeing all of us dance so enthusiastically. In Brahma Samhita Lord Brahma describes that in the transcendental realm of Vrndavana, all walking is dancing (natyam gamanam). Dancing is therefore a natural characteristic of the soul for the glorification of Krsna. Otherwise, why would Srila Prabhupada be so pleased to see all of us dancing? If dancing brings Srila Prabhupada pleasure, then that is sufficient reason in itself for jumping up and down in a kirtan with enthusiasm. Although our natural proclivity to dance (as the residents of Vrndavana) may not be fully manifest, nonetheless, if we go on dancing to glorify Krsna and to bring pleasure to our spiritual master, then such natural dance will gradually be restored.

However, as with anything, it can also turn into something frivolous and the original intention can be lost. Therefore, we must always keep in mind why we are dancing, and we should not turn it into a distraction from the holy name of the Lord.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Based upon my past communications to , I'm assuming that he informed you about the reasons for being unable to answer. Just yesterday, I finished a very intensive six weeks of studying Russian. It is an extremely difficult language, and I could therefore see that it required my undivided attention if I were to expect any result from my endeavors. At this point, I have limited reading, writing, and speaking skills. I wasn't expecting miracles, but I am now confident that I have a solid foundation to build on, and will continue to practice to speak and have dialogue in Russian as a daily routine. This is also one principle reason why I will be spending a significantly less amount of time in North America this year than usual. I must keep myself in a totally Russian-speaking environment so that I do not lose all that I've gotten in the last six weeks.

Right now, my health is fine. Thank you for your concern. Yes, Adam's treatment did actually help get my digestion functioning better. I still had bronchial problems when I left the U.S., but even those seem to have temporarily subsided over the past couple of months.

Although I've read your account of your duties as wife more than once, I'm still finding it difficult to catch onto something worthy of expanding upon in the form of an instruction. It seems that everything that's needed to be said has been said, at least for now. That part of your life is your duty which cannot be artificially renounced. Somehow it must be done in the proper consciousness. It brings to mind a previous talk I once had with Steve Ellis. He was telling me how he was trying to see his work at the church as his work which he cannot artificially give up, but he was trying to be a devotee in his consciousness. It's not exactly the same situation, but there are similarities.

I don't know if I ever told you this story. I've always tried to keep confidentiality for the person who revealed this to me in a letter. She is an elderly Indian woman I know from the West, who was opening herself up to me in a personal letter. She was telling me how she had never touched meat, fish, alcohol, or any other tamasic substance in her whole life. She was raised in a Vaisnava family. When she was young her father arranged her marriage, and she dutifully accepted the arrangement made by him. She later married that man, had several children from him, and lived her whole life with him, while still following the same standards she had learned in the association of her Vaisnava family. However, she revealed to me that she learned shortly after her marriage that her husband could not follow even one of the principles. Still she dutifully accepted the arrangement that was made for her. But all that time she was waiting for her children to grow up, get married, and when the last child married, she was seeing that as Krsna allowing her to devote more of her time to devotional practices. She is a strong woman, and no doubt, has many realizations from her life's experience.

Now, I'm not saying that this is always the example that is to be followed. There are even references in Srila Prabhupada's books that a fallen husband should be rejected. But one should very carefully consider just what criteria should be used for someone to be fallen. It's true, that it appears that your husband has lost his taste for devotional activities, but please remember the words of Narada Muni to Vyasa. (SB 1.5.19). If all of Srila Prabhupada's instructions are to be included, then the instruction that a woman should not remarry should be considered as well. For a woman with a small child, that is a very serious undertaking, and cannot be taken lightly.

Anyway, these are just some of the thoughts that are surfacing after six very intensive weeks of exclusive studies in Russian, but I hope that you can find some words of encouragement there.

I'm also pleased to hear that you are closer in your relationship with Mother . I always hear so many good things about her. I'm sure that she has many wonderful realizations to share with those who approach her in friendship and respect. Please just try anyway to do something together with Mother …for the Prabhupada Centennial this year. Even if you can't convince the community members to sponsor kalashes, you can still work together to assist others to glorify Prabhupada this year. Why don't the two of you beg Maharaja for some direction as to how the two of you could best contribute to the Prabhupada Centennial this year? With Maharaja's keen vision, and his enthusiasm to build up Gita Nagari, I'm quite confident that he would not hold back with something inspirational for the two of you to work towards. Then just work together and do it, depending upon the strength given in the instruction from Maharaja. I'm sure that if you faithfully try to execute his vision, you will elicit his mercy, and he'll help you in some way as well.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Thank you very much for your letter dated May 8, 1996. It was transmitted to me by electronic mail by Parama Karuna dasi on May 13th, however, for the following reason, I was unable to answer it until now.

I have been taking an intensive course in Russian for the last six weeks in Moscow. I just completed the course this weekend. It was quite an experience. There was no time for kirtans, classes, or even reading Srila Prabhupada's books. Every day I was studying Russian no less than 8 hours, (4 of those hours with a professor in Russian) reading, memorizing, and reciting from ridiculous Russian texts about going out on picnics in the forest, picking berries, boating, picnicking, texts about family life, descriptions of homes, etc. etc. I guess the text was all relevant for developing conversational skills, which unfortunately, because of the extreme complexities of this language, cannot be fully acquired within only six weeks, but I am definitely now hankering for a re-immersion into Prabhupada's books. I'm going to start reading from Prabhupada's books in Russian a little bit each day.

Anyway, that is the reason why I was unable to get to your letter right away.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

… recently wrote to me, and told me that he has been corresponding with you regularly. I'm very happy to hear that you've got somebody who is taking the time to write to you on a regular basis. Devotees' association is so vital. However, he also related to me that you got yourself into trouble by throwing milk onto another inmate. Is that why your address is changed? You really ought to be more careful.

The news about your chanting and giving up smoking was great to hear. Please continue by all means. It will have a profound effect on your consciousness, and it will give you the strength to stay on course when you get out. Please, as your friend, please continue to do this!

There is a reference in Caitanya Bhagavat about Haridasa Thakur. Because he was always loudly chanting Krsna's names in the area where Muslims ruled, the King had Haridasa dragged into prison to be punished. As he entered the prison, the pious souls who had also been imprisoned were all anxious to greet Haridasa as they had heard that he was being brought into the prison. Caitanya Bhagavat describes this as follows:

"By Lord Krsna's mercy, saintly Haridasa was not afraid of the Muslim king. Saying "Krsna! Krsna!" as he walked, to the king of the country he gave the right to see him. Hearing that Haridasa had come, the pious persons became both happy and sad. Many very pious and saintly people were confined in the prison. Hearing that Haridasa was coming, they became happy at heart. They said, 'Saintly Haridasa is a great Vaisnava. When we see him, all the sufferings of our prison life will be destroyed'. After begging the guards with sweet words, with a single pair of eyes the prisoners gazed on Haridasa. When he came there, with a glance of mercy Haridasa looked at all the prisoners. Seeing Haridasa Thakur's feet, everyone bowed down to offer respect. Haridasa's eyes were like lotus flowers. His face was a moon. His arms reached to his knees.
