\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Thank you very much for taking responsibility for . This is what it means to mature in Krsna consciousness. When you take care of the Krsna consciousness of others, Krsna takes care of you. You have done the right thing by encouraging Lyuba to continue with her Krsna consciousness living outside of the asrama. In many cases devotees can advance nicely living outside if they are regulated and are strict with their association with devotees living inside the asrama. This does not mean that those who are living inside the asrama should move out. But it does mean that those who are living outside can make progress without having to renounce their work or school. Practically speaking, in most cases, I encourage devotees to continue with their education, unless their spiritual life is in great jeopardy. If they are determined, they can become very strong in Krsna consciousness this way and can develop even a deeper appreciation for association with devotees than they would have by immediately moving into the temple. So please continue giving her nice association.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

September 29, 1993

Dear Bhakta,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you very much for your letter and for all the devotional sentiments that you express in it.

We should always remember that the only real happiness is in Krsna consciousness and, therefore, we should always try our best to follow the simple and sublime process that Srila Prabhupada has taught us. So I request you again to please follow the four principles very strictly and chant your sixteen rounds of the Hare Krsna maha-mantra with great attention every day. If you do this and you cooperate to the best of your ability with the senior devotees in ISKCON who are continuing Srila Prabhupada's mission on this planet, you will be not only making progress on your path back home, back to Godhead, but at the same time you will be helping so many other living entities who are presently suffering in this material world. So please be enthusiastic in your devotional service every day thinking of the pleasure of guru and Krsna.

Hoping this letter finds you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

My dear ,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you very much for the nice sentiments you expressed in your letter dated 01.04.92.1 can understand that Krsna is giving you intelligence through the process of surrender; even though you are sometimes bewildered as to what is the best judgment in each circumstance you're faced with. You should know that the best judgment is to always endeavor to do what is best for pleasing guru and Krsna. Krsna will take care of the rest. He sees the attitude more than anything else. We have to make decisions, because spiritual life means that we sometimes have to take some risk. We don't take foolish risks. But for preaching we cannot sit by idly, always waiting for some message from the clouds to come down to confirm our actions. Krsna helps us, right from the very beginning of devotional service, and will continue to help if we just remain sincere. That is the most important qualification.

If you have some particular question about what is right or what is wrong in reference to your service, you can also confirm it with higher authorities. And, if necessary, you can check with me. Just like, whenever I visit different temples, I often sit down with the leaders and tell them what are the important things that they should focus on and what things are not as important. And since you have the responsible service there in of training the new women in the asrama, you also should have the opportunity to ask questions related to your service; especially since there are no senior householder devotees there, and the temple president is a bramacari. So, if you have some particular questions about what is right or not right, you can ask me.

You ask how to understand how a devotee feels pain and transcendental ecstasy simultaneously because they appear to be contradictory experiences. The word that Srila Prabhupada uses is the "pangs" of separation. The word pang here, means very strong feelings. This is not like the pain that one feels when a beloved leaves this mortal world, and someone cries piteously out of lamentation because they will not be able to enjoy their presence anymore. That is not what a pure devotee experiences during these strong feelings of separation. When Mother Saci would lose sight of Nimai for a moment she would swoon and both eyes would lose their ability to see. She was so attached to Nimai that it caused her great pain to not see Him even for a moment. But that pain brings the greatest pleasure because the Lord reciprocates with that attitude of His devotee. When a materialistic person departs, he cannot reciprocate with those he's left behind. He is not present in the remembrance of his name or his activities. He's either being punished by the Yamadutas, or being prepared for his next materialistic birth. But the Lord, being absolute, willfully appears in the minds of those devotees who are absorbed in thoughts of Him. So although it may seem like pain, it is an intense spiritual emotion.

There is a very detailed description of these points in the chapter in Krsna book entitled "Delivery of the message of Krsna to the Gopis". I suggest that you read through this whole chapter, and it should help to increase your understanding.

Gradually as one advances these things become more understandable, so you should not become so discouraged if you cannot completely understand now. In time, Krsna reveals these things when we are ready for them.

In the meantime, I hope that this letter meets you in good health, and fully enthusiastic in Lord Caitanya's Sankirtan Mission.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

## Difficulties in devotional service

Dear …,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada. I'm writing to respond to your inquiry about the disturbances in your devotional service, and your desire to overcome them.

You've quoted the following verse in reference to your situation:

**«For one whose mind is unbridled, self-realization is difficult work. But he whose mind is controlled and who strives by appropriate means is assured of success. That is My opinion.» (BG** [**6.36**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/6.36)**)**

You seem to have concluded that since you are struggling with your mind that you will not be able to advance in devotional service. But you should take everything that Lord Krishna has said on this matter. Don't overlook the more encouraging verse that He spoke just before that one.

**«Lord Shri Krishna said: O mighty-armed son of Kunti, it is undoubtedly very difficult to curb the restless mind, but it is possible by suitable practice and by detachment.» (BG** [**6.35**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/6.35)**)**

Krishna knows that by our efforts alone, it is impossible to control the restless mind. He wants us to learn how to depend upon Him. That learning process requires practice. Without it, we can never come to the level of bringing it under control. Just like a child who is learning how to walk. A child has the innate propensity for walking, but it takes time before he develops his natural walking abilities. First, he begins to crawl, and gradually he starts to raise himself up to stand on his feet. When he first starts to walk, he falls often. But by practice, gradually he can walk without difficulty. Similarly, it is natural for us to always remember Krsna, and to be peaceful in that constant remembrance of Him. But due to our long association with this material energy, we still strongly identify with the illusory designations of family, friendship and society. But the more we practice hearing and chanting about Krsna, gradually the material coverings of the mind will become cleansed, we'll see everything and everyone in relationship to Krsna, and all disturbances will go away. Krsna will help you by your constant endeavors.

Just become like the sparrow, who is determined to drink up the ocean in order to get the return of her eggs. Just as Garuda came to give His assistance, and the ocean immediately returned the eggs out of fear, in the same way Krsna will help you overcome your mind. Do not become discouraged. You have every reason to believe that you can and will be successful in your endeavors. Just don't stop practicing your remembrance of Krsna by constantly chanting and associating with devotees through discussions of Lord Krishna's transcendental pastimes and service.

Hoping this meets you in good health, and completely spiritually rejuvenated from your visit to the holy dhamas.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

You ask how to overcome your struggle to get up early in the morning. Although there are different ways to approach this problem, I find that the most effective way is a very practical one. You should participate in a regular morning function with other devotees. This may sound like an unreasonable request. There could be so many impediments that need to be overcome to accomplish this. I realize that adjustments like this are not so easy in the Soviet Union. Not everyone can live in the temple. And it's also not easy to just move and live near a temple. But, if the desire is strong, then Krsna can help to adjust the situation in such a way that you can have this arrangement. When you become part of a regular early morning program of kirtan, japa, and class, along with other devotees, you naturally feel obliged to be there, and this obligation towards other devotees, can give you the extra boost that you need to get up early. That's why temple life is so important. When you're by yourself, then it's so easy to make up excuses. But when you're executing the sadhana process with other devotees, then your excuses will not be as easily accepted by them.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

You say, «Krsna took off my gross attachments, but left those which I can not give up.» You should not think that you «can't give them up». Krsna can do anything, and that means that He can take away these attachments, too, if you let Him. The more subtle attachments don't go away as quickly. And when you're facing some difficulty, your mind will naturally seek shelter in these more subtle attachments. Yes, the solution is to take shelter in the holy name, chant your rounds very attentively, and meditate upon the instructions of your spiritual master and Krsna. When Krsna sees that you're trying to take shelter in Him, by taking shelter of the process of hearing and chanting, He will certainly help you. Gradually you will begin to dwell upon these attachments less. It is simply maya's test. Krsna surya sama; maya haya andhakara/ yaha krsna tahan nahi mayara adhikara, «Krsna is compared to sunshine, and maya is compared to darkness. Wherever there is sunshine there cannot be darkness. As soon as one takes to Krsna consciousness, the darkness of illusion (the influence of the external energy) will immediately vanish.» Just bring Krsna into your mind and maya will go away.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

You wrote that, «It seems to be that Krsna is going away from me.» It seems that you are experiencing the same thing that we talked about during our meeting in Moscow last Monday, when the devotee asked why he experienced a taste in the beginning but it soon went away. Maybe you were thinking then that my answer also applied to you. Do not be disturbed by this apparent loss of Krsna consciousness. It is simply a test to see how determined you are to continue to make advancement. In one lecture in Los Angeles in 1973, Srila Prabhupada mentions this exact point. It's quoted as follows:

…"If one takes to Krishna consciousness, in the beginning there will be many disturbances caused by Maya, the material energy of illusion. Maya will test us to see how firmly we are fixed in Krishna consciousness. Because she is also an agent of Krishna, she does not allow anyone the freedom to disturb Krishna. Therefore she tests very rigidly to see whether we have taken to Krishna consciousness to disturb Krishna or are actually serious. That is Maya's business. So in the beginning there will be tests by Maya, and we shall feel so many disturbances while making progress in Krishna consciousness. But if we follow the rules and regulations and chant regularly as prescribed, then we shall remain steady. If we neglect these principles, Maya will capture us immediately. Maya is always ready. We are in the ocean, and at any moment we may be disturbed...»

Please remember this always, and Krsna will help you overcome these tests.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

You say in your letter, «I've been exerting all my powers for its (the gurukula's) organization, but I've got nothing except diseases The disease, you may have, but it's not the result of your exertion for the gurukula organization. Any endeavor made for Lord Krsna's pleasure never goes in vain, and thus, the results of those endeavors are incalculable. In fact, you should see that whatever Krsna consciousness you have is a direct result from your efforts to please Him. A better way to understand your present situation is that Lord Krsna has very kindly and timely given you the opportunity to endeavor for His pleasure, to help you overcome the disease that you are presently combating. Whether or not you become relieved from this disease is ultimately up to Him. But, whatever happens, your endeavors to please will bear permanent and auspicious results. Please do not despair.

Now you must pray to Krsna that He allow you to go on with this service, since it is our service that increases our attachment to Him. Then let him decide where and how we shall serve. But for now, this is your service- along with your continuous chanting and hearing of His Holy Names.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear Bhaktin,

Hare Krsna. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have your letter dated 1/13/92 and have noted the contents. You ask how does one sever a friendship with someone when you know it's not good association. My best recommendation is that you should make every effort to be good association for that «friend». If they're really your friend then they'll accept that good association and then they'll become good themselves. If they don't like that good association, then they'll become exposed, and a natural disinclination to continue associating with you will automatically follow. Of course, it's easier to see this principle when applied to nondevotees. But it can also be applied to devotees. One should naturally seek out that association which helps one the most to become Krsna conscious. There's a nice verse and purport in the S.B. which nicely describes how we should deal with other devotees:

Every man should act like this: when he meets a person more qualified than himself, he should be very pleased; when he meets someone less qualified than himself, he should be compassionate toward him; and when he meets someone equal to himself, he should make friendship with him. In this way one is never affected by the threefold miseries of this material world.

PURPORT

Generally when we find someone more qualified than ourselves, we become envious of him; when we find someone less qualified, we deride him; and when we find someone equal we become very proud of our activities. These are the causes of all material tribulations. The great sage Narada therefore advised that a devotee should act perfectly. Instead of being envious of a more qualified man, one should be jolly to receive him. Instead of being oppressive to a less qualified man, one should be compassionate toward him just to raise him to the proper standard. And when one meets an equal, instead of being proud of one's own activities before him, one should treat him as a friend. One should also have compassion for the people in general, who are suffering due to forgetfulness of Krishna. These important functions will make one happy within this material world.

We can't push along realizations by our own efforts alone. In order to have any realization at all it must be given by Krsna. He reveals according to our surrender to Him. He is the ultimate sanctioning agent, and therefore we must simply make every effort to please Him and patiently wait for His mercy. When He chooses to reveal Himself, then and only then, can we have realizations. The following verse from the Mundaka Upanisad nicely expresses this point:

***nayam atma pravacanena labhyo***  
***ne medhaya na bahuna srutena yam evaisa***  
***vrnute ten labhyas tasyaisa atma***  
***vivrnute tanum svam***

**The Supreme Lord is not attained by expert explanations, by vast intelligence, nor by much hearing. He is attained only by one who He Himself chooses. To such a person He manifests His own form.**

Since you've asked for the simple answer, then your last question will be answered in its most simplistic form. You ask how to get over the illusion of pride. Of course, pride does not go away so quickly, but if we want to work on decreasing it, then we must simply voluntarily accept the role as a servant of Krsna. Note that becoming just a «servant» is not sufficient. We must become Krsna's servant by becoming the servant of Vaisnavas.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you for your letter dated 7.04.92. I have read it and carefully noted the contents.

I was very happy to hear about your successful preaching in ... From the appearance of the number of new faces I'm seeing during this visit, it seems as though many new people are becoming interested in our movement. Please continue this preaching with great enthusiasm.

You ask if we should force ourselves to engage in undesirable service, or simply do only that service which is desirable. From your letter it seems that your responsibilities in are now increasing. You're now one of the more senior matajis in the asrama, and with that comes more responsibility towards the less experienced matajis. You're also one of only a few initiated devotees living in the temple, and I'm sure that brings some responsibilities for you, too. And beyond all that, you also have a duty to distribute Srila Prabhupada's books. That duty should not stop, although I understand that your time out on book distribution may have to become less, due to your added responsibilities. We should be prepared to do the needful to see that Lord Caitanya's Sankirtan mission goes on increasing, and that means sometimes having to accept duties that we may not like to do. The important thing is to make sure that your duties are always connected to Lord Caitanya's Sankirtan mission, and that way there will be no room for maya. You must also maintain a good sadhana if you want to keep your strength for preaching and book distribution. Chant your rounds as early as possible, so that they can be good and attentive. Attend the morning program, and also read regularly from Srila Prabhupada's books. Don't let your other duties interfere with this. Otherwise, you can become weakened and forget why you're doing everything.

Regarding which service will give you the quickest progress, from you I expect that you'll engage in preaching and book distribution. «Quick progress» means to be recognized by Lord Krsna, and the best way to be recognized by Him is by staying absorbed in this mission. Preaching also means to train and take care of new devotees. This is very important preaching, because by your preaching, other devotees should also take up this mission. And, of course, preaching also means to give Krsna consciousness to the conditioned souls.

You also ask that if you perform some service without devotion, is it still devotional service. Even if it seems that you don't have an ounce of devotion, if you do it simply because your spiritual master wants you to do it, then that is the first stage of devotion. Such service is never useless.

It is stated in the sastra that those persons who are perfected by strict adherence to rules and regulations can enter into the Vaikuntha region where the Lord is worshipped in His four armed form in awe and reverence. But it is not true that devotees cannot be elevated beyond the level of Lakshmi-Narayana worship. It is also stated in the Nectar of Devotion that the residents of Vaikuntha can also become attracted the Lord Krsna's features, and can thus be elevated from there into Goloka Vrndavana.

I cannot understand what you mean by the expression comparing the living entities to a «dog sitting on the chain», so there's no way I can make you understand.

You ask if I could have my own deities, but I prefer to make preaching my Deity. I have never thought much about which manifestation of Krsna is most attractive to me. However, it seems that I'm most attracted by His asraya-vigraha, or His manifestation of whom I must take shelter, namely His pure devotee, Srila Prabhupada.

Hoping this meets you in good health and fully absorbed in devotional service.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear Bhaktin,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you for your letter which was hand-delivered to me during my last visit. It was translated for me, and I have since noted the contents.

You ask, «How can I serve Krishna peacefully?» However, you must know that when you take to Krishna consciousness, there will always be so many disturbances. Maya will test to see if you are sincere in your desire to go back to Godhead. So if you want to serve Krishna peacefully, then you must learn how to overcome maya's influence by becoming steady in your determination to always be engaged in Krishna's service, and always chant His holy names. For the nondevotee, maya attracts them to fulfill the objects of their material desires. But she also punishes them for misuse of their independence. But for a devotee, maya helps him, by giving him the impetus to take shelter of Krishna in devotional service.

Therefore, when maya puts some disturbance in our path, we should immediately think, «Oh, now I must take shelter of Krishna». If we do this then Krishna will protect us. But if we don't then maya can easily capture us.

So, please take shelter of Krishna in all circumstances, and gradually, you will become peaceful. In time, you'll see that Krishna will always give protection to His surrendered devotee.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health, and happy in Krishna consciousness.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Regarding your doubts, don't hide them away. Feel free to express them, otherwise how will you overcome them? For instance, I once received a letter from one very sincere aspiring disciple who expressed doubts about her inability to have full faith in me as Krsna's representative, although externally she wanted to become my disciple. This was a doubt, and she honestly confronted it. Of course, I wrote her a long letter, dealing with the issue philosophically, and she now claims that it has helped her a lot towards becoming more steady. Srila Prabhupada also wrote to his disciples in this way. For instance, the following letter:

«Of course, weakness in Krishna Consciousness we should always feel. That is a good symptom. We should never think that we are strong enough. But the source of strength is Krishna and His manifested representative, the Spiritual Master. We get this instruction from Chaitanya Charitamrita that we receive the seed of Krishna Consciousness through the Spiritual Master and Krishna. Therefore, we should serve both simultaneously for continuous supply of spiritual vitality. So you are now serving Krishna under the instruction of your Spiritual Master, so there will be no scarcity of supply of strength unless there is some weakness on your part in the matter of absolute faith in these two shelters. So if you have still some doubts in putting your faith in the service of your Spiritual Master and Krishna, then you can clear it up. I am always prepared to assist you.» (SPL tojayagovinda, Oct. 15th, 1969.)

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear Bhaktin,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

2\) Regarding your difficulties on sankirtan and the conflict of your desire to leave or remain without suffering, certainly you can leave, but that won't be the solution. I think that you know that, and that's why you're asking me this question. Although it may seem like you're suffering by remaining, that voluntary suffering is actually very purifying, and gradually it grows into something more relishable. It's only due to our diseased condition that we experience the suffering. If you keep pushing yourself a little bit, gradually you may gain the realization you need to transcend the suffering. However, I know that in the beginning it's not easy to just remain on sankirtan artificially. But there are certain things that you can do that would help make it more natural. For instance, you should go out on sankirtan with a godsister. Don't go out alone. I don't approve of inexperienced book distributors going out alone. Association is very important. Then when you feel that you're suffering, you can take shelter in the association of another Vaisnava and reveal your mind. It helps so much to have that association, and you begin to appreciate the association of devotees much more as well.

Another thing you can do is stop every once in awhile and read together with your godsister. Reading Srila Prabhupada's books when you're out in the preaching field helps brings his books to life. You can just lift your eyes from the pages every once in awhile, and you'll see everything that Srila Prabhupada is talking about going right before your eyes. That also increases your enthusiasm to remain in your sankirtan place and take part in this glorious preaching mission.

So, these are some suggestions of things you can do to help you to stay and at least, suffer less. Gradually it will turn into nectar.

3\) If you are regulated in your sankirtan and are going out daily, then you should not be disturbed during your japa to go in the kitchen. But if you are not regulated in your sankirtan then the temple commander will just think that you have plenty of time to chant your rounds during the day, so he'll send you in the kitchen. It's natural, because you should have plenty of time to chant your rounds during the day, unless, of course, your schedule is so completely full that you don't. But somehow, I don't think that that is the case. So adjust your activities accordingly. If you don't go out on sankirtan daily, then the first thing you should do immediately after the morning program is finish your rounds. Then you can just help in the kitchen during japa as a regular duty. But, better you just go out on sankirtan daily.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health.

I am, Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear Bhaktin,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I received your latest message just before my departure from Moscow. It was translated for me by Bhaktin Larisa.

You ask me for my forgiveness for everything. I can forgive, but it's not me who has been offended. It's Prabhu, and many other devotees who have complained to me about your overly aggressive behavior. Even in other cities, there are leading and respected devotees who know you by this trait.

In the third Canto of the Srimad Bhagavatam Lord Krsna says the following:

«A wrong act committed by a servant leads people in general to blame his master, just as a spot of white leprosy on any part of the body pollutes all of the skin.»

If you continue in this aggressive behavior and maintain your claim to be my servant, then I must take the blame for your behavior. Therefore, in order to clear myself of this responsibility, I must preach to you to rectify your attitude towards others, and don't just ask me for forgiveness. The proper Vaisnava behavior is to approach those devotees whom we may have offended, either knowingly or unknowingly, and with all sincerity, ask to be forgiven. This won't work if your attitude remains the same as it has been. You must see some fault in yourself and be prepared to correct it. Otherwise, the whole thing will be pretentious and it will just be a show.

So, why wait for «some day» to become humble. Why not try following my instructions right now and act accordingly. Then your letter to me will have a little bit more credibility.

I remain, Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I suspect that you may think that I'm ignoring you, because you've written me so many letters, and I don't answer them. It's not that I don't want to answer them, it's just that it's not possible for me to answer them. I don't think that I need to go into giving you any long explanations why. You should know by now.

It's the duty of the spiritual master to see right through all the superficial problems and go right to the root of the problem. If I try to satisfy all of your inquiries superficially, what good will it do? You may get some satisfaction from feeling that I'm giving you some attention whenever you go through some emotional changes, but that satisfaction is very temporary. The spiritual master is always giving his attention, even when it seems that he's not. It's his duty to give his attention to all of his disciples, and to non-devotees as well, by dedicating himself fully to serving his guru and Krsna. It's the disciple's duty to assist him, by becoming responsible and dependent upon his instructions. Not that every time the disciple has some question, he needs to get a new instruction. Being dependent upon the spiritual master's instructions means learning how to mold one's life with firm faith on what the spiritual master has «already» given. Then the spiritual master is very pleased, because the disciple shows by this example that he is advancing.

One of your biggest problems is humility and this whole experience that you're going through is Krsna's way of teaching you how to become more humble. Your letters to me contain so much humility and you try to create an external air of humility around others, but actually it's becoming much clearer that you still have to work very hard on developing this humility.

So what does all this mean? It doesn't mean that you become all emotional and think, «Oh I'm a demon! I have no love and compassion! I have such a hard heart! I can't even please my spiritual master!» No! That's not the kind of response that I'm looking for by writing all of this to you. And neither should you start foolishly thinking that you should live outside the society of devotees and become independent like you've written to me before, too. You've written so many things to me. It's not that I forget what you've written. It's just that I have to go deeper into the problem, like I explained earlier to you. Now you have to withstand the test, and be genuinely humble enough to be willing to hear what I'm presenting to you. Otherwise, all your humility in your letters cannot be taken seriously. Humility is not an external show, so that you can make everyone «think» that you're humble. Humility means to always think oneself to be the servant of Krsna, guru and the Vaisna- vas. When you think like this you won't feel so isolated from everyone else. You'll feel very connected in service to Krsna.

So, Krsna is now personally intervening to help you along on your path back home, back to Godhead. There is so much for you to learn now, and there is still so much more for you to learn. If you see Krsna working behind everything, then you'll advance nicely, and you'll become more peaceful. But if you try to adjust everything artificially, you will never be able to experience peace. So, please take shelter in Him and go on in your devotional service, and be confident that I am pleased by your efforts to follow these instructions.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Yes, it is very dangerous if you become detached from the association of devotees, because that means you'll associate with nondevotees. Nondevotees simply try to gratify their own senses, and although they say so many things about how they want to please you, they always have their motives. It may look nice on the surface, but just below the surface, there will be so many problems, and you will get cheated. Of course, devotees can have impure motives, too. But the difference, is that if they continue to follow the practices of Krsna consciousness, these impure motives will become purified. Nondevotees cannot and will not become purified, unless they associate with devotees. Therefore, association with devotees is the most important thing in life. Please do not reject it!

And you also ask what a devotee who cannot accept vani should do. What you're saying is that you don't want to associate with me either. You can accept vani, it's not that, as you say you can't. Vani means to follow my instructions. Just don't think that my instructions are the same as everything else that people tell you to do, which you don't want to do. I'm giving you these instructions because I want to see you happy. I don't have anything to gain by giving you these instructions. I'm not doing it for some other personal motive. What do I get if you follow my instructions? My duty is to accept everything that you give me, and offer it to Srila Prabhupada in my service to him. That's why I ask you to associate with devotees. That's why I ask you to distribute Srila Prabhupada's books. That's why I ask you to please continue to chant your rounds. If you follow what I say, it's for Srila Prabhupada's and Krsna's pleasure, because they become very happy when they see nice girls like you taking this Krsna consciousness very seriously. They don't want to see anyone suffer in this material world.

So what you should do, is to stop thinking that you can't accept vani, and just do it by following these very basic practices of Krsna consciousness. Then when I come back to , I'll see you happy to be in the association of devotees.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

I am, Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I'm sure that you know I've been back from my latest overseas trip for well over a month. I would have preferred to return a letter to you sooner, but circumstances just did not allow it.

Regarding your letter of March 28,1 understand that your later letter nullified the feelings expressed in the 28th letter, however, I would like to first respond with a couple of quotes from Srila Prabhupada that may help you, should such feelings ever come up again.

«It is not so much that because there may be some faults in our godbrothers and godsisters, or because there may be some mismanagement or lack of cooperation, that this is due to being impersonalists, no. It is the nature of the living condition to always have some fault. Even in the Spiritual World there is some fault and envy - sometimes the Gopis will quarrel over Krishna's favour, and once Krishna was so much attracted to Radharani that by mistake he tried to milk the bull instead of the cow, and sometimes when the Gopis used to put on their dress and make-up for seeing Krishna, they would be too much hasty and smear kum kum and mascara in the wrong places and their ornaments and dresses would appear as if small children had been trying to dress themselves and they were not very expert, like that. There are so many examples. But it is not the same as material fault or material envy, it is transcendental because it is all based on Krishna. Sometimes when Gopis would serve Krishna very nicely, the others would say, Oh, she has done so nicely, now let me do better for Krishna. That is envy, but it is transcendental, without malice. So we shall not expect that anywhere there is any Utopia. Rather, that is impersonalism. People should not expect that even in the Krishna Consciousness Society there will be Utopia. Because devotees are persons, therefore there will always be some lacking - but the difference is that their lacking, because they have given up everything to serve Krishna - money, jobs, reputation, wealth, big educations, everything - their lackings have become transcendental because, despite everything they may do, their topmost intention is to serve Krishna. «One who is engaged in devotional service, despite the most abominable action, is to be considered saintly because he is rightly situated.» The devotees of Krishna are the most exalted persons on this planet, better than kings, all of them, so we should always remember that and, like the bumble -bee, always look for the nectar or the best qualities of a person. Not like the Utopians, who are like the flies who always go to the open sores or find the faults in a person, and because they cannot find any utopia, or because they cannot find anyone without faults, they want to become void, merge, nothing - they think that is utopia, to become void of personality. So if there is sometimes slight disagreements between devotees, it is not due to impersonalism, but it is because they are persons, and such disagreements should not be taken very seriously. The devotee is always pessimistic about the material world, but he is very optimistic about the spiritual life; so in this way, you should consider that anyone engaged in Krishna's service is always the best person.» (SPL to Atreya Rishi, Feb 4th, 1972.)

«I have noted your several complaints against the devotees but it would be better to set the example rather than to criticize the defects of the devotees. We should always remember that we recruit members from people in general. It is not expected that every one of our members should be immediately to the standard qualification. The best thing for you is to set the example by your personal behavior and try to reform the others, not by criticizing but by friendly behavior. If sometimes there are any disagreements, we should try to forget such incidences and be always in friendship with each other.» (Letter to Vrn- da devi, Oct. 14, 1971)

In my estimation, the devotees that you are finding fault with are sincere, and they are trying their best to serve their spiritual master and Krsna. It's not that I will defend all of their actions. I'm sure that there are many areas that could be improved upon. But, it's the way you present it. A devotee does not like to find fault with anyone; what to speak of other devotees. But sometimes it may be necessary. Just like the spiritual master's duty is to find fault with the disciple. It's not that he likes finding fault. But he must point out the defects in the disciple so that the disciple can advance.

«I know you are working hard and sincerely. I have no business to criticize you but as head of the institution or your spiritual master, it is my duty to find out your faults. Even Caitanya Mahaprabhu presented himself as faulty before his spiritual master. To remain faulty before the spiritual master is a good qualification so he is subjected to rectification. But if one thinks he is all perfect then there is no scope for rectification. Don't be sorry when I find fault. That is my primary duty. Canakya pandit says one must find fault with disciples and sons, it is good for them.» (Letter to Jayapataka Swami, April 24, 1974)

«Actually it is the duty of the spiritual master to find fault with his students so that they may make progress, not that he should always be praising them. So if you find some criticism, kindly accept it in that spirit. I am only interested in that you along with all my other students should become Krishna Conscious.» (Letter to Giriraja, May 9.1972)

Similarly if there are some faults in the temple devotees that you find incompatible with our philosophy, and you're genuinely concerned that these faults become rectified, please present them to me without being so condescending. It's also not necessary to go out and look for reinforcements, or to give reinforcement for a «campaign for rectification». Purity is the force, and if any real rectification is going to be made within ISKCON, it will only be successful by pure preaching and example. That's how «one can be part of the solution, and not part of the problem».

I must also say that I don't know what you heard in the way of criticism of yourself among the devotees in the temple. If there is some criticism being discussed among them, then that also is not right, and it must be corrected. However, the last letter I sent you was not prompted by any malicious criticism of you at all. In my opinion, what I heard was quite objective, and free from opinionation. I also did not write that letter because I was irritated. I was simply doing my duty.

Under the present conditions, I can understand the reason behind your inability to come to the temple. Just please take advantage of the association that Krsna sends you by keeping Krsna always at the center of your discussions.

It's also nice that you are preaching to your friends, and you don't have to water your preaching down. But be careful not to become so broad in your topics of discussion that Krsna gets left out. If you're not going to bring them to the temple or closer to ISKCON, then it's your duty to bring them to Krsna. Yes, we are people of high moral character, and we want to actively raise the consciousness of others to a more humanitarian way of life. But the ultimate aim is to go back home, back to Godhead, and only Krsna can help us with that. Don't forget, He's the purifying ingredient. Krsna's name, form, pastimes, and instructions are all non-different from Him, and He is the most pure. He is all-at- tractive, and the more we associate with Him, in the above-mentioned ways, that attraction will increase. We can't expect to leave this material world on distaste for it alone. We must become attracted to Krsna. And that is the mission that each and every one of us have in this life and the next.

Hoping this meets you in the best health that can be expected.

I am always, Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear Bhaktin,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Having acknowledged the contents of your last two letters, I'm writing to give you some direction.

Regarding marriage, my statements in my last letter still stand. At this point, there is nothing further for me to discuss in this regard.

I'm not sure that I understand what you're suggesting when you say in your letter, «I think that you are the only one who can completely rid me of this contamination^ but I'll try answering it anyway. Krsna has already given us His assurance that if we surrender unto Him, He will deliver us from all sinful reactions. And even more so, He insists that there is no need to fear. What more assurance do you need than that? It's not possible to even begin to calculate the heaps of sinful reactions that we've stored up from our past sinful activities. But through the process of surrender and absorption in Krsna, they will all gradually be destroyed.

In Srimad-Bhagavatam ([11.14.19](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/14.19)) Lord Krishna tells Uddhava: «My dear Uddhava, devotional service in relationship with Me is like a blazing fire that can burn to ashes all the fuel of sinful activities supplied to it.». If you have a blazing fire, no matter how much fuel you add to it, the fire will consume it. In the same way, if you are always engaged in devotional service, no matter how sinful you were in your previous karmi life, the reactions can be burned to ashes. Every morning we are chanting while watering Srimati Tulasi devi that just by circumambulating Tulasi, we can destroy the reactions accumulated from even killing a brahmana. That is the power of devotional service. So make the fire blaze by your Krsna consciousness. You keep telling me how much you want to serve me, but I keep reminding you that you should serve Krsna. So you should test the effectiveness of the service you are rendering by your ability to remember Krsna and to be always engaged in His service.

You also connect this incident of the past with your violent temper, but that is no excuse for not changing. It greatly disturbs me when I hear you unleash your anger to an unsuspecting target, and during my last stay in , I witnessed this again on at least two occasions (that I know of).

Lord Krsna tells the four Kumaras in the Srimad Bhagavatam ([3.16.5](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/16.5)): «A wrong act committed by a servant leads people in general to blame his master, just as a spot of white leprosy on any part of the body pollutes all of the skin.» You claim to be my servant, so that means that if I don't do something to try to rectify this «wrong» mentality of yours, then some people will put some of the blame on me. This anger, and your tendency to engage in idle, critical talk about other devotees will make your situation such, that, no matter what I try to do to help, I will not be able to help you. Krsna will tolerate it only for so long.

I'm mentioning this tendency for idle, critical talk because this has also been brought to my attention in the «not too distant» past. Unfortunately, it has been brought to my attention too many times for me to dismiss it as an unjust attack against you. Once or twice, I can overlook it. But when it starts coming back to me more than that, and from different people, it's a good indication that there is some substance to these complaints. Please take this as a strong word of warning that you must make every effort to correct this tendency lest you face the unfortunate consequences of finding yourself bereft of the association of devotees. Krsna warns us in Bhagavad-gita 9.30: «Even if one commits the most abominable action, if he is engaged in devotional service he is to be considered saintly because he is properly situated in his determination.» That means that even if a devotee falls from the path of devotional service, it's not that we take the opportunity to criticize him(her); what to speak of those who remain continuously endeavoring.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have two letters from you. One dated way back in February, and the other one is undated.

In your first letter you were writing about this stone wall that was existing between yourself and myself, but in the course of your writing you were, from time to time, describing (maybe unknowingly) how this wall was appearing to have large holes in it. Your letter graphically depicted the internal struggles of a sincere devotee, struggling to increase her devotion, while getting brief glimpses of mixed devotion from associating with the instructions of the spiritual master. Your anxiety exists because these glimpses are just too infrequent and you want them to increase. Yes, I'm sure that both of us would like to smash this wall for good, and never let it get built again, but the wall is made up of dirty desires that remain in the heart. They must be completely destroyed before the wall can become completely removed.

Please do not feel too despondent about the condition of your heart. It is natural to feel so impure in the presence of the spiritual master. I will never forget the first time Srila Prabhupada's eyes met my eyes as I was sitting and listening to him give Srimad Bhagavatam class. Our eye contact lasted only for a moment, because I had to look down. My experience was just as if he was looking right through me, exposing every little piece of dirt in my heart for me to look at. It was very difficult to tolerate. I felt too impure to even be in his presence. But, to this day, I always keep my hope, that by his mercy, he can help me to get rid of these impurities. Not by my efforts alone, but ultimately, due to his mercy. His mercy is so powerful that it can even come through me, to you.So you are asking me to help you to break down this barrier. I will always help. That is my duty. But you must maintain your eagerness to be helped, and act upon the instructions I give you. I cannot be there personally, at every moment, to guide you, correct you, and support you whenever you start to deviate from the path. But I am there, when you are remembering my instructions. That is the disciple's primary duty. Each time you become bewildered, you must strengthen your intelligence by remembering the instructions I have given for that particular situation in which you are finding yourself. And then act on those instructions. If you are uncertain about what my instruction is for that particular situation, then ask your authority. And if your authority is not available, then there are still so many instructions that I've given that can be applied to every situation. «Act for Krsna's pleasure, and leave the result up to Him.» Always remember these two verses in the Bhagavad-gita ([18.57-58](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/18.57)):

**«ln all activities just depend upon Me and work always under My protection. In such devotional service, be fully conscious of Me.»**

**«If you become conscious of Me, you will pass over all the obstacles of conditioned life by My grace. If, however, you do not work in such consciousness but act through false ego, not hearing Me, you will be lost.»**

Do not become discouraged when you sometimes waver in this determination. That is the symptom of a neophyte devotee. The more you endeavor the more Krsna will help you.

You ask what it means to open the mind to the spiritual master. This you are already doing in these two letters. It means that you should disclose your doubts. It doesn't mean that you have to graphically describe every thought that comes into your mind. There is no need to dump a truckload of garbage onto the spiritual master. Opening the mind means to express your doubts. Then the spiritual master can destroy these doubts with the weapon of transcendental knowledge.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have your letter (undated) and have noted the contents.

You've asked a couple of questions about the ability to remember my instructions, and the flickering manifestation of sincerity that oftentimes goes as quickly as it comes.

These «highs and lows», are all a symptom of the neophyte stages of devotional service. Lord Krsna describes the following in the sixth chapter of the Bhagavad-gita:

«From whatever and wherever the mind wanders due to its flickering and unsteady nature, one must certainly withdraw it and bring it back under the control of the Self.»

«Arjuna said: O Madhusudana, the system of yoga which You have summarized appears impractical and unendurable to me, for the mind is restless and unsteady.»

«Lord Shri Krsna said: O mighty-armed son of Kunti, it is undoubtedly very difficult to curb the restless mind, but it is possible by suitable practice and by detachment.»

«For one whose mind is unbridled, self-realization is difficult work. But he whose mind is controlled and who strives by appropriate means is assured of success. That is My opinion.»

These verses are perfect responses to the points that you've raised in your letter. You must practice overcoming this business of sankalpa-vikalpa, or, accepting and rejecting. I've seen how you become practically suspended in this accepting and rejecting business, and have already given you many relevant instructions for overcoming this tendency. But, as you say, you have difficulty remembering them.

But do not become discouraged. Krsna knows the sincerity within your heart, and will help you to become stronger. Just don't stop endeavoring. He also explains in the Bhagavad-gita that it is from Him that memory, knowledge, and forgetfulness are coming. He gives us the ability to remember in proportion to our determination to practice this remembrance.

So please go forward in your endeavors to remember Him, my instructions, and always chant your 16 rounds daily with great enthusiasm. Gradually your memory will become clearer, and you'll become fixed in Krsna consciousness.

Hoping this meets you in good health and absorbed in your most important service of book distribution.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have your letter dated 8 August, 1992, and have noted the contents.

One of your questions, in which you misunderstood that I was dissatisfied that you were going on traveling sankirtan has, hopefully, been already cleared up during my brief discussion with you on this matter.

Regarding your other questions, you are mainly asking how to improve the lack of cooperation and envy that you are experiencing. But you also mentioned in this same letter that you remember my instructions on this subject, but cannot control yourself despite this remembrance. So you already know what my instructions are. I have given so many instructions on these points for a very good reason. I know, only too well, that you, and so many of the other devotees are victims of these two major disturbances within this material world. We're here because we don't want to cooperate with Krsna and because we became envious of Him. Therefore, doesn't it seem likely that it will take some time for these disturbances to go away?

This is what the process of purification in devotional service is all about— how to become free from envy. It is deeply ingrained within the heart, but it is only by remaining in the process of devotional service that it can be uprooted. The karmis, jnanis, and yogis, are still envious of Krsna. Only the devotee can become completely freed from this envy. So, presently you are in the beginning stages of devotional service. All these anarthas are still there within the heart and are giving you so much disturbance. But you are much more fortunate than the karmi, jnani or yogi, because Lord Krsna has assured that He will help His devotee become free from these unwanted things. You're not alone in your efforts. Krsna is helping, your spiritual master is helping, and the all the Vai- snavas are helping you. Simply take their help by always trying to remember and act upon their instructions. There will be so much resistance, but by your constant endeavor, gradually the resistance will diminish. Be patient.

You ask what it means to be sincere. Sincerity means that despite all the difficulties a devotee must experience due to the presence of his anarthas, a devotee will never stop endeavoring to serve Krsna. That is sincerity.

So please push on with your sincere devotional service, and certainly Krsna will pour His blessings upon you and will take you back home, back to Godhead.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have before me two letters from you. One letter is undated and the other is dated back in February. Your February letter did not have any questions, but simply, and sincerely, expressed your desire to be engaged in devotional service. I trust that this letter has been answered by Lord Krsna, and that you are now on your way back to recovery so that you can take up your service of book distribution once again. As I mentioned to you in our discussion in , I fully understand why it was too difficult for you to distribute books while your health was in such a terrible state. I never pressure devotees to go out on sankirtan when their health is as bad as your health was. However, under those conditions, a devotee should be very careful to not use their bad health as an excuse to remain unengaged. Recovery is only part of such a devotee's engagement. There should also be other service according to one's means.

I'm concerned about these points that you've raised in your other letter. You seem to be suggesting that you are feeling humiliated by your authorities, and that they are using intimidating methods to get you to be a productive member of the ashram. My question to you would be, «Why is this so»? Could it be that they are feeling forced to employ such methods with you because you need to be pushed a little bit? Sometimes a person in a position of authority has to push a little bit, if the devotees under their care cannot be self-motivated enough to push themselves on the order of their guru. You should consider this point and look within yourself to see if this may possibly be the case. Or, are you thinking that you are being treated unfairly?

I never push anyone by threats or intimidation. Krsna does not threaten or force us. Devotional service must be voluntary. The best method of pushing others is to present Krsna's and the spiritual master's instructions purely, and to be a good example oneself. Srila Prabhupada has given us the principle that «Puri- ty is the force». So my desire is to develop the ability to push you in this way. But you must also develop the desire to be pushed in this way. Then Krsna will help rectify everything for you. I want you to be a good, steady servant of Lord Caitanya's Mission. I must push a little so that you don't fall into maya. If this were to happen it would bring me great distress.

I cannot understand why you are being threatened to be sent back to , and none of your authorities have mentioned anything to me about this. To make such a threat means that there must be some grave deviation on your part, but nobody has said a word to me about any such deviation. If they are thinking that they have to make such threats, they should inform me about your behavior, before they make them, so that I can do my duty and preach and encourage you to rectify yourself. But nobody has said anything. Your authorities should know this. Please let me know if this is still happening.

I hope that this letter finds you in much improved health, and enthusiastically engaged in devotional service.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Please accept my blessings.

All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

The best way to see your own faults is to regularly associate with devotees who are more advanced than you. By observation and through discussion, devotees, more advanced than yourself, can oftentimes reflect your own faults so that they can become more visible to you.

You are right. You cannot force others to speak the truth about you, but if a trusting relationship develops, then out of a natural concern, they will want to point these things out for your welfare. However, it is not necessarily a symptom that a devotee is more advanced just because he(she) can pick out some faults in you. In fact, faultfinding is one of the symptoms of a very neopyhyte devotee, and such faultfinders are generally just trying to avoid seeing the faults within themselves by trying to find them in others. The most reliable devotees are those devotees who naturally point out faults to others — first— by their exemplary character and steadiness in devotional service. They are generally not eager to find faults in others, but will do so only out of a sense of duty. And, of course, your designated authority may very well be in a position to see your faults as well.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

January 13, 1993

Dear Bhaktin,

Hare Krsna. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

This letter is in reply to your previous letter dated 10/30/92. This is the first opportunity that I've gotten to answer it.

Your first question about surrender is, undoubtedly, difficult to understand in the neophyte stage. If we could fully grasp how to "fully" surrender "immediately", we would do it, since it's result would bring us more happiness than we could possibly ever hope to experience. Of course, it is possible, but our nature is that we are very conditioned. So when you hear this term "just surrender", it is more commonly applied to the acceptance of an instruction or situation coming from Krsna to which we are resisting. For instance, it may be that you have been told by a well-wishing devotee that it seems that a particular situation has been arranged for you by no one other than Krsna, Himself. However, from an initial perspective, that situation may appear to you as one which may result in distress. You're seeing the situation separate from Krsna. You may try to resist, by looking for options. But all indications suggest that this situation is becoming increasingly inevitable. In fear, you try to escape by making some temporary adjustment, but it is becoming painfully obvious that that is just not going to provide you with any solution. You return to your well-wishing friend, the devotee, and make an appeal once again for their guidance. "Just surrender" may be the words that you would hear.

In other words, we must try to see how Krsna is behind every arrangement, and try to see how He is our "most" well-wishing friend. As long as we are endeavoring to serve Him, He will arrange a particular situation for our advancement, and He wants us to become more and more dependent upon the decisions He makes for us. This is a "step" in the process of "full" surrender, and Krsna wants every one of His devotees to take it— one after the other. You take one step to Krsna and He will take 100 steps to you. "How" to surrender, means that you just have to do it. But make sure that it's Krsna to Whom you are surrendering. This requires intelligence and proper guidance from a well-wishing devotee.

Yes, the Yamadutas are definitely devotees, and they are sincere servants of Yamaraja. They have their thankless duties to perform, just as Mayadevi herself performs her thankless task of keeping conditioned souls under her clutches.

Yes, according to the Vedic literatures a living entity certainly can come back into the association of those with whom he shared experiences in his previous life. It's not automatic, but it is certainly possible. For example, the living entity who took birth as the son of Maharaja Citraketu was an enemy in a previous life, and he caused suffering to him in the next life, by becoming his son and dying very shortly after his birth. This is described in the Sixth Canto of Srimad Bhagavatam.

Krsna does not like to see pride in His devotees, and therefore any act performed out of false pride will inevitably have to be rectified. That is what advancement is all about. But if we are, as you say, to "get smashed", then let it be by Krsna. Whatever He does, is for our benefit. However, an intelligent person will avoid the "smash" by acting upon what he hears from proper authorities.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

1\) I often quote one very important verse spoken by Sri Narada Muni. This verse directly deals with the same fear expressed in your first question.

**«One who has forsaken his material occupations to engage in the devotional service of the Lord may sometimes fall down while in an immature stage, yet there is no danger of his being unsuccessful. On the other hand, a nondevotee, though fully engaged in occupational duties, does not gain anything.» (SB** [**1.5.7**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/5.7)**)**

There is always that potential that we will not be able to complete our work in this life. In fact, Arjuna asked Lord Krsna the same question in the Bhagavad-gita, «0 Krsna, what is the destination of the unsuccessful transcendentalist, who in the beginning takes to the process of self-realization with faith but who later desists due to worldly-mindedness and thus does not attain perfection in mysticism?» (BG [6.37](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/6.37)) Lord Krsna tells him that «A transcendentalist engaged in auspicious activities does not meet with destruction either in this world or in the spiritual world; one who does good, My friend, is never overcome by evil.» (BG [6.38](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/6.38)) And the Krsna goes on to describe the various destinations of such transcendentalists.

The point is that there will always be that risk, but there will never be any loss for making the attempt. Krsna also says «In this endeavor there will never be any loss or diminution, and a little advancement on this path will protect one from the most dangerous type of fear.» (BG [2.41](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/2.41)) It's understood that your faith is not strong. That's why you're in the material world. But how will you ever develop it, if you don't place yourself in a more dependent position? We are always dependent, but our duty is to be dependent upon Krsna.

2\) Once Srila Prabhupada was asked a provocative question by a female newspaper reporter. She asked him, «Is it true that in your movement the women follow the orders of men?» Srila Prabhupada's concise reply was, «We all follow the orders of Krsna; both men and women!» When you really think about it, that's the complete answer. Of course, there may be so many socially relative perspectives of our movement, but we must try to bring ourselves up to the superior level of understanding. Ultimately, we're all servants of Krsna, but as long as there is any trace of identification with this body, there must be a set of guidelines to follow for our own purification. Our roles are defined according our psychological and physical natures, and if we follow those roles accordingly, we will advance. We all must be submissive to guru and Krsna, and then the society we take part in will facilitate our elevation towards Krsna consciousness; whether our body is male or female. Otherwise there will be exploitation; (also whether our body is male or female). Do you actually think that there's equality in the material world? Equality can only exist from Krsna's point of view. Krsna is God, and we are all His servants. And anyone who renders a little service to Him is a friend, is in Him, and He is a friend to them. So my humble appeal would be to seek the purity of purpose behind the instructions you get (also, whether they're coming from someone in a male or female's body), and if you find it, then why not follow? Krsna's aim is to not suppress anyone, and our aim is to coincide with His.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

April 27, 1993

Dear Bhaktin,

Hare Krsna. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I received your letter dated April 23, 1993, and carefully noted the contents.

First, I must say that I am definitely familiar with the dilemma you're in. Although the specifics in your case are tailored for you, according to your previous activities, the confusion to renounce obligations such as these or not, applies to everyone when the desire to serve Krsna begins to grow in the heart. As soon as one gets a little serious about Krsna consciousness, there will be a test of one's conviction— for Maya tests according to one's level of advancement.

However, you must decide whether or not you feel the conviction to take more fully to the process of devotional service. It will always seem as though there are some obligations, because that's the nature of the material world. Maya is always giving us some other obligation to attend to. Right now, for you, one such obligation is your debts. If you try to satisfy these obligations exclusively, you sense that you won't feel satisfied without being engaged in Krsna's service. I'm not suggesting that you should default on your debts, remain unemployed, and just live in the temple. But neither am I saying that you should adjust your life, primarily, just to fulfill these obligations either. You must find the right balance so that whatever obligation you feel towards becoming Krsna consciousness does not get compromised.

While writing this letter, I was reminded of a letter that I wrote to a Russian disciple of mine. She was living at home, and was caring for her disabled father who was completely dependent upon her. She wrote to me, asking what she should do. Since my reply seems applicable to your situation, although the «specifics» are obviously different as explained above, I thought I would share this letter with you:

This letter is in response to your question regarding your commitments to your father.

In your letter you have stated, «If I shall deprive him (your father) of all care and attention, and also the possibility to know something about Krsna, it will be bad.» Good and bad, are considerations of materialistic duality. We think that something is good, or something is bad, based upon our own imperfect standards. On the absolute platform Krsna is all-good, and whatever is done for the service of Krsna is also all-good, Absolute. Therefore your confusion is based upon whether or not your remaining at home to fulfill these obligations will be pleasing to Krsna.

Arjuna also found himself in a similar dilemma, but his situation was far more severe. He found himself in the midst of a battlefield, with his family, friends, and teachers all the opposing side, and was faced with the decision on whether or not he could kill them in battle. He was also thinking of good and bad, but it was based upon a mundane standard of morality. However, when the Bhagavad-gita was spoken by the Lord, and Arjuna heard and realized the highest standard of morality, he fought with full conviction that this was what Krsna wanted him to do.

In your own situation, because you are thinking that depriving your father of care and attention is bad, then you should probably continue to do give him this attention. I cannot tell you that you should leave your father. You must act upon your own convictions, just as Arjuna acted upon his, and if those convictions are not there, then you should pray to Krsna for them. The conviction to perform what is most pleasing to Krsna, and the ability to execute that conviction, can be developed by continuing to follow the most essential practices of devotional service. One must daily chant Hare Krsna, take only Krsna prasadam, read Srila Prabhupada's books, and hear about Krsna in the association of devotees.

In that sense, yes, it is possible to make spiritual progress and simultaneously live at home. But, one must make the above- mentioned spiritual duties the number one priority. Otherwise, the pressure from other obligations will distract us, forcing us to place our commitments to Krsna into a secondary position. The tension between worldly and spiritual obligations will always be there, until we fully surrender to Krsna. In the meantime, we must take help from Lord Krsna and the Vaisnavas.» So I hope that this little information will help to shed some light on your situation, and that you will become more resolute to be Krsna conscious in «all» situations.

In closing, I also want to say that there was no need to apologize for your letter. I sensed no disrespect whatsoever.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

May 6, 1993

Dear Bhakta,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you very much for your honest and sincere letter, dated Feb. 12, 1993. Although I received it along with some other mail in Mayapura, as you had expected, the demands placed upon me, both in India and subsequently in the former USSR, did not allow me the time to reply. Now that I'm back in North America, I'm taking extra time to gradually catch up.

I'm very pleased by the attitude you've expressed in this letter. You have the required sincerity to look forward to a bright future in Krsna consciousness. Ultimately, that is the most important ingredient. In one sense, it was not wrong to approach me in the way that you did. Asking to become one's disciple includes asking how to become qualified. The spiritual master's duty is to train his disciple, so that he may become qualified, not only for initiation, but for entering into an eternal relationship with Krsna.

It seems that the main problem you face at this point in your life is how to balance your social and familial commitments with your duties in Krsna consciousness. This is common; especially for those who take to the spiritual path from within the householder status. In your case, your problem is compounded by the fact that your wife is not completely supportive; although, as you say, she agrees— but unwillingly. However, whether within family life, or not within family life, the living entity will always be pulled by worldly obligations; if not family— then friends, society, the bodily demands, and the list could go on and on. The conditioned soul is tied into a network of obligations that will always pull him away from Krsna. If he wants to slacken the knots, he must take shelter of Krsna. It's not that the struggle will stop. It's always a struggle in the material world, but we can get help from guru and Krsna.

There are no clear definitions for how much time you should spend with your family, how much money is required to maintain them, or how much time to give for the temple. What is most important is to see how Krsna consciousness incorporates both worlds that you are feeling divided into. A Krsna conscious grhas- ta's duties include spending time with his family, earning money to support them, and doing the needful to earn an honest means of support. But because he also fulfills his spiritual duties (i.e. chanting Hare Krsna, hearing about Krsna in the association of devotees, reading Srila Prabhupada's books, etc.) he does not lose sight of the ultimate goal. He's dutiful, and does the needful for his family, but he does not become too attached. If too attached, he will get pulled by these obligations, and will lose the strength to resist.

Unfortunately, it seems as though your wife would find it difficult to share such an outlook as this with you. She's probably feeling threatened by your commitments to Krsna and cannot understand how it's in her best interest that she support them. In time, she could come to understand, and time is what you must be willing to give it. You must also be patient. Krsna knows what's inside your heart, and He sees the struggle that you are undergoing on His behalf. Just don't stop your endeavors to please Him, and He will surely remove all the obstacles on your path. I have no doubt that He will help you, and that He will help you fulfill your commitments to become qualified for initiation.

I'm hoping to visit before returning to the former USSR in July. At this point, my plans are still unclear, but I'll probably be there sometime in June. Maybe at that time I could be available to answer any new questions that may arise as a result of this letter.

Hoping the meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

In answer to the questions from your last letter:

I was just talking about steadiness in my lecture the other evening here in Kiev. I was describing how there are often so many influences upon us which affect our ability to be steady, and sometimes we falter, just like a young child who falters in his early attempts to learn how to walk. Srila Krsnadasa Kaviraja Goswami describes in the «Caitanya Caritamrta»\\ «The path is very difficult, I am blind, and my feet are slipping again and again. Therefore I am constantly praying to the saints to grant me the stick of their mercy for my support.» We must have the desire to take the assistance from the Vaisnavas, and sincerely accept it as «the stick of their mercy». We all need support from others. Nobody should try to overcome maya alone. Krsna will send help, and He can act through His devotees. If we learn how to develop such loving relations, it will help us gain strength when maya's tests become strong.

Yes, it is certainly possible to gain the understanding that we are always dependent upon the mercy of guru and Krsna. You must be patient. Such changes in our consciousness take place over time. Each time you make an effort to act in this mood of dependence, Krsna takes note of your effort, and gradually you begin to realize more and more just how you are dependent. As you endeavor, Krsna awards. Do not become discouraged.

We all need some goal in life. However, under the influence of maya we place importance on material goals. The more that we hear about Krsna, gradually the illusory covering is removed, and the impetus to make Krsna our ultimate goal becomes stronger and stronger.

Regarding the different relationships Krsna/guru/disciple, Srila Prabhupada has given the example that guru and Krsna are like the two rails that make up a track. The train cannot go without both rails. They must exist side by side. The spiritual master is Krsna's representative, and it is through his instructions that one learns about the process of surrender and the means by which we can approach Krsna. Without his instructions, we do not know how to serve Krsna. But we must also learn how to depend upon Krsna in all of our endeavors. The spiritual master's instructions are nondifferent than Krsna's instructions, but it is Krsna who must be remembered in all of our endeavors. The spiritual master is always teaching us how to depend on Krsna. So both personalities must be there.
