Srila Prabhupada very nicely summarized the perfect relationship that exists between the spiritual master and the disciple in one letter as follows: «Unless there is loving feeling, how is it possiblefor you to always make offerings to me? The spiritual master is always instructing his disciples and they in turn are always trying to serve their spiritual master. It is a reciprocal relationship of love.»

When I come to , I am planning to spend at least five days there, so we can certainly speak more on these subjects at that time.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health and happy in Krsna consciousness.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

September 25, 1993

Dear Bhaktin,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you very much for your letter, undated, wherein you have given a detailed account of some of your internal struggles.

Having just read your letter, I've found that it left me with a strong impression of the seriousness with which you are executing your devotional service. You are anxious to develop genuine devotional qualities, and such «anxiety» for Krsna is the only price we must pay if we want to obtain His mercy. Don't be disappointed when you see that the bad qualities don't immediately go away. Patience is also an essential quality, and you have every reason to believe that if you remain patient, your real desires for pure devotional service will be obtained.

When accepting the decision of the senior authorities, you should see it as «cooperation». It's not that you have to accept their instructions as something which requires «absolute» surrender. Absolute surrender comes from cultivating a relationship with your spiritual master and Krsna. As that faith becomes strengthened through the process of «hearing» from the spiritual master, the impetus to surrender will naturally develop as well. This is not something that comes cheaply. It takes determination and patience. And most important, in «absolute surrender» the element of free choice must be always preserved. You must «want» to surrender, knowing it to be the means for pleasing guru and Krsna. So, in one sense, you can surrender «absolute- ly» by «cooperating» with your authorities, which is one of the instructions given to you by your spiritual master. There will always be inconvenience, whether you cooperate with your authorities in Krsna consciousness, or, the «authorities» in the material world. In either place, you must cooperate with authorities. In the material world, it's forced cooperation, but in Krsna consciousness it's voluntary cooperation.

Just like a citizen, who voluntarily follows the laws of the state, he enjoys life as a free citizen. But a citizen who breaks the laws is forced to cooperate by going to prison. Voluntary cooperation is bhakti, and forced cooperation is karma. Just like a person who needs money, he's forced to cooperate with his employer, otherwise, he won't receive his salary. But a devotee voluntarily cooperates, and depends upon whatever Krsna provides him. Bhakti is so far superior, because Krsna becomes purchased by bhakti, and He always provides His surrendered devotee with whatever he needs to remain engaged in His service.

So try to learn to tolerate these inconveniences, and Krsna will surely help you. Even if you're given an instruction which you feel is unreasonable, unless you feel that it will completely jeopardize your spiritual life, you should try your best to cooperate. However, if it does put your spiritual life in jeopardy, then, obviously, you should bring it to a higher authority for clarification. For that, you must be the judge.

Regarding your question as to «when» devotional service begins, the first stages of «devotion» as given by Srila Rupa Goswami is when one voluntarily follows the instructions of the spiritual master, even if full devotion has not matured. This is «vaidhi bhakti», which is the first stage of devotion. By following the instructions of the spiritual master, even though your consciousness is not fully devoted, still, the spiritual master is accepting the offering, and he is offering the service to his guru— and it goes all the way up to Krsna. So that is when devotional service begins. If we continue following the rules and regulations with faith, gradually, the devotion becomes more and more pure.

I don't know why your authorities are disturbed by your schedule, unless, by following this schedule, you are disturbing others in the asram. This may certainly be possible. If, by waking up at 1:30 AM, and by chanting your japa, you wake many other devotees who need their sleep, then I could see why your authorities are disturbed. You would either have to figure out a way that you could wake up and chant without disturbing others, or you would have to follow the same schedule. Otherwise, if you strongly feel that it is helping you in your service, I do not see any reason why you could not follow this schedule, as long as you are fully attending the morning program, and that you're fully attentive.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health and happy in Krsna consciousness.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

January 26, 1994

Dear Bhaktin,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you for your informative letter dated 23 November, 1993.1 have carefully read it and noted the contents.

I'd also like to thank you for your efforts on the book distribution you did during the October marathon. Despite all the pressures, obligations, and difficulties that you're confronting you made this effort to distribute Srila Prabhupada's mercy to others. Do not be ashamed. It is the effort that was pleasing, and the results were of less importance. Twenty people got the opportunity to learn about Krsna who probably would not have gotten that opportunity had you not made that effort. Lord Caitanya will certainly take notice.

But you say that soon afterwards you lost your determination to go out on book distribution. It is difficult without devotee association, and therefore you should always try to go out with other devotees and not alone.

Regarding your family, studies, and the other attachments you've described as impediments to your progress in Krsna consciousness, there will always be obstacles that will have to be overcome. One must learn to overcome them, not by falsely renouncing, but by genuine attachment to Krsna. Do not be artificial Srila Prabhupada gives the example of the woman who always thinks of her paramour, but externally she's even more attentive to her household duties so that her husband does not suspect. In the same way, within, you must learn to develop your attachment to Krsna by always thinking about Him, and how to approach Him, but you must go on with these obligations unless or until Krsna makes it perfectly clear that you have no other choice.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health and happy in Krsna consciousness.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Please accept my blessings.

All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have your letter dated December 8, 1993, and have carefully noted the contents.

Krsna can do anything that He wants. That's the meaning of God. So yes, if Krsna wants, He can take away someone's devotional service even if they're attached to that service. But He does not take away someone's «devotion». Whatever devotion one gains through the performance of one's service will always remain. A devotee should be attached to «serving»; not to his «ser- vice». We're not editors, translators, pot-washers, presidents, GBC or any of these other designations. We're eternal servants of Krsna, and whatever duty that we perform is meant to help us come to this highest realization. Devotional service is eternal. The duties are temporary.

Of course, we must be careful, to not become indifferent towards our duties, by thinking that if it's all temporary and we shouldn't become attached, then what difference does it make if I put all of my energy into the duty or not. Such an attitude is due to a lack of devotion. One who is devoted is always thinking how he can perform his duty nicely for the pleasure of guru and Krsna. And we must be prepared to always accept Krsna's will; no matter what changes He brings upon us.

In this regard, I must inquire about these pains in your heart. What exactly are you talking about, and what are you doing about it? Chest pains are not something that should just be overlooked as something insignificant. Don't be foolish, and go to a doctor. You asked me what you should do. So that's what you should do. Of course, you should also make every effort to fix your mind on Krsna. «Krsna is the origin of Lord Visnu. He must always be remembered and never forgotten at any time. All the rules and prohibitions which are given in the sastra are meant to be the servants of these two principles.» (Padma Purana) That instruction goes without fail. But you also must make an effort to correct something within the body if there is actually something wrong. That body is what Krsna has given you for making progress in this life.

Regarding the translation of Srila Prabhupada's books, yes there is certainly a risk that a translation of Srila Prabhupada's books, if improperly done, could influence the pure message that Srila Prabhupada has given. But that's from those who perform unauthorized translations. For those who engage in authorized work, by guru and Krsna, and for those who sincerely try to translate in service to guru and Krsna, then Krsna accepts the essence of that service and overlooks the shortcomings. Another name for Krsna is bhava-grahijanardana because He takes only the essence of a devotee's attitude.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada. I have your letter dated December 8, 1993, and have noted the contents.

Yes, this is an example of the «being attached to the results» syndrome. From your description, I see no reason why you should feel so distressed by your efforts in translating Srila Prabhupada's books. You say that you don't know the philosophy enough to translate them, but on the other hand Krsna is giving you the ideal opportunity to learn the philosophy by having to go through it in such detail.

But the main point is that you must leave the decision up to your authorities as to whether or not you are spoiling Srila Prabhupada's books. You may think that you're spoiling them, but others may find that your translation is satisfactory enough for the level of translation work that you're doing. My understanding is that others will check your translation and if it is as «spoiled» as you say, then they would tell you that it is so. So you should not be so disturbed by that. And even if it was spoiled (from the external point of view), there would be absolutely no loss for you. The fact is, that I'm pleased by the sincere efforts. I would never think that you've spoiled Srila Prabhupada's books. Don't let your mind trick you.

When Srila Prabhupada was publishing Bhagavad-gita, he had very inexperienced devotees doing the editing work. From the external point of view there were many errors in Srila Prabhupada's earlier editions of Bhagavad-gita, but Srila Prabhupada accepted the attitude of service and offered that to Krsna. Despite the errors, thousands of people became devotees by reading these books. Thus is the power of pure devotion to Krsna.

I'm also very happy to hear that you're distributing books for two days every two weeks. I highly recommend that you continue this program and that you take advantage of the opportunity to be reminded of your connection to Srila Prabhupada's book distribution mission by personally experiencing it yourself. Moreover, you should always be attentive to maintaining a strong sadhana of rising early, attending mangala arati, and chanting good attentive rounds during brahma muhurta. This will also keep you strong and fit for a lifetime (and more) of devotion to Krsna.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

February 10, 1994

Dear Bhaktin,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you very much for your letter (undated). I have carefully noted the contents.

Excelling in school and advancing in Krsna consciousness do not have to be contradictory to one another. In one sense, however, I do appreciate your statements about the uselessness of mundane education when the pursuit of pure love of Krsna is one's goal in life. I have to appreciate them, because ultimately it's true. Because you are maturing in Krsna consciousness you are understanding these points. But maturity in Krsna consciousness also includes the ability to discriminate between real renunciation and artificial renunciation. A really renounced person can love and serve Krsna in any condition of life— even in school, surrounded by non-devotees and meateaters. Krsna has placed you there, in school, at this point in your life. Now you must learn how to take the best advantage of His arrangement. Just because it's so hard being in school is not sufficient grounds to give it up. Wherever you go in this material world, it's going to be hard. It's hard living in the temple, too. But Krsna helps to make it easier as you learn to depend upon Him in every situation. Rather than artificially adjusting the situation, you must adjust your consciousness.

The knowledge you're gaining in school can be used in Krsna's service. If you study with this view in mind, and when given the opportunity to choose your subjects, you choose those which could be most directly related to service to Krsna, there will never be any loss or diminution. But beyond all that knowledge, lies the superior knowledge of Krsna. This knowledge is revealed within the heart by the mercy of the Lord. The more that you think of Him, hear about Him, pray to Him, worship Him, the more He will reveal this knowledge to you from within. Real renunciation will grow, even while you remain in school. Then Krsna will perfectly arrange everything required for your further progress in His devotional service. You must have firm faith that He knows what is best and that He will arrange it.

As an additional support to this I will include the following letter that Srila Prabhupada wrote to one Indian boy 20 years ago, when he was asked similar questions that you asked to me.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

December, 1974

Southall, England

My dear Durgesh, Please accept my blessings. I am in due receipt of your letter dated Dec. 12, 1974 and have noted the contents carefully. Thank you very much for your sentiments. It appears that you have understood our philosophy very nicely, and that is a great boon for someone who is as young as yourself. The perfection of human existence is to somehow or another develop Krsna Consciousness and the sooner that one can develop his Krsna Consciousness the better. Not that I will wait until I become old man, then I will become spiritually advanced. No, it is better if one starts immediately. So there is no question of your discontinuing your efforts in Krsna Consciousness. By all means you should continue practicing Krsna Consciousness everyday. But that does not necessarily mean that you have to move into the Temple immediately. It is also important to get some education. So for some years you should remain studying in school. But on the weekends with your parents permission you can go to the Temple and stay there but do not fall behind in your school work. And everyday you should follow the Krsna Conscious principles that our devotees in our temples follow which means rising early, chanting 16 rounds, eating only Krsna prasadam, no meat, fish or eggs, no illicit sex life, no gambling and no intoxication. This is the process. So follow it strictly and at the same time learn some practical knowledge from your school studies. And then later it may be more appropriate for you to join our temple as a full time brahmachari. But in all circumstances always chant Hare Krsna. That is a must. In the Bhagavad-gita it says; tesam satata-yuktanam, bhajatam priti-purvakam, dadami buddhi-yogam tam, yena mam upayanti te, Ch.10, text 10. To those who are constantly devoted and worship Me with love, I give the understanding by which they can come to Me. So do not be in distress. Simply try to engage yourself constantly in devotional service and develop your love for Krsna. And in this way Krsna will give you all facilities for coming to Him. I hope this meets you in good health.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Regarding your preaching in school, But how can I say that it's bad to preach? No, it's not bad. But you should be discreet. Don't be confrontational. Just be a devotee, and by so doing, you'll see that Krsna will make all kinds of arrangements for you to tell others about Him.

In fact many years ago, I used to regularly preach in Amherst where there are many colleges and universities. At one school, Hampshire College, I would go as a devotee, and teach the students how to cook vegetarian food. I was not confrontational at all. I just showed them how to cook, served them a small feast, and gradually, maybe even after many weeks of doing this, the students would start asking me questions about Krsna consciousness. In this way, their interests started to grow. Many people became devotees this way.

Regarding your chanting, you simply must make the effort to bring your mind back every time you realize that it's wandering. Don't become discouraged. It's understood that there will be offenses committed in the early stages of chanting the holy names. But, gradually, if one simply continues to chant, under all circumstances, one will become free from these offenses.

Hoping this meets you in good health and happy in Krsna consciousness.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

April 3, 1994

Dear Bhaktin,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have your letter dated 08.02.94, and have noted the contents.

You are right in your analysis that my instructions can be received through your godsisters and godbrothers. I expect my instructions to be passed on in this way. Otherwise, how would it be possible to instruct everyone that approaches me? Just like Raghunath das Goswami. He approached Caitanya Mahaprabhu for instruction, but Lord Caitanya entrusted him under the care of Svarupa Damodar Goswami. Lord Caitanya told him that all the necessary details could be learned from Svarupa Damodar Goswami. I'm not saying that devi dasi can give you all necessary details for devotional service, but certainly as your senior godsister, she can help you. You should definitely take it. If my disciples cannot instruct others how to make progress in devotional service, then what kind of spiritual master am I? Is there any purpose for accepting disciples if one's disciples are not capable of teaching what they've learned and have put into practice?

Do not worry about your lack of qualification as a gurukula teacher. Qualification will come if you sincerely take up the instruction. All necessary qualifications for devotional service are contained in the instructions. We should never think of ourselves as elevated devotees. As soon as we think, «Oh just see how elevated I am», then we should know that we're not so elevated. But if we think ourselves as unqualified, then we'll always look to improve by taking good association. So just continue with this service, unless you hear otherwise from either your authorities or myself.

It's becoming increasingly difficult for me to maintain correspondence with so many devotees, along with all my other duties. Please bear this in mind. I do not want you to think that you cannot write to me if there's actually a need. But you should first try to find answers by other means, such as from my lectures, other letters, your authorities, or senior godsisters. However, you can always write informational letters, just to let me know how you are doing. I'm always happy to receive letters like that.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

April 6, 1994

Dear devi dasi,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada

I have your letter which was forwarded to me in India by Mahasakti dasa. I would have liked to have replied to your letter then, but I wanted to speak with Prabhu, to hear what he had to say about the matter. I was also extremely busy with GBC meetings when your letter arrived. When Prabhu was here in Moscow last week, I asked him to please explain everything to me. So now I will answer.

There are two levels by which we can approach this problem— the practical level and the superior level of Krsna consciousness. On the practical side, I have no intentions of reducing this explanation to the level of who was wrong and who was right. Solutions are not found on that level. If I felt that you had been grossly mishandled, I would say something. But, from both your description and the explanation given to me by Prabhu, I do not get the impression that you were grossly abused. I'm sensitive to your concerns, in that it would not be proper to take away somebody's service without replacing it with another. You must have some service. But I do not get the impression that that happened either. There was just a redefinition of your service, which may have been temporary or not. You were emotionally unprepared for it, and reacted in the way that you did.

The point is that we must try to become attached to «serving» Krsna, and should be careful that we do not become so conditional as to demand «how» we must serve. Just today, I was telling one story to a devotee here in Moscow about an experience I had in 1977.I was the sankirtan leader in Boston, and was also accustomed to regularly going out and distributing books. I was attached to the service, especially because I felt safe and confident that it was pleasing to Srila Prabhupada. I was then encouraged by the GBC to take charge of a center for training new book distributors in our region. I enthusiastically accepted this service because I knew that I would still be able to distribute books, but would increase my service by training others as well. However, unknown to me at the time, the temple president of Boston was expressing a strong desire to the GBC to engage me in a completely different service for preaching to make new devotees and for training new devotees in the basics of Krsna consciousness. The GBC had agreed that it was a very important service that was needed. When I finally heard about this several months later, I was thinking, «Why is this happening? I already have a nice service, I'm enlivened by this service, and everyone else seems to appreciate my service.» I couldn't understand why the temple president wanted to do this to me, but I just started thinking over and over again in my mind that, «I'm just a servant of guru and Krsna.» The new service that I was asked to do was certainly very important, and somebody needed to do it.

So my service was changed, I was in an entirely different situation, and didn't know the first thing to do with my new service. But I clearly remember, that because I accepted this service as coming from Krsna, that I was able to remain enthusiastic. Although it was a struggle for many months, I kept reminding myself that «I'm just a servant», and soon Krsna sent so many new devotees for me to preach to and to engage.

I think that you understand the point that I'm trying to make. Krsna makes these arrangements to help us increase our realization of «who» we actually are. We're servants of Krsna, and if we want to realize our eternal identity we must act on that platform. There is never any loss for serving Krsna in this way only eternal gain.

In fact, just about 3 months ago, when I was visiting Boston, one person came to the temple for SB class and for prasadam. When we sat down for prasadam, the person said to me, «Do you remember me?» «Do you remember how you preached to me here in this temple back in the late "70"s?» I looked at him, and said, «Sure I remember you. You were Bhakta Dave.» Then he went on and on for about 15 minutes saying how he could never forget all the instructions I had given him about chanting Hare Krsna. He pulled out some japa beads and said that he'd never given up chanting, and in fact just recently, he was thinking about all those instructions, and he decided to start chanting 16 rounds a day. This person was one of the first persons that I preached to when I was brought back to Boston to take up that new service back in 1977. We simply cannot fully comprehend why Krsna does what He does, but we should always be prepared to serve Him in any way that He wants.

So, I've tried to emphasize the Krsna conscious level of approaching this problem. I trust that it will help you to deal with this matter, and that it will help you to deal with similar matters in the future.

I heard from Prabhu that you were agreeable to use more of your time for preaching and book distribution, and that you were no longer so disturbed about these changes in your service. Is this true? If so, then certainly this is very enlivening for me to hear. There is unlimited service in preaching and giving Krsna consciousness to others. I would definitely be pleased by this service.

If everything goes according to plan, I will be in at the end of this month. If necessary, you can provide me with more information at that time. In the meantime, please go on and be an enthusiastic servant of guru and Krsna, and make your life successful and happy.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health.

I am, Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

April 19, 1994

Dear dasa,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I would have preferred to discuss the following matter with you personally when I was in , however time did not permit me. I'm therefore left with no alternative but to write to you, since I don't expect to be back in until much later on in the year.

… dasa, the head -pujari, came to meet with me a few hours before I left . One of the topics he wished to discuss with me concerned your attitude towards cooking and preparing the plates for the Deities. The previous day he had brought me into the pujari room to show me the arrangement you had made on the Lord's plates, so that I could see for myself one of the things that he was talking about. I did not have the opportunity to taste the maha-prasadam, but he, and others, also complained that you often create concocted recipes that come out tasting very unpalatable. dasa claims that when he tries to appeal to you to change, you reply that it's your own individual expression of devotion, and that he has no right to complain or criticize the work that you're doing.

Anyway, one main reason why I would have preferred to have met with you, is because it would have been better if I could have heard from you first. As I said, the only evidence that I have of his complaints, was the arrangement that you had made on the Lord's plates. Therefore, all I can say is that if the following applies, then you should take heed of these instructions and correct yourself.

Srila Prabhupada did give devotees the opportunity to express creativity when cooking for the Deities. For example he wrote the following in one letter:

«So far as explaining my letter to Kris, I have listed the ingredients which may use for preparing prasadam but these various varieties of foodstuffs may be prepared either in the given recipes or if you like you may invent nice new formulas for offering. THE IMPORTANT THING IS THAT YOUR PREPARATIONS BE PALATABLE FOR LORD KRISHNA and that the ingredients be within the groups of ingredients already listed.»

Srila Prabhupada had previously given a large group of ingredients which are the primary ingredients we use when we're cooking, and they are naturally based upon their availability. Those ingredients were foodstuffs in the modes of goodness — wheat, rice, pulse (beans, peas), sugar, honey, butter and all milk preparations, vegetables, flowers, fruits, grains. But if you'll notice, he stressed what the most important thing was— that the preparations MUST be palatable. From the many complaints I received, it seems that your creativity has gotten out of control, and your ability to consistently create palatable preparations has not been sufficiently developed. Don't experiment when it comes to cooking for the Lord. You should follow clearly defined recipes, and you may also develop your own recipes that are recognized, not only by you, but by other authorities such as the temple president or the head -pujari, as PALATABLE. Then stick to those recipes. Do not continuously concoct! Please do not forget that you are cooking for the Lord.

Regarding the arrangement of food on the Lord's plate is concerned, the first thing that I noticed on the plate was that your slices of lemon were strewn in different places all over the plate. This is not proper. The lemon should be evenly sliced, and placed within a bowl. As much as possible, every preparation should be placed in its own bowl or plate. And when offering any other type of fruit, the fruit should always be peeled. But you must peel fruits such as apples or pears just before you bring the plates to be offered. These types of fruits should not be peeled and then left to sit, otherwise they turn brown quickly. Cooking for the Lord takes organization. You should not be rushed, therefore you should always arrange your schedule in such a way so that you can do everything nicely.

I believe I've emphasized these points enough during our question and answer session on Sunday morning, and I also stressed these points during the Istaghosti I previously gave on Deity worship. Please try to remember these points.

Other than these points, I want to say that I'm pleased that you are committed to serving Their Lordships. You are now one of the older devotees in the temple, and you should be thinking how you can set a good example for the newer devotees. Yad yad acarati sresthas, tad tad evetaro janah (Whatever actions are performed by great men, then common men will follow in their footsteps. And whatever examples they set by their exemplary acts, all the world pursues.) (BG [3.21](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/3.21)) That is the responsibility of the senior devotees. When the newer devotees see that you keep high standards for serving Their Lordships, then everything comes up in the temple. This is a big responsibility and you should take this responsibility seriously.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health.

I am, Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

July 3, 1994

Dear devi dasi,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have your letter dated May 31, 1994, which was hand-delivered to me by Bhaktin Tanya in Moscow. I have carefully noted the contents.

I'm naturally anxious to hear that you're experiencing a loss of enthusiasm for preaching Krsna consciousness. But you should not feel so self-condemning. Loss of enthusiasm may come in due course of time, just as in due course of time the winter will come, although we would always like it to be summer. Can you stop the appearance of winter? It will come, and you simply have to tolerate all the cold and inconveniences that come with it. It is beyond your control. Similarly, Krsna says that we must tolerate these changes from happiness to distress without being disturbed. So just as summer comes after winter goes away, similarly your enthusiasm for preaching will also return, as long as you remain steady in the basic practices that I've always emphasized to you.

Now I'm hearing that you're thinking that you require a change of climate for the improvement of your health. However, I don't understand why you're saying that now; right at the beginning of summer. Although your consciousness has been compared to the winter, nonetheless the actual season in is summer. What is the necessity of going to a warmer climate during the summer? This leads me to think that you are not happy in the association of the devotees in , and you're just looking for some change. Sometimes a change is required, but it should not be a whimsical change. You should consult with the senior devotees there, and if becomes necessary to adjust your situation, then do so only under their guidance.

In answer to your questions:

1\) Surrender means to give up our own personal interests to ultimately serve the desire of the Lord. Under the spell of illusion we hold on to our false conceptions of shelter, trying to choose a comfortable situation that will not put our attachments in jeopardy. We're thinking, «I don't want to give up my attachments, so let me find a situation where I can keep them and still make advancements But Krsna knows better. He knows what we need so that we can continue to make progress. The fact is that He knows much better than we do, and therefore sometimes He tries to lovingly pull us along by showing us the attachments that we need to give up — not artificially — but by developing our attachments to Him through devotional service.

Externally your situations will always change. Change is something that you will not be able to stop as long as you're here in the material world. Your body is changing, your consciousness is changing— everything around you is changing. Even if you have a service that you feel you can fully dedicate yourself to, you will experience changes. What must not change is your desire to serve Krsna. This is your only real protection against the constant changes of the material world. That's why we are always emphasizing that what is most important is that you continue to hear about Krsna so that you do not lose this desire. That is your surrender!! Surrender means to take shelter in Krsna under ALL circumstances— wherever He puts you, whatever service He gives you, and no matter how much difficulty He places you in. Krsna can, and will, give you shelter, if you seek it from Him. There is really no shelter in these foolish attachments. They will all be ripped away in due course of time.

Of course, this is not a reason for your authorities to whimsically change your service, but in your case, it seems that it is Krsna who is forcing this change. What can you do to prevent it? You want to distribute books, but your body is not fit for that service right now— and it seems that neither is your consciousness. Stop feeling guilty because you don't have the body nor determination to go out and distribute books. I've given you other service to do. Please take my previous instructions for practical service seriously. They were not given to you whimsically. And above all, please understand that «surrender» to Krsna means that you must always take shelter in Him. Surrender to His holy name. Surrender to hearing His glories. Surrender to inquiring about Him and telling others whatever you know about Him.

He wants to see every one of His devotees take full shelter of Him in this way. He is very inclined towards His devotees. This is our surrender! Lord Brahma explains that a devotee can conquer Krsna by developing this desire to hear about Him.

2\) Even if one engages in devotional service out of fear of material existence, although it is not pure devotional service, still one can make progress. Moreover, if you actually «FEAR» material existence, then you won't put yourself in a situation where maya can easily grab you. This means that you will keep yourself in the association of devotees.

3\) In regards to your question about pessimism it made me think about one very nice letter from Srila Prabhupada. It was written in 1972, and is as follows:

«...It is not so much that because there may be some faults in our godbrothers and godsisters, or because there may be some mismanagement or lack of cooperation, that this is due to being impersonalists, no. It is the nature of the living condition to always have some fault. Even in the Spiritual World there is some fault and envy—sometimes the Gopis will quarrel over Krishna's favor, and once Krishna was so much attracted to Radharani that by mistake he tried to milk the bull instead of the cow, and sometimes when the Gopis used to put on their dress and make-up for seeing Krishna, they would be too much hasty and smear kumkum and mascara in the wrong places and their ornaments and dresses would appear as if small children had been trying to dress themselves and they were not very expert, like that. There are so many examples. But it is not the same as material fault or material envy, it is transcendental because it is all based on Krishna. Sometimes when one Gopi would serve Krishna very nicely, the others would say, Oh, she has done so nicely, now let me do better for pleasing Krishna. That is envy, but it is transcendental, without malice. So we shall not expect that anywhere there is any Utopia. Rather, that is impersonalism. People should not expect that even in the Krishna Consciousness Society there will be Utopia. Because devotees are persons, therefore there will always be some lacking—but the difference is that their lacking, because they have given up everything to serve Krishna—money, jobs, reputation, wealth, big educations, everything—their lackings have become transcendental because, despite everything they may do, their topmost intention is to serve Krishna. "One who is engaged in devotional service, despite the most abominable action, is to be considered saintly because he is rightly situated." The devotees of Krishna are the most exalted persons on this planet, better than kings, all of them, so we should always remember that and, like the bumblebee, always look for the nectar or the best qualities of a person. Not like the Utopians, who are like the flies who always go to the open sores or find the faults in a person, and because they cannot find any utopia, or because they cannot find anyone without faults, they want to become void, merge, nothing—they think that is utopia, to become void of personality. So if there is sometimes slight disagreements between devotees, it is not due to impersonalism, but it is because they are persons, and such disagreements should not be taken very seriously. THE DEVOTEE IS ALWAYS PESSIMISTIC ABOUT THE MATERIAL WORLD, BUT HE IS VERY OPTIMISTIC ABOUT THE SPIRITUAL LIFE; so in this way, you should consider that anyone engaged in Krishna's service is always the best person...»

In this letter Srila Prabhupada clearly explains that it is good to be pessimistic about the material world. But you should NOT be pessimistic about devotional service. Such pessimism is due to lack of faith. If you want to increase your faith (which you should) then you must follow the process. Prabhupada explains: « The faith increases in Krishna Consciousness by our voluntary increase of serving Krishna. Serving Krishna is unlimited and he can accept our unlimited service, and award us unlimited transcendental pleasure in the unlimited service of the Lord.»

4\) I'm not quite sure what Bhaktivaibhava Maharaja said, but from what you said it seems that he was explaining that we must develop genuine relationships amongst devotees. Devotees must learn how to express their love and concern for one another, and should not become so mechanical that they don't care about anyone except themselves. Beyond that, there are spiritual emotions that one cultivates on advanced levels of spontaneous devotional service in direct relation to the Lord, but such emotions cannot be cultivated artificially. They manifest only when one is advanced enough in Krsna consciousness.

5\) With regards to your parents, the best thing to do is to encourage them to meet with senior devotees. Maybe the next time dasa visits the area where your parents are living, you can arrange to have your parents meet with him and you can go there at the same time. Also, I would be happy to meet with your parents the next time I'm in Ukraine. All you have to do is ask them if they would like to meet, and we can set up a meeting and you can also be there at the same time.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health.

I remain, Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

July 23, 1994

Dear Bhaktin,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have your letter (undated) and have noted the contents.

You ask what to do to help the situation you're in with your friends in school. The best protection against bad association is good association. Therefore you have to build a good friendship with devotees. It's natural that you may prefer to associate with some devotees over others, so you should not feel bad about being selective. But there should be at least one older female devotee that you associate with or communicate with regularly, who can «watch over» you, to make sure that you don't drift into too much bad association. There is always that danger.

You should also try to always behave as a devotee should behave, and to not be afraid about being seen as a devotee. This will help you to remain strong, and to resist temptations that you will be subjected to.

And, lastly, you should continue to pray to Krsna to protect you. The best way to pray for protection is to always chant as much as possible. In that respect, I'm very glad to hear that you are chanting 10 rounds a day. Please chant them with great care and attention. Do not become slack. Your chanting is actually a prayer, and you should be conscious that you are petitioning the Lord to be always engaged in His loving devotional service.

You ask what else you should do to advance in becoming a devotee. This question is very nice, because you're thinking that you want to become a devotee, and you're not proud of being a devotee. Actually, however, you ARE a devotee, but you want to become a better devotee. Krsna will help you become devoted the more you try to remember Him in your efforts to serve the instructions coming from His representatives. Srila Prabhupada always used to say if you take one step to Krsna, He takes 100 steps to you.

Also, you should be very conscious of what you are eating, especially when you are away from home. You should make sure that you offer everything before you eat it, and don't offer foods that should not be offered to Krsna. If you haven't already done this, then you should make sure you find out what foods can be offered, and what foods can never be offered.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

May 27, 1994

Dear Bhaktin,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

During my last visit to , I requested Prabhu togive me a report on each one of my disciples and aspiring disciples. He informed me that although you were dutifully performing your prescribed duties, he was concerned about your attitude towards any kind of authority. My time in was very limited, as you probably noticed. So rather than speaking to you about this while I was there, I chose to write this brief letter to express my concern about this type of behavior.

I become particularly concerned when I hear that younger, less experienced devotees such as yourself, develop a strong resistance to any form of guidance from the senior, more experienced devotees. Prabhu has indicated that you have this tendency. Hearing this, causes me to become even less inclined to allow you to return to someday. How can you lead if you don't know how to follow?

By bringing this to your attention, I'm hoping that you can recognize that you must accept some form of discipline or correction. Otherwise, what does it mean to become a disciple? A disciple is one who accepts discipline. And discipline is imposed by authority, and not according to whim. If you are approaching me as an authority in your life, then you should know that I generally request younger, less experienced devotees such as yourself to look up to a more experienced devotee for guidance and for being justifiably corrected. Without humility, it is very difficult to make progress in the devotional path. If you feel that no such person is there for you to look up to in , then I would have to say that you definitely have a problem with humility.

Please, therefore, do not just make an external show of submission. Try to go deeper, and understand that these instructions are given for your interest and not necessarily for the interest of your authorities. You will become more qualified in spiritual life by developing humility, and thus hopefully, you will someday become qualified to properly represent authority in the lives of those who will be less experienced than you. Without such a qualification, it will remain too difficult for me to ever sanction your desired return to to lead the younger women devotees.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health and happily engaged in devotional service.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

June 29, 1994

Dear d.d.,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada. I have your letter, which was translated and forwarded to me by Mahasakti dasa, and have noted the contents.

It is proper to become attached to the association of devotees, even if they are not completely freed from attachment. But, as you know, there is a big difference between material attachment and spiritual attachment. It is possible to be materially attached to a devotee, or in other words, being attached to them for fulfilling our own impure motivations. Such attachments will lead us away from Krsna. However, if we become attached to those devotees who help us to increase our attachment to guru and Krsna, and who help us to forget our attachments to mundane friendship, family and society, then such attachment is healthy.

Regarding the answer about Madhavendra Puri, his desire to taste the sweet rice was definitely not a material desire. He was simply thinking about tasting it so that he could make a similar preparation for His worshipful Deity Gopal. In fact, the whole pastime was meant to illustrate just how pure Madhavendra Puri's desire was, that the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Himself, stole that sweet rice for Madhavendra Puri, so that he could have his desire fulfilled.

Regarding your questions about thinking about the Lord, certainly we should think about the descriptions and instructions that have been handed down to us from authoritative sources. This also includes making plans for the service of the Lord. The point is that before thinking how to do our service better, and before making plans to do our service, FIRST we must meditate upon and have firm faith in the actual instruction which has been handed down. Then we should see how our plans, and our thoughts are fully connected to the instructions handed down to us. It is the duty of the disciple to expand upon the instructions given to him by his spiritual master. Such plan- making must be there, otherwise, we will simply think about fulfilling our own desires. Srila Prabhupada perfectly describes this in the following analogy from a Srimad Bhagavatam purport to verse 39, Chapter 22, Canto 4:

«...One should take shelter of the lotus feet of the Lord instead of trying unsuccessfully to stop desiresfor material enjoyment. As long as one is unable to stop the desire for material enjoyment, there is no possibility of becoming liberated from the entanglement of material existence. It may be argued that the waves of a river are incessantly flowing and that they cannot be stopped, but the waves of the river flow toward the sea. When the tide comes over the river, it overwhelms the flowing of the river, and the river itself becomes overflooded, and the waves from the sea become more prominent than the waves from the river. Similarly, a devotee with intelligence plans so many things for the service of the Lord in Krishna consciousness that stagnant material desires become overflooded by the desire to serve the Lord...»

Unfortunately, listening to criticism of devotees is one of the unpalatable duties of a leader. We have to be willing to listen to criticism if such criticism is factual, and the fault which is being pointed out is a genuine cause of distress for other devotees. Of course, there's a difference between genuine criticism and just blatant faultfinding. More often than not, devotees are just indulging in faultfinding, and when that is obvious you should try to correct the faultfinder by straightforward preaching. You should help that devotee to see that her propensity for criticizing is a defect in her own character. But you have to do it very tactfully and with a genuine concern to help that devotee.

On the other hand when many devotees bring forward a criticism about one particular devotee, then you must be willing to take into consideration that maybe the accused devotee is actually doing something which needs to be corrected, and if necessary, you should bring the matter to a higher authority. However, that does not mean that you should allow everyone to feel that they are right to start a campaign against another devotee. Each devotee with a complaint should try to learn something from their experience with the accused devotee. Everyone must learn tolerance and humility, and sometimes Krsna puts His devotee into a situation in which he can develop these qualities. Every devotee should learn to see that Krsna is teaching him how to become a better devotee.

If a devotee wants to live in the asrama, she should first show her interest in Krsna consciousness by regular attendance of lectures over a period of time. Then, if she is interested in moving into the asrama she should be made aware of everything that will be expected of her, before she moves in the asrama, and she should agree to follow the standard that is expected of anyone who lives in the asrama. There should be no reason for her to say after she moves in that she wasn't aware of what was expected of her. That is your duty to make her aware. You shouldn't try to scare people away. But you should at least check to make sure that they are really sincere about becoming Krsna conscious, and not that they're just moving into the asrama because they have no other place to live, or that they have nothing else to do. Our asramas are not places for lazy and irresponsible people. We should try to create such an enlivened atmosphere centered around preaching, so that even those who may be a little bit lazy will become inspired to become more fully engaged.

Rather than being forced to ask people to leave the asrama, first we should be careful who we let into the asrama, so that we don't have to ask people to leave. Once somebody comes into the association of devotees, the devotees should try to take it as their responsibility to raise that person's consciousness, so that they don't fall back into materialistic life. That should be a preacher's meditation. However, sometimes, there may be somebody who is living in the asrama who continues to have a negative effect on the Krsna consciousness of everyone else in the asrama, despite many sincere attempts to correct the behavior of that person. This should be the criteria for asking someone to leave. However, even at that point, the person should be helped to understand that leaving the asrama doesn't mean that one has to leave Krsna consciousness. Asrama life is just not possible for everyone. Sometimes, a devotee can advance better living outside the asrama. But by coming to hear and serve in the association of devotees as much as possible, they can gradually make progress.

The subtle body is a storehouse of all material desires. At night when one is sleeping, although the gross body is inactive, the subtle body is active, and therefore one's desires can be fulfilled on the subtle platform without the same reactions as willfully experiencing it on the gross platform. Naturally a Krsna conscious person finds such desires distasteful on both the gross and subtle platform, and therefore wants to become free from both the gross and subtle forms of enjoyment. As the living entity becomes more purified by devotional service, even the subtle desires will become purified. On a practical level, to help avoid such experiences, before taking rest you should read a little from Krsna book, and try to fix your mind upon Krsna for protection.

You can help the devotees who are dependent upon you for instruction to become steady in their acceptance of a spiritual master, by preaching about the qualifications of a genuine spiritual master. Every devotee should learn what the actual qualifications of a bona fide spiritual master are. But as I mentioned in my letter to Bhaktin, they should not feel pressured into accepting someone too prematurely. There should first be some purification, so that there is some basis for recognizing who is guru, from someone who one may feel some fleeting sentiment.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health, and with increasing determination in devotional service.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear dasa,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

In regards to your question about playing guitar, the first thing I would like to say is that I am happy that you are asking for my opinion. The fact that you have asked, indicates that you understand that it could possibly be something that I would object to, so rather than taking that risk you have inquired first. This is proper.

I have always maintained the same opinion about guitar playing and the dovetailing of mundane music in Krsna consciousness. With the exception of a few extremely rare cases such as participating in the occasional performance of the Gauranga Bhajan Band, or other such group of very advanced devotees who have the ability to always maintain a clear understanding of «yuk- ta vairagya», I cannot think of any way a bramacari disciple of mine could satisfy me by playing guitar and modem music. In other words, I see no compatibility, whatsoever, between bramacari life and playing guitar. In fact, just a few weeks ago, in Ekaterinburg, an aspiring disciple of mine, also a bramachari, asked me the following question, «What is the best way to dovetail my inclination for writing and playing music with the Cintamani Band?» My answer was, «By completely forgetting about it!» He did not expect such an answer from me, but then I went on to explain how accepting initiation means breaking the attachments to one's previous attachments in material life. Narada Muni made a similar request to Mrgari the hunter, by establishing the condition for accepting Mrgari as a disciple. He said that Mrgari would first have to break the bow which he had previously used for hunting animals.

I can say firmly that this is my opinion for all of my disciples. If there are any disciples of mine who are indulging in guitar playing and modern music, then you should know that it's only because they never bother to asked me as you did.

Another exception which could be acceptable to me is if one is a householder and requires to earn an honest means of livelihood. Then I could accept guitar playing and presenting songs about Krsna as an honest means to earn an income for a householder. Certainly it's better if one can earn a means of livelihood by that means, then to have to work in a factory or other similarly hellish places infected with ugrakarma.

This is and always has been my opinion on this matter, and it is my sincere hope that you will remain firm in your commitment to sankirtan, which is always the best engagement for a bramacari in Krsna consciousness.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

I am always, Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

November 15, 1994

Dear Bhakta,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada. In our meeting in it was decided that you should go to… where you are to work under the direction of Prabhu— sometimes staying in and sometimes traveling with him. When this decision was made, I was warned by your authorities that you have a tendency to be overly independent, sometimes to the point of being uncooperative. They claim that you are accustomed to a somewhat self-styled program for Krsna consciousness. Having heard these things about you, I thought that I should tell you that this decision to send you to was my own, primarily because I felt that as a new devotee you must learn to work cooperatively under an authority. In fact, it is primarily for this reason that other changes have been undertaken in , so that other bramacaris such as yourself would be placed under proper authority and this self-styled formula for Krsna consciousness would not spread any more than it has already spread. I am not very encouraged when I see young, uninitiated devotees, simply doing whatever they want and calling it devotional service. This is a very low standard, and it must be improved.

I have placed you under the direction of Prabhu, and I am expecting that you will give him the proper respect that he deserves having been in a leading position for so many years in... He is a senior and respected devotee, and I've told him that he is to act as your authority, and that he should see that you are suitably engaged in some responsible service. I am expecting you to assist him in all respects, and I am looking forward to some encouraging reports from Prabhu.

Please accept this arrangement in the proper consciousness, and see it as an opportunity to advance in Krsna consciousness which is the ultimate objective of all sincere devotees who take part in this movement.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health and happily engaged in devotional service.

I am, Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

December 1, 1994

Dear Bhaktin,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you very much for your letter dated October 3, 1994. I received it a little while back when a devotee from Ukraine came to meet me in Belarus. I have carefully noted the contents.

Before answering your questions, I would like to say that I can see from your letter that you are developing a good spirit for preaching. Just hearing the descriptions of your visit to Kingston, your concerns about the people of Canada, your enthusiasm for associating with the enlivened book distributors in Poland, and your hankering to increase your own distribution of books, leads me to think that your understanding of Lord Cait- anya's Sankirtan Movement is increasing. Preaching is our life! But to give our whole life to preaching, we must also have a solid foundation in philosophy. Therefore, hearing classes from the advanced devotees is essential, but you must also find the time to read. Moreover, if you want to give your life for preaching, you must also not allow yourself to become proud. Do not think that because you are distributing books and other devotees are not, that they are somehow inferior. You've given no indication that you are thinking this way. But since it's a dangerous trap that neophytes can fall into, I thought it best to warn you, if you should ever see the indications of such a trap.

My leg is improving, but it's still going to take quite a bit more time. I've abandoned the use of a cane, but I must continue with therapy. We'll see, in time, if it will ever come close to being normal. It's in Krsna's hands.

Yes, this tendency to fall into a routine exists within everyone. When we first take up devotional service, we feel such enthusiasm from our surrender in devotional service. It's like taking off in an airplane. When we first take off from the ground, immediately, there is such a great contrast between where we are and the ground. But once we're in the air, it's not so easy to make distinctions in distance anymore. It's the same thing with devotional service. When we leave the material way of living, we immediately get some taste from devotional engagements. But once we become a little familiar, we don't find the same taste. This is also because in the beginning, Krsna is overlooking whatever offenses we may be committing to encourage us. But after being around for awhile, it's understood that we should become more aware of our offenses and should stop committing them. If we don't, Krsna does not overlook them as easily as He did in the beginning. By committing offenses, we lose our taste. So it's not that we were so pure when we first came to Krsna consciousness. Material desires were still there. It's just that Krsna was overlooking our offenses so that we could gain some taste. By experiencing that taste, then we always have something to fall back on when we are not experiencing that taste. Or as Narada Muni says, «having once experienced the taste of the lotus feet of the Lord, one will forget that ecstasy.» The only way to get the taste back is to surrender, something which we must continue to do every step of the way.

So to avoid falling into this «routine» consciousness, one must make the adjustment within, by seeing everything we do in its relation to service to Krsna. It takes practice, but Krsna says that by constant practice and detachment, we can begin to gain control of the mind.

Anger comes from unfulfilled desires. Just as Krsna explains in the Bhagavad-gita. By contemplating the objects of the senses, one develops attachment, then lust, then anger. Or as Lord Krsna also says, «It is lust only Arjuna, which is later transformed into wrath, which is the all-devouring sinful enemy of this world.» This was Krsna's answer when Arjuna asked the question, «What is it that impels a person to sinful acts, unwillingly, as if engaged by force?» Anger which is uncontrolled is a result of this unfulfilled lust.

How to get rid of it? Well, that comes in time, along with purification. However, Srila Prabhupada often quoted Srila Narottama dasa Thakur from his Prema-bhakti-candrika, describing how a devotee does not give up the bad qualities of lust, anger, greed, bewilderment or madness, but engages them in the Lord's service. Srila Narottama dasa Thakur says krodha bhakta-dvesi-jane or «Anger I will direct towards the enemies of the devotees.» He also says, «Lust I will engage by offering the fruits of my labor to Krsna. Greed I will engage by becoming greedy to hear the topics of Lord Hari in the association of saintly devotees. Bewilderment will be manifest because I cannot attain my worshipable Lord, and madness will be there when I madly glorify the transcendental attributes of Lord Krsna.» The only material quality left is matsarya (envy), but that one cannot be engaged. It must be given up completely.

With regards to your questions about self-defense, I will simply quote Srila Prabhupada from a letter he wrote in 1972:

«So long we are engaged in the service of the Lord, He has assured us all protection. But if we are attacked, of course we must defend ourselves as best we can. Nobody can compare with the Merciful Nityananda. He can defend Himself quite well; He could have killed the sinners in an instant, but He had decided that He wanted to save these sinners. He was setting an example that we have to preach Krishna Consciousness even at the risk of personal violence. Just like the children sometimes become violent, but the father knows he can defend himself at any time, and so he tries to teach them correctly by reprimanding them.»

The point is that we should not imitate such great personalities as Lord Nityananda. If we are attacked, we should try to defend, but at the same time, we must always remember that Krsna will always protect us whether we are physically hurt or not. We want our consciousness to be protected so that we can remember Krsna, even in the midst of the greatest difficulties. That protection from Krsna we want.

Hoping this letter meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear devi dasi,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada. I've received both letters of yours dated Nov. 17 and Nov. 18, and have noted the contents.

Yes, I am aware of what is happening now both to your consciousness and your body. Krsna is now testing you to see how much you have actually heard from His devotees. Krsna is testing all of us, each according to our ability to pass the tests that He gives us. He does not give us a test that we cannot pass. This test for you may seem like one that you are insufficiently prepared for, but Krsna is now very mercifully urging you to prepare. There are no material boundaries for devotional service. Devotional service is completely on the spiritual platform, and therefore cannot be hindered by these material bodies. So please do not think that you are incapable of pleasing me. You can most certainly please me by your efforts in Krsna consciousness.

Naturally I am not aware of the full details of your body's condition. And it seems, neither are you. I expect that the doctors will have their diagnosis, and they will eventually propose various treatments, hoping that one or another may produce a favorable result. You must try your best to determine if there is any scope for improvement by such treatments, or whether these treatments will only worsen both your physical and mental condition. As a matter of duty, certainly it's possible to accept treatment, if your disease is actually treatable. But whether or not it is actually treatable must be carefully analyzed by you in consultation with your physicians and with those whom you can trust. Don't just accept what the doctors say on the surface. Naturally, they will try to think positive. But you must go below the surface of their talks, and find out as best as possible, whether they are just offering false hopes. You must also be concerned about your consciousness, and what these treatments could potentially do to that as well. Ultimately, we must always depend upon Krsna.

I was just explaining to all the devotees here in Kiev last night this very point. I was realizing just how fortunate devotees are, that they have Krsna to depend on, and how terribly unfortunate it is for nondevotees, who do not know what it means to depend upon Krsna. Without Krsna, there is no shelter. There are only illusory beliefs in shelters that do not really exist. Maybe these beliefs can sustain nondevotees for sometime, giving them temporary hopes of encouragement. But material nature will not allow them the shelter that they so strongly believe in, and thus their hopes are always shattered in one way or another. But when we have Krsna as our only shelter, it is not a belief. He is the transcendent reality, and He is the only true shelter for His devotees. Fortunate are those rare souls who know about Krsna, and therefore place everything in His hands for His ultimate decision. Only then can they become peaceful.

So, despite your material condition, you are so very fortunate, because you know who is Krsna, and you have Krsna to depend upon every step of the way. Please remember this always, and be confident that this will bring me the greatest pleasure. Also please be assured that I am thinking about you, and will pray that Krsna will help you to remember Him always.

I remain, Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear devi dasi,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you very much for your letter which I received through Mahasakti dasa between 2-3 weeks ago. I believe that he has already explained the reason why it was not possible for me to answer sooner than this.

I read your letter several times, trying to understand as much as possible. I fully trust that your letter was a sincere expression of your state of mind, nonetheless, I could not help noticing conflicting attitudes. On one hand, you expressed sincere humility and self-reproach regarding your present position, but on the other hand you also seem to be putting a significant amount of blame upon the St. Petersburg devotees' lack of devotional qualifications for being where you are today. Of course, it's not that both of these states of mind cannot exist simultaneously. It's possible that there is substance to both of these views. However, what we really need to address is, «Which attitude will help you to move forward, or in other words, to regain your own Krsna consciousness?»

I am 100% convinced that «Krsna consciousness» is there within your heart. Contrary to what you're suggesting, it's not that you never had any devotion. However, maya is so strong, that if there is a slight discrepancy in the execution of devotional service, she can easily capture us and make us forget once again. She is far more powerful than we are, and her service is to provide us with unlimited ways to forget that we are servants of Krsna— in fulfillment of our own desires. Therefore, I admit that undoubtedly you have deep-rooted material desires. But they are not the only occupants of your heart. Right now they appear to be more prominent than your desire to serve Krsna, but it will not stay that way. Anyone who has once tasted the nectar of Krsna's lotus feet will never be able to forget them.

Just two days ago, one devotee came here to the Boston temple that I served together with back in 1974. He took initiation from Srila Prabhupada but shortly thereafter he left the association of devotees. I was happy to see him, since it had been many years since I had seen him last. We talked for awhile, and he was telling me about some of the thoughts he was having about his experiences practicing Krsna consciousness. I asked him how often he thought about Krsna, and he said honestly with a very serious look on his face, «You know, there is not one day that goes by when I don't think something about Krsna.» It's not possible to stay away from Krsna, having once been reminded that we are His servants. Of course, one may stay away from devotees, and one may also stray from the practices of regulative devotional service, but what one must come to accept is that such a diversion is the result of offenses committed while practicing devotional service at an immature stage. And when one is more influenced by maya than by Krsna, one's perspective towards everything is also affected.

Srila Prabhupada would very often quote a verse from Caitanya Caritamrta: dvaite bhadrabhadra-jnana, saba manodharma/ ei bhala ei manda ei saba brahma, «In the material world, conceptions of good and bad are all mental speculations. Therefore, saying, "This is good, this is bad," is all a mistake.» It is a mistake because anything which is not conceived in relationship to Krsna should be understood to be illusion, or maya. Krsna consciousness puts everything in proper perspective, and when we are not Krsna conscious, we see everything in terms of good and bad according to our own imperfect standards.

So, to some degree, this factor also bears some influence upon the way you perceive the devotees. I'm not going say that everything you are saying is totally false. Neither will I say that you are totally in maya, and that is why you are thinking this way. The above explanation is not intended to establish the conclusion that you are totally in maya. Certainly there are many imperfections in the devotees' behavior. They are also grappling with their motives and their desires, just as you are. But we should also try to not forget that they are also following a process for improving their character, much in the same way that you are looking for a way to improve yours. Otherwise why would you be writing to me?

So what is it that helps us to develop «GOOD» qualities by which we can make further advancement towards the real goal in life? As explained above, conceptions of «good» and «bad» with no relationship to Krsna are all mental speculation. Good qualities are automatically developed in association with Krsna, because Krsna is «all-good», absolute. There is no bad in Krsna. Even though you may see some «bad» in your association with devotees, you still should not allow that to prevent you from keeping your association with Krsna.

Srila Prabhupada even wrote the following in a purport to Caitanya Caritamrta, Mad. [19.157](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-madhya/19.157):

**«If one thinks that there are many pseudo devotees or nondevotees in the Krsna Consciousness Society, one can keep direct company with the spiritual master, and if there is any doubt, one should consult the spiritual master. However, unless one follows the spiritual master's instructions and the regulative principles governing the chanting and hearing the holy name of the Lord, one cannot become a pure devotee. By one's mental concoctions, one falls down.»**

There are two important points that one can draw from this quote. 1) That Srila Prabhupada was also aware that one may develop the view that there are pseudo devotees or even nondevotees within the Krsna Consciousness Society. But even with that view, it should not become the cause for giving up one's association with one's spiritual master. And 2) That if one gives up the regulative practices of Krsna consciousness, one falls onto the platform of mental concoction and falls down.

I sincerely hope that this letter does not place me into the category you so sharply spoke out against in your letter. I am now also quoting from Srila Prabhupada's books; the same books that you say you are reading. Is it wrong to quote Srila Prabhupada? Aren't we supposed to present the statements of the higher authorities? Isn't the genuine spiritual master supposed to speak on behalf of guru and Krsna? If he doesn't, according to scripture he is supposed to be rejected. However, the spiritual master is also supposed to be worthy of trust and faith. He must present the instructions of his guru and Krsna without any material motive. He must give Krsna, and only Krsna to his disciple. He must be free from the desire for wealth, followers and material enjoyment. And the disciple should want only Krsna from his/her spiritual master. Then there is genuine reciprocation in that relationship. Then the disciple can learn to see Krsna in the words of his spiritual master, and thus develop the desire to make progress towards Krsna. This is my desire. And therefore I'm trying to reach out to you to bring back that desire to serve Krsna because I know that it's still there in your heart.

So, at this point, I can only stress that which is most important. 1) Associating with devotees who help you to think about Krsna. (If you like you can continue to write to me.) 2) Associating with Krsna's holy names. 3) Reading Srila Prabhupada's books. My impression is that you are willing to maintain all three of these requests. To what degree you follow them will depend largely upon your own commitment. I would be happy to hear from you.

I hope that this letter meets you and in good health.

I remain, Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear dasa,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

You have said that you are very often changing your service. Why are you very often changing your service? This seems to indicate that you are very unsteady and that you have forgotten that you are a servant of Krsna. If every time there is some difficulty you simply renounce your duty because it is too troublesome, then how will you ever make any advancement? You are asking for instructions, but how many times have you heard me preach about the importance of becoming steady in devotional service, and about the proper mentality that one must adopt for becoming steady? So many instructions I have given.

Instead of thinking that the service is intended for your pleasure, you must change your way of thinking. The service we are performing is for Krsna's pleasure. When we actually adopt that consciousness, then we can become peaceful. No peace can ever come from constantly changing from one service to the next. Each time you change, you think, «Now I can enjoy». But very quickly Krsna shows you that you still have to surrender to your duty. You foolishly think that it is nice being whimsical and doing what you want to do. But as soon as you MUST do it, because your duty MUST be done for Krsna and not for your own pleasure, then out of ignorance you think that you don't want to make the commitment. Such a consciousness will only make it more difficult for you to become peaceful. Whimsical people can never have peace. Krsna clearly states this in the 14th Chapter of the Bhagavad-gita. A whimsical person can have neither happiness nor peace, nor can they ever attain the Supreme destination.

I suspect that your authorities will not let you work in the kitchen and are dissatisfied with you because you must have behaved in such a way that your authorities do not trust that you will take your kitchen duties seriously. This is what happens when devotees always change from one service to the next.

I therefore suggest that you seriously consider this point, and make your decision that you will accept some serious duties for Krsna's pleasure and not your own. Then Krsna will surely make all the necessary arrangements for you to be engaged in some responsible service. Don't think that you have to arrange everything yourself. Krsna will help, if you learn to depend upon Him.

I hope that this meets you in improved Krsna consciousness. Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear devi dasi,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

There is a very good reason why you cannot believe that Krsna would put you in such a «hung up» position. It's not Krsna that's making you think like that. It is you.
