Krsna is giving you an opportunity to deepen an attachment to His lotus feet. Instead, you are «hung up». It is your choice. That ability to choose is Krsna's doing, because He wants you to love Him.

We must always analyze things properly. When we are overwhelmed by the illusory energy, we cannot see things properly. And so the pure soul appears to be associated with matter just as the reflection of the moon upon the water appears to be moving. The moon in the sky is fixed, and like the original moon, the moon reflected on the water should also not quiver. But because of being associated with water, the reflection appears to be quivering. Similarly, the pure soul appears to be tainted by material qualities such as illusion, lamentation and miseries, although in the pure soul such qualities are completely absent. This is not your factual existence. All this lamentation is only due to the false identification. You must try to see beyond these things.

I cannot force you to give up this illusion. I can only try to help you to open your eyes, and to show you that there is a whole realm of existence beyond this temporary material world. In that place Lord Krsna eternally resides with His loving servants, and we are all meant to take part in that eternal lila.

In the meantime, we must learn how to live in the material world but to not be a part of it. Our protection depends upon our ability to remain engaged in loving service to the Lord. Krsna assures us that He will give us this protection. He wants us to call out and helplessly pray for the shelter at His lotus feet. Only then can we approach him with sincere feeling.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear dasa,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you very much for your heartfelt letter. I am very happy to hear that your intelligence has become clearer while writing your letter to me. Just see! One should never minimize the order one receives from Krsna's representative. Having seriously taken the order to write this letter instead of acting impulsively, Krsna protected you from following a course that would have led you deeper into illusion. We may not understand why the instruction was given, but if we follow it, never minimizing it as something that can be overlooked, then we can see Krsna's presence in that instruction.

Srila Prabhupada writes in the Srimad Bhagavatam [4.28.51](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-4/28.51): «ln conclusion, if a disciple is very serious to execute the mission of the spiritual master, he immediately associates with the Supreme Personality of Godhead by vani or vapuh. This is the only secret of success in seeing the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Instead of being eager to see the Lord in some bush in Vrndavana while at the same time engaging in sense gratification, if one instead sticks to the principle of following the words of the spiritual master, he will see the Supreme Lord without difficulty.»

You ask how you can develop this feeling of a sense of duty towards myself. This feeling comes by association. Just like right now you are associating through hearing. You revealed your mind in confidence, and therefore I have responded to your sincere attempts to set yourself on the right course. Everyone must be engaged in some duty. A husband feels dutiful towards his wife and family. A citizen feels dutiful towards his country. But by serving such illusory masters one only becomes further entangled. However, if we want to break away from mundane duties, we must follow duties of a higher order. We must follow those duties which are coming down from Krsna, and then we can rise beyond the influence oimaya's entrapment. The spiritual master's instructions are our lifeline to Krsna. It's the rope, which when held tightly, can gradually pull us out of our suffering condition of life. To increase this sense of duty we must simply tightly hold on to the rope, and as we begin to see the material world behind us, we will feel more and more bound to the orders of guru and Krsna.

So naturally it is best if you can avoid householder life. It is always best to avoid householder life, although not everyone can avoid it. However, even if it becomes necessary to enter into householder life, it should never be done whimsically. Householder life is never the solution, and it is foolish to think that taking householder life will solve all of one's problems with sex desire. Sex life, whether licit or illicit, is always a problem. Krsna consciousness is the only solution. Therefore, whether you are bramachari or grhastha, your primary duty is to come to the point of always remembering Krsna while performing your duties. This will protect you.

So please go on with your Sankirtan duties. This is most pleasing to me. Presently, especially in Ukraine, there is so much social unrest. The people are so disturbed because they can see that society is not giving them protection. Everyone is worried. How will we eat? How will we live? They have forgotten that their only duty in life is to serve Krsna and all protection will be there. We must show them the way to get out of maya's clutches. We must show them compassion. This is how to capture Krsna's attention. When we give up all thoughts of self-interest and see that our real self-interest is to satisfy the Lord, Krsna takes care of all of our needs. One who preaches in this mood will always feel the Lord's protection.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear dd,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have your letter which was forwarded to me on 15.3.95. Thank you very much.

From your letter I am feeling the need to help strengthen your desire for devotional service. The spiritual master is supposed to be the link between the disciple and Krsna. I must make a forceful effort to enter into your world and to deepen your attachment to Krsna. Of course, even if I am more forceful and actually do enter into your world, I must have the strength, purity, realization, and everything else required to reveal to you Krsna. I can say, «Here is Krsna!» But how much of Him I can give, I cannot say. I can say with all honesty that I want to give all of Him to you. I want you to see Him, to be with Him always, to remember Him always, and to engage uninterruptedly in His service for all of eternity. This I can honestly say that I want. I do not want you to serve me. I must accept your service, but I cannot keep any of it for myself. Everything must go to Krsna. I want you to serve Krsna, and to taste the wonderful bliss of even one moment's association with Him through service. Even one moment of such consciousness is sufficient to pave one's way back home, back to Godhead, so that one will want nothing else in life except to experience that one moment again. That is the nectar of Krsna's association. It cannot be compared to anything within this material world. Your world must include Krsna. Your world must become Vaikuntha, a place where there is no anxiety. And you have a right to make this world yours, because you are His part and parcel, jivera Svarupa haya nitya krsna dasa. You are His eternal servant.

Do not think that I have given up. This letter is an indication that I have not given up. The spiritual master will never reject a disciple that is sincere. You say that you are insincere, but I do not believe it. Otherwise, why would you write such a letter? An insincere disciple would not reach out for a helping hand from the spiritual master.

As far as your lamentation is concerned, it seems that you are too hard on yourself. Certainly, I was hoping that you would be able to spend time in the temple, and it seems that you were putting a lot of hopes into that, too. But not everyone can live in a temple. You would think that if everyone could, that there would be a lot more people living in the temple right now. The temple is meant to serve those who cannot live in the temple, by providing an atmosphere where they can come and hear about Krsna, chant Krsna's name congregationally, and behold the beautiful Area Vigraha so that they can fix their minds upon a worshipful Deity in the course of living outside. Krsna consciousness is all-embracing, and certainly is not meant to be exclusive, where only those who are «full-time» devotees can become recipients of Krsna's mercy. Lord Caitanya is far more merciful than that. And therefore He so kindly distributed the holy names of the Lord as the primary means of deliverance in this age. Krsna appears in His name, and anyone who associates with His name can find that it's as good as associating with the Lord directly. Why do we then not always experience His association when we chant? Because our hearts need to be purified. Should we condemn ourselves for having impure hearts? No, we should rejoice, that despite my impure heart, Lord Caitanya has given His assurance that anyone who dedicates his life to chanting these holy names will ultimately meet with success.

So please do maintain such self-condemnation. It will not help you. Do not think that the devotees living in the temple are looking upon you condescendingly. They are your servants. It is for devotees like you that they are living in the temple, serving the Deity, telling others about Krsna, and maintaining a Vaikuntha atmosphere where you can regularly come to take shelter.

Be patient, and go on chanting as much as possible. If you have time to chant more, then why only 16 rounds? Chant as much as you can. Visit the temple as much as you can. Lord Caitanya will not overlook your sincere efforts to glorify Him.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

April 25, 1995

Dear Bhaktin,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Avoiding japa is certainly not the best adjustment. You want to be able to lift your consciousness above the pressures of day to day duties. Whether our duties are cleaning the temple, worshipping the Deity, or studying at school, they must be performed in the proper consciousness. This point was already stressed in my previous letter. Although your parents are putting pressure on you to excel in school, if you could do both, (chant and excel), I'm sure that they would not be opposed. They know by their own experience that there is more to life than having a secure job and a secure income. They are just afraid that you will abandon your studies and will possibly find that you really should not have. Whether their fears are valid or not, I am confident that if you don't neglect your japa, you continue associating with the devotees, and dutifully continue your studies, Krsna will clarify the issues in due course of time. We must have that trust in Krsna.

Regarding the the loss of your friends, Narada Muni, when instructing Maharaja Yudhisthira quoted the following verse from the First Canto of S.B., «As a player sets up and disperses his playthings according to his own sweet will, so the supreme will of the Lord brings men together and separates them.» Certainly it is not mundane to be attached to the association of devotees. But if we remain conscious of the real value of their association, then when Krsna separates us from one another, we will see that Krsna was always in the background of that relationship. Seeing the presence of Krsna, will free us from any unnecessary lamentation, and He will certainly not allow us to remain bereft of devotee association.

It is definitely not offensive to make jewelry for the Deities if you are not chanting, but naturally it's better if you ARE chanting.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

May 3, 1995

Dear Bhaktin,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you for your letter dated April 26, 1995.

I am naturally concerned that you are neglecting your rounds. It seems as though is having the same problem, and I just wrote her a letter about that as well.

You are right, your whole perspective towards the devotees, towards life, towards everything, will change if you stop chanting your rounds. Chanting is our protection from maya's influence. When we are chanting, and increasing our shelter in Krsna's holy name, our consciousness rises above the day to day worldly obligations that make us forget our actual identity. Devotees start appearing to be ordinary people, devotional service is seen as material activity, and the qualities existing in worldly-minded people seem more attractive. Srila Prabhupada would always stress the point that spiritual life is like walking on a razor's edge. It is very dangerous, and at any moment we can slip and fall, meaning that at any moment we can forget Krsna, and that we are His servants.

I accept that you have so many obligations, such as school, work and dance class. I'm sure that your list of obligations does not even end there. However, you owe it to yourself to not forget the highest obligation in life. Srila Prabhupada would always emphasize the point that without Krsna, everything else is zero. This means that without Krsna, your school, your work, and your dance, really has no significant meaning. It is all centered around the maintenance of this body. But when you add Krsna, then everything becomes meaningful, just as each zero becomes meaningful when you add a one before them. You must find the means to add this one to your zeros. Your infrequent visits to the temple will help, but as you are finding, this is not enough. You must bring Krsna into your day to day life. The best way to do that is by faithfully chanting.

So please try your best to increase your chanting. Your mother is very concerned, and I am concerned as well. You say that your parents don't care about your future, but the impression I'm getting is that your mother must certainly be caring. Otherwise, why would she beg me to please speak to you about the fact that you are not chanting your rounds faithfully? She's definitely caring about your future— the future which goes beyond this temporary body.

In the meantime, you must still continue with your studies, and try your best to increase your friendship with the different devotees who are living here now. I'm sure that if you develop a more confidential relationship with one of the women, that they will certainly care about your needs.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

May 12, 1995

Dear Bhakta,

Hare Krsna. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you very much for your kind letter dated March 18, 1995, and your donation contained within. I am sorry that I could not answer your letter sooner. As Nanda Suta dasa will testify, I was exceptionally busy this last trip back to the United States. I therefore allowed myself to fall behind in some of my correspondence.

Of course, now that I am in Russia, I'm even more busy than I was in the States. But I decided to make a determined effort to catch up on correspondence today, and your letter was on top of the pile.

I'm sure that now that warmer weather is approaching you must be thinking about increasing your time out on the course. So, it seems that the question asked in your letter must be even more relevant now than before.

Regarding the goal of your work, as we learn from Bhagavad-gita, the goal of all work must be aimed for the satisfaction of Lord Visnu. As you have indicated, your work is professional golf, both competing and teaching. The problem that you seem to be facing is that it has now become difficult for you to reconcile your work in relation to Lord Krsna's teachings. But such reconciliation can only become possible when the understanding of «duty» is passed down from higher authority. Lord Krsna explains in the Bhagavad-gita ([16.24](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/16.24)) «One should therefore understand what is duty and what is not duty by the regulations of the scriptures. Knowing such rules and regulations, one should act so that he may gradually be elevated.»

Of course, it's not that one will open the scriptures like a catalogue, and look within to find «professional golfer» under a list of duties which one may select from. Duties are defined according to one's psychophysical nature, and there are both spiritual duties required for development of consciousness, as well as social duties defined for those who are householders requiring maintenance for family.

In your case you have accepted professional golf as a livelihood, but as Krsna explains in the Bhagavad-gita ([12.10](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/12.10)): «Ifyou cannot practice the regulations of bhakti-yoga, then just try to work for Me, because by working for Me you will come to the perfect stage.»

In the purport Srila Prabhupada makes it quite clear how one can work for Krsna.

«One who is not able even to practice the regulative principles of bhakti-yoga, under the guidance of a spiritual master, can still be drawn to this perfectional stage by working for the Supreme Lord. How to do this work has already been explained in the fifty-fifth verse of the Eleventh Chapter. One should be sympathetic to the propagation of Krsna consciousness. There are many devotees who are engaged in the propagation of Krsna consciousness, and they require help. So, even if one cannot directly practice the regulative principles of bhakti-yoga, he can try to help such work. Every endeavor requires land, capital, organization and labor. Just as in business one requires a place to stay, some capital to use, some labor and some organization to expand, so the same is required in the service of Krsna. The only difference is that in materialism one works for sense gratification. The same work, however, can be performed for the satisfaction of Krsna, and that is spiritual activity. If one has sufficient money, he can help in building an office or temple for propagating Krsna consciousness. Or he can help with publications. There are various fields of activity, and one should be interested in such activities. If one cannot sacrifice the results of his activities, the same person can still sacrifice some percentage to propagate Krsna consciousness. This voluntary service to the cause of Krsna consciousness will help one to rise to a higher state of love for God, whereupon one becomes perfect.»

It is all a matter of consciousness. If you perform your work with the consciousness that you will offer some fruit to Krsna, then such consciousness simultaneously invokes the desire and determination to better yourself in that work. Not only was the result of Arjuna's work destined prior to his fighting in battle, but the result was even shown to Arjuna in the form of the Lord's virata rupa. However, although knowing the result, Arjuna was quite determined in battle and always strived for success in the execution of his duties. Why? Because Krsna had instilled within him the impetus for such work. Arjuna was no longer interested in the enjoyment of the fruits of his work, as he had previously considered. His interest was to perform that work which was handed down to him from higher authority for the pleasure of Krsna. In other words whether you become an exceptional golfer and win hundreds of tournaments or not, is not up to you. Your duty is to strive for improvement for Krsna.

As I'm sure you already know, I am very inclined to see my disciples remain and steadily serve in one temple, and I am very disappointed when I see my disciples whimsically move around from one temple to another. There will be so many shortcomings, no matter what temple one serves in. The best way to improve the situation is to become part of the solution and not part of the problem. When devotees stay, voluntarily accept inconveniences and mature in their Krsna consciousness, a temple will naturally improve. It becomes the kind of temple that new devotees will want to take shelter in and join. But if a temple is constantly drained by this whimsical moving around, the opposite effect will always take place. Therefore I am asking you to please bear this in mind, and return to Kamchatka where you are very much wanted. This will please me very much.

Moreover, you must now do the needful to restore your daily sadhana to the proper standard. Having admitted to me how much you have suffered from poor sadhana in the past, you must now take it as a firm instruction from me to immediately correct this. You must daily attend mangala arati, visibly chant your rounds in the morning, and attend Srimad Bhagavatam class. If you are resolute to make this change, then all help will be there, and you will have the required strength to take up the most important service to go out and distribute Srila Prabhupada's books to the suffering souls. Surely Srila Prabhupada will be pleased by this effort.

Srila Prabhupada has explained that in all temples both pan- caratrika vidhi and bhagavat vidhi must go on side by side. When there is both preaching and Deity worship going on, then the temple is well-balanced. Without preaching, and especially book distribution, there is no meaning to temple life. You should not be proud of the fact that you are distributing books. Better to be a sincere pot-washer, always meditating on service to the lotus feet of the Deity, then to be a proud book distributor, always meditating on one's own honor. But the fact is that when the book distribution is going on in a sincere way, the whole community benefits. Each member of the community must perform their prescribed duty. And prescribed duties are all meant to be performed so that the members of the temple are interdependent upon each other by the performance of their duties. The fact is, that the temple devotees are dependent upon you to go out and contact conditioned souls, for by so doing, this gives life and purpose to the temple. And you are dependent upon the devotees in the temple to serve the Deities nicely, so that you can return from book distribution and take advantage of the spiritual atmosphere that has been created by services rendered by the devotees in the temple. It always helps when the temple leaders create this awareness of interdependence and mutual respect that should exist amongst those who serve in the temple and those who go out to distribute books. Then everything goes on nicely, and you should not have to feel the way that you've described in your letter. But even though the leaders may sometimes neglect to do this, these words of encouragement should serve to remind you, whenever you feel that you've forgotten.

To answer your next question whether it is by intellectual endeavor or by the mercy of the Lord that the truth can be revealed to us, it is stated in the Mundaka Upanisad, «The Supreme Lord is not attained by expert explanations, by vast intelligence, nor by much hearing. He is attained only by one who He Himself chooses. To such a person He manifests His own form.» In other words it certainly only by Krsna's mercy that we can understand Him. Krsna says in BG [15.15](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/15.15), that He is giving knowledge, remembrance and forgetfulness. Of course, we must still use our intelligence to try to understand Krsna, but without Krsna's sanction, we cannot understand anything about Him. When He is pleased by our efforts to try understand Him, knowing our motives for «trying», to be sincere, then He will help us to understand Him. He reveals Himself in proportion to our surrender.

Association is always important, both in the beginning, the middle, and even in the most advanced stages. That's why even the topmost devotees are always praying to the Lord to have the opportunity to associate with the Lord's devotees. Bhaktivinode Thakur prays that he prefers to take birth as an insect, as long as it can be in the house of a devotee, rather than taking birth as Lord Brahma but to be bereft of the association of devotees. But as you say, it is especially important at the beginning, and that strong association gives us the strength to go on with our efforts. Srila Prabhupada would give the example that one piece of straw breaks easily when alone, but when bundled into a big bundle of straw, it becomes very difficult to break. Similarly, as much as possible, we should always tie ourselves into the association of strong devotees.

I hope that your sankirtan is going on nicely, and that a nice arrangement was made for you to have nice association for doing this valuable service.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear dasa,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Please work closely and cooperatively with dasa there in …, and follow his good example. He is a householder with several children, and he is meeting the demands of his householder life without neglecting his duties in spiritual life. It would be very nice if you could work towards opening a restaurant in , as well as catering prasadam to office workers.

Why do you need to purchase a computer? You just sent a message complaining that the reason why you must leave is because you were not making enough money to live there. Does making enough money mean that you must have enough money to buy a computer? Is a computer an absolute necessity for your life right now? I don't see the connection between doing Food for Money and needing a computer. Be careful. Don't think that, «Now that I am making money, I must think of ways to accumulate so many things». This will only lead to more and more problems. Live simply, keeping your necessities to a minimum so that you don't waste all of your valuable energy in simply accumulating assets, which will take whatever energy you have left to maintain. And if you say that you need a computer to communicate with me, then my answer is that you are already doing that right now, and I am already answering you. For the few times that you would contact me, it's not necessary to go out and buy a computer.

That is my answer to that question.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

September 15, 1995

Dear devi dasi,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada. I have your letter, received September 4, 1995, and have noted the contents.

In your letter you have written: «Now I realize how much I had the tendency to try to gain some recognition or attention from you, but this relationship actually never satisfied me. However, when I was engaged in devotional service with my mind fixed on your instructions then I felt much more connected. It seems that I am still struggling to overcome this anartha. Can you help me understand this point better.»

Just yesterday I was giving a lecture at a Nama Hatta center in . In this city, they have no strong spiritual leader, how ever there is a moderate group of people who gather regularly to have kirtan, prasadam, and a discussion on Krsna consciousness. Although there were over 40 people present for the talk, I could see that most of them did not have a very deep understanding of Krsna consciousness. I gave a short lecture about the necessity to become obedient to God, before one could actually love God, and placed emphasis on adhering to the basic injunctions given in sastra for regulating one's life, and minimizing the demands of the body. I ended with a brief explanation of devotional service. After the talk, one elderly woman asked a question how devotional service begins. I started to give a somewhat detailed explanation, explaining how devotional service is eternal, is in the heart of all living entities, but it becomes manifest in a conditioned soul when the desire to serve Krsna is awakened by the association of Krsna's devotee. I then explained how this all begins when one hears about Krsna with Sraddha or faith, and that faithful hearing was the actual beginning of that awakening of devotional service. All the while I was speaking, I was watching the expression on her face as she was very attentively listening to the translation of everything I said. I was watching her smile grow as she seemed to be getting some realization. She, as well as others, were concerned about their connection to devotional service. They were eagerly looking for hope that devotional service could become a part of their lives, and they could be recognized as a devotee of Krsna. When I finished, she continued to smile, and then begged me to stay in and give all of them the opportunity to learn about devotional service, and to further give them the opportunity to have someone available whose in structions they could serve. I then told her that I would stay in… as long as she would remember what I had said. Her questioning stopped, and I could see that she knew what I meant. She thanked me again and again, and although I «physically» left Mogilev very shortly thereafter, I left thinking that she would honestly try to remember the instructions I gave in the lecture. It was very very fulfilling to see this desire to serve Krsna increase on the strength of repeating Srila Prabhupada's words. Perhaps, these experiences more than anything else, serve to increase my own faith that by always remembering Srila Prabhupada's words and instructions to give Krsna consciousness to everyone, I can be with Srila Prabhupada, and others can come in contact with Krsna and devotional service.

I was also giving a lecture the other day, and I was using the example that Srila Prabhupada uses, how the connection to the spiritual master and -parampara is like being connected to a copper wire. If the copper wire is connected to the powerhouse, the electricity is coming through. But what is the «symptom» of that connection? Is it just the formality of accepting initiation? If so, then why would Srila Prabhupada write in the purport to CC, Madhya [15.111](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-madhya/15.111), «Whether the Vaisnava is properly initiated or not, is not a subject for consideration. One may be initiated and yet contaminated by Mayavadi philosophy, but a person who chants the Holy Name of the Lord offenselessly will not be so contaminated. A properly initiated Vaisnava may be imperfect, but one who chants the Holy Name of the Lord offenselessly, is all perfect. Althought he may apparently be a neophyte, he still has to be considered a pure unalloyed Vaisnava.»

The symptom of being connected is not just the initiation, but one must be strictly following. Just like that same copper wire. If you tape an insulated wire to another insulated wire that's connected to an electrical current, from the outside it may appear to be strongly connected. But if «inside» the actual wires are not touching, then no current will come through. Externally it may appear to be connected, but without the internal connection what is the value? Similarly, we may accept initiation, but if we are not internally connected, by always remembering and acting on the instructions of the spiritual master, then we will not feel the current coming through. When the current does comes through, however, then we can feel satisfaction with that connection.

In other words, it's not something external, that there must be some external recognition for all the efforts we make in devotional service. Certainly, that is one form of encouragement. But our lasting encouragement comes on the strength of that internal connection.

I am very happy to hear that you are actively taking part in preaching and book distribution. These activities will satisfy both of you, and just as Lord Caitanya instructed Kurma the brahmana, they will serve to remove all obstacles from householder life.

Hoping this meets you and your husband in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

First, regarding your letter of 28.08.95, you should discuss the matter with Prabhu. That is practically always my opinion. My disciples should determine their engagements with their local authorities, unless for some reason, I question a temple authority's ability to properly analyze a particular problem that a disciple of mine may be having. But that is not the case with you, or for that matter with any of my disciples who are presently engaged in the temple. You now have a very competent temple authority, and I trust that he will listen to all of your concerns and will come up with the best proposal for your future engagement. So please have that discussion first, and if, for some reason, you are incapable of accepting what he proposes, then you may bring the matter to my attention.

The point is, that every temple is organized locally, and the prescribed duties for all full-time members of the temple must be accepted accordingly. Devotees living dependency within the temple should not demand to be engaged in a particular way. Duties are assigned according to the community's needs, so that ultimately everyone is taking part in a collective yajna, or sacrifice. At the same time, a responsible leader will always try his best to engage devotees according to their particular inclinations, but such «conditional» service on the part of the members should always be kept to the absolute minimum. The goal, obviously, is to become free from all conditions, and to serve with full faith in guru and Krsna, accepting the service as the means to satisfy both. Please, therefore, bear this in mind, and patiently wait for the first opportunity to meet with Ekanath Prabhu, at his convenience, to discuss your future engagement.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear devi dasi,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Having just reread your letter, I realized that the directives I had previously given were actually more significant of a treatment for the lamentations described, than I had originally thought. If one wants to overcome tendencies towards selfishness, then he must voluntarily accept opportunities for giving up selfish interests. Krsna consciousness gives us the opportunity by which we can give up all bad tendencies by the positive acceptance of favorable service to Krsna. In other words, rather than artificially curbing selfish tendencies, a devotee automatically curbs selfish tendencies by voluntarily accepting opportunities to serve other devotees. The mind will resist, and sometimes, under maya's influence we will begin to think, «The association of devotees is making me more selfish. There are too many demands, and I need to give more attention to my own needs.» But in the association of devotees we must find the association which increases our hankering for the service of Krsna. Then we should serve them. Automatically we diminish our tendencies to fulfill our own selfish interests. This is called sacrifice. Srila Prabhupada defines sacrifice as the voluntary acceptance of some inconvenience for the pleasure of somebody else.

I've heard that you are returning to very soon. I suggest that when you arrive, you should carefully listen to one lecture I gave on my second day in . In this lecture I carefully gave details on how a devotee should behave with other devotees, and the criteria a devotee should use for taking association from other devotees. I believe that once you listen to this lecture you will have a better understanding of what I am saying to you here.

I honestly believe that although you did have opportunities for such association in , but because the service requires being alone so much, you became neglectful of this principle of association. I therefore hope that your change to will afford you the association that is so much needed for you.

Beyond that, if you voluntarily accept every opportunity to preach or to take part in a collective effort to preach, you will also see the great benefit that Srila Prabhupada has give every one of us with his order to preach. Preaching means that we have to think of the welfare of others. Automatically it curbs those same selfish tendencies. By associating with those devotees who inspire within us the service to preach Krsna consciousness, then gradually all the selfish desires in the heart will be washed away.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear Bhakta,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I've read your letter dated 21.02.92, and have noted the contents.

Yes, I'm aware of your unsteady participation in temple life. I know that I've received at least two (if not more) letters from you in the past, but each time I returned to Moscow, I was informed that nobody knew where you were, so I did not bother to answer them.

You ask me to please tell you what your prescribed duties are. It seems that based upon your history in devotional service that you have a hard time maintaining steadiness in the basic spiritual duties. Irregardless of the service that was given to you, a devotee should always guard against deviation from the basic devotional practices.

However, you are now asking about the prescribed duties which relate to your psychophysical nature. These duties are different from the spiritual duties of chanting, worshipping the Deity, etc. These duties relate to one's particular varna or social duty. In order to effectively execute one's prescribed social duties, one still must function within a social system which has as its aim the satisfaction of Lord Visnu. Such duties must be overseen by the priestly members of society (or brahmanas), and enforced by the administrative members who are submissive to the brahmanas. Within such a system, there are also spiritual masters, who teach the younger members of society, and then later train them, or arrange for their training, according to their particular occupational tendency. These social duties are usually determined only after many years of observation of the student.

It is beyond my scope to say what your social tendencies are, having little opportunity to observe your behavior. And it also seems that it is beyond the means of the local temple authorities to engage you in duties which can keep you preoccupied. Under the circumstances, I would recommend that you continue with your plan to attend technical college, but just what technical skill you should develop, you will have to determine yourself. These things are secondary, although it seems, that you still cannot neglect them. However, the duties of greater significance, are those which you are hoping to reinstate in your life after acquiring your own room. I would suggest that you anxiously work towards achieving this freedom to practice your devotional practices of 16 rounds daily, 4 regulative principles, worshipping the Deity, and accepting only Krsna prasadam. I'm confident that once you take up these practices again, that Krsna will help you in your efforts to find stability between your spiritual and material duties.

Although I have not given a definitive answer to your appeal, nonetheless I have given you guidelines, and priorities of action. I hope that you now have something more tangible to fix in your mind, and that you will be able to move forward, without lethargy, in your efforts to find a stable position from which you can advance in Krsna consciousness.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear Bhaktin,

Hare Krsna. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have read your most recent letter and have carefully noted the contents.

I appreciate the concerns that you've raised in your letter and can understand that you are sincerely trying to understand the difference between quantity and quality, and the criteria for recognizing spiritual progress. I can think of a reference that Srila Prabhupada himself made in this regard, which may not be exactly the same situation, but it seems as though the principle is there to act as our guide. In the Science of Self-Realization, Srila Prabhupada refers to the following point in a discussion:

«You cannot judge a movement simply by the number of its followers. If one man is genuine, then the movement is successful. It is not a question of quantity, but quality.»

Srila Prabhupada was describing how a genuine movement should not be judged by the number of followers, but by the quality of its members. Similarly, we cannot judge somebody's spiritual progress exclusively on the basis of the quantity of books he distributes or the number of pages he prints.

Prabhupada would also give us the example of Hanuman, the great devotee of Lord Ramacandra, and the spider. When Hanuman was throwing huge mountain peaks into the ocean in order to build a bridge to Sri Lanka, a small spider was kicking small pebbles into the ocean. Hanuman was about to push the spider out of the way, but Lord Ramacandra stopped him and insisted that the spider was working to his full capacity to serve Him. The Lord was accepting that service. Hanuman should not have discriminated on the quantity of work achieved by the spider, but was encouraged to look at the quality. The spider was offering everything he could for the Lord's service, according to his capacity.

On the other hand, Srila Prabhupada would also use the expression phalena pariciyate: one is recognized by the result of his actions. Or, in other words, «One can judge a tree by its fruit». Srila Prabhupada did push us to be result-oriented, but insisted that we offer the results to Krsna, and not try to take the credit ourselves.

I can think of many examples in this connection, but most prominently, what comes to my mind, is the way that Srila Prabhupada pushed us to publish and distribute his books. Srila Prabhupada also pushed, very hard, to build certain temples, especially the 3 major temples in India— Bombay, Mayapura and Vrndavana.

But even though we felt pushed by Srila Prabhupada's desire to see these projects manifest for the pleasure of his spiritual master and Krsna, he took every opportunity to encourage everyone according their ability to render some service. He would even say that his spiritual master, Srila Bhaktisiddhanta, would be very, very pleased when he would send young boys out to sell literature of Krsna consciousness, even if they returned having sold only a few issues. Why? Because they made the endeavor.

So, from these few examples, we must try to see what we should look for in ourselves, in order to understand the proper criteria for spiritual advancement. It seems that if the quality of the service is there, then everything else will follow. What we really want from our service is to bring pleasure to our guru and Krsna. In the above examples, we see that the spiritual master is pleased when we are making the most sincere endeavor.

In your situation, I would say that you should look within yourself to see if you are really making the best possible endeavor you can to execute your service. Maybe there is something that you could do to adjust your life in such a way that you could be making more of an endeavor. But, if you honestly feel that you are already making the most endeavor that you possibly can, then you should not be discouraged. Krsna is in your heart, and he knows the sincere desire that is manifest there. He will dictate to your spiritual master everything that he needs to know the sincerity in the heart of his disciple. Although you may be asked to wait, you will definitely not be neglected.

I hope that this letter finds you healthy and happy in Krsna consciousness.

I am, Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

There is no question of deciding who you should become in this life. The decision has already been made by Krsna. You are His servant. Now it is up to you to realize this in this life. I am sensitive to the thoughts you are having at this difficult period in your life. You are looking for the best means to express yourself as Krsna's servant, while feeling the pressure from family to give this life to a service lesser in value. Moreover, you do not see adequate shelter within the temple to convince both yourself and your family that your «necessities» in life will be met.

Considering everything I've mentioned above, you need to strengthen your shelter in those activities that nurture your Krsna consciousness. Krsna consciousness is not restricted to the four walls of the temple. Krsna consciousness eternally exists within the hearts of all living entities. It must be awakened by the association of those who are freely giving it to others. Look for those sources of Krsna consciousness that inspire you to deepen your thoughts about Krsna and His unlimited pastimes with His pure devotees. You must constantly hanker for their association, and whenever possible, listen to their transcendental narrations. This is real shelter. The four walls of the temple are not shelter at all if the transcendental glories of the Lord are not constantly vibrating within. Of course, the service rendered within the temple by those devotees who are absorbed in thought of the Lord and the order of their spiritual master, is also transcendental glorification of the Lord. But such service can continue only if it is genuinely inspired by hearing from such a right source. The point is that devotional service begins and then grows on the basis of transcendental sound. You say that there is no service in the temple, but devotional service will be revealed to those who develop the intense desire to have it by hearing. Devotional service is not the offering of a service with the assurance of some false shelter in exchange. That is something else. Devotional service is awakened by hearing.

I have no doubt that your desire to dedicate your life to Krsna is the result of having heard in this way. Now just make your decision to remain awake, and don't let the illusory dictations of maya cradle you back into deep sleep. Whether you further your education, take up some outside work, or whether you can find some work with other devotees; none of these things are an end within themselves. The goal of all work is to increase your attraction for hearing about Krsna, and if you always remember this, you will be safe in whatever position of life you pursue.

Thank you very much for maintaining your serious thoughts about life. May Krsna bless you with unalloyed devotional service.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

When I received your letter about three weeks ago, I was concerned and wanted to write back immediately. But by now I believe that you have already received an explanation why I was unable to do so. Still, I wanted to do something to ease your anxiety, since from your letter it appeared that your anxiety level was high. Not that I'm expecting you to be without any anxiety. There will always be anxiety until we are completely free from the bodily conception of life. «One who always sees all living entities as spiritual sparks, in quality one with the Lord, becomes a true knower of things. What, then, can be illusion or anxiety for him?» (Isopanisad, [Mantra 7](https://vedarama.com/en/books/iso/7)).

Nonetheless, until complete Krsna consciousness is achieved and we are completely free from the bodily conception, we must know how to deal with anxiety in a way that helps us continue the endeavor for «Krsna consciousness.» That endeavor must never be given up. If there is too much anxiety and we give up the endeavor for Krsna consciousness, the only alternative is to take shelter in an illusory plan for temporarily relieving anxiety (thus only causing more and more anxiety in forgetfulness of Krsna). On the other hand, anxiety may go away or not go away, but when we are actually conscious of «WHY,» as devotees of Krsna, we must voluntarily accept anxiety in service to Him, then we can go on faithfully serving in whatever situation Krsna puts us, always expecting His mercy. This is Krsna consciousness, as Srila Prabhupada so clearly describes in this purport from Bhagavad-gita [5.13](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/5.13):

«The difference between a person in Krsna consciousness and a person in bodily consciousness is that the former is attached to Krsna whereas the latter is attached to the results of his activities. The person who is attached to Krsna and works for Him only is certainly a liberated person, and he has no anxiety over the results of his work. In the Bhagavatam, the cause of anxiety over the result of an activity is explained as being one's functioning in the conception of duality, that is, without knowledge of the Absolute Truth. Krsna is the Supreme Absolute Truth, the Personality of Godhead. In Krsna consciousness, there is no duality. All that exists is a product of Krsna's energy, and Krsna is all good. Therefore, activities in Krsna consciousness are on the absolute plane, they are transcendental and have no material effect. One is therefore filled with peace in Krsna consciousness. But one who is entangled in profit calculation for sense gratification cannot have that peace. This is the secret of Krsna consciousness «realization that there is no existence besides Krsna is the platform of peace and fearlessness.»

In the neophyte stage of devotional service sometimes we get a glimpse of this Krsna consciousness and immediately all anxiety goes away. But as you know, these glimpses are rare and anxiety exists more often than not. Therefore, we can find in Srila Prabhupada's writings, instructions, verses, and purports so many examples, which serve to remind and inspire us to voluntarily accept anxiety for the service of Krsna.

In Srimad Bhagavatam [8.7.44](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-8/7.44), it is stated:

**«It is said that great personalities almost always accept voluntary suffering because of the suffering of people in general. This is considered the highest method of worshiping the Supreme Personality of Godhead, who is present in everyone's heart.»**

And in a purport to Srimad Bhagavatam [2.9.4](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-2/9.4), Srila Prabhupada writes:

«In executing penance, one must be determined to return home, back to Godhead, and must decide to undergo all types of tribulations for that end. Even for material prosperity, name and fame, one has to undergo severe types of penance, otherwise no one can become an important figure in this material world. Why, then, are there severe types of penance for the perfection of devotional service? An easygoing life and attainment of perfection in transcendental realization cannot go together. The Lord is cleverer than any living entity; therefore He wants to see how painstaking the devotee is in devotional service. The order is received from the Lord, either directly or through the bonafide spiritual master. To execute that order, however painstaking, is the severe type of penance. One who follows the principle rigidly is sure to achieve success in attaining the Lord's mercy.»

Penance is the voluntary acceptance of some personal inconvenience or distress for achieving a higher goal. In the above purport, we hear how one is sure to achieve success by the execution of penance in Krsna consciousness. By hearing these words from Srila Prabhupada and by associating with exemplary devotees, we can find the inspiration to continue to accept anxiety and inconvenience for Krsna, faithful that one day everything necessary for perfection in Krsna consciousness will be revealed. Hearing about Krsna from the right source is always the best way to adjust because hearing adjusts the consciousness.

On the other hand, if anxiety becomes too overwhelming, we begin looking elsewhere for shelter, and the voluntary service attitude to please Krsna decreases as well. This voluntary spirit should never become lost. Unless the inspiration to serve Krsna (the highest austerity) remains healthy, forced service will not endure. It is therefore the duty of the Society's leaders to inspire this service attitude by their unmotivated preaching and by their example.

Srila Prabhupada explains the difference between voluntary service and forced service in the following conversation:

«So automatic cooperation is bhakti, and forced cooperation is karma. That is the... It looks the same thing. Karmis and the bhaktas are working just like we are working in the same way. Karmi is typing and a bhakta is typing. It looks like the same thing, but karmi is typing under force. His master has ordered, «You work it; otherwise you won't get salary.» And a bhakta is typing for pleasing Krsna and for glorifying Krsna. So the typing looks the same, but the bhakta's typing and a karmi's typing is different.» (Discussion with Syamasundara)

Without bhakti, every living entity must be subjected to force. This is what it means to be in the material world. The living entities are forced to struggle for their existence. Whether subtle or gross, the governments are forcing their citizens, the parents are forcing their children, the husbands are forcing their wives, the employers are forcing their employees, etc., etc.. However, bhakti cannot be forced. It must be awakened by hearing.

In the meantime, however, until bhakti is fully awakened, the spiritual master must know how to apply treatment so that the disciple can remain in a healthy condition (voluntarily always endeavoring for Krsna consciousness). He must know when to be forceful in his preaching and when not to be.

Srila Prabhupada always serves as a perfect example for everything. I, therefore looked for an example of how Prabhupada dealt with a disciple who was in anxiety about his service. Naturally, in some cases Srila Prabhupada was very forceful. But the following letter is a perfect example of how Srila Prabhupada dealt with a disciple who apparently was under too much pressure and anxiety. Prabhupada made an adjustment to not only ease the disciple's pressure about his service, but to remind him of the ultimate goal so that he would not give up the endeavor to achieve it:

Dear, Please accept my blessings. I am in due receipt of your letter dated December 10, 1972, and I am very much disturbed to hear from you that you have become disturbed in your mind. Do not be disturbed. There is no cause for anxiety. You are doing your best to serve Krishna, that is very much appreciated, so do not lose enthusiasm out of frustration, that will spoil everything. Krishna Consciousness means we should always be satisfied and happy, not that we must work something impossible, becoming overburdened, and then because we are unhappy by so much trouble we lose enthusiasm altogether and give up all hope. No, if too much endeavor is there, that is to be avoided. By all means we must preserve our spiritual status, that is the point, not that we are mad after big buildings, many devotees, life members, this, that, no, these are only ways to engage the devotees so that they may apply the principles of devotional living to some kind of work for practical realization of these principles. It is not the result of the work we want. If only one person daily, if we sincerely preach to such one person in a day, that is sufficient, never mind big, big programs. So my request to you is that these things not bother you. I have instructed Tamala Krishna and Syamasundara to send you men so they will do it, rest assured. Krishna does not like to see His sincere devotee suffer or become frustrated or depressed. He will not stand idly by in any such case; so do not fear on that account. Krishna has got some plan for you, always think in that way, and very soon He will provide everything to your heart's desire. (End of quote)

Similarly, your letter indicates that you are also under excessive pressure (at least at the time that the letter was written). I cannot force you to continue under these circumstances. I must preach, but I must also know when to be forceful in that preaching and when not to be. Your letter indicates that I should not use forceful preaching. Nonetheless, I must try to inspire you to go on in your pursuit of Krsna consciousness, no matter what.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Thank you for your letter, although after reading, it still did not address my concerns. What does the word «sometimes» mean in the sentence that you «sometimes» visit the bramachari ashram? It leaves me with the impression that your visits are rare. And what is your «prasadam distribution? Does that mean that you are just cooking and selling prasadam and keeping the profits for yourselves? And as far as Sunday Feast attendance is concerned, that is not so impressive. Uninitiated congregational members attend the feasts every Sunday, but an initiated bramachari is enthused to attend the Sunday Feast because it is a wonderful opportunity to preach. The rest of the week, he should also be absorbed in some preaching activity.

You made no mention of regular attendance of morning programs. You gave no assurance that you are daily chanting all of your rounds. You mentioned nothing about regularly reading Srila Prabhupada's books. This leaves me wondering about your consciousness.

So, you claim that you don't want to take my precious time by burdening me with explanations about your devotional service, but the only result is that by not giving me more information, you are taking more of my time by putting me into greater concern about your spiritual life. Please alleviate some of my concerns.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

I really do not know what you are expecting from me. Your letter states that you have no faith in the process of Krsna consciousness, you have no faith that a spiritual master can help you, you've stopped chanting japa, and you are still attached to your former habits of taking intoxication. Of course, I can accept that you must have at least a little faith that I can help you, otherwise you would not have written to me. But what more can I say than what I would say in any lecture that you might have heard me give in Odessa? Faith begins and develops from hearing.

Just this morning I was using the example in class that Vai- snavas must sometimes act like physicians who must tell a patient who has an illness that could lead to death, that they have such an illness. The patient may not be able to accept it, but still the physician has to tell them. Usually he has to describe the dangers and consequences of the patient's disease before the patient can realize just how serious their illness is, and from that point, they can be prompted to undergo whatever is necessary to become cured. It seems you have not realized how serious the disease of material life is. Otherwise you would be more serious about taking the treatment.

Material life in the form of sense gratification and intoxication leads to an endless repetition of suffering. There is absolutely no end, unless one stops committing the sins which are the cause of future suffering. Lord Caitanya appeared to distribute the most potent medicine for curing this disease, and Srila Prabhupada and his followers have proven that the medicine works. You may ask, «Where is the proof? Everyone is still growing old, suffering and dying, but the suffering of the devotee and the suffering of the nondevotee are simply not the same. The devotee who voluntarily accepts suffering as the Lord's mercy, experiences the Lord's protection even within that so-called suffering condition. Just as Queen Kunti prayed to the Lord for calamities to happen in her life again and again, because when the calamities appeared, she could only remember Krsna. And by only remembering Krsna she was completely calm because she knew that she would no longer see repeated births and deaths within the material world. Someone may doubt that such freedom from repeated birth and death will come, but a devotee experiences «relief» in this life. He need not wait until death. The devotee's relief comes from his remembrance of Krsna, and his confidence in Krsna's constant protection. There is no safer place than in Krsna consciousness. Just like a child who cries for his mother, when the mother picks up the child, the child naturally feels safe simply by knowing they are in the mother's arms. Or, just like the story Srila Prabhupada tells of his son when he was in householder life. Prabhupada was riding on the train with his young son. The conductor approached them and asked the son for his ticket. The son said that he didn't have a ticket. The conductor, attempting to have a little fun with the boy, looked at him very seriously and said, «Oh, you have no ticket? Then you must immediately get off the train?» Prabhupada's son looked very frightened, but within a moment, the fear immediately left his face and he turned to the conductor and said, «Oh, there's no problem. He (pointing to Srila Prabhupada) is my father. It was spontaneous and natural. All fear went away just by knowing his father was there. Similarly, a devotee who constantly feels the Lord's presence can be relieved from all anxieties of this material world. Therefore, if one does not practice with faith the activities which invoke remembrance of Krsna, one will not experience this relief. It is simple as that.

So it is up to you. When you are in difficulty, what will you do? Will you look for relief in intoxication? Or will you look for relief in Krsna consciousness? You must practice this in your day to day life. Do not think that it will automatically happen at the end of life without practicing now? Krsna wants to see just how much you want to be Krsna conscious. If he sees that your desire is strong and determined, then He will help. Be assured of this.

So you should begin chanting in this spirit-not as some ritual. Chant with faith that Krsna is present in His name and He is listening to the sincerity of your chanting. If He sees that you really want to remember Him, He will appear to you in His name, and He will appear to you in your mind. Relief is as close as your desire to remember Him always.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

I'm sorry, but if you recall a letter that I sent to yourself and… dd once before, I told you that if some practical problem comes up regarding your service or others' service, before you bring the matter to my attention, you must follow the proper procedure and bring it to the attention of a higher authority. Although the action that you've described was committed by Prabhu, your Temple President, there is still … Prabhu who is the Regional Secretary. You made no mention in your letter that you brought the matter to his attention. And if you also remember, I also said in my previous letter, that if you have gone through all the higher authorities, and have no alternative but to bring the matter to my attention, then you must include in your presentation of the «complete picture» by including the explanations of all those who have been involved.

Now judging from what you have said about …dasa, it seems that he is not willing to give any explanation. So, if that is a fact, then you obviously cannot include his explanation to me. But, then you must approach Prabhu. If he is not in , then he can be reached by E-mail just as easily as I can be reached by E-mail, and I am sure that must know his E-mail address.

I have already forwarded your complaint to , so he already has it. Now please appeal to him for resolving this matter. It is not something that I can investigate while I'm here in Vrndavana.

Meanwhile, my concerns are certainly there to all of you, and I am anxious to hear how this matter gets resolved so that everyone can go on in their service in a cooperative mood. I am very disturbed to hear your allegation that the women are not treated as human beings. If you are going to make such a strong statement, then there better be some substance to this claim. Otherwise, it is a very strong statement to make. If there is substance, then the source of this mistreatment must be rooted out. But is it possible that you are exaggerating a little bit, and that you are just using such strong words to emphasize this point about the car? Please consider this point when you discuss the matter with dasa.

Thank you for your concern about the welfare of the Sankirtan mataji's. I am always concerned for their welfare, too, as well as for all the sincere devotees who are struggling to push on Lord Caitanya's Sankirtan Movement under very austere conditions. I am indebted to all of you.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Thank you for your last letter. I am very happy to read that you are feeling some progress... so much so that I read your letter several times.

The other day we were giving a class about spiritual strength, explaining how the source of all spiritual strength is Lord Balarama. By the power of His devotional service, Lord Ananta Sesa (Balarama) holds up countless universes. He is not even aware of the universes, which rest like a single drop on one of His thousand hoods. Although He does not rest upon anything, everything rests upon Him. This is inconceivable, isn't it? How can someone not rest upon anything but at the same time unlimited universes rest upon Him? He must be the source of all strength. It is the strength of Lord Ananta's devotional service which gives Him that strength. Devotional service is the most powerful, because by the strength of devotional service, one can invoke the Supreme power from the Supreme Lord.

Similarly, in Bhagavad-gita, ([3.43](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/3.43)), Lord Krsna states, «Thus knowing oneself to be transcendental to the material senses, mind and intelligence, O mighty-armed Arjuna, one should steady the mind by deliberate spiritual intelligence \[Krsna consciousness\] and thus, by spiritual strength, conquer this insatiable enemy known as lust.»

If we want the strength to overcome the fight against maya, \[lust\] then we must seek that strength from the source. There is no other way that one can be successful in the fight against the illusory energy. There may be so many diversions, distractions, and seemingly failures in the effort to fight against the illusory energy, but most important of all, is that one should never give up fighting. If we surrender to the enemy \[lust\], then, just as in war, when one side surrenders themselves to the other, they are immediately taken prisoner, their independence is lost, and they are forced to work under the jurisdiction of the enemy. Similarly, if we give up the fight against maya, then she will take us captive, and we will be forced to serve the illusory masters of lust, anger and greed with no hope for release.

But as long as one can fight, one must fight. Don't allow yourself to become so weak that you can no longer fight. The more sense gratification you engage in, the more difficult it will be to fight. But the more struggle you take for devotional service, the more you will get strength from Lord Balarama to go on fighting.

Do not become disappointed that your devotional service is not always unbroken. You will have to pass through many more tests before devotional service will come to the unbroken stage. But, as you are now experiencing, your «desire to solve and to stop this problem has become a lot STRONGER». This means that you are getting help from the source. Please always remember this, and you will be successful.

Regarding the curse, you should beg Krsna to forgive you for this, and be willing to accept whatever reaction He wishes to give you for such behavior. Such willingness will also invoke the Lord's mercy, so that one day He will release you completely from all reactions to past sinful life. Just when He will do this is ultimately up to Him. But you must also beware that there must be an effort on your part as well.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

I thought that you should know that although I did receive your letter during the Sucharevo seminars, it wasn't until just this morning, August 27, that I found out that the letter was from you. Had I known it was from you, I would have probably contacted you much sooner.

Although I can read Russian and understand a significant amount of what I read, I have a very difficult time reading handwritten Russian. Although your letter was written in English, it was signed in handwritten Russian. Therefore, when I read your letter back in July, at the time, I was unable to determine who the letter was from. I often get letters from neophytes with questions leaning towards sahajiyaism. I have grown a little bit callous towards such letters, and I tend not to take them seriously. I therefore, just put your letter in my stack of unanswered letters, thinking that it was probably from another immature bhaktin.

However, now that I know that this letter is from a senior disciple, if a senior disciple of mine expresses anything about themselves related to sahajiya ideas, I must take it much more seriously. Just yesterday, on Srila Prabhupada's Vyasa Puja, we were reading and speaking about this very subject about the dangers of sahajiyaism, and its potential for causing so much disturbance.

Therefore had I known that this letter was from you back in July, I probably would have arranged a meeting during that visit. Now I am faced with holding this letter and not being able to meet with you. At the same time, I am concerned that six weeks have passed since the time that you wrote that letter, and it could be a lot longer before we would get the next opportunity to meet. Maybe you could write me another letter, and be more specific? I could then try to address your problem by letter.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

From time to time devotees approach me thinking that I get involved in making marriage arrangements for my disciples. Some devotees have even approached me asking what I thought about them marrying you. I did not give them an answer, mainly because I can not be a match-maker for my disciples. However, having read your latest letter, I am thinking more and more that you are a prime candidate to be married. In fact, as you should know, having hopefully read the letter printed in my letters book about the conditions under which I felt my female disciples should be married, in that letter I clearly explained all those conditions:.

It is not that I'm thinking that the above answer will solve all of your problems. I will not change the manner with which I try to deal with problems disciples have in the execution of their duties. We still must look for Krsna conscious solutions. You have suggested returning to as a solution, but I cannot give an answer to that. I have no idea what facilities are available in… for you to return to. You would have to inquire about that from devi dasi yourself. Nonetheless, you do have my blessings to go ahead and inquire.

You have written that at the present moment you don't see how you can live in Krsna consciousness. My answer to that is you must see how to live in Krsna consciousness, no matter how or where you are living. Life without Krsna consciousness is hell. I have given the example so many times that life without Krsna consciousness is like a bellows in a blacksmith shop. It breathes but there is really no life. Moreover, real life is eternal. So there is never a choice of not «living». We are always living. Krsna consciousness is really our only choice. Maya is never an option except for those who are mudha-dhi, or ignorant about the real goal of life. They may appear to be living, but there is no real gain. Ultimately only loss. Therefore if adjustments are necessary, we should adjust our lives in such a way that we may continue in Krsna consciousness. I believe that the recommendation I have given above is a viable way for you to adjust, without having to lose sight of how to «live» in Krsna consciousness.

Whatever adjustments that will come for you, please, in the meantime, I beg you to faithfully chant your rounds, read Srila Prabhupada's books, and take the association of more experienced devotees who are willing and able to encourage you in devotional service. You should know that I am praying to Sri Sri Krsna Balarama here in Vrndavana to give you and all my disciples the strength to live «in» Krsna consciousness.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

If the desire to associate with a woman is there in your heart, then it is not necessarily a fact that by going someplace else, you will leave the desire behind. It's very likely that you will just carry the desire with you no matter where you go.

Of course, it is always best to avoid householder life if it is at all possible. As you know, householder life brings so many problems. But if you really want to avoid householder life, then you must know that you will not be able to avoid it simply by getting away from this mataji who has allured you into her trap (by targeting you and directly approaching you). The only way to avoid householder life is by gaining the higher taste in preaching, by being fully engaged in meaningful service, and by developing a genuine attachment to associate with and serve those who are genuinely renounced. Such association is meant to inspire you to «tighten up your spiritual life», by leaving no gaps both in your service and in your hearing and chanting. In other words, you must not leave any room for maya. If you have already become weakened by having contemplated this desire, then it will not be so easy to pull yourself back into the spirit of renunciation. It will take great determination and surrender.

If no such determination exists in your heart, then it will be of no value to just move elsewhere. You must be convinced that this determination for spiritual life must be your main goal, now and for the future. Then, and only then, would I even consider allowing you to go to another yatra, and even then, even if your authorities permitted you to do this, I would suggest that it should be only for a brief period of time with the understanding that you will return to shortly after your respite.

… Maharaja will be arriving in very soon (if he already isn't there). I suggest that you discuss this matter with him, and then take his decision about whether or not you have the determination which I am talking about. If he feels that you do, and if he agrees that going to another yatra would be beneficial for you, then you can work out some plan with him for AFTER the marathon. Meanwhile, please give all the determination you have to making this a very successful marathon. Do not even think of going elsewhere now. Right now, you should be thinking about preaching and book distribution.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Regarding and his wife, although his behavior may appear that he has been possessed by a demon, I find it difficult to conclude that that is the case. Most probably their attachments simply run too deep to allow them to take wholeheartedly to the process of devotional service at this time. This does not mean that they will not be able to come back. There is never any loss for past service rendered. But either it may take a greater spiritual force to relieve them from past reactions than that which has been available to them up until now, or they may have to go through more material life before they become exhausted enough to become more resolute in devotional service. This may happen very quickly, or it could take many lives. Whichever it may be, still, they have come in contact with the Lord's holy names, and the Lord's devotees, and therefore all we can do is try to attract them to the beauty of Krsna consciousness by showing them how to best serve the holy name. Nobody can be forced to execute devotional service. We must try to attract them by devotional service. It is not that we are powerless to help those who are illusioned. We must always sincerely try as if their devotional service depends upon us. But ultimately, after giving our most sincere effort, the result must be left in Lord Caitanya's hands.

I definitely recommend that if there is still hope to speak with him, he should definitely reduce his number of rounds back to 16. He should be encouraged to earn an honest means of livelihood to maintain his family, and he should be encouraged to attend Nama Hatta programs. If he is unable to do that, we may just have to patiently wait for either of the above to take place.

## Letters to godbrothers

Dear Prabhu,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Today my heart is very heavy. I am greatly feeling the loss of a wonderful Vaisnava whose presence on this planet gave me much inspiration in my own Krsna consciousness. Last night Lord Krsna took Gour Govinda Maharaja back to His eternal abode. I am sharing the following thoughts with you because you are a very dear godbrother, and I want to share these feelings with somebody dear.

Last night, I was in the flat where I'm staying near Prabhupada's Samadhi Mandir, speaking with Dhanurdhara Swami and Bhurijana Prabhu. My servant, Vedasara dasa knocked on the door, entered, and said that H.H. Gour Govinda Maharaja had left the planet just about 30 minutes prior. I was shocked. My mind was reeling and the only thing I could think of was «Why would Krsna take him from us now?» Somehow, I couldn't believe it. I jumped up, quickly put on my shoes, and moved faster than my legs have ever been able to carry me since my right one was broken here in Navadvipa two years ago.

The three of us walked briskly to the Conch Building where Maharaja always stays. I had to force my way through a crowd of devotees who had gathered outside his door. The chanting of Hare Krsna was emanating from within the room and outside as well. As I got to the door, the few devotees stationed there to hold back devotees acknowledged who I was, and fortunately, they immediately allowed me to enter. The room was full. Many senior Vaisnavas were present, GBC men and sannyasis. About 25 of Gour Govinda Maharaja's disciples were also present. I looked towards the bed and saw Maharaja's body propped up against the back of the bed, legs extended forward, and immediately dropped to pay full dandavats.
