At this point, somehow I still could not believe that he had passed. «Maybe he's in samadhi, I thought. About six to eight of his disciples were feverishly massaging Maharaja's legs. Another was holding a picture before his eyes. I couldn't see the picture, but I suspected that it might have been a picture of Srila Prabhupada. Another disciple was holding on to Maharaja's head so that it would not fall forward. I looked intently at his face. His mouth was slightly opened and his eyes were closed. His face seemed effulgent and had a very peaceful appearance. His body was garlanded with several garlands. He was not wearing a kurta, though it could barely be noticed since many garlands were hung around his neck, covering the whole front of his body.

Naturally everyone was quite sober. Some of his disciples were crying loudly, some softly. Tears were streaming down their faces as they stared intently, seemingly waiting for a sign that he would come back to external consciousness. Seeing their faces, and then looking back and forth between them and Maharaja's face, I gradually began to feel my eyes start swelling up, too. It wasn't long before I found myself crying, although not incessantly as some of his disciples were crying, but enough to realize that I was experiencing separation from someone who was very dear to me. I remembered the lamentation I felt when I found out that Srila Prabhupada left the planet. Though not as intense, still I was lamenting that I had not taken advantage of Gour Govinda Maharaja's association while he was with us, and now I was facing (even if still not fully convinced of it) the thought that I may never have that opportunity again. All I could do was stand in awe, chant, and within my mind pray that maybe Krsna would let Gour Govinda Maharaja stay with us a little while longer. I stood motionless for about 20 minutes, continuing to chant and then finally decided to move elsewhere towards the back of the room where I could see several GBC men having discussion. I walked over and saw that Jayapataka Maharaja was holding a pad of paper on which there was a diagram, which looked like an explanation of how to place Maharaja's body into samadhi. It was right about then that it finally hit, «Gour Govinda Maharaja really HAS left the planet!» Tears came again. I wiped my face with my upper cloth, and decided to ask someone what had happened. The first person who seemed available for questioning was Virabahu Prabhu. So I asked him if he knew of the events leading up to his departure and if anyone had actually confirmed that he had left.

He told me that Gour Govinda Maharaja was having a meeting with someone. He was speaking about Lord Jagannath's lila in Dvaraka, and then all of a sudden he started having difficulty breathing. He said that he could not speak anymore. His voice was strained like he was slightly choking. Then his disciple ran over to help him. Externally it seemed as though he was having a heart attack. I was then told that many devotees were called and they began chanting, but very quickly Maharaja just looked to either side, then looked straight ahead. His stare became distant and then he effortlessly opened his mouth and departed. A doctor came shortly thereafter, pulled out a stethoscope, took his pulse and confirmed that Gour Govinda Maharaja had left his body. Many of his disciples were having difficulties accepting that he had gone, and therefore they were continuing with kirtan, massage, and were holding the picture. When any suggestion was made to bring his body on -parikrama, they were resistant to the idea. However, a few of his disciples, who had more of a grasp on the situation, were consulting with the leaders that were present and were dealing with all the practical details related to taking Maharaja's body on -parikrama. They were also discussing where his body should be placed in Samadhi—Bhu- banesvara or Mayapura.

By this time, I began deliberating on the significance of Gour Govinda Maharaja's departure. It was the Appearance Day of Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakur, the year of Srila Prabhupada's Centennial. He left his body in Mayapura Dhama. Many devotees were present, both godbrothers and disciples, loudly chanting the holy names. He was speaking about Lord Jagannatha just minutes before he departed. How could there be a more auspicious departure from this world? So many more things could be said about this and I have no doubt they will be said in due course of time. There is no trace of doubt in my mind that Gour Govinda Maharaja entered into Lord Krsna's eternal lila last night and this world is now bearing the loss of a highly elevated soul and intimate associate of Lord Krsna.

At approximately 11:00 PM, Maharaja's disciples finished preparing his body for parikrama. No less than 150 devotees went in the procession down the stairs and into the courtyard and down the long walk to Prabhupada's Samadhi. Upon entering the Samadhi Mandir, they blew conchshells, the doors were opened and one of Maharaja's disciples led a very sweet kirtan, ei baro karuna vaisnava gosai. At the end of the kirtan, we all loudly chanted «Jaya Gour Govinda Swami, Vaisnava Thakur». Nobody seemed reserved to loudly chant out his name, even from amongst all of the godbrothers. To me, it all seemed quite natural and spontaneous and again I shed tears seeing the devotion coming from all those who were present. We continued the chanting and circumambulating inside the Samadhi three times, then we left and carried Maharaja's body to Srila Prabhupada's hut to take darshan of Sri Sri Gour Nitai. There his disciple continued the chanting, this time leading us in chanting Ohe Vaisnava Thakur. Again we chanted Gour Govinda Maharaja's name, Jayapataka Swami offered another garland to him and the procession continued into the temple room. Upon entering, Maharaja's body was brought before Srila Bhaktisiddhanta and Srila Prabhupada, who were both sitting on Vyasasanas in the rear of the temple. The kirtan continued for a short time and then the procession next moved to Lord Nrsmhadeva's altar. The doors were opened and then Lord Nrsmhadeva cast His merciful glance. Jananivasa Prabhu brought tulasi from Lord Nrsmhadeva's lotus feet, and placed it on Maharaja's head. Then the curtains were opened to reveal Their Lordships Sri Sri Radha Madhava, Sriman Mahaprabhu, and Asta Sakhi Vrnda. Maharaja's body was placed on the high step before Radha Madhava's altar. At this point, we had puspanjali, Jayapataka Maharaja offered a full arotik, while we all chanted Gurvastakam, and each senior devotee came to offer flower petals to his feet. I offered my petals, and then once again fully prostrated myself at his feet. The kirtan continued for approximately 20 minutes more. The procession then went outside, and Maharaja's body was placed in his car. His disciples had decided that they wanted to bring Maharaja's body back to Bhubanesvara where he would be placed in Samadhi according to full Vaisnava rituals. Shortly thereafter, the car left, I paid full dandavats once again, and then returned to my room. It was 12:30 AM. I was tired, but I simply could not get to sleep. All night long I was reliving the events of the evening, while asking myself again and again, "Why did Krsna take Maharaja from us now?" It was a very sobering experience for me. As you can see, I was very attached to Gour Govinda Maharaja. And throughout the day, I've come to find that so many other senior Vaisnavas were similarly attached to him, too. As I said once before, there is more that I could say. There is more that I want to say. But at this point, I can't find the words to express all that I'm feeling. I'm just barely getting a glimpse of what it means to be attached to asadhu. Can you believe that I've spoken to Gour Govinda Maharaja only once for two hours? And that meeting with Maharaja was when he was there in Hartford almost two years ago. It was then, that I really got the opportunity to see the qualities of someone who is really attached to speaking only about topics related to Krsna. Pyari Prabhu, I beg you to please bless me that I too, will some day develop a taste for glorifying and broadcasting the Lord's pastimes in the same way that our godbrother has shown me by his wonderful example. I know that I have no hope for Krsna consciousness in this life unless I can get mercy from the Vaisnavas.

Your fallen servant, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear Prabhu,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Having you along was definitely my pleasure. I can't begin to tell you how much happiness I feel seeing you living in the association of devotees. Don't worry about what you've not done in the past, devotees like your good self will do even more, having been through so much and come back. It is simply separated by time.

Meanwhile, if I can assist in any humble way, please give me the opportunity to serve an old friend and godbrother. It is a great pleasure for me.

I haven't ordered the tapes yet, but I will very soon, and I'll ask them to mail them out to you immediately. Unfortunately, it's Russia, and it may take a little time— especially because it's Christmas time. But it shouldn't be too long.

Hoping this meets you and your family in good health.

Your servant, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear Prabhu,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

How are you?

My health is holding up, although I'm always having to deal with nagging bronchial asthma attacks. But by now I just accept that as part of living in this material body. Otherwise, I can't complain. The body is holding up enough to enable me to travel and preach. That is Lord Caitanya's mercy on me.

How are things in CIS? Did you successfully overcome the demoniac attempt of the government to get us thrown out of the country?

By Krsna's grace, everything is still developing, although things are starting to take a little different shape as ISKCON starts to mature there, and all the old problems of the past are working their way into the scene as well. (i.e. bramacharis becoming householders, temples struggling to maintain, some negative propaganda, etc.). I have some nice videos of some of our preaching there. If you have a VHS, I can bring some of them with me when I come to Mayapura for you to look at.

Also, I've attached a bunch of files that contain more similar letters that were sent to the BBT. I thought that you might like to browse through them when you get a little time.

Are you able to come to the Mayapura festival this year?

I'm definitely coming, although I wish I could call it a festival. I have no choice in that I MUST attend the GBC meetings. They are always a bit austere for someone so disinclined as myself to deal with such matters. But associating with devotees like your good self whenever possible makes it into a festival for me. I don't know yet how much longer I will remain after the GBC meetings. It fluctuates from year to year, depending upon the demands that I'm faced with by the end of the meetings. But, I'm sure that I will be there long enough after the meetings to be with you.

Thank you very much for delivering all of those messages for me. They have all been replied to, and I have no doubt that your assistance has been instrumental in getting the replies. However, at least for now, it seems that I've been able to establish a more direct connection. But I will definitely keep your offer in mind, should the connections become cloudy again. Thank you very much for offering.

I'm presently writing this letter in Madhuha Prabhu's home in Prabhupada Village, North Carolina. There is a growing community of Prabhupada disciples here, and I regularly visit (twice a year) to associate with all of them. Yesterday, over 20 devotees went out to distribute books for World Enlightenment Day. They even inspired an old deadbeat like me to grab a book bag and hit the parking lots. It was ecstatic. Everyone got together last night at Madhuha's for a feast of spaghetti and bharats, Gauranga potatoes and halavah. We had some nice kirtan and then I showed a couple of videos about our preaching activities in the Ukraine and Russia. Everyone applauded after my short talk and the video, so I think everyone really appreciated seeing that our movement is going on nicely and expanding in such places as the former Soviet Union. Such nice association with my godbrothers really gives me life. I also had some very nice association with Radhanath Maharaja about two months ago in Moscow and St. Petersburg. It was really ecstatic. I wrote a little it about it in a letter to one of my disciples. I thought that I would share a small excerpt with you, to illustrate how much I appreciate this kind of association.

Jayadvaita Maharaja left Moscow a couple of days before Radhanath Maharaja arrived on the 13th. In Mayapura this year, I begged Radhanath Maharaja to visit Russia. I have been hankering for more of his association ever since I visited his temple in Chowpatty (Bombay) in January 1994. Radhanath Maharaja is a very dear godbrother to me. He became a great source of inspiration for me to take up Nama Hatta preaching after I traveled with him about 12-13 years ago, just before I accepted sannyasa. At that time, Maharaja had a very successful preaching circuit in the region of Ohio and West Virginia, giving cooking classes, lectures, and inspiring so many people to take up Krsna consciousness. Satsvarupa Maharaja, who was at that time my GBC, encouraged me to travel with Radhanath Maharaja, and to this day, I am forever grateful to him for his strong recommendation. After traveling with Radhanath Maharaja, I simply tried to follow his pure, simple, and enthusiastic preaching example, and gradually began generating similar programs throughout New England on the strength of what he had taught me. Although I can't say the programs I conducted were anywhere near as potent as those which were conducted by Radhanath Maharaja, still, the mere fact that these programs were going on is a testimony to the help that he gave me. Radhanath Maharaja is already so busy with his GBC duties, but after three years of persistence, he finally relented to my appeal to visit Russia. Although he claimed to have come only to spend time with me (which was the basis of my request), my plan was to share his association with the devotees in Russia. We attended practically all the preaching programs together, sometimes sitting and giving lectures together, but since I preferred to sit at his feet and relish hearing his simple but highly potent presentation of Krsna consciousness, I avoided speaking as much as possible. Maharaja has a wonderfully inspiring way of telling pastimes, especially those from Caitanya Bhagavat. Knowing how attracted I am to Caitanya Bhagavat, you can just imagine how much I relished sitting and hearing him speak. And when he was not lecturing or leading kirtans, practically all the rest of the time we were sitting and discussing pastimes from Caitanya Bhagavat. His association is always a real boost for me. Also during Maharaja's visit, both of us went to Moscow's Radio Krsnaloka one afternoon to answer questions from the callers. Our radio station in Moscow is gaining greater and greater popularity. In fact, to give you a little picture of how popular the station is, last year Radio Krsnaloka invited their listening audience to attend a special program for all Radio Krsnaloka listeners. Of course, it was expected that not all listeners would come. I don't know what the standard ratio is between those who respond to invitations to those who are listening, but over 1,500 people came and over 700 big books were sold as well! Those are pretty impressive statistics. And the radio station has even increased in popularity since then. Anyway, usually whenever there is a visiting preacher in Moscow the radio station invites him/her for a two-hour session to answer questions for the listening audience. They were expecting me to come, but since Maharaja and myself were going everywhere together, we decided to go on air together as well. We switched back and forth, Maharaja answering one question and myself answering the next. Although a couple of questions were a little «strange» (one was from a person who wanted to know how to deal with being possessed by ghosts), all the rest of the questions were very nice. The calls just kept on coming in, and we were both surprised that there was not one single break between callers. We could have continued for a lot longer, but, since we were taking the train to St. Petersburg that same evening, we had to finish after 2 1/2 hours. Before leaving one devotee who ran the recording studio, which was part of the radio station, asked if I would come to do another recording. I agreed, and when Radhanath Maharaja and myself returned to Moscow three days later, we returned to the studio and recorded several bhajans together which the devotees now have plans to make into a CD. They were hoping to have it produced by January, but I have since heard that there were some problems with the recordings and it may take them a little bit longer. Radhanath Maharaja left Moscow early in the morning on September 20th. Although he didn't promise that he would come back, I could see that he was overwhelmed by his visit. The devotees really loved him, and were anxious to have him come back again soon. Moreover, shortly after Maharaja's departure I wrote him the following letter:

Dear Radhanath Maharaja, Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Srila Prabhupada. I would like to take this opportunity to express my gratitude for conceding to my repeated requests to visit us here in Russia, and to give me your much- appreciated association. I know that if I want to continue to render some insignificant service to Srila Prabhupada's mission, I must associate with devotees like your good self and take inspiration from your exemplary preaching. I beg you to please be merciful, and to not forget this insignificant//pa's desire for more of your association in the future. The devotees here are anxious for this association as well, and moreover, I am anxious to spread it into other regions. I promise that I will not let you get overextended by your visits. I will do all that I can to protect you from that. I am 100% convinced that if we are to give shelter to the devotees under our care, they must see their leaders engaged in loving exchanges with each other. When they see their GBC's sitting and relishing Gauranga's katha from the lips of their godbrothers, when they see them dancing together and embracing each other in a mood of loving affection, and when they hear their GBC offering genuine words of appreciation and glorification of his godbrothers, this will strengthen the devotees appreciation for the movement in which Srila Prabhupada has left us to preach. Unlike many other artificial attempts that unfortunately we sometimes witness to keep devotees in ISKCON (i.e. by overt criticism of Vaisnavas), I am convinced that this is the most genuine way to convince devotees of the greatness of our Society's leaders. When I am in your association there is no pretension on my part. I genuinely relish it. I am still planning to arrive in the United States by the middle of November. If there is any chance in seeing you then, please send me an email.

Whether it was my letter, Radhanath Maharaja's experience in Russia, or the appeals of the Russian devotees, I cannot say at this time, but I have since heard that Maharaja is going to be making another short visit to Moscow in early December. It is my misfortune that I will not be there, but I'm sure that Moscow devotees will be excited to see him again.

Thank you very much for the opportunity to associate with you Prabhu. I look forward to more when I come in February.

Your servant, Niranjana Swami

## About family relationships

The only way I see that you can potentially avoid the scenario you've described is by directly confronting your parents about your personal convictions. I say «poten- tially» because you may not be successful. However, the most important aspect of your devotional service at this point in time, is to intelligently, maturely and sensitively explain how you have come to accept a deeper commitment to Lord Krsna's teachings. This may take a little time, and you should be prepared to give it some time. As in all aspects of devotional service, you must learn how to be active for the service of Krsna, but, at the same time, ultimately you must leave the result up to Him. Your parents may not accept your chosen way of life. But, at least if you deal with them in a mature way, and show them that by taking to Krsna consciousness more fully, you do not intend to reject or disrespect them. Show them, that by the mere fact that you are approaching them is because you do respect them, and therefore you respect that they will be sensitive enough to listen to you. If they don't, then at least they have exposed themselves as the ones who are being insensitive.

If you thoughtfully approach them in this way, they may gradually come to realize that they are dealing with you, their daughter, and not that they are dealing with the Hare Krsna Movement, through you. Even Lord Krsna tells Arjuna «vimrsyaitad asesena yathecchasi tatha kuru». «Deliberate on this fully, and then do what you wish to do.» Arjuna was encouraged to make his own decision. Otherwise, how can there be devotion, if one is forced or manipulated into doing something. You must show them how you are making your own decisions as a result of having your intelligence satisfied by the words of Lord Krsna. And you have chosen a life of devotion.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

There are a couple of statements within your letter which lead me to think that you are not yet ready to move into the temple, however, there is one particular statement which leads me to believe that you should soon take some more decisive action about where you live. I'm referring to the statement, «When I go home, I come under the influence of illusion and have no power to resist them» (your parents). First, I would like to refer to a letter written by Srila Prabhupada to one of his disciples:

«...Regarding your manner of behavior with your parents who are not in Krishna Consciousness; I may inform you that you should treat four different classes of men in four different ways. A devotee should love God and God's devotees. A devotee should make friendship with devotees. A devotee should try to enlighten innocent persons, and a devotee should reject opposite elements. As Father and Mother they should be offered proper respect according to social custom, but you cannot accept their non- Godly instructions. Best thing is to avoid misunderstanding, to remain silent without any affirmation or negation of their instructions. We should try to keep our friendship with everyone in the world, but we cannot sacrifice the principles of Krishna Consciousness on being employed by some relative of this world. Don't let them know that you do not approve your parents instructions, but at the same time you should be very careful in dealing with them...»

I agree that you should not want to listen to offensive statements about the Lord and His devotees. And if your attempts to discuss about living in the temple with your parents always leads to this, then it seems that such discussions may have to be avoided. However, it seems that, at some point, you will have to at least confront your parents about your personal convictions and aspirations of how you want to live your life as a devotee. You will then have to intelligently, maturely and sensitively explain how you have come to accept a deeper commitment to Lord Krsna's teachings. As in all aspects of devotional service, you must learn how to be active for the service of Krsna, but, at the same time, ultimately you must leave the result up to Him. Your parents may not accept your chosen way of life. But, at least if you deal with them in a mature way, and show them that by taking to Krsna consciousness more fully, you do not intend to reject or disrespect them. Show them, that by the mere fact that you are approaching them is because you do respect them, and therefore you respect that they will be sensitive enough to listen to you. If they don't, then at least they have exposed themselves as the ones who are being insensitive.

If you thoughtfully approach them in this way, they may gradually come to realize that they are dealing with you, their daughter, and not that they are dealing with the Hare Krsna Movement, through you. Even Lord Krsna tells Arjuna « vimrsyaitad asesena yathecchasi tatha kuru». «Deliberate on this fully, and then do what you wish to do.» Arjuna was encouraged to make his own decision. Otherwise, how can there be devotion, if one is forced or manipulated into doing something. You must show them how you are making your own decisions as a result of having your intelligence satisfied by the words of Lord Krsna. And you have chosen a life of devotion.

If they refuse to accept your way of thinking, then it seems that you will have to make some kind of decision in regards to your residence, in order to avoid accepting their «non-Godly instructions».

I hope that this letter brings you a clearer understanding of the task that lies ahead of you, and that you will always remain committed to service to Lord Caitanya with love and devotion.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

One and a half years is not long for a war. When one takes to Krsna consciousness he or she is declaring war against the illusory energy. That war will continue right up until one becomes completely free from maya's influence. Whether the war is with parents, friends, relatives, attachments, or whatever, we will always be under attack. For a devotee, the best strategy for war is to be well protected. Duryodhana wanted Krsna's army for war. Arjuna was happy to have Krsna because as stated in Bhagavad-gita, wherever there is Krsna and wherever there is Arjuna, certainly there will be victory. Krsna will always protect that devotee who depends exclusively upon Him for protection.

As I have told you before, the best way to deal with your parents is to externally go through the motions of a materialist, but internally, within your heart, you should cultivate your attachment to Krsna by constant remembrance. Remembrance of Krsna is the best protection. For instance, you should try to remember the following verses of Caitanya Caritamrta. These verses are very instructive for you:

Raghunatha dasa thought, «How shall I be able to get free from the hands of the watchmen? How shall I be able to go with Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu to Nilacala? Since Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu was omniscient, He could understand Raghunatha dasa's mind. The Lord therefore instructed him with the following reassuring words. «Be patient and return home. Don't be a crazy fellow. By and by you will be able to cross the ocean of material existence. «You should not make yourself a show bottie devotee and become a false renunciant. For the time being, enjoy the material world in a befitting way and do not become attached to it.» Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu continued: «Within your heart, you should keep yourself very faithful, but externally you may behave like an ordinary man. Thus, Krsna will soon be very pleased and deliver you from the clutches of maya. «You may see me at Nilacala and Jagannatha Puri, when I return after visiting Vrndavana. By that time, you can think of some trick to escape. «What kind of means you will have to use at that time will be revealed by Krsna. If one has Krsna's mercy, no one can check him.» In his way, Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu bade farewell to Raghunatha dasa, who returned home and did exactly what the Lord told him. After returning home, Raghunatha dasa gave up all craziness and external pseudo renunciation and engaged in his household duties without attachment. When Raghunatha dasa's father and mother saw that their son was acting like a householder, they became very happy. Because of this, they slackened their guard. (Madhya Lila, Chapter 16, [Verses 236-244](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-madhya/16.236))

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

You seem to have a few different desires burning within you, and they are all starting to put pressure on you at the same time. If I were to thoroughly answer each question you've asked individually, without considering each of the other questions in your letter, then each answer would in some way have to contradict the other. This phenomena that you are experiencing is described by Lord Krsna in the Bhagavad-gita as the «intelligence of those who are irresolute is many-branched». Therefore you are looking for an instruction to hold on to so that you can become more resolute.

However, it is for this very reason that I am publishing excerpts from my letters for all of my disciples to read. For instance, you are asking me if you should be married. You are also asking if you should take responsibility for your son. The mere fact that you are asking me these things is an indication that these are desires that are there in your heart, and you're asking me if you are capable of giving up these desires. Yes. Potentially, every living entity can immediately surrender fully to Krsna. But, experience shows that most cannot. «Sa mahatma sudurlabhah». Therefore gradual purification is necessary.

In my letters you will find a broad spectrum of instructions, which are replies to questions I get about matters such os those you are sending to me. I am giving the broad spectrum because somewhere within there is an instruction that most disciples can hold onto if they are serious about advancing in Krsna consciousness. Gradually all of my disciples will need to find that place from which they can move forward in their advancement without having to fall back once again into materialistic life. Materialistic life is NEVER an option. Life without Krsna is HELL. And I never want to see a disciple step back into life without Krsna again. This brings great pain to the spiritual master.

I will not tell you to abandon your son. But if you do not, then you must decide if your attempts to rescue him could prove to be fatal to your own spiritual life. There is a graphic story in the 11th Canto of Srimad Bhagavatam about two pigeons. An excerpt from that story is as follows:

«One day the two heads of the family went out to find food for the children. Being very anxious to feed their offspring properly, they wandered all over the forest for a long time. At that time a certain hunter who happened to be wandering through the forest saw the young pigeons moving about near their nest. Spreading out his net he captured them all. The pigeon and his wife were always anxious for the maintenance of their children, and they were wandering in the forest for that purpose. Having obtained proper food, they now returned to their nest. When the lady pigeon caught sight of her own children trapped within the hunter's net, she was overwhelmed with anguish, and crying out, she rushed toward them as they cried out to her in return. The lady pigeon had always allowed herself to be bound by the ropes of intense material affection, and thus her mind was overwhelmed by anguish. Being in the grip of the illusory energy of the Lord, she completely forgot herself, and rushing forward to her helpless children, she was immediately bound in the hunter's net. Seeing his own children, who were more dear to him than life itself, fatally bound in the hunter's net along with his dear- most wife, whom he considered equal in every way to himself, the poor male pigeon began to lament wretchedly. The male pigeon said: Alas, just see how I am now destroyed! I am obviously a great fool, for I did not properly execute pious activities. I could not satisfy myself, nor could I fulfill the purpose of life. My dear family, which was the basis of my religiosity, economic development and sense gratification, is now hopelessly ruined. My wife and I were an ideal match. She always faithfully obeyed me and, in fact accepted me as her worshipable deity. But now, seeing her children lost and her home empty, she has left me behind and gone to heaven with our saintly children. Now lama wretched person living in an empty home. My wife is dead; my children are dead. Why should I possibly want to live? My heart is so pained by separation from my family that life itself has become simply suffering. As the father pigeon wretchedly stared at his poor children trapped in the net and on the verge of death, pathetically struggling to free themselves, his mind went blank, and thus he himself fell into the hunter's net. The cruel hunter, having fulfilled his desire by capturing the head pigeon, his wife and all of their children, set off for his own home. In this way, one who is too attached to family life becomes disturbed at heart. Like the pigeon, he tries to find pleasure in mundane sex attraction. Busily engaged in maintaining his own family, the miserly person is fated to suffer greatly, along with all his family members. The doors of liberation are opened wide to one who has achieved human life. But if a human being simply devotes himself to family life like the foolish bird in this story, then he is to be considered as one who has climbed to a high place only to trip and fall down.»

So you must be careful to not become like the father pigeon who was thrown into so much lamentation about his offspring that he fell into the hunter's net. Certainly Mother Rambhoru's recommendations may be a more stable position for you to move forward from. But, ultimately whatever position we may want, we can get only by the sanction of Krsna. If we depend upon His decisions, then we can be safe. But if we sacrifice our Krsna consciousness, there will be so much danger. Please always remember this, and Krsna will help.

Whatever you decide to do about your son, it seems that it would be far safer for you to take him on your terms, and not on the terms of your parents. This means that it would be best that you are not financially dependent upon them, that you are not forced to live with them, and that you have the means to maintain your Krsna consciousness (and his). Otherwise, it sounds too much like the net of the hunter.

Regarding your decision to surrender to Prabhu's wishes to devote your full time to fundraising, in principle, you made the right decision. You will never be a loser by taking the humble position and surrendering to instructions which may seem unpalatable for you to surrender to. I'm grateful that you accepted the instructions of your authorities. I suggest that you continue to follow that plan until my next visit, and we can then reevaluate everything again at that time. Naturally, this would also mean that your desire to be involved in scientific preaching will have to wait until then, too.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

When I heard from dasa that you were often heard criticizing devotees, I was naturally disturbed to hear this, especially since I have given so many lectures on this subject.

However, you should understand that my decision that you should return to was based on the evidence that I had, that was not happy staying in the gurukula. I have all along said to you that you should take care of ... It is far more important that YOU, as her mother, are caring for her growth in Krsna consciousness, rather than the gurukula. Forcing her to stay in gurukula will be neither beneficial for her, nor for your relationship with her.

Therefore please understand that I want you take responsibility as …'s mother to help her during this time. There may be many difficulties that will come from this, but you should not leave yourself feeling that if loses interest in Krsna consciousness, that it was due to neglect on your part. I am not saying that you are neglecting her now. I just don't want you to neglect her, if the thoughts do come to your mind.

Moreover, I've had long discussions with dasa about his relationship with the congregation in . I am hopeful that if you behave as a Vaisnavi, and restrict yourself from unnecessary criticism, that you will be able to live as a productive congregational member of the yatra. Although I am not very pleased to hear that you have been overly critical of devotees, still I am very much inclined to encourage you to move in this direction. Please try your best to do this and hopefully when I visit towards the end of this year, I will find both you and situated in a much better position than you have been situated in ...

## Marriages

I only give my blessings for disciples to get married, after the following conditions have been met:

1\) the man and woman have properly associated for some time according to appropriate standards (I do not have time to stipulate what the «appropriate» standards are. I believe that they have been delineated in previous resolutions from 1991 that were given to all CIS temples).

2\) «AFTER» the local authorities give a favorable written recommendation that they have been conducting their affairs in a responsible and mature way.

3\) If it's a male disciple of mine who wishes to get blessings from me for entering married life, he should give me a clear written proposal for their future as householders including: a) How long are they planning to continue living separately in the ashram. b) Whether or not there is an approved plan by the local authorities. There are instructions in my book of letters about this, and they should be thoroughly reviewed and considered by both the man and the woman. Then their proposal should be discussed with local authorities. When the local authorities are satisfied that there is a reasonable plan on the part of the husband whereby... dasa had already written to me about what to do when a bramachari under his care wanted to change ashramas. I answered his question. In principle, I will support the decision of any bramachari disciple who wants to be married, as long as he goes about his transition from bramachari to grhastha in a socially acceptable, moral, and responsible way. Thebramachari should sit down with his temple authorities, express his desires, and should have a serious discussion about a realistic future as a householder within his temple's community. Usually, there are two primary considerations. 1) Who will he marry? 2) How will support himself as a householder both in the short range and the long range?

Of course, there are many other considerations for becoming a householder. But these are the obvious and immediate considerations that arise as soon as the bramachari has decided that he wants to change ashramas.

Then a very serious discussion should take place between the bramachari and his temple authorities. The bramachari must be patient for the answer to #1. There may be no immediate candidate. Furthermore, the Temple President may be unable to help, though he should certainly try. Patience, however, is an absolute necessity, along with dependence upon Krsna. Regarding #2, some kind of answer should come out of a discussion with his Temple President on that question. I believe that I have already given some guidelines to this within my published letters.

For now, that is my answer. Please continue the discussion with dasa.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

When a bramachari decides to get married, if he is actually responsible, he will think about these things before he rushes into a marriage. Your letter makes it sound as if the Temple was responsible for finding a place for you to live just because your husband and yourself were helping with the Sunday Feast. Then, on your own initiative, you moved to , but, for whatever reason, they did not want you to continue to stay there. Again, this was your husband's responsibility to see that he fulfilled the conditions which were being asked of him. It seems to me that your husband is now facing the reality that married life means taking responsibility. He cannot expect to be financially supported by an ISKCON temple if he wants to live independently as a householder. There are exceptional cases when ISKCON will give «basic» financial support to a householder, but it largely depends upon the level of responsibility the householder is taking, and the qualification the householder has for fulfilling that responsibility. This must be determined between the Temple President and the householder on a case by case basis, and should be done «BEFORE» the householder decides to marry and live independently. My understanding is that both of you rushed into marriage without taking these things into consideration. You are therefore now experiencing these problems.

If you are now hoping that another temple will accept both of you, then your husband will have to approach other Temple Presidents, and he will have to give a solid proposal for substantial service from both himself and yourself to the Temple President. The Temple President will need to be convinced that both of you are capable and willing to be servants within the Temple, and that you will be willing to live separate within the temple. There may be some rare cases where the Temple President may agree to provide independent householder facilities, but such cases are very rare. If the Temple President wants a reference then he can be directed to dasa, and if necessary, I can be contacted as well.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Thank you for providing me this information about yourself. I am always anxious to hear reports from my disciples.

Regarding your present plans for marriage and for supporting yourself and your family life by accepting service from the temple, in principle, this is always an acceptable way to live as a householder. However, experience shows that the temples can not always provide the financial support that a householder needs (or in some cases, «wants»). If Prabhu is confident that you will be responsible, and that you will give your full attention to the service he gives to you, then he may be willing to support you. But, you must be prepared to be responsible. Grhastha life forces responsibility, because a man is driven by his need for economic development. Once there is wife, family, possessions, then there must be so much endeavor to maintain them. Grhastha life is a very serious commitment, I hope that you are responsible enough to get yourself educated about the commitments which must be fulfilled as a grhastha from those who are senior and experienced grhasthas. Do not be fooled by the «glitter» of grhastha life. Not only are there duties to family, but in every step of the way you must always be conscious of your duties to guru and Krsna. There must be sacrifice. Otherwise to work with a fruitive mentality will only cause further suffering.

In principle, I support those who feel the need to be married. But I do not support irresponsible grhastha life. You must be prepared to work hard. Krsna can take care of everyone's needs. That is a fact. But that does not mean that we should live irresponsibly and expect that Krsna will take care of us. Human life is meant for responsibility. Please remember this, and then act on it. Only on those conditions do I approve anyone's proposal for householder life.

Regarding future letters, the first request is to get a good English translation. Your last letter was not terrible, but it wasn't that good, either. It makes it difficult for me to answer letters which are not clear. Otherwise, you may write any kind of a report that you would like me to know about. It is also good to include the impressions that your authorities have about your service and about your general character as well. This helps me to have a well-rounded picture of yourself, rather than just giving me your own impression which may be slanted to make yourself look better than you are actually doing. Honesty is a very important characteristic of the guru/disciple relationship. I always appreciate honesty when my disciples write letters to me.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

As I previously mentioned to you, the renounced order of life from bramachari life is for those bramacharis who never consider marriage as an option to life. Do you fall into that category? It seems not. Therefore, you should decide whether or not you want to remain a bramachari or prepare to enter into householder life. In my opinion, you have been thinking so much about whether or not you should become a householder, then you should just move in that direction

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Thank you very much for your thoughtful letter. It's clear that you are taking the idea about entering householder life seriously, and I approve of this approach.

As soon as the thought of becoming a grhastha starts to linger within the mind of a serious bramachari, then that in itself is a pretty good indication that householder life is most likely the direction that he will need to move in. This does not make the bramachari any less serious because he is contemplating householder life. I used the word «serious» only because I do not necessarily believe that householder life is the solution for somebody who was never even a very serious bramachari. In your case, I have therefore used the word serious.

Now please continue to remain serious, and become a responsible grhastha. Do not just jump into householder life without «seriously» considering how you will be able to live as a serious householder. Consult with your temple president, and consult with senior, and exemplary grhasthas as well. Remember Narada's instructions to Yudhisthira Maharaja on ideal family life. «One should desire as much wealth as necessary to maintain body and soul together. Those who desire more than that are to be considered as thieves and therefore deserve to be punished by the laws of nature.» Accept a wife who is also serious about sharing your own serious views on how you must live as a householder. There will be many tests, but if you know what your duties are as a householder, and do not neglect your spiritual duties of chanting, hearing and associating with Vaisnavas for the pursuit of Krsna consciousness, then you will pass through all tests under Krsna's protection.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

By now, I am sure that Prabhu has given you the explanation of why I have not been answering letters. So I will therefore not bother explaining it to you here.

I appreciated your honest and straightforward letter. You accurately described the plight of a woman in the Krsna consciousness movement who has realized that her body is not getting younger, life is passing her by, and she still has not found the protection of a Krsna conscious husband. It's a story that I have read many times, more than you could probably imagine. And up until now, I have not been able to take any direct action to alleviate the problem described in similar stories other than emphasizing the same points I always speak about in my lectures. «We have to depend upon Krsna.» Some devotees benefit from my words. Some benefit less than others.

I am not a matchmaker. I will not become a matchmaker for my disciples. I personally see it as a very dangerous business for a sannyasi. If other godbrothers of mine can do it, without getting themselves too entangled, that is their glory. My policy is that it must be the business of local leaders. They are in the best position to help. In your case, it seems as though either there is nobody there who fits your expectations of someone who could be a suitable husband, or, there is nobody there who has a similar feeling about you. Otherwise, obviously, if both of these criteria were fulfilled, you would undoubtedly be married by now. That being the case, and the fact that you are dangerously associating with someone for reasons other than pursuing marriage, then it seems that you should look for other alternatives, at least try to disentangle yourself from improper association and get yourself situated closer to full temple activities. Aren't there flats closer to the new temple that you could switch with?

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

In principle, I do not want bramacharis engaging in business of any kind, unless they are very serious about preparing themselves for marriage. Up until now, there has been no serious discussion, that I know of, that you are mentally prepared to make a commitment in marriage. Neither does it seem to me (from your letter) that you are ready for marriage. Generally, when a bramachari becomes restless about his status as a menial servant, and when he wants to seek some profession, it is an indication that marriage should be considered. However, too many times, marriage is not given enough serious consideration and preparation. I would not want you to pursue marriage whimsically. It is much better to be informed about the responsibilities of married life, and to have a clear picture of what lies ahead after marriage. You have none of these right now. Therefore, I do not want you to pursue marriage at this time without first speaking to you about it myself.

Neither am I very inclined to permit you to go to either Vrn- davana or Bhaktivedanta Manor. I see neither of those options as solutions to your restlessness (although it might be possible in the future). Again, you must be better informed about the commitments required for those options as well.

It seems obvious, however, that you need some kind of change in your life soon. Your letter sounds very restless. If you would take it very seriously to study in the Bhakti Sastri course in Sucharevo, I would inquire on your behalf whether or not they would be willing to accept you. But, you MUST take it seriously. It is not a fact that only Temple Presidents will be permitted to take this course. Neither is it necessary that you become a Temple President after taking the course. However, you must seriously take the course with the intention of increasing your understanding of the basic Krsna consciousness philosophy. Something like that is always beneficial, no matter what plans you will make in the future.

So please tell me if you would seriously take up the Bhakti Sastri studies. If you will, I will speak to Vaidyanath dasa when I am in Moscow next week, and will ask him to consider your application to take part in the course. I cannot promise that they have room. I know that they have a limited space. But I will try. In my humble opinion, that would be the best course for you to follow right now.

I will wait for your reply.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

A few days ago I met with Prabhu in Moscow, and he told me that the prasadam business is now donating regularly to maintain the Temple and to pay for the Sunday Feasts. I was very happy to hear about this very nice example. One of the primary duties of a grhastha is to give in charity, and the best charity is supporting temples where Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu's glories and names are being spread. Please remain steady in this example, and know that it has my full support. Our movement needs more and more exemplary grhasthas who know what their duties are, and are prepared to undergo whatever austerities are required to fulfill them. Of course, please also remember that another primary duty of a grhastha is to daily hear the pastimes of the Lord. As Narada Muni describes in his instructions on «Ideal Family Life», such daily hearing will act to gradually awaken detachment within the heart of a grhastha, in the same way as a man gradually awakens from a dream. If you build your grhastha life on the spiritual platform, then surely you will become successful in your endeavors.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

My position regarding the marriage of my disciples is that I give my blessings for the marriage once every other factor has been considered, and both candidates for marriage are themselves convinced on the strength of their own association (according to moral standards), that they want to be married to each other. From the sound of your letter, it appears that you have come to this decision. dasa, however, mentioned as a footnote, that, astrologically, the two of you appear to be a incompatible to some degree. Whether or not that astrological calculation needs to be considered is something I am not qualified to speak on. Personally, I have very little faith in astrological calculations. I have rarely found somebody that I would consider qualified enough to give «qualified» astrological calculations. It is on that basis, that I have such little faith in astrology, although, in principle, naturally, I accept astrology as a bona fide Vedic science. I leave it up to the two of you to decide whether or not you want to take the «astrological advice». As for me, I have never made any decision in my life based upon an astrological calculation. In fact, I have never even requested an astrological calculation for my life. I've heard some householders say that it is essential when considering marriage compatibility. I have heard others say it is not.

Therefore, in conclusion, my «blessings» for this marriage will follow the same course as it follows for all marriages. If the two of you are convinced enough to go ahead with the marriage, taking all necessary factors into consideration, then you certainly have my blessings to go ahead with the marriage. As you know, in principle, I believe that practically all my female disciples should be married. There may be some few rare exceptions, but they would be considered very rare. A good Krsna conscious marriage is always the best choice for a sincere, young devotee as yourself.

Hoping this meets you in good health, and blissfully engaged in taking part in Lord Caitanya's glorious movement.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

I am very happy to hear that you are «dependent» upon the morning program in the temple. Actually, when I hear statements like this it gives me great hope for the future. It is as if my prayers to Sri Sri Gaura Nitai are gradually being answered. When I meditate on the Kiev temple construction (or for that matter, any temple construction), I am always thinking how the temple can serve the needs of the devotees living outside the temple, just as much as the devotees living inside. The temples are meant to serve the householders (those who are maintaining a home) by giving them a spiritual atmosphere to come and hear and chant about Krsna. Therefore, when my disciples become householders, I expect them to keep their connection with their local temple, attend lectures and kirtans, and as much as possible to give their support (financial or otherwise) to the temple that nurtures their Krsna consciousness. Now that you have understood the importance of the temple morning programs, and since you also know how difficult it is to maintain a temple, you must try your best to reciprocate with the temple for providing you the nice spiritual atmosphere which is so necessary for maintaining your spiritual life.

Therefore your idea to do a catering service for selling hot prasadam meals is an excellent idea. It is an ideal way for a householder to earn an honest means of livelihood and it has my full support. The householders must have some facility to live nearby the temple, and if the prasadam business can gradually grow to provide work for more and more householders to work together to both live near the temple and to give support to the temple, that would be very, very nice.

Please try to begin work on this, and when I visit Kiev next (next month) you can give me a report of your progress.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear dasa,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Yes, I did receive your letter dated 14.11.94, and carefully went through it. I'm sorry I forgot to respond to your question about the two letters, primarily because I had no interest to receive this letter from the devotee who is looking for a wife. I know who it is, and he should first think about becoming a serious devotee before he starts bothering me with such concerns. I'm not a matchmaker. As far as the other letter is concerned, you can send it to me on COM.

You can tell dasa that I am very disappointed to hear that he would even think of doing such a thing as refusing to cook prasadam for the Sunday feast and not telling anybody that he did not do it. All my lectures about the importance of executing one's duties, and he does something like this. How am I supposed to think? If he's having a problem performing his duties, then he should say something. But to just not do it, and not say anything is totally irresponsible and very disappointing. You can tell him that I am asking for an explanation from him and an assurance that he will not do something like this again. How can we even depend upon him to worship the deities during the marathon? What if one day he decides that he doesn't want to offer Sri Sri Gour Nitai Their lunch or offer Their arati? This would be terrible.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Regarding your question about marriage, in principle, if any of my female disciples feel the need to be married, then I would probably agree, because in principle, most women need the protection of a husband. But just when and how this comes about, is not always the same for each devotee. Some women feel the need to go longer than others without being married, and there is nothing wrong with that. But it should not be too long. I generally tell the temple leaders that if a woman is older, and has proven herself to be a steady and sincere devotee over many years of service, if she expresses a desire to be married then she deserves to be married to a good husband who will give her protection. It should not be that such a woman should just be forced to marry some irresponsible bramacari, who simply cannot control his senses and runs around proposing marriage to women, thinking that that will solve all of his problems. But unfortunately, I continue to find here in the CIS that when the more senior and responsible bramacaris are desiring to marry, they look for and insist on getting wives who are practically half their age. Thus I've seen so many senior, respectable matajis simply waiting and hoping that someday they will be married, and they are still patiently waiting. The only encouragement I can give them is to read Srila Prabhupada's purport to Srimad Bhagavatam, 3rd Canto, 21st Chapter, 28th verse. As much as possible, I therefore leave this matter in the hands of the local authorities, and constantly ask them to please help in this regard.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

January 9, 1995

Dear devi dasi,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

A few days ago, I received your letter (undated) from …d.d.. I'm now back in the United States, so I must answer your letter in a very complicated way. I hope that it gets to you.

I understand your reasons for being unable to live in the asrama. I also fully trust your intentions to remain committed to your vows taken at the time of initiation.

As far as your decision to marry is concerned, I don't know him, so I also have to trust your judgment. You say that he is sincere, so I am hopeful. As you know, I expect more from my own disciples than just a commitment to chant 16 rounds and follow the regulative principles. If they are serving their husbands, then their husbands should at least be committed in some way to serving Lord Caitanya's Sankirtan Mission. The wife's duty is to assist her husband, but the husband's duty must go beyond the sphere of maintaining his family. Lord Caitanya declares that there will be no impediments in family life, as long as one dedicates his life to telling everyone he meets about the instructions of Lord Krsna as given in Bhagavad-gita and Srimad Bhagavatam. Otherwise, there is so much entanglement. One does not have to give up his work, but he is to offer the fruits of his work unto Krsna. In this age, the best way to offer one's fruits is by serving the sankirtan mission. If one cannot engage in Sankirtan himself, then he should assist someone who is constantly preaching the Lord's glories. Then he can do good to others as well as himself.

I hope that this letter meets you in improved health.

As always, I remain, Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear dasa,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I am simultaneously dealing with your problems and desires to become a grhastha along with similar desires from other disciples. I am therefore sending you a copy of a letter to another disciple, which addresses many of the same concerns that you have. This letter also includes a copy of a letter which I had written to containing instructions which were to be given to you. In this portion of the letter you will see that I left blank any words which would indicate who this portion of the letter was addressed to. So I am sending it to you exactly as I had sent it to this other disciple and it is as follows:

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear dasa,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

In regards to your letter wherein you requested further instructions for the future, now that you have decided that you want to become a householder, I must leave part of this matter in the hands of your local authorities. Although you have made your decision to be married, it does not mean that Krsna is obliged to immediately hand over a woman to satisfy your sexual longings. Kardama Muni performed austerities for thousands of years to prepare for householder life. The qualification for householder life is NOT just because a bramacari cannot control his senses.

In principle, I will support a disciple's desire to pursue householder life, provided the disciple demonstrates at least some maturity in his Krsna consciousness and in his approach to householder life. A significant determination for this maturity lies in one's history as a bramachari. It is not just a matter of saying that one wants to, or needs to be married. There must be some evidence of responsibility. If a devotee has shown himself to be excessively irresponsible throughout his bramachari life, then it is just an illusion to think that householder life will solve his problems. Householder life is also a tremendous responsibility. It is NOT just an opportunity for sex life. If you want to associate intimately with a woman, you must expect to assume the burden of responsibility for getting that opportunity. Don't think that it's simply a matter of changing the color of one's cloth, and then all license is there to be with a woman!

For instance, I have recently written the following to a couple of leaders about another disciple who also started to consider entering householder life:

«Regarding dasa, he has written me a letter explaining his situation. I have not been able to answer it yet, due to lack of time. I'm presently traveling in Canada and have been very busy, but I hope to be able to answer his letter within one or two weeks. In the meantime, however, even if he wants to become a householder, it does not give him a license to become completely independent from all ISKCON authority in , which is what he seems to be doing right now.

If he wants to distribute books at a higher percentage to start earning money for becoming a householder, in principle, that is all right. But he should make an appropriate arrangement with one of the existing authorities. That authority should determine a reasonable percentage which would allow him to both support himself by covering his own maintenance costs if he is either living in the temple or traveling with a book distribution party (in which case he should give a fixed amount per day to the temple for food, etc.), and to give a small percentage to that temple. This is the way it is usually done in most temples when a devotee decides that he wants to become a householder and 1) his desire is supported by his authorities, and 2) he wants to distribute books to prepare himself economically instead of going out to work for karmis. In fact, considering the economy in and the number of books that are distributed on an average, 40% to the future householder and 10% to the temple is a very reasonable arrangement. In other countries, I have even seen higher percentages, (even 50%) especially when the devotee covers the cost of his own maintenance. If they want to become householders, they should get used to the idea of meeting their own maintenance costs. Moreover, they should not think that the money they make is theirs to do whatever they want with. It is to prepare for their future as a grhastha, and should therefore be set aside for their future in a TEMPLE account. Not that the future grhastha can just use the money to go out and buy fancy clothes or fancy commodities at his own whim. This is totally irresponsible and cannot be supported.

The point is, that dasa should not think that he can just immediately become independent from all local authority just because he wants to become a grhastha. This is not good. If he cooperated with the authorities, I'm sure that a suitable arrangement could be made for him to distribute books with other distributors rather than just living by himself. He would feel more support this way. And if he is cooperative, a leader should try to support such an arrangement.» (end of quote)

The point in the above message, is that the devotee was given instruction for accepting responsibility for grhastha life. In this particular case, the devotee chose to distribute books and to begin earning some independent income in preparation for accepting the responsibility for getting the opportunity to associate with a woman in married life. This is a sign of responsibility. If this is not done by distributing books, then some other alternative must be pursued, such as working with prasadam sales, or with other householders who are earning an income. In some cases, although these cases are certainly more rare, the aspiring householder may have been holding a very responsible position within the temple, and the temple may be in a position to provide some support for the aspiring householder during his transition from bramachari to householder and even beyond. But the temple is not obliged to support every bramachari who wants to become a householder. This is simply not done.

So now a significant part of the instruction for your future has been delivered to you. I will leave the rest of the matter up to your local authorities based upon your own ability to fulfill the qualifications which I have outlined here in this letter. Just how long this will take will depend upon your own actual history as a bramachari, your ability to act upon these instructions now, the consideration of your authorities, and ultimately Krsna's decision in this whole matter, which is the ultimate perspective that we must always pursue in all aspects of our devotional life.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

As I have already said, the above letter applies to you as well. I have therefore impressed upon you the need to be responsible and to not be independent from authority. If you are not willing to follow these instructions, then I am unable to help you at this time. I will not even consider your appeal for going elsewhere until I'm actually there in which will not be for at least another two months. So, in the meantime, I expect you to adjust your life to these instructions which I have so carefully presented to you and to your godbrother for your progress in Krsna consciousness.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health.

I remain, Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

July 16, 1995

Dear dasa,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have your letter which was given to me by Mahasakti dasa when he arrived to travel with me about one month ago. Now that I've taken one day completely off from all other responsibilities, I am getting an opportunity to catch up on some of my correspondence.

It seems that you have already made your own decisions with the exception of who you will marry. I do hope that you have included some plan for Krsna consciousness in your decisions. You've made no mention about that in your letter, and neither did you mention anything about your commitment to the vows you had taken at the time of initiation.

Although I am not condemning your actions, it would have been better if there could have been some dialogue between yourself and myself before you charted your course back into materialistic life. Marriage is NEVER a solution to one's problems. Krsna consciousness is the only solution. It is possible that, if properly followed, marriage may help an unsettled bramachari become more steady and responsible. But if Krsna consciousness is neglected, what is the difference between your work for the maintenance of a family and the work of a fruitive worker? To avoid the materialistic way of life as a householder requires great determination. It makes the difference between a grhastha and a grhamedhi. If you neglect the association of saintly persons, and neglect your spiritual practices, then there is very little chance that you will be able to maintain the required determination - especially since you have already indicated that your determination is already so weak.

Therefore it would be far better if you could inform me what decisions you have made to remain a devotee, despite your desire to be married.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear devi dasi,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I received your message and noted the contents.

Yes,… Prabhu's advice was appropriate, although, due to the nature of the topic, unfortunately, it was inconclusive.

Neither Prabhu, nor I, can make arrangements for your marriage. Such arrangements should be made locally, with the help of the leaders of the temples. But even then, there is no guarantee that there will be somebody who will marry you. Neither the authorities, nor the spiritual master can demand that a devotee MUST marry a particular devotee. There must be some compatibility, or at least a willingness to associate to determine compatibility, and with the husband, there must be some good indication that he is capable of handling the responsibility of family life without allowing his own determination for Krsna consciousness to deteriorate.

Unfortunately, in many cases, I have found an unwillingness on the part of the temple leaders to get involved in these affairs. It is most unfortunate, and therefore, oftentimes many good devotees feeling the need for a marital partner are being neglected. On the other hand, also unfortunately, there are many cases where an unqualified candidate for marriage pushes himself (or herself) forward as a qualified candidate, although marriage, from a spiritual point of view, will offer them no solution. Instead they only degrade by their uncontrollable material desires. It is immature, irresponsible devotees like these who have largely contributed to the indifference in the minds of many leaders, who have therefore chosen to not get involved in helping anyone find a suitable marriage partner.

In your case, I do not question your sincerity, since as you say, you have never even meditated upon the idea very seriously. Those who constantly meditate on the idea, neglecting their spiritual practices, while aggressively seeking any possible means to convince the chosen partner of their heart, are generally the ones who create problems for everyone, including themselves. Because you are sincere, I don't expect that you will become like this. But I do expect you to humbly express your need to one of the local leaders. Then it is up to you to remain patient and dependent upon Krsna. Execute the duties that you have been given by your spiritual master, depending upon Krsna for everything, and have faith that only Krsna can satisfy your needs. In this regard you should read SB 3.21.28 text and purport.

I will also include here excerpts from a letter I recently wrote to one of your godsisters on this subject. It is as follows:

«Now if you want to know what my desire is, then I'll tell you quite clearly. I desire that you will make spiritual advancement in Krsna consciousness. That is my desire. If you require the relationship with a husband who can help you become steady, chaste and devoted in service to Krsna, then I also desire that you accept whatever arrangement that is ordained by Krsna....

…If you want a good husband, then approach Krsna for that, and patiently wait for Krsna's choice. Nothing can happen without Krsna's sanction. Just like Kardama Muni— he meditated upon Krsna for the fulfillment of his desire to get a wife of like disposition. Why? Because he knew that only Krsna could fulfill this desire. Therefore Krsna fulfilled Kardama Muni's desire beyond his expectation. Similarly, you should also depend upon Krsna's choice, and engage steadily in service to guru and Krsna thus invoking Krsna's mercy....

....Now, of course, Krsna has also given you a choice, and your choice must also play a role in the final decision of who you should accept as a husband. But at least, FIRST there must be some RELIABLE and TANGIBLE indication that someone is interested in marrying you. That should be the very first indication.» (end of quote)

Considering everything that I've written above, you still have been given clear instructions for the immediate future.

During my visit to , I asked you to go ahead and resume your education. And I also gave you some guidelines. You should continue your association with devotees as much as possible, attend the morning programs, maintain your initiation vows, and you should distribute books and preach whenever possible. I also asked you to be careful in your philosophical studies in school, to always analyze according to the Krsna conscious perspective so that you will not get contaminated by all the impersonal philosophy that is taught in the schools. That's all. How do any of these instructions come into conflict with a pursuit for a diploma? I do not understand. Whatever duties you will have there will always be some struggle... some test from Krsna. This is your test. You should now perform your duties, depending upon Krsna, and if Krsna sanctions this need for you to be married, then you should accept it, and go on in Krsna consciousness with the help and support of a Krsna conscious husband. If He does not sanction it as quickly as you would like, you still cannot lose. Either way, there is always gain when one acts in Krsna consciousness.

When I was in Samara from August 8-10, one evening I gave a Bhagavad-gita lecture which would be very beneficial for you to hear. It directly relates to this point. I honestly do not know who would have a copy of this lecture, but I suggest that you contact Syamesvari devi dasi in Tolyatti and ask her if she has a copy. If anyone has a copy of it, I'm sure it would be her. Then tell her that I asked you to ask her to copy this lecture and to mail it to you. I'm sure that she would be happy to do it.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health.

Your well-wisher, Niranjana Swami

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Dear Bhakta,

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

The reason why you were told that you were not following my instructions was because if you read and understood my last letter to you, you would see that I had never intended for you to leave your wife and son, and travel all over the place to distribute books. I told you to preach «IN », and to try to support your family by book distribution. The main thrust of my letter was instructing you about the importance of preaching in... And my letter to your wife explained the importance of her supporting you in that effort.

I also approved of your idea to purchase a van, but I was under the impression that you were going to be using this van for doing book distribution locally. Somehow this plan to travel all over developed, but it didn't come from me. Of course, yes, you did show me the van when I was in , and I was told then that you were going to travel with other bramacharis, but I was simply not aware of the full situation.

The point is that you have a wife and child, and that they are devotees. It is artificial for you to abandon them, to leave them alone all the time, and even worse, your wife explained to me last time I saw her in that you were forcing her to go out and make money while you were traveling around everywhere. When I was told that you were not even distributing books to support your family, this is what I did not approve of. Therefore my instructions to you (in my letter) were that you were to preach in , distribute books in the region near …,and you were to engage your wife by having her assist you.

Now I hear that you do not have any money, and you want to go to Moscow to distribute books. The impression this leaves me is that you are simply going to abandon your family once again. This is too artificial. You're not ready to take sannyasa, and your family is not either. Now that Ukrainian books are so cheap can't you somehow support your family by distributing Ukrainian books— in Ukraine? If not, then what can I say? You cannot expect the temple to support your family. You're either going to have to distribute books SOMEWHERE or you're going to have to support them by some other way. I gave some guidelines to Bhakta about how householders could support themselves by prasadam sales. Maybe you could talk to him about this, if book distribution in Ukraine does not work.

Because you have this family responsibility, I cannot give you exact detailed instructions about what to do to support your family. I can only give you guidelines, which is what I've tried to give you here in this letter.
